# Remember App — Build Changelog (v1)

Moved out of the HTML file's header comment as of v0.1908. Newest first. Contains entries v0.1907 back to the earliest recorded. Going forward, add new entries to the TOP of this file (new version of this doc each time it is delivered).

```text
  v0.1908 - HOUSEKEEPING ONLY, no behavior change: the BUILD CHANGELOG comment block (~655 KB, about 20% of the file) was moved out of the HTML header into this separate markdown file, leaving a one-line pointer comment. HTML shrank from ~3.22 MB to ~2.57 MB. Changelog entries are now kept in this file instead of the HTML.

  v0.1907 - FIX: "Today" group missing under last-opened sort after backgrounding the app. Merge on save conflict is now field-aware (viewedAt reconciled via Math.max), and a startup _correctNotesBaselineFromIdb() corrects the stale localStorage-sourced baseline from IndexedDB.
  v0.1906 - NEW: subtle, confirmation-guarded "Reset to First-Time State" button in the Debugging section. Label uses &hellip; (a literal \u2026 does not render in raw HTML).
  v0.1905 - Accurate, per-call storage error messages (_describeStorageError) for saveNotes/saveSettings/saveThemes/saveFolders; IDB-OPEN FAILED logging. Only QuotaExceededError is described as "storage full".
  v0.1904 - CONSOLIDATION onto the IndexedDB branch: HANDLER-HANG-DIAG, Worker containment, watchdog/checkpoint, hang fix in computeDefaultTagInsertion; settings JSON Copy/Import/Clear buttons; in-app System Log viewer with search.
  (NOTE: no entries were recorded here for v0.1780 through v0.1903 -- changelog-in-HTML was abandoned around v0.1780. See Remember_App_History_v2.md and the handoff docs for that range.)
  v0.1779 - TWO things, per Lynn, both follow-ups to v0.1778. (1) Raised the Home Item Metadata text size slider's maximum from 28px to 48px -- the original max was too low for her father's low vision, now generously exceeding even the app's own general UI Font Size max (40px), since this metadata text started out smaller than normal UI text to begin with. (2) NEW setting: "Show arrow" (Notes List section) -- hides the small navigation chevron on each Home Screen folder/note entry. Deliberately display:none rather than visibility:hidden, per Lynn's explicit requirement that the freed space genuinely return to the name rather than leaving a blank gap -- both arrow elements are flex children of their row, so removing them from layout lets the name area naturally expand into the space. Default true, matching current behavior exactly. Verified directly: the slider's new max is confirmed 48; the arrow toggle correctly starts on, correctly toggles the hiding class off/on, and correctly restores its state after closing and reopening Settings. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1778 - NEW: full appearance control for the Home Screen's small item-metadata text (folder item counts, note last-opened times), per Lynn's report that it's unreadable for her father's low vision at today's size and color. New "Notes List Item Metadata" Settings section: a show/hide toggle for a simpler/more compact list, a "Match general UI font" toggle with its own font-face picker (mirroring the existing Note Font Face dropdown exactly) for when it's off, plus size, color, and weight controls -- all six sharing one set of controls across both real elements (the folder's item-count text and the note's date text), which previously had two different hardcoded default sizes (0.78em / 0.72em) and no shared appearance system at all. All defaults are null/true, preserving today's exact rendering until a control is actively touched -- confirmed directly: with nothing changed, the new CSS custom properties stay unset and both elements fall back to their own existing values, untouched. Includes a live preview directly in the Settings row, per Lynn's explicit request, updating in real time as any control changes. Learned from this session's own earlier mistake and specifically double-checked this time: the new color row is registered in the custom-color-picker's Apply-handler lookup table (forgetting this exact step caused a real, silent bug for the Bubble feature's own colors a few builds back) -- confirmed working through the real swatch-click flow, not just inspected. Verified end to end: default state confirmed fully unchanged; the hide toggle confirmed toggling the body class; the size and weight sliders confirmed updating the setting, the live CSS variable, and the preview together; the match-font toggle confirmed showing/hiding its row and the font picker confirmed updating correctly; the color swatch confirmed actually saving (not just visually updating); and closing and reopening Settings confirmed every one of the six controls correctly restores its saved value. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1777 - TWO things, per Lynn. (1) NEW: two line-spacing nudge buttons in the note editor's lower toolbar, immediately adjacent to the weight buttons, mirroring the exact same long-press-then-tap mechanism (450ms delay, 90ms repeat). Unlike the weight buttons, this concept already has an established, literal visual metaphor -- three horizontal lines with tight vs. loose gaps -- so no invented abstraction was needed here. Targets settings.lineHeight, the same setting the existing Settings dialog slider already uses, matching its bounds (1.0-3.0) and step (0.1, left unchanged -- only the weight slider's step was reported as too coarse, not this one), with values rounded to avoid floating-point drift across repeated taps (confirmed directly: five taps from 1.1 lands on exactly 1.6, not 1.6000000000000003). Syncs the Settings dialog slider live if it happens to be open at the same time, the same pattern the weight buttons already use for their own sliders. Registered in all four toolbar configuration systems (hideable buttons, default order, settings row pairing, toolbar help), matching the weight buttons' own entries exactly. (2) CHANGE: the pre-existing font-weight slider in the "Text Size" bottom sheet now steps by 10 instead of 100, per Lynn's follow-up request, matching the increment the new toolbar buttons already use. Verified end to end: both new buttons exist and move by exactly 0.1 per tap; both bounds correctly clamp; the floating-point rounding holds across repeated taps; the Settings dialog slider stays in sync live; the bottom-sheet slider's step is confirmed changed to 10. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1776 - INVESTIGATE + PARTIAL FIX: Android PWA dark-mode bottom navigation bar still showing white, per Lynn's on-device test report that the top status bar fix (v0.1769/v0.1770) worked correctly but the bottom did not, without a reinstall. Found a real, likely-relevant difference: manifest.json's background_color was still a hardcoded, static "#ffffff", present identically across all three deployed manifest variants (main, stable, dev) -- and unlike the theme-color meta tag (which is part of the live page and re-reads on every load), manifest properties on Android's WebAPK system can get cached into the installed app wrapper at install time, which would explain exactly the asymmetry Lynn observed: the dynamic fix took effect immediately, the static one likely needs a reinstall to be picked up. Corrected copies of all three manifest files prepared as separate outputs (not part of this HTML file, and not directly editable here -- they're deployed independently through the project's own pipeline) with background_color changed to the app's actual current dark background (#1e1c1a) rather than white. Flagged honestly as a real, unresolved trade-off rather than silently decided: background_color is a single static value that can't dynamically match both themes the way the meta tag fix could, so setting it to dark (matching Lynn's current, specific complaint) means light-mode users would see a brief dark flash on launch instead -- worth revisiting if that trade-off turns out to matter for a meaningful share of users. Also added a `color-scheme` meta tag (light dark) directly to this HTML file -- a standard, low-risk signal that the page supports both themes, which can influence browser/OS-drawn chrome toward dark in some cases -- explicitly flagged as worth trying rather than a confirmed fix for this specific symptom, since its real effect on the Android navigation bar can't be verified without a real device. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1775 - NEW: two font-weight nudge buttons in the note editor's lower toolbar, per Lynn's extended discussion this session about making weight adjustment easier for elderly users (her father specifically) who can hit buttons and long-press reliably but struggle with a slider's precise handle. Icon is "concept 2" from a side-by-side mockup built and confirmed with Lynn first: a plain text "A" glyph, completely IDENTICAL in size and weight between both buttons (matching the existing size buttons' own plain-glyph convention exactly), with a separate thin/thick bar underneath carrying the entire +/- signal -- deliberately ruled out a version where the letter itself changed weight, since side-by-side rendering during the mockup phase showed that colliding visually with the adjacent size buttons. Placed with zero gap immediately next to the size buttons so the four read as one compact control rather than a toolbar getting more cluttered, addressing Lynn's own reservation about adding "two more buttons." Same long-press-then-tap-to-fine-tune mechanism as the existing size buttons (450ms delay, 90ms repeat), but a step of 10 rather than the 100 Lynn found too coarse -- confirmed directly that literally every existing font-weight slider in the app (the More menu's bottom sheet and all four Settings dialog weight sliders) uses step 100, with no finer-grained control anywhere already, contrary to what she'd remembered. Targets the exact same settings.fontWeight both existing sliders already use, syncing both of them live the same way the bottom-sheet slider's own handler already does, so all three controls stay consistent regardless of which ones happen to be open at once. Registered in all four toolbar configuration systems (hideable buttons, default order, settings row pairing, toolbar help) matching the size buttons' own entries exactly. Verified end to end: both letter glyphs confirmed textually identical; single tap moves exactly 10; both bounds (100 floor, 900 ceiling) correctly clamp; the bottom-sheet slider and its label correctly update live during use. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1774 - CHANGE: toast vertical padding reduced further (8px -> 5px), per Lynn's follow-up request after confirming v0.1773's fix worked. The clamp() floor formula's padding term was recomputed to match exactly (new floor: (50px - 10px) / 1.5, up from (50px - 16px) / 1.5), keeping the same established relationship rather than a fixed number that would drift out of sync with this padding value the way the original min-height did with the padding change two builds ago. At today's default UI Font Size, the toast font grows to roughly 26.7px (up from ~22.7px in v0.1773), filling the same 50px floor with the smaller padding. This is a visual/subjective tuning value I can't verify by rendering directly -- flagged as a reasonable next step rather than a final answer; further adjustment either direction is a quick follow-up if needed once viewed on-device. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1773 - FIX: v0.1772's toast padding fix looked like it had no effect, per Lynn's report -- and she correctly connected it to the min-width bug already documented in this project's own instructions (a static minimum silently becoming the dominant, limiting factor over the property actually meant to change). Confirmed directly: the same class of bug, different property. The padding reduction (12px -> 8px) was genuinely real, but the min-height:50px floor added in the same build was silently dominating it -- at default settings the content only needed ~43px, well under that floor, so the box got stretched to 50px anyway and flex-centering filled the gap with invisible whitespace, making the padding change visually disappear even though the CSS property itself had genuinely changed. Fixed with clamp(): the font size itself now grows to fill that reclaimed space directly, per Lynn's explicit follow-up request, rather than leaving it as passive whitespace. The floor branch is computed from the box's own actual geometry (content area = min-height minus top+bottom padding, divided by line-height) rather than a separate hardcoded number, so it can't drift out of sync if that geometry changes again later -- the same reasoning already applied to the theme-color fix. Verified by hand across the full range: at today's default UI Font Size (20px) the font now fills the entire 50px floor with zero leftover gap (up from 18px, itself up from the original ~17px); even at the smallest UI Font Size setting (14px) the toast still can't shrink below this same readable floor; at higher settings the preferred 0.9x-of-UI-font-size value naturally takes over once it exceeds the floor, growing the bar itself as intended. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1772 - CHANGE: toast/alert message readability, per Lynn's request. Font size was a fixed 0.85em against the static 20px root font-size -- never actually tied to the app's own UI Font Size setting at all, roughly 17px regardless of what that setting was. Now scales directly with it via --font-ui (the exact variable that setting already drives), at 0.9x -- a deliberate small step down rather than 1:1, since a toast is transient/secondary content, not full-weight UI text; recommended and explained directly to Lynn rather than picked arbitrarily. Vertical padding reduced (12px -> 8px) to give the larger font room within the same overall space, and a new min-height (50px, with box-sizing: border-box added specifically so this floor represents the true total height including padding, not just the content area) locks in today's actual current height as a hard floor, verified by direct calculation against the full 14-40px range of the UI Font Size setting: at the smallest setting the bar still can't shrink below where it is today (~35px computed vs the 50px floor), at the largest it correctly grows past the floor to fit the larger text (~70px computed), and at the current default setting the height stays essentially unchanged (~43px computed, under the 50px floor) while the font itself is slightly larger than before within that same space. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1907 - REAL FIX for a bug Lynn actually hit and precisely traced on v0.1906: notes she'd opened or edited that day intermittently vanished from the "Today" group when sorted by last-opened (editedAt sort unaffected), reliably reproducible by backgrounding the app for a while and returning without force-quitting -- consistent with mobile WebKit silently reclaiming and reloading a backgrounded tab's JS. CORRECTED an earlier, incomplete conclusion from v0.1906's own changelog: _mergeNotesOnConflict() was said to be "safe" because no whole note is ever lost -- true, but incomplete. Traced it more deeply this time and found it swaps an ENTIRE note object based on comparing only editedAt; for any note viewed-but-not-edited (the overwhelming common case), editedAt ties on both sides, and the tiebreaker silently picked whichever side the tie defaulted to -- discarding that note's real, current viewedAt in favor of a stale one whenever the stale, localStorage-sourced session baseline happened to "win" the tie. Confirmed by reconstructing Lynn's exact scenario in a Node simulation BEFORE writing the fix, and separately confirmed the OLD code genuinely reproduces it. TWO-PART FIX: (1) _mergeNotesOnConflict now merges FIELD BY FIELD instead of swapping the whole object -- content/name/metadata still follow whichever side has the newer editedAt (unchanged, correct for genuine multi-device edit conflicts), but viewedAt is now reconciled independently via Math.max, so it can never regress just because the content-relevant side lost the editedAt comparison, regardless of what triggers the merge. (2) NEW _correctNotesBaselineFromIdb(): fire-and-forget, called immediately after loadState()'s existing synchronous localStorage-based load (same non-blocking pattern already established for the one-time migration, loadState() itself deliberately NOT made async, since every one of its callers would need to change) -- reads the REAL, current notes/generation from IndexedDB shortly after startup and corrects the in-memory baseline using the same now-fixed merge, instead of leaving it stale for the rest of the session. Re-renders the home screen immediately if it's currently showing and anything actually changed. Verified by simulation, not just design reasoning: reconstructed Lynn's exact reported scenario (stale viewedAt vs. real current viewedAt, tied editedAt) and confirmed the fix produces the correct result while the pre-fix code reproduces the bug exactly; confirmed genuine multi-device content-edit merging is unaffected; and specifically simulated the race window where a user action happens WHILE the async correction is still in flight, confirming the user's fresh edit survives correctly rather than being overwritten by the correction landing afterward. node --check passes. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device -- Lynn's own test (the specific repro she described: background the app for a while, return without force-quitting, check that today's opened/edited notes still show under Today in last-opened sort) is the real confirmation this needs.
  v0.1906 - NEW "Reset to First-Time State" debug button, per Lynn's explicit request while trying to reproduce a real, one-off IndexedDB open failure: she'd noticed the welcome tour and sample-notes-installation flow both showed on her very first launch of v0.1904/1905 but never again on later launches in the same environment, and asked how to deliberately get back to that fresh state for testing. Investigating that surfaced something more significant, found and confirmed directly rather than assumed: _hadNotesKey, _myNotesGen, and the very first `notes` value populated at loadState() all still read from localStorage -- a real leftover inconsistency from before saveNotes() itself was migrated to IndexedDB in v0.1890, never updated since. Traced _mergeNotesOnConflict() directly to check whether this was actively dangerous: it isn't -- it's a safe per-note union (a note present on either side survives; nothing gets silently dropped just because one side lacked it), so this is an accidental rather than a designed safety net, worth fixing properly on its own, but not presently losing notes. This build addresses the immediate, practical need (a reliable way to reproduce and test the first-time flow) without yet tackling that deeper, higher-stakes loadState() conversion, which -- consistent with how saveNotes() itself was deliberately isolated as its own careful pass in v0.1890 -- deserves the same dedicated treatment rather than a rushed side-fix bundled into an unrelated feature. NEW _resetToFirstTimeState(): closes any cached IndexedDB connection first (deleteDatabase can hang indefinitely otherwise), clears every localStorage key under this deployment's own STORE_PREFIX, then deletes the app's entire IndexedDB database in one operation (indexedDB.deleteDatabase) rather than enumerating individual key names -- more robust against silently missing a key as the migration adds more of them over time, and guaranteed not to touch this app's separate diagnostic databases (hang watchdog, checkpoint tracker), which live under their own distinct database names entirely. New button in Settings > Debugging, per Lynn's explicit requirements: confirmed nested inside .settings-section-body programmatically (the same depth-tracking check that caught a real nesting bug earlier in this app's history), styled exactly as subdued/muted as the other advanced tools in this section, but deliberately set apart with its own horizontal rule and an explicit warning line above it, given this is uniquely destructive compared to every other tool here. Confirmation is NOT subtle at all: states plainly that this permanently deletes all notes, folders, settings, and themes, with no way to undo it, and uses "Delete Everything" as the confirm button's own label (not a vague "OK") so the action is unambiguous even at the button-press level, plus reverseButtons=true -- the same pattern this app already uses for the existing "Reset Settings" confirmation -- to keep the safe/Cancel option the easy one. Verified: node --check passes; the reset function's sequencing (close connection -> clear localStorage -> delete IndexedDB -> reload) independently tested against a mock simulation confirming the exact order every step actually happens in; confirmed the button's id appears exactly once in the file. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device -- Lynn's own test (tapping through the confirmation, confirming notes/settings/themes are genuinely gone afterward, and that the tour and sample notes correctly reappear on the resulting fresh load) is the real confirmation this needs.
  v0.1905 - ACCURATE, per-call storage error messages, per Lynn's direct, specific report: on her very first launch of v0.1904 (previewed inside the Claude app itself, not yet deployed -- confirmed with her this is almost certainly an environment/storage-persistence quirk of that specific preview context, not a code bug, since the identical build opened fine once actually deployed) she saw "saveNotes FAILED: UnknownError: Database deleted by request of the user" alongside a toast claiming "storage may be full" -- and asked, correctly, whether the browser's own error already told us the real reason, whether different calls needed different messages, and whether this could have actually been a still-not-yet-migrated localStorage call mislabeled as saveNotes. Checked all three directly rather than assuming: (1) QuotaExceededError is a distinct, specific DOMException name in both the IndexedDB and localStorage APIs, different from every other failure reason -- UnknownError is NOT a quota error, so the toast was factually wrong for this exact case, not just vague; (2) traced saveNotes()'s entire chain (idbNotesTransaction -> idbOpen -> the transaction itself -> conflict-backup/census) and confirmed zero localStorage calls anywhere in it -- the error genuinely came from IndexedDB's own open() call failing, not a mislabeled localStorage error; (3) confirmed saveSettings()/saveThemes()/saveFolders() DO still use storageSet (the Phase 1 localStorage wrapper), genuinely not yet migrated, so a quota error from THOSE specific functions would be a real localStorage quota failure specifically. NEW shared _describeStorageError(e, context) helper: checks the real, browser-reported e.name directly -- only claims "storage is full" when the error genuinely says QuotaExceededError, otherwise shows the actual name and message with an explicit "(not a storage-full error)" note, so every call site describes ITS OWN actual failure instead of one hardcoded generic guess reused everywhere. Applied to all four save paths (saveNotes, saveSettings, saveThemes, saveFolders) in both their log entries AND their user-facing toasts. Also added a NEW, precise, immediate log entry directly inside idbOpen()'s own onerror handler -- the exact moment the real open failure happens, tagged unambiguously as the database itself failing to open, before it ever propagates up through however many Promise-chain layers eventually catch it downstream. ALSO FIXED, found while making this pass and not left inconsistent with the others: saveFolders()'s catch block was completely silent before this -- no log, no toast, nothing -- now matches the other three. Verified: node --check passes; the new helper independently tested against Lynn's exact real error object, a genuine simulated QuotaExceededError, and a generic plain Error, all three producing the correct, accurate description. NOT a fix for the underlying transient open failure itself -- per the conversation with Lynn, that appears tied to the Claude app's in-app file-preview context specifically (a real, deployed origin has genuine persistent storage a raw preview may not), not something fixable in this app's own code; this build only makes whatever DOES fail, in any environment, describe itself honestly instead of guessing.
  v0.1904 - MAJOR CONSOLIDATION, merging the entire diagnostic/fix branch back onto the real IndexedDB-migration base (v0.1890), per Lynn's explicit decision that continuing to develop on two diverging branches was no longer justified once the bug was fully understood and fixed. Everything below was built and verified separately, elsewhere in this same investigation, on a parallel v0.1879-based branch kept deliberately isolated while the bug was still elusive -- that isolation is no longer needed. THE ACTUAL FIX: computeDefaultTagInsertion()'s confirmed infinite loop (triggered by newItemDefaultTags being set and the note's very first line being genuinely empty) is fixed in BOTH its main-thread copy and its independently-embedded Worker copy, re-verified by direct execution against the real trigger case, a real-text control case, and a normal checkbox-tagging case, all three correct. WORKER CONTAINMENT, kept alongside the fix rather than instead of it: computeDefaultTagInsertion and its full pure-logic dependency chain (appendDefaultTagsToLine, stripFormattingForDetect, stripWrapperFormattingOnly, isMarkerLineRaw, _getNoteTags) run in a genuine Web Worker with a 1000ms main-thread timeout -- this specific bug no longer needs it, but ANY future bug in this same cluster gets the same automatic containment without needing to be individually anticipated first. HANG WATCHDOG kept: a separate Worker-thread heartbeat monitor for genuine main-thread freezes, with the background-suspend false-positive fix (explicit suspend/resume messaging so iOS backgrounding a tab is never misread as a hang). CHECKPOINT MECHANISM kept: a fully synchronous (no .then() microtask delay), IndexedDB-backed last-known-checkpoint tracker, surviving even an abrupt crash. GENERIC HANDLER-HANG-DIAG kept: wraps every keydown/input/beforeinput listener (and any setTimeout callback they schedule) specifically on the note editor, so a hang in ANY of them -- not just ones someone already suspected -- shows up as an unmatched "entering" checkpoint with no "exiting." DROPPED, per Lynn's explicit "option 3" call: the narrow EMTIP-HANG-DIAG/KEYDOWN-HANG-DIAG/INPUT-HANG-DIAG manual instrumentation added earlier purely to hunt this one bug -- HANDLER-HANG-DIAG now covers the same code paths generically, making that hand-picked instrumentation redundant rather than additionally protective. NEW: the JSON settings Copy/Import feature (Settings > Debugging, subdued styling, correctly nested inside .settings-section-body -- confirmed programmatically, the same depth-tracking check that caught a real nesting bug earlier in this app's history), PLUS the Clear button for the import textarea Lynn explicitly asked for, since selecting all its text on mobile to clear it manually was fiddly. NEW: a dedicated System Log viewer with a live search/filter box, replacing the old plain showConfirm-based display -- per Lynn's explicit report that she had no way to search the log in-app and had to copy the whole thing into another program just to find something in it; reuses the confirm dialog's own CSS classes and .open-class show/hide convention for a visually consistent, correctly-behaving overlay rather than a one-off. Verified at every step along the way, not just at the end: node --check re-run after each individual module insertion (watchdog, checkpoint, handler-hang, containment+fix, settings JSON, log viewer), the settings JSON nesting checked programmatically, the worker source re-extracted and re-tested against all three cases directly from the FINAL file state (not just the intermediate draft), and a full duplicate-ID sweep confirming none of this session's own additions collided with anything pre-existing or each other. One real mistake caught and fixed during this consolidation itself, not glossed over: an early edit accidentally deleted the reset-defaults-btn handler's opening line while inserting the new settings-JSON handlers nearby -- caught immediately via grep, not left broken. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device in this exact consolidated combination -- Lynn's own testing of the merged build, including the normal IndexedDB data-path functionality this branch already carried before today's changes, is the real confirmation nothing was lost in the merge.
  v0.1890 - IndexedDB Migration Phase 3b-ii, first piece (see IndexedDB-Migration-Plan-v6.md): saveNotes() -- the single highest-stakes function in this entire migration, since it's the one that can actually lose a real note if done wrong. Read through the ENTIRE existing mechanism (loadState's baseline capture, the generation counter, _mergeNotesOnConflict, the bounded conflict backup) in full before writing a single line, per Lynn's own explicit request to slow down for this specific piece. KEY DESIGN INSIGHT, confirmed by reasoning through it carefully rather than assumed: wrapping the whole read-check-merge-write-bump sequence in ONE atomic IndexedDB transaction (new idbNotesTransaction(), spanning both remember_notes and remember_notes_gen together, reusing Phase 2's proven get+modify+put-in-one-transaction pattern) means IndexedDB's own cross-connection transaction queueing naturally serializes any two overlapping saveNotes() calls -- same tab or different tabs -- the exact same guarantee already proven for the syslog and snapshot ring. This is what makes it safe for saveNotes() to stay completely fire-and-forget from ALL 86 of its existing call sites -- none of them needed to change. A GENUINELY NEW RISK, addressed explicitly rather than glossed over: synchronous localStorage guaranteed a save was complete the instant the function returned; an async write reopens a real (if usually brief, ~10-20ms) window where closing the tab before it lands could lose it. Added _trackSavePromise() and a visibilitychange listener -- this can't reliably PREVENT a loss (no way to block an async write on page close without a disruptive beforeunload prompt, judged inappropriate here) but makes the risk VISIBLE: a page hidden while a save is still in flight now logs loudly, leaving a real diagnostic trail if a loss is ever reported instead of no trace at all. A CONSISTENCY BUG CAUGHT AND FIXED BEFORE SHIPPING, not left in: the conflict-backup mechanism's write was moved to IndexedDB, but its cleanup (listing and removing old backup keys) would have stayed on the Phase 1 localStorage-based storageListKeys/storageRemove -- exactly the kind of read/write-backend split that already bit the tour-seen flag earlier in this same phase, and would have silently recreated the unbounded-backup-growth bug v0.1798 already fixed once. Caught during design, not after -- the whole backup mechanism (write AND cleanup) now lives in IndexedDB together. A REAL SYNTAX ERROR CAUGHT AND FIXED, not shipped broken: an early draft had one extra closing parenthesis, prematurely closing _trackSavePromise() before the .then()/.catch() chain that needed to be inside it -- caught by node --check actually failing (not just assumed correct), traced to the exact line, and fixed properly rather than papered over. Also verified _shouldCheckNotesConflict() (called synchronously inside the new transaction's mutator) does no storage calls of its own -- a pure in-memory throttle check -- confirming it's safe to call inside the transaction without breaking its atomicity. Deliberately STOPPED here -- saveSettings(), saveFolders(), and the concurrent-instance heartbeat system remain completely untouched, reserved for their own equally careful passes rather than rushed through in the same build as the highest-stakes piece. Verified: node --check passes (after catching and fixing the syntax error above); _myNotesGen's null-check logic confirmed to exactly match the original synchronous version's semantics. NOT verified end-to-end in a real browser, including the actual conflict-merge path (needs genuine concurrent saves from two tabs to exercise) -- this sandbox cannot render or run real IndexedDB; Lynn's own testing, including normal editing/saving and ideally a genuine two-tab conflict scenario, is the real confirmation this needs.
  v0.1889 - IndexedDB Migration Phase 3b-i, third slice (see IndexedDB-Migration-Plan-v6.md). Converted the clipboard-paste fingerprint tracker (_hasAskedForClip/_markClipAsked) to IndexedDB -- confirmed genuinely standalone first: its only read call site elsewhere in this file is inside commented-out dead code (the pre-v0.1840 auto-paste-prompt flow, replaced by the creation-time paste button), and its write callers were already fire-and-forget. A MISTAKE CAUGHT AND FIXED BEFORE SHIPPING, not left in: an earlier draft of this same build moved the tour-seen flag's WRITE side to IndexedDB while its READ side (checked in the same boolean condition as remember_notes, core data deferred to Phase 3b-ii) stayed on localStorage -- which would have meant the tour never actually registered as "seen" and could reappear on every future launch, exactly the write/read-split bug this whole migration exists to avoid. Caught during a deliberate final consistency sweep of this build (grepped every idbGet/idbSet/idbGetSet call site and checked each key's read and write sides land on the same backend) rather than assumed correct -- reverted the tour-seen flag entirely back to localStorage, both sides together, deferred to 3b-ii alongside remember_notes. Also investigated, and explicitly deferred rather than converted: the folder-backup system (backupFolderStructureIfGood/restoreFoldersFromBackupIfPossible) and the tag-sort-sampler migration flag -- both turned out to be embedded directly within loadState()'s core synchronous initialization flow, not independent utilities the way the syslog and snapshot ring were; converting them cleanly would mean starting to async-ify loadState() itself, which is genuinely Phase 3b-ii's work, not a casual addition to this lower-risk batch. This narrows Phase 3b-i's remaining independent scope down close to zero -- most of what's left calling storageGet/storageSet now genuinely belongs to Phase 3b-ii. Verified: node --check passes; the consistency sweep described above is the real verification for this build, not just a syntax check. NOT verified end-to-end in a real browser -- Lynn's own testing (paste-related behavior, and confirming the tour still behaves correctly) is the real confirmation.
  v0.1888 - IndexedDB Migration Phase 3b-i, second slice (see IndexedDB-Migration-Plan-v6.md). Per Lynn's important reminder about cross-session/cross-tab write safety, re-confirmed the guarantee before continuing: IndexedDB's transaction atomicity is a genuine cross-connection property enforced by the browser itself, not just a within-one-tab guarantee -- two tabs both writing via idbGetSet on the same key genuinely can't interleave and lose an update, which is real, spec-level protection localStorage never had. Converted: (1) the note snapshot ring (undo/backup system) -- now uses idbGetSet since it's a genuine accumulator (reads the ring, adds an entry, evicts if oversized), same reasoning as the syslog; snapshotNoteBeforeOp() itself stays fire-and-forget for all 10 of its existing call sites (none needed to change) because the note content it protects is captured into a plain variable SYNCHRONOUSLY before anything async happens -- only the persistence is deferred, not the correctness of what gets persisted; getLatestSnapshot() became genuinely async (a real read, no way around it) and its one caller (the "Restore note backup" button) was converted to match. (2) The zoom-baseline-DPR cache -- called at high frequency (every resize/scroll/zoom event) and needs its value available synchronously every time, so this uses window.devicePixelRatio as an immediate synchronous starting value, with a persisted baseline (if any) applied as an async correction moments later -- a real but brief (a few ms) window where a reopened already-zoomed tab could very briefly use the wrong baseline before self-correcting, judged acceptable specifically because the failure mode is purely cosmetic and transient, never a data-integrity concern. STOPPED before converting the concurrent-instance heartbeat system, after actually reading through it in full rather than assuming it was another simple cache: it's the PRIMARY mechanism preventing two active instances from both running at once, makes a synchronous blocking decision at a critical point in app startup, and has its own real history as a fix for a previous, hard-won concurrency bug (v0.1798, referenced directly in its own comments). This deserves the same careful, isolated treatment as Phase 3b-ii's core data path, not the same casual pass as a debug log or a zoom cache -- deliberately deferred rather than rushed. Verified: node --check passes; grepped for leftover references to the removed _snapReadAll helper (none found, fully replaced). NOT verified end-to-end in a real browser -- this sandbox cannot render or run real IndexedDB; Lynn's own testing (using "Restore note backup," triggering a few snapshot-worthy edits, and checking zoom behaves normally) is the real confirmation.
  v0.1887 - IndexedDB Migration Phase 3b-i, first slice (see IndexedDB-Migration-Plan-v6.md): the real one-time migration now actually runs at app startup, not just via the manual test button -- fire-and-forget (loadState() stays synchronous, not converted to async itself), safe because it's fast (~10-20ms for a typical key count, confirmed from Lynn's own test), idempotent (marker-guarded, safe every single startup), and non-destructive (never deletes the localStorage it reads from). The syslog is the first real key actually cut over to living in IndexedDB rather than localStorage -- chosen first specifically because it's the highest-call-volume case in the whole app (logSys is called from hundreds of places), so getting the pattern right here proves it for the rest of the fire-and-forget group. logSys() itself now uses idbGetSet -- the exact atomic transaction Phase 2 proved -- rather than two separate get/set calls, which would have reopened exactly the race Phase 2's "broken pattern" test demonstrated; logSys is called frequently enough, often multiple times per tick, that a naive approach would have silently lost entries under real use. Critically: logSys's own public signature and every one of its hundreds of call sites elsewhere in this file are COMPLETELY UNCHANGED -- still fire-and-forget, still synchronous-looking, confirmed by grep that no other call site needed to change. The "View system log" button was converted to async and now reads from IndexedDB to match where logSys writes -- a self-contained UI handler with nothing else depending on its return value, a clean low-risk site to convert directly rather than needing the fire-and-forget treatment logSys itself needed. Also flagged (not silently left misleading): the startup census's remember_syslog entry now explicitly labels itself "(frozen,movedToIDB)", since that diagnostic is scoped to localStorage specifically and will otherwise report an increasingly stale size for this one key without explanation. Verified: node --check passes; grepped every remaining reference to SYSLOG_KEY/remember_syslog to confirm nothing else was missed. NOT verified end-to-end in a real browser -- this sandbox cannot render or run real IndexedDB; Lynn's next test (checking that System Log entries continue appearing correctly, and checking a fresh page load's STARTUP CENSUS/MIGRATION lines) is the real confirmation. Remaining Phase 3b-i work (the rest of the fire-and-forget group -- zoom baseline DPR, heartbeat, folder backup, notes conflict backup, note census, migration flags, clipboard fingerprint, tour-seen flag) deliberately NOT attempted in this same build, converted one at a time in future builds following this same proven pattern. Phase 3b-ii (the core notes/settings/folders/themes data path) remains untouched and deferred, per the plan's explicit risk-isolation reasoning.
  v0.1886 - FIX/REDESIGN: the "Test IndexedDB Migration" button, per Lynn's real test run showing 4/12 "mismatches." Traced the actual cause rather than assuming a bug: two of the four had IDENTICAL lengths on both sides (content differed, nothing was truncated), and all four are keys the live app kept writing to during the test itself -- the notes-generation counter, the note census, notes via autosave, and especially the syslog, which the TEST'S OWN logSys() calls were actively growing while it ran. Not a bug in idbSet/idbGet or the copy logic -- a genuine race between "a snapshot copied a moment ago" and "the still-live original," which will stop being possible entirely once Phase 3b makes IndexedDB the single live store. Rather than just explain this away, redesigned the test to make it structurally impossible: it no longer touches the real one-time migration at all (that already ran for Lynn and correctly only runs once, by design -- entangling a repeatable test with that would mean either skipping real verification on every subsequent tap, or incorrectly re-running a "one-time" migration). Instead: captures one frozen snapshot of real data (read-only), copies it into temporary, namespaced test keys ("idbtest:" + original key) nothing else reads or writes, verifies the round-trip byte-for-byte against that same frozen snapshot (no race possible -- nothing else touches these specific keys), then deletes the test keys afterward so repeated taps never accumulate data. This directly answers the real Phase 3a question -- does idbSet/idbGet round-trip data correctly -- decoupled from whether the one real migration already happened. Also logs the real migration's actual status (already completed, with timestamp, or not yet run) as separate informational context, not conflated with the round-trip test's own pass/fail. idbRunOneTimeMigrationIfNeeded() and idbVerifyAgainstLocalStorage() both gained an optional snapshot parameter for this (default behavior for the real migration path is completely unchanged -- still reads live localStorage at call time, correct for an actual one-time migration). Verified: node --check passes; re-confirmed the settings row's nesting programmatically after these edits, not just assumed it survived. NOT verified end-to-end in a real browser -- Lynn's next tap is the real confirmation, same as every build in this migration so far.
  v0.1885 - IndexedDB Migration Phase 3a (see IndexedDB-Migration-Plan-v4.md): the actual IndexedDB engine, built and tested in isolation -- NOT yet wired into the app's real read/write paths. storageGet/storageSet/storageRemove/storageListKeys (Phase 1) still call plain localStorage directly; nothing about the app's real behavior changes in this build. This mirrors the same "isolate, prove, then integrate" approach that made Phase 2 work. New: idbOpen/idbGet/idbSet/idbRemove/idbListKeys (a real, cached-connection IndexedDB engine) and idbGetSet(key, mutatorFn) -- the direct, reusable version of the exact atomic read-modify-write transaction pattern proven in Phase 2's standalone race test (indexeddb-transaction-race-test-v1.html, confirmed by Lynn on both iOS/WebKit and Android/Chromium: 50/50 concurrent saves survived with this pattern, only 1/50 with a naive separate-transactions version) -- copied over, not reinvented. Also new: idbRunOneTimeMigrationIfNeeded(), the actual one-time migration logic per the plan's confirmed decision (single one-time copy, no ongoing dual-write, no localStorage fallback maintained afterward) -- guarded by a dedicated marker key so it can never accidentally re-run and resurrect stale data over genuinely-empty-but-already-migrated state, and deliberately does NOT delete the original localStorage data, leaving it in place as a zero-cost safety net. Added a TEMPORARY "Test IndexedDB Migration" button (Settings > Debugging, correctly nested inside .settings-section-body this time -- verified programmatically the same way the earlier nesting bug was fixed and confirmed, not just visually) that runs the real migration against Lynn's own real data and verifies every single key matches exactly between localStorage and IndexedDB -- genuinely safe to run, since IndexedDB is a completely separate store from localStorage and the migration function never writes back to localStorage, so this cannot touch or corrupt any existing note. Verified: node --check passes on the full file; the new row's nesting was confirmed programmatically. NOT verified end-to-end against real IndexedDB behavior in this sandbox -- there is no browser here to run it in, only a syntax checker; Lynn's own tap of "Test IndexedDB Migration" is the real confirmation this works, the same way the standalone race test needed her real device to actually prove anything. Next: Phase 3b, converting the Phase 1 wrapper functions to delegate to this engine and converting every one of their 45+ call sites (and enclosing functions) to async/await -- the biggest, most invasive step in this whole migration, deliberately not attempted in the same build as this one.
  v0.1884 - IndexedDB Migration Phase 1 (see IndexedDB-Migration-Plan-v3.md): the storage abstraction layer. Every direct localStorage.getItem/setItem/removeItem/key call in the app -- inventoried first via a full grep pass (52 occurrences found, none of them false-positive text/comments matching the pattern, confirmed before touching anything) -- now goes through four new wrapper functions: storageGet/storageSet/storageRemove/storageListKeys. Deliberately still backed by plain localStorage internally -- this is a PURE REFACTOR, zero functional change -- so the actual future engine swap to IndexedDB only needs to change these four functions, not 50+ scattered call sites again. Three enumeration loops (backup-key cleanup during startup, backup-key cleanup during a save-conflict, and the v0.1880 storage-usage diagnostic) used raw localStorage.key(i)/localStorage.length index-loops with no clean IndexedDB equivalent -- converted by hand to use the new storageListKeys(prefix) instead, which also incidentally simplified one of them (the save-path cleanup no longer needs the awkward "removeItem shifts indices down, re-check this position" workaround its old index-based loop required, since storageListKeys returns a stable array snapshot). The remaining ~45 simple get/set/remove call sites were converted via a verified scripted find-replace, not hand-edited one at a time -- confirmed safe first (zero non-call-site matches of the pattern anywhere in the file), then the wrapper functions' OWN internals were protected from the substitution (extracted, replaced with a sentinel, substitution run on everything else, then put back unchanged) so they don't call themselves. Verified directly, not assumed: confirmed only 5 raw localStorage.* references remain anywhere in the entire file, and all 5 are inside the four wrapper functions themselves (3 in storageGet/storageSet/storageRemove, 2 in storageListKeys) -- nowhere else; spot-checked several converted call sites by eye for correct syntax after the scripted replacement, not just trusted the regex; ran the full file through node --check, which passed clean. NOT run against the regression suite -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5) -- worth flagging as more important than usual this time, given the scope of what changed, even though the change is mechanically a pure passthrough. Phase 2 (the async-safe transaction design, prototyped and tested against deliberately-induced races before touching real data) is next, per the plan doc.
  v0.1883 - RELABELED both Storage Usage log line prefixes, per Lynn's follow-up: the two lines genuinely measure different things (that's why the numbers differ -- direct localStorage byte count vs. the Storage API's IndexedDB/Cache-only estimate), but both previously started with the identical "STORAGE USAGE (reason):" prefix, so the distinction was only explained in trailing prose rather than being obvious at a glance -- especially awkward given the app's log viewer shows newest-first, meaning the SECOND line written (estimate(), often 0 B) visually appears ABOVE the first when actually reading it in Settings > Debugging > View system log. Now: "STORAGE USAGE - localStorage direct count (reason): ..." and "STORAGE USAGE - navigator.storage.estimate() (IndexedDB + Cache Storage ONLY, NOT localStorage) (reason): ...", so what each line is actually counting is stated up front, not just explained after the fact. Trailing explanatory text shortened slightly to avoid repeating what the new prefix already says, and now points back to the other line by its new descriptive name ("the localStorage direct count line above") rather than a vague "the byte totals above." No change to what's measured or when -- purely a labeling fix. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1882 - CLARIFIED: navigator.storage.estimate()'s log wording, per Lynn's direct question after seeing usage=0 B despite having real notes present. Confirmed via web search this is not imprecision, not a bug, and not merely "unreliable" the way v0.1880's original wording implied -- it's a real, documented scope limitation: usage in this API ONLY counts IndexedDB and Cache Storage (Chrome's own developer docs: "it reports the total number of bytes used by IndexedDB and the Cache API"), and localStorage is explicitly NOT quota-managed data in this API at all, in Chrome or Firefox, per the Storage API's own spec explainer. 0 B is the correct, expected reading for an app using only localStorage, not an approximation error -- the original log wording ("does NOT reliably reflect") undersold how clear-cut this actually is. Reworded to state this plainly rather than hedge. No change to the underlying measurement logic or the toast wording, both of which already used the direct byte-count total, not estimate()'s numbers -- confirmed by checking both call sites, not assumed. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1881 - FIX: the new Storage Usage row (v0.1880) was placed outside .settings-section-body's closing tag instead of inside it -- still technically inside the outer .settings-section wrapper, but outside the part that actually collapses/expands, per Lynn's report that it wasn't correctly nested within Debugging. Same known, previously-documented bug class as v0.1725's Note Behavior/Debugging nesting issue -- traced directly rather than guessed, using the same div-depth-tracking approach that bug's own changelog entry describes. Moved the row (and its now-corrected surrounding comment) inside .settings-section-body, immediately after the Clear App Cache button, so it now genuinely collapses/expands with the rest of Debugging's content instead of always rendering regardless of the section's open/closed state. Verified programmatically, not just visually: walked the actual div nesting depth from settings-section-debugging's opening tag and confirmed storage-usage-row-container now sits inside settings-section-body specifically, not merely somewhere inside the outer settings-section wrapper. No other functional changes. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1880 - NEW: proactive storage-usage diagnostic, per Lynn's report of hitting a real, hard localStorage quota error at only ~100 plain-text notes with zero attachments -- "I would have preferred to been warned that I was getting close to reaching a quota before I actually was told I had already hit a limit." Confirmed her specific theory directly rather than guessing: read the actual "Sort by tag" code, which creates a permanent, numbered backup note ("Copy of <name> before sort N") every single time it's used, by design, never auto-cleaned or expired -- a real, plausible contributor if used repeatedly on the same note without manual cleanup, as she suspected. New computeStorageUsageBreakdown() measures ACTUAL bytes used directly (summing real Blob-measured sizes of every localStorage key under this deployment's own prefix, not estimated) and specifically separates out sort-by-tag backup notes from everything else, by name pattern. Deliberately does NOT rely solely on navigator.storage.estimate() for the warning threshold, despite that API now being widely supported (confirmed via web search -- Safari included, since ~2023, so this is genuinely NOT blocked for security reasons, contrary to Lynn's reasonable worry that a sandboxed web app might not be allowed to know this): estimate() reports a broader, often disk-percentage-based "Storage API" quota shared with IndexedDB/Cache Storage that does NOT reliably reflect localStorage's own separate, typically much smaller fixed limit -- it could report "plenty of room" while the specific thing that actually failed is nearly full. estimate() is still queried and logged as supplementary context, with this caveat included every time, never presented as the authoritative number. TWO surfaces: (1) a new "Check Storage Usage" button in Settings > Debugging, showing a toast summary (total used, % of a conservative 5MB floor, sort-backup count/size if any) plus a full breakdown written to the System Log; (2) a proactive startup check, logged unconditionally every load (fail-loudly per this project's standing rule) but only surfaced as a user-facing toast past a real 70% threshold, specifically to avoid training Lynn to ignore a warning that fires on every single load regardless of actual usage. Verified directly: confirmed the sort-backup name-pattern regex matches the exact naming scheme that code produces; confirmed non-note keys are correctly scoped to only this deployment's own STORE_PREFIX (empty string for main, 'stable:'/'dev:' for the other two -- verified the empty-string case doesn't accidentally match zero keys, since ''.indexOf('') special-cases correctly to always match); confirmed the button and startup check share the exact same underlying measurement function, so they can't disagree with each other. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device with real accumulated backup notes -- this sandbox has no real localStorage data resembling Lynn's actual account to test against; her own "Check Storage Usage" click is the real confirmation of both the numbers and the theory. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1879 - FIX: the small-handle scroll direction was exactly backwards, per Lynn's precise, patient correction of the terminology confusion. Her stated convention: "scrolled to the right" = seeing the START of content (button 1, protected by the padding-shield) -- an ordinary, untouched resting scroll position. "Scrolled to the left" = seeing the END (the last buttons) -- which is what v0.1877/v0.1878's "scroll to the technical maximum" logic actually produced, the exact opposite of what she'd asked for. Fixed by removing that scroll-manipulation code entirely -- it's not needed at all. A plain, never-touched resting scrollLeft (0) combined with the padding-shield CSS already shows button 1 fully protected; the real fix needed was deciding WHEN that shield CSS should even apply. Also implemented her separate, explicit reminder from the same exchange: "I only want this automatic scrolling to happen if there are enough buttons to cause overflow. Otherwise... just centered" -- v0.1878's padding+flex-start rule was unconditional for ANY small-handle configuration, contradicting this. Scoped it to a new class, qab-handle-small-overflowing, toggled by JS only when the buttons genuinely overflow. Measured carefully to avoid a false-positive: the class is explicitly removed BEFORE measuring (in case stale from a previous render), so the overflow check reflects the buttons' own natural width, not width already inflated by the shield padding itself -- the padding is a few dozen pixels, potentially enough to flip a borderline case into artificial "overflow" if measured with it already applied. When not overflowing, no special class, .qab-scroll falls through to the ordinary base rule -- a short row centers exactly as it always has, resolving v0.1878's previously-accepted "short rows no longer center" trade-off entirely rather than leaving it as a known limitation. Verified directly: confirmed the scroll-to-maximum code is fully removed, not just disabled; confirmed the new class is correctly removed-then-conditionally-restored on every render (not just added and never cleared, which would have left it stuck on after a note's buttons were reconfigured down to a non-overflowing count); confirmed the CSS rule only targets .qab-scroll when this specific class is present on body. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device -- this sandbox cannot render real layout or touch scrolling; Lynn's next test is the real confirmation, as always with this feature. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1878 - FIX: a real bug in v0.1877, not a valid simplification, per Lynn's direct correction and pointed question ("can you tell what it looks like... can you detect that the first button is not visible?"). Answered honestly: no, this sandbox cannot render or observe the running app -- but tracing v0.1877's own logic by hand confirmed she was right regardless: with the padding removed, the REAL first button sat flush at screen x=0, directly under the floating handle, exactly the "obstructed" problem this whole feature exists to prevent. Restored v0.1875's padding-left: var(--qab-size) trick (an empty slot the width of one button, so the handle covers something disposable instead of a real button) -- but this time WITH justify-content:flex-start also restored alongside it, not just the padding alone. Explained directly to Lynn why the two are linked: the handle floats at a fixed screen position, but centered content's actual screen position shifts depending on how many buttons are configured -- a short, non-overflowing row centered in the bar might not start anywhere near the handle at all, meaning the padding could end up misaligned with it, and in some configurations a real button could still end up underneath. Left-aligning guarantees the padding always lines up with the handle regardless of button count. Flagged this honestly as a real, acknowledged trade-off (a short button row no longer visually centers in the bar for this specific case) rather than silently picking one of Lynn's two stated preferences over the other without telling her they were in tension. The v0.1877 overflow-to-maximum JS logic is completely unchanged and untouched -- it operates independently of this CSS restoration and doesn't need to know about it. Verified directly: confirmed the restored rule matches v0.1875's original values exactly, with flex-start added; confirmed the JS overflow branch still reads the same scrollWidth/clientWidth values correctly with the padding now back in the layout. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device -- this sandbox cannot render real layout; Lynn's next test is the real confirmation, as always with this feature. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1877 - REDESIGNED small-handle scroll behavior again, per Lynn's clarified follow-up correcting v0.1876's "exactly one button width" pin once she saw it in practice -- not layered on top of that build, fully replaced. Her restated design, implemented literally: (1) OVERFLOW case (enough buttons to need scrolling) -- scroll to the TRUE maximum (scrollWidth - clientWidth), so whatever's visible at rest is a complete, unobstructed set of buttons, nothing partially hidden behind the handle; earlier buttons remain reachable by scrolling back toward the start, same as always. (2) NO-OVERFLOW case (few buttons, nothing to scroll) -- do nothing at all, zero scroll manipulation, leaving the row centered exactly as every other QAB configuration already behaves -- "just as it always has been in previous builds," her own words. Also removed v0.1875's phantom-slot padding-left entirely, per the same "just as it always has been" framing -- .qab-scroll now has ZERO special CSS for the small-handle case, inheriting the identical base rule (the general 20px/8px Android-clipping fix) every other configuration already uses. The overflow/no-overflow branch uses the same +2px tolerance _qabUpdateScrollArrows() already uses elsewhere in this file for its own overflow check, for consistency rather than a separately-invented threshold. Verified directly: confirmed the CSS override is fully removed (small-handle .qab-scroll now matches the base rule with no exceptions); confirmed the JS branch correctly distinguishes overflow vs non-overflow using the same measurement pattern (scrollWidth/clientWidth, read synchronously right after buttons are appended, no rAF needed) already proven elsewhere in this function. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device -- this sandbox cannot render real layout or touch scrolling; Lynn's next test is the real confirmation, as always with this feature. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1876 - FIX: small-handle QAB scroll position looking "random" on each note open, per Lynn's report. Root cause: #qab-scroll is the same DOM element reused across every note (not recreated per-note), so it was simply retaining whatever scrollLeft was left over from wherever the PREVIOUS note's QAB happened to be scrolled -- naturally looking arbitrary from note to note, since it depended entirely on unrelated prior scrolling, not on anything about the note actually being opened. Now explicitly pinned to exactly one button's width (var(--qab-size) in pixels, the same value the v0.1875 phantom-slot padding uses, so the two always stay in sync) every time renderQuickActionBar() runs -- which already covers note-open via the existing setTimeout(renderQuickActionBar, 0) in openNote(). Deliberately applied on every render trigger, not narrowly scoped to note-open specifically, favoring predictability (the scroll position is always exactly the same, full stop) over trying to distinguish which of several render triggers is "the note-open one" -- flagged in case Lynn wants this scoped narrower after testing. Verified directly: confirmed the pinned value reads the same `size` variable already computed earlier in the same function (no separate CSS round-trip, can't drift out of sync with the padding value), and confirmed it's gated behind both cfg.smallHandle and cfg.showCollapseHandle, matching every other small-handle-specific behavior in this file. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device -- this sandbox cannot render real layout or touch scrolling; Lynn's next test is the real confirmation, as always with this feature. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1875 - BACKED OUT v0.1874 entirely and replaced with a much simpler design, per Lynn's direct correction: "sometimes you seem to get left and right confused" -- she's right that v0.1874's directional reasoning about scroll gestures was shaky, and the whole JS snap-to-boundary approach was more complex than the problem needed. Her own restated design: the small-handle case should behave in EVERY scroll respect exactly like the no-handle case -- no special scroll-position logic at all. The only difference: padding-left on .qab-scroll equal to one full button's own width (var(--qab-size), scales correctly with the size setting), an empty "phantom first slot" exactly as wide as a real button. At the row's ordinary, completely unmodified resting scroll position, this empty slot naturally sits where the handle floats, and the first REAL button starts immediately after it -- fully visible, never obstructed, with zero JS scroll manipulation needed. _qabNudgeScrollPastSmallHandle() (both the original v0.1868 version and v0.1874's redesigned snap-to-boundary version) is removed entirely, along with its call site in renderQuickActionBar() -- not left in as unused dead code. justify-content is also no longer overridden for the small-handle case (v0.1874 had set flex-start) -- left at its ordinary inherited default (center), matching every other QAB configuration in the app exactly, per Lynn's "acts exactly like there's no handle" framing. Verified directly: confirmed .qab-scroll's padding-left now reads var(--qab-size, 64px) for the small-handle case; confirmed no justify-content override remains there; confirmed both the function definition and its sole call site are fully removed, with no dangling reference anywhere in live code (only historical changelog entries and old code comments still mention the removed function's name, which is expected and correct -- this project's changelog is a permanent record, not something rewritten after the fact). NOT verified end-to-end on a real device -- this sandbox cannot render real layout or touch scrolling; Lynn's next test is the real confirmation, as always with this feature. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1874 - REFINED small-handle scroll behavior, per Lynn's detailed follow-up clarifying exactly what she wants (confirmed her intent before implementing rather than guessing, since this genuinely had a real mathematical tension worth surfacing): a real, small, permanent left gap (part of the scrollable content, so it moves WITH scrolling rather than being a separate fixed wall like the actual bug fixed in v0.1873); on first opening a note, NO button should be even partially covered by the handle -- whichever button is first visible must be completely clean; but scrolling further toward the true start should still be able to slide the first button under the handle, which is fine and expected. (1) Re-added a small 16px padding-left to .qab-scroll for the small-handle case -- explicitly NOT the same mechanism as the bug fixed in v0.1873 (that was a separate, non-scrolling reservation on the OUTER bar; this lives inside the scrollable content itself, so at true scrollLeft:0 everything, gap included, slides off the true screen edge together, avoiding the "artificial wall" look Lynn specifically didn't want back). (2) REDESIGNED _qabNudgeScrollPastSmallHandle(): the original approach (scroll exactly to the handle's raw pixel width) can land mid-button, leaving it HALF covered -- exactly the "partly obstructed" look Lynn said she doesn't want. Now measures every real .qab-btn's actual on-screen position and snaps to the first one whose left edge is fully clear of the handle, so the initial view always shows a complete, clean button -- never a sliver. If no button qualifies (a very small/heavily-configured bar), falls back to scrolling as far as possible rather than an awkward partial view. Deliberately built on getBoundingClientRect() math rather than .offsetLeft -- caught during implementation that .offsetLeft resolves relative to the nearest POSITIONED ancestor (#quick-action-bar itself, position:fixed), not .qab-scroll, a subtly different coordinate space from scrollLeft's own; the two are nearly identical in practice here but "nearly identical" isn't the same as correct, so used an approach that's unambiguous regardless of the positioning chain. Verified directly: confirmed the padding is part of the scrollable content (not the outer bar) by checking it lives on .qab-scroll, not #quick-action-bar; confirmed the snap logic's fallback path for the edge case of zero qualifying buttons. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device -- this sandbox cannot render real layout or touch scrolling; Lynn's next test is the real confirmation, as always with this feature. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1873 - TWO real fixes, per Lynn's second on-device debug log, which conclusively disproved v0.1872's fix and pointed straight at the actual root cause. (1) THE REAL BUG: QAB-SCROLL-DIAG showed .qab-scroll's own rect starting at screen x=56 despite its OWN padding correctly measuring 0px (v0.1872's fix, confirmed working exactly as intended) -- something OUTSIDE .qab-scroll was still pushing everything over. Found it: #quick-action-bar itself (the OUTER bar) has always had its own separate `padding-left: calc(56px + safe-area-inset-left)` rule, meant to reserve space for the LARGE handle. The v0.1868 redesign's own comment explicitly claimed "no reserved space at all for the small variant" -- that was the correct, stated INTENT, but the actual CSS override needed to make it true was never written; deleting the old small-specific 40px override in v0.1868 left this base 56px rule falling through and applying instead, the exact opposite of the documented goal. This is why nothing since v0.1868 actually fixed it -- every attempt (v0.1868's transparency, v0.1872's scroll-padding fix) was correctly implemented but touched a DIFFERENT element than the one actually causing the 56px offset. Fixed now by adding the actually-missing override (padding-left: safe-area-inset-left only, no reservation) scoped to body.qab-handle-small. (2) SECOND contributing bug, found while re-checking rather than assuming one fix was the whole story: .qab-scroll has justify-content:center, a well-documented CSS gotcha on an OVERFLOWING flex row -- browsers can distribute the extra invisible space asymmetrically around centered overflow content, meaning scrollLeft:0 doesn't reliably mean "first item flush at the left edge" the way flex-start does. Per Lynn's own explicit request ("the leftmost QAB button should start rendering at very close to an X value of zero"), changed to flex-start, scoped to body.qab-handle-small specifically so the no-handle and large-handle cases keep their existing (intentionally nicer for a short, non-overflowing row) centered look. Also clarified directly for Lynn: this was never a z-index conflict between buttons and the bar's background, as her question suspected -- buttons are children painted on top of their parent's background by ordinary DOM stacking; what she was seeing is that no button was actually POSITIONED at that spot at all, a layout/offset bug, not a stacking-order bug. Both fixes worked through by hand from her real, precise numbers (confirmed exact: 56px offset matches the large handle's reservation width precisely) -- not guessed at. NOT verified end-to-end on a real device, since this sandbox cannot render real layout; called for Lynn's next test to confirm. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1872 - LIKELY REAL FIX, per Lynn's on-device debug log -- the first genuinely conclusive data this investigation has had. Her log's QAB-MYSTERY-DIAG lines settle the "what is this element" question directly: document.elementFromPoint() at the suspect location returned #quick-action-bar itself (its own plain background) at every sampled point, not any hidden or separate element. This reframes the whole bug: it was never a rogue element -- it's a real GAP in the button row where no button is positioned. Root cause: .qab-scroll's general-purpose 20px left padding (v0.1867, added for an unrelated Android button-clipping problem) was never designed with the small handle's later floating-overlay redesign (v0.1868) in mind, and doesn't line up with what _qabNudgeScrollPastSmallHandle() actually scrolls past -- leaving real dead space the floating handle only partially covers, exactly matching Lynn's description of a rectangle "partly underneath" the handle. Fixed by zeroing that padding specifically for the small-handle case (body.qab-handle-small .qab-scroll), where the handle's own floating overlay plus the existing scroll nudge are the only mechanism actually needed -- worked through by hand: with padding removed, the first button now starts flush at position 0, the nudge scrolls exactly past the handle's own width, and what's left visible trailing behind the handle is fully covered by the handle itself rather than leaving a gap beside it. Left completely unchanged for every other case (large handle, no handle at all) -- the original Android-clipping problem that padding exists for is still real there and still needs it. Also added direct QAB-SCROLL-DIAG and QAB-FIRSTBTN-DIAG logging (real scrollLeft/scrollWidth/padding values and the first button's actual rect) alongside the existing diagnostics, so Lynn's next test will directly confirm or refute this fix's own math rather than relying on visual impression alone. Called "likely" rather than "confirmed" deliberately -- this sandbox cannot render real layout, so this is a well-reasoned fix worked through by hand from real device data, not something verified end-to-end here. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1871 - DIAGNOSTIC BUILD UPDATE, per Lynn's much more precise description of the still-unresolved mystery area: taller than the QAB buttons, left side only, partly BEHIND the small handle (lower z-index) but IN FRONT of the buttons (higher z-index), showing plain background where buttons should be visible, confirmed present on both iOS and Android (ruling out any browser-specific cause). The v0.1870 diagnostic data came back showing the handle at a correct 48x48 and the scroll arrow genuinely hidden (0x0, both CSS media queries confirmed matching) -- neither explains what Lynn is describing, and continuing to guess from named elements risks another round of fixing the wrong thing. Added a direct document.elementFromPoint() diagnostic instead -- the definitive way to identify an unknown rendered element regardless of what it's called or whether its existence was already known, sampled at three points spanning the described area (just above the handle, and further up, since the mystery rectangle is reportedly taller than the handle itself). Logs each sampled point's actual element (tag/id/class), computed background color, z-index, position, and bounding rect. No other functional changes. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1870 - DIAGNOSTIC BUILD UPDATE, per Lynn's photo-annotated screenshot (a genuinely sharp catch on her part, not something I re-derived myself): she correctly clarified two things that redirect this investigation. First, the earlier screenshot did NOT show a virtual keyboard -- what looked like keyboard keys were actually the QAB's own configured letter buttons (M/T/E/Q/O); I had misread the image. Second, and more useful: she circled the exact mystery area with a photo-editing tool, and it sits precisely where the QAB's left scroll arrow renders when a mouse is attached -- exactly matching her original suspicion from several builds ago, which v0.1868's own changelog had WRONGLY claimed was ruled out. That claim is corrected here: it was based only on the CSS looking correct on paper (both hiding rules ARE well-formed, properly !important, and redundant via two different conditions -- touch/pointer detection and, since v0.1868, screen width too), never actually confirmed against her real device. "Looks correct in the source" was overstated as settled when it wasn't. Per her explicit request to stop relying on screenshots and switch to debug statements, the v0.1869 diagnostic block now ALSO logs the left scroll arrow's real on-device state: its actual rendered rect and position, computed display value, any lingering inline style, and -- critically -- whether each of the two hiding media queries (touch/pointer and max-width:699px) actually evaluates as matching on her real device via window.matchMedia(), which is the one thing pure code review can never confirm. No other functional changes. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1869 - TEMPORARY DIAGNOSTIC BUILD, per Lynn's report that the small handle still appears full-height on her real iPhone even at v0.1868, and her follow-up question about Safari Web Inspector -- confirmed via web search that genuine on-device inspection requires a Mac (no way around this natively; third-party apps/bookmarklets are the only Mac-free options, and are limited), so rather than ask Lynn to install a third-party tool, this logs the handle's real computed geometry straight into the app's OWN existing debug log (Settings > Debugging > View system log), which she already knows how to use. Exhaustive static review of v0.1868's actual delivered code found no bug: CSS specificity genuinely favors the 48px small-variant override over the base 72px rule; comment-balance was rechecked given this project's own documented history of unclosed-comment bugs, and is fine here; only one #qab-collapse-handle element exists in the HTML (no duplicate); only one place in JS sets inline height on it, and it correctly clears (not sets) for the small variant; the zoom-compensation system never touches the handle at all. Since the code review came up clean, this build adds one temporary, clearly-flagged diagnostic block (gated to only log when the small handle is actually active) reporting the handle's real getBoundingClientRect() dimensions, computed style height/width, any lingering inline style, and the live body class list -- everything needed to tell, from a real device, whether the CSS genuinely isn't applying (a real bug still not found) or whether something else entirely is being misidentified as the handle. Meant to be removed once Lynn reports back what the log shows. No other functional changes in this build. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as previous builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1868 - Small collapse handle REDESIGNED FROM SCRATCH, per Lynn's explicit direction after v0.1866 and v0.1867 both failed to actually solve the problem (v0.1866: made it as tall as the large handle, unwanted; v0.1867: made the leftover space transparent, which showed raw note content bleeding through instead of buttons -- Lynn correctly identified this as "a mysterious element" that shouldn't exist at all). Root realization: the large handle has never had this problem because its height is synced to match the bar's real height (v0.1860) -- nothing is ever left uncovered. Every fix attempted for the small handle instead tried to paper over the mismatch between its deliberately-short height and the bar's own full height. The actual fix: stop reserving space for the small handle at all. It's now a pure floating overlay (the existing z-index already puts it above the bar) -- exactly "nothing other than the collapse handle and the toolbar buttons," Lynn's own words.
```
