Both the padding-left reservation AND the v0.1867 transparent-gradient background are removed entirely for the small-handle case; the bar's background is now always its normal, plain, single color, in every state. To keep the first button from starting out permanently hidden under this now-unreserved floating handle (which would happen most of the time, right after opening a note), added _qabNudgeScrollPastSmallHandle(): a one-time initial scroll-position nudge (NOT a reserved space, NOT a new element) that starts the row just past the handle's current width. A user can still scroll back left under the handle if they choose to -- the handle will visibly overlap that one button in that case, an accepted, expected consequence of a floating control, not a bug. Also, per Lynn's on-device report that the small handle was still too small in BOTH dimensions even at last build's 40px: bumped to a fixed 48x48px (up from 40px width only), a comfortable, easy-to-hit square -- still clearly, meaningfully smaller than the large variant. Separately investigated Lynn's suspicion that the QAB scroll arrows were her "mystery element": confirmed they were NOT (they're already hidden on any touch device via the existing v0.1854 media query, and were never the actual cause) -- but added a second, width-based hard-hide (`max-width: 699px`) as defense-in-depth and for consistency with her stated preference for width-based rather than touch-based logic elsewhere in this same session. CAUGHT AND FIXED A REAL BUG BEFORE SHIPPING, not left in: while writing the new _qabNudgeScrollPastSmallHandle() comment, several lines were accidentally written with `#` instead of `//` (a genuine typo, not JS comment syntax at all) -- this would have been a hard syntax error, breaking the entire script block and likely the whole app. Caught by re-reading the actual diff before delivery, not assumed correct on the first pass -- fixed to proper `//` comments before this build was finalized. Verified directly: confirmed no stray `#`-prefixed lines remain anywhere in the affected region; confirmed the small-handle-specific padding-left and transparent-gradient CSS rules are both fully removed (the large handle's own matching rules are completely untouched); confirmed the new 48x48 dimensions; confirmed the nudge function is correctly gated to only affect the small-handle-plus-enabled-handle case, and is wired into renderQuickActionBar() right after the existing height-sync call. NOT verified on a real iPhone or Android device -- whether the floating-overlay approach actually reads correctly, and whether 48px is now comfortable to tap, both need Lynn's own on-device testing; this sandbox cannot render real device touch/geometry. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as the previous three builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1867 - THREE items, per Lynn's testing report on real iPhone and Android devices right after v0.1866. (1) FIX/REVERT: v0.1866's height-sync unification made the small collapse handle just as tall as the large one, which Lynn explicitly does not want -- small needs to stay short. Reverted _qabSyncHandleHeight() to skip the small variant again (back to a fixed, short 40x40px box), but this time solved the original exposed-background-rectangle problem a genuinely different way, per Lynn's own explicitly offered alternative ("get rid of this rectangular area or make it transparent"): the QAB bar's own background is now a linear-gradient, transparent for exactly the reserved handle-width column and opaque everywhere else, instead of trying to make the handle tall enough to fill that space. Scoped to body.qab-handle-small specifically -- the large variant's existing, already-correct height-sync behavior is completely untouched. (2) FIX: Android still clipping the first QAB button at the scroll start even after v0.1866's 8px edge padding -- bumped left-edge padding to 20px specifically, per Lynn's own direct suggestion, right-edge left at 8px since that side wasn't reported as still broken. Honestly flagged: this is an empirical bump matching her suggested fix, not a root-cause diagnosis -- unclear why 8px wasn't sufficient on Android without being able to test on a real device. (3) NEW: the tri-state "Use Subtle Highlighting" setting (Off/On/Quiet mode only), wired to Lynn's chosen prototype Option D (thicker border + a faint 8% tint of the button's own existing scheme color as background -- no accent color, no new color introduced). Deliberately NOT implemented with color-mix() or currentColor -- color-mix() is too recent to trust across older phones this app needs to run on, and currentColor got genuinely ambiguous against this file's own pre-existing cascade (a specificity tie between .qab-active and the scheme rules that predates this change and wasn't this change's job to untangle); explicit per-scheme rgba() rules instead, simple and unambiguous. New settings.quickActionBar.subtleHighlight value, defaulting to 'off' (unchanged accent-fill behavior for anyone who doesn't touch this). 'quietOnly' is resolved LIVE against the current reading-mode state every time _qabRefreshActiveStates() runs -- already correctly called from both toggleReadingMode() and clearReadingMode(), so no new call sites were needed there; one new call added at the end of renderQuickActionBar() so a Settings-dialog change to this setting itself applies immediately on save. Settings UI (a 3-option dropdown, matching the existing Button Colors row's exact pattern) automatically got the native-select-on-iOS-ignores-font-size fix (v0.1857) for free, since it shares that existing static-select enhancement/search-registration loop rather than needing its own separate wiring. Verified directly: confirmed the small handle's height is once again independent of the bar's real height (back to a fixed 40px); confirmed the transparent-gradient math correctly matches the handle's reserved width; confirmed the new select's change handler, load-into-UI default ('off' when unset), and settings-search entry are all present and match the color-select's proven pattern exactly. NOT verified on a real iPhone or Android device, including whether the transparent-column fix actually looks right and whether 20px genuinely fixes the Android clipping -- this sandbox cannot render real device geometry; Lynn's own on-device testing is what actually confirms both. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as the previous two builds (Core Instructions Rule 5).
  v0.1866 - FOUR QAB fixes, per Lynn's detailed report with iPhone/Android screenshots (three items from her list deferred -- see below, and NOT included here). (1) FIX: small collapse handle clipped by the iPhone's curved corner -- root cause: the safe-area compensation was only ever applied as padding-bottom (pushing the icon up inside the box) while the box's own edge/background stayed flush at bottom:0, still sitting inside the corner-clip/home-indicator dead zone. Moved the compensation to an actual `bottom: env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px)` position offset instead, lifting the whole box clear of that zone -- same total safe height, correctly placed. (2) FIX: solid opaque background strip above the small handle, blocking QAB buttons -- this is the exact same class of bug v0.1860 already fixed for the LARGE handle (handle height not synced to the bar's real, taller content height), reintroduced for the small variant specifically because v0.1861 deliberately excluded it from that sync. _qabSyncHandleHeight() no longer has a small-variant skip branch -- both variants now sync height identically; only WIDTH stays variant-specific. (3) FIX: small handle too tiny to reliably tap -- width bumped 30px -> 40px (at/near the ~44px minimum touch target both Apple's and Google's platform guidelines recommend), still meaningfully smaller than the large variant's 56px; icon 16px -> 18px; the bar's own reserved padding-left updated to match. (4) NEW: on medium/large screens (a `min-width: 700px` media query -- confirmed for Lynn: this is genuinely viewport WIDTH, not touch-vs-mouse capability, so a mouse-driven iPad in landscape gets this too, same as any wide phone) both the QAB and the main lower toolbar now use noticeably wider button spacing, since cramped-together spacing only ever made sense on small-phone-portrait widths. Reused the app's existing 700px breakpoint (v0.1771) rather than inventing a new one. Also added real horizontal edge padding (8px, both sides) plus scroll-padding-inline to .qab-scroll, per Lynn's report that Android specifically could clip the first button at the left scroll limit and had no breathing room after the last button at the right limit (iOS's native overscroll "bounce" was masking the same underlying missing-padding issue there). DEFERRED, not guessed at blindly: (a) Lynn's report that Android could sit ~20px lower with room to spare -- no reliable existing way in this codebase to detect Android specifically via CSS, and a wrong platform-wide guess risks a regression on some Android devices/browsers while looking fine on others; needs either a specific measurement or a screenshot showing the gap clearly before attempting. (b) The new tri-state "use subtle highlighting" setting for QAB toggle-buttons (off/on/quiet-mode-only) -- per Lynn's own explicit request to see prototype style options before committing to one, a separate small prototype artifact with several candidate subtle-highlight styles was delivered alongside this build instead of guessing at final CSS; the real setting will be wired in once she picks a direction. Verified directly: confirmed the handle's new bottom offset computes correctly with a simulated safe-area-inset-bottom value; confirmed BOTH handle variants now measure the same height as the bar itself (previously only true for the large variant); confirmed the small handle's new 40px width and the bar's matching updated padding-left; confirmed the 700px breakpoint activates/deactivates at the correct width for both .qab-scroll and .toolbar-scroll; confirmed the new qab-scroll edge padding is present. NOT verified on a real iPhone or Android device -- this sandbox cannot render or measure real device safe-area/notch/gesture-bar geometry, so Lynn's own on-device testing is the real confirmation this needs. Regression suite: NOT run -- same network-blocked jsdom-install limitation as v0.1865 (see that entry and the Core Instructions' Rule 5).
  v0.1865 - NEW: "Clear app cache & reload" button in Settings > Debugging, per Lynn's report of Playwrite cursive fonts working correctly in a Safari tab but staying stuck on the fallback font inside the installed iOS PWA at the same URL. Root cause identified from the service worker's own fetch handler (unchanged since v0.1832/dev v0.1064): the non-HTML branch does cache.match() -> fetch-and-cache-if-missing, with no distinction between "never fetched" and "fetched and failed" -- a request made before the woff2 files existed on the server (a 404) gets cached exactly like a success would, and is then served from that cache forever, since match() never re-checks. This explained the Safari/PWA asymmetry precisely: Safari's cache never made that early failed request, the PWA's did. The new button (caches.keys() -> caches.delete() for every entry, then a forced reload) gives a direct, on-device way to clear exactly this kind of stuck bad cache entry without a version bump or PWA reinstall -- notes are explicitly unaffected, since Cache Storage (this button) and localStorage/IndexedDB (notes) are separate browser storage systems entirely. Confirmed via direct read of both the main and dev service worker source that this fix requires no service worker changes -- the existing fetch handler already re-caches correctly once the stale entry is gone. Button placed and styled to match the existing "Clear debug log" row exactly, reusing showConfirm/showToast rather than new UI.
  v0.1864 - Increased the QAB button letter size substantially on the actual note editor toolbar (distinct from the Settings dialog fix in v0.1863), per Lynn's report and screenshot of excessive whitespace inside the buttons -- a real accessibility concern, since these large buttons exist specifically for low-vision users. Investigated first: confirmed the button itself has zero CSS padding (padding: 0) -- the empty space was purely from the font-size multiplier being too conservative relative to the button's own dimensions (0.42x for one character, 0.28x for two), not any padding value to trim. Raised both multipliers substantially (0.42 to 0.58 for one character, 0.28 to 0.4 for two), tuned via iterative visual testing against real screenshots rather than guessed at once and assumed correct. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed no overflow even at the worst-case wide-character combinations (MM, WM, W, WW) both numerically (scrollWidth stays comfortably under the button's own width at every combination tested) and visually via screenshot; separately confirmed the exact letters from Lynn's own screenshot (S, E, F, TP) render dramatically larger and fuller than before; re-ran the 2-character-label test from an earlier build and confirmed nothing regressed; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed there either. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1863 - FIX: the QAB button letter label in the Settings dialog didn't respect the app's own UI font size setting, per Lynn's report -- was using the browser's tiny default font-size, completely independent of --font-ui, unlike the action-picker trigger button immediately to its left (which correctly inherits .settings-select's font-size: var(--font-ui)). Made relative to --font-ui as she explicitly requested (larger than the base size specifically, since this input only ever holds 1-2 characters and can afford to be visually prominent), with minimized vertical padding so the letter itself fills more of the box height rather than being surrounded by whitespace. Height matching: initially tried a static CSS ratio (a font-size multiplier tuned against real measurement at the default UI size), but confirmed directly by testing at a much larger font size setting that this approach doesn't hold -- the gap between the two elements' heights widened from 1px to 6px, since both elements have fixed-pixel padding that doesn't scale proportionally with font-size the same way at every base size. Replaced with a JS-based live height sync (a ResizeObserver on the trigger button specifically, since this row can be created while its settings section is still collapsed and reporting zero height at creation time -- the observer correctly re-fires once the section actually expands and the trigger's real height becomes measurable), guaranteeing an exact match at any UI font size rather than an approximation tuned for one specific size. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed an exact height match (not just close) at three different UI font size settings -- the default (42px/42px), a much larger one (50px/50px), and a smaller one (36px/36px); confirmed the visual result via screenshot -- a large, clear two-character label correctly matching the height of the action picker beside it, with minimal wasted vertical space; re-ran the QAB 2-character-label test from an earlier build and confirmed nothing regressed; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed there either. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1862 - THREE items, per Lynn's requests and follow-up questions. (1) MAJOR FIX: audited every copy/cut-confirmation toast in the app, not just the one Lynn noticed -- found and fixed 12 total (the 11 from the previous audit, plus one genuinely missed "Cut to clipboard" toast, only found via a broader sweep for the word "clipboard" rather than just "copied") that would silently overwrite the very thing just copied/cut if tapped by accident. Also protected the 5 "Pasted text was URL-encoded... decoded it automatically" toasts, a related but distinct risk Lynn's question prompted checking for -- not a copy confirmation, but the clipboard may still hold something worth reusing for another paste. (2) ARCHITECTURAL FIX, per Lynn's direct question about the parameter design: added showCopiedToast(), a dedicated helper specifically for confirming something was just copied, rather than relying on every call site to remember showToast()'s optional third parameter (noCopyOnTap) -- exactly how all 12 existing bugs happened, since it's easy to simply omit an optional flag. Deliberately did NOT flip showToast()'s own global default, which would have disabled the genuinely useful "tap an error to copy it for reporting" behavior across the other ~190 non-copy toasts in the app -- confirmed directly that a normal, unrelated toast still correctly allows copy-on-tap after this change. Migrated all 12 copy/cut-confirmation toasts to the new helper. Also reduced genuinely redundant wording per her explicit question about that too -- "Copied" (scratch pad) and "Copied to clipboard" (two other call sites) were the same generic confirmation with different phrasing for no real reason; merged to one consistent "Copied to clipboard" everywhere. (3) MAJOR CHANGE to the QAB color scheme, per Lynn's sharp follow-up question and then her own explicit decision: she pointed out the smart dark/light-auto behavior built in v0.1858 was only reachable by leaving the setting untouched, with no way to deliberately choose it if someone had picked something else first. Added an explicit, selectable "System" option to the dropdown resolving exactly the same way unset always has. Then, on further clarification, deleted the earlier "Match app theme" option entirely -- confusion between this session's legacy sample-theme edits and how future app themes/note styles will actually work made it the wrong concept for this feature. Confirmed directly, per her explicit request, that the QAB's existing "lit up" active-button color already correctly uses the NOTE accent color specifically (var(--note-accent, var(--accent))), not the general app accent -- already correct, no change needed. Fixed the dropdown's own display to show the actual STORED preference (including "System" itself when that's the real choice) rather than the resolved effective color, which would have meant the dropdown could never actually show "System" as selected. A legacy 'theme' value from before this change now gracefully falls through to the same dynamic default, rather than breaking. Verified directly and thoroughly throughout: confirmed all 12 copy/cut toasts individually preserve clipboard content when tapped; confirmed a normal, unrelated toast is unaffected and still allows copy-on-tap; confirmed the dropdown now has exactly 3 options; confirmed the dropdown correctly displays "System" both when genuinely unset and when explicitly chosen, confirmed an explicit blackOnWhite/whiteOnBlack choice still displays and stays locked correctly, and confirmed a legacy 'theme' value resolves gracefully; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1861 - TWO items, per Lynn's requests. (1) NEW SETTING: a small, subtle collapse-handle variant, per her explicit request -- the large handle is great for her father's low-vision needs, but she wanted something taking much less space for herself. New settings.quickActionBar.smallHandle toggle (off by default, matching her explicit request), only meaningful when the existing Collapse Handle setting is also on. Fixed small dimensions (30x30px, versus the large variant's 56px width and full-bar-height) at the same bottom-left spot, with a proportionally smaller icon and border radius, and a correspondingly smaller reserved space in the bar's own padding (30px instead of 56px) so QAB button content still never overlaps it. Explicitly made the height-sync logic from v0.1860 skip the small variant entirely (and clear any stale inline height left over from switching from large to small without a full reload) -- an inline style always wins over CSS regardless of selector specificity, so simply adding a CSS rule for the small fixed size wasn't enough on its own while that function kept setting one inline. CAUGHT A REAL TYPO during implementation: an invalid CSS selector ("body.qab-handle-small#qab-collapse-handle", missing the space that makes it a proper descendant selector -- a body element can never also be the handle element, so it would have silently matched nothing) -- found and fixed before shipping, not left in as dead, non-functional CSS. (2) INVESTIGATED Lynn's question about whether the QAB color scheme's real default is "Match App theme": confirmed directly in the actual resolution logic that it is NOT -- the real default remains the smart, dark/light-aware blackOnWhite/whiteOnBlack pairing built in v0.1858; "theme" stays a separate, explicit-choice-only option, never the default. Flagged this as a likely terminology mismatch rather than a bug, and offered the most likely explanation for what she observed (testing via a preview sandbox with no saved settings and no real device-level dark-mode detection, rather than the actual deployed site) -- consistent with the same category of environment-specific difference already seen several times this project. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed the small handle's actual computed dimensions (30x30px) with no stale inline height blocking the CSS; separately confirmed the large handle variant is completely unaffected and still correctly syncs to the bar's full height; confirmed the visual result via side-by-side screenshots of both variants; re-ran the color-scheme, height-sync, and new-action tests from previous builds together with this change and confirmed nothing regressed; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed there either. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1860 - FIX: solid background strip visible above the QAB collapse handle, per Lynn's report and screenshot from a real iOS PWA. Could not reproduce visually in this sandbox's own screenshot at first, but rather than guess, measured the actual computed geometry directly -- confirmed a genuine, numeric mismatch: the bar's own real content height (85px at the default QAB size, 141px confirmed at a much larger one) versus the handle's fixed 72px, leaving a real gap where the bar's own background was visible above where the handle actually starts. Invisible in this sandbox's own screenshot only because bar and handle happened to share the exact same background color there, masking the seam that would show on any device where they differ even slightly. Confirmed this is independent of whether the scroll arrows are shown or hidden -- purely about the handle's fixed height never having matched the bar's actual content height -- so fixed unconditionally for both touch and non-touch devices, not scoped to the arrow-hiding condition Lynn's original request was about. Added _qabSyncHandleHeight(), syncing the handle's height live to the bar's own measured height, hooked into the existing ResizeObserver already watching the bar for a related purpose (FAB repositioning) so it stays correct through any QAB size change or safe-area-inset change on rotation, plus called directly at the end of renderQuickActionBar() for immediate correctness on first render, matching the same measure-don't-assume pattern _qabReserveSpace() already uses right next to it. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed bar and handle heights and top positions now match exactly (both 85px at default QAB size); separately confirmed the sync stays correct at a much larger QAB size setting (both 141px); confirmed the visual result via a tight screenshot crop of the actual QAB region -- clean button tops, no cut-off letters, no visible seam; re-ran the color-scheme, scroll-arrow, and new-action tests from the previous two builds together with this fix and confirmed nothing regressed; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed there either. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1859 - FIX: a real ordering bug caught while precisely verifying Lynn's direct follow-up question about whether the new QAB auto-color-scheme (v0.1858) correctly handles the app's full light/dark/auto theme setting, not just a simple dark check. Confirmed the underlying detection itself was already correct (it reads the app's own single resolved dark-mode class, which already correctly accounts for 'auto' checking the OS's real prefers-color-scheme) -- but tracing the exact order of operations inside applySettings() surfaced a genuine bug: renderQuickActionBar() was being called BEFORE the dark-mode class got (re)computed in that same function, meaning the QAB would render using the PREVIOUS, stale theme state specifically in the moment settings.theme is 'auto' and the OS preference has just changed -- it would only catch up whenever something else happened to trigger another render, not immediately. Fixed by moving the QAB render to after the dark class is freshly applied. Verified directly and thoroughly, not just by reading the code: simulated the OS itself switching from light to dark while the page was already open and settings.theme set to 'auto' (via a real prefers-color-scheme media query change, not a manual setting toggle) and confirmed the QAB correctly and immediately flips from blackOnWhite to whiteOnBlack with no manual re-render call needed -- the exact live scenario Lynn's question was really asking about; re-ran the full set of QAB tests from the previous two builds together with this fix and confirmed nothing regressed; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed there either. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1858 - QAB color scheme now defaults intelligently to dark mode, per Lynn's request and the design discussed with her directly (she explicitly asked for my opinion first, given a real tension: the locked blackOnWhite/whiteOnBlack pairs exist specifically because they guarantee maximum contrast for low-vision use, which a theme's own accent color has no guarantee of meeting -- but a fixed white background is genuinely uncomfortable in a dark room for someone without low vision). Agreed with her instinct: kept the guaranteed-maximum-contrast property fully intact (nothing about the low-vision benefit is diluted) while letting WHICH of the two locked pairs applies by default follow the app's own light/dark mode, rather than assuming a bright room. Not a new setting, per her explicit request -- the existing three-option dropdown (blackOnWhite/whiteOnBlack/theme) is unchanged; only the DEFAULT changed. Implementation: removed the previously-fixed 'blackOnWhite' default value entirely so "never touched" can genuinely be distinguished from "explicitly chose blackOnWhite" (both looked identical in storage before this change) -- added a shared resolution helper used by both the QAB's own rendering and the settings dialog's display sync, so they can never drift into showing two different answers. Any explicit choice (including explicitly choosing blackOnWhite while in dark mode) stays completely locked, matching the exact same rationale that already justified these being theme-independent in the first place. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed the unset default correctly resolves to blackOnWhite in light mode and whiteOnBlack in dark mode; separately confirmed an explicit blackOnWhite choice stays locked even after switching the app to dark mode (not silently overridden by the new auto-logic); confirmed no timing issue between dark-mode application and QAB rendering; confirmed the visual result via screenshot -- dark buttons with light text in dark mode, no jarring white background; re-ran the QAB arrow, action, and 2-character-label tests from earlier this session together with this change and confirmed nothing regressed; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed there either. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1857 - TWO fixes, per Lynn's report. (1) FIX: the Typing Mode QAB button not lighting up in the note accent color while in typing mode, unlike Tap to Edit and Quiet Mode. Root cause confirmed directly: every OTHER toggle-type QAB action already calls _qabRefreshActiveStates() from its own toggle function (quiet mode, tap-to-edit, toolbar collapse), but none of those live inside setViewMode() itself -- so until the Typing Mode action existed last build, nothing had ever needed setViewMode() to trigger this refresh too. Added the missing call at the end of setViewMode(). (2) MAJOR FIX: the QAB action-picker dropdown (added last build) was using a native <select> instead of this app's own custom picker component, per Lynn's explicit reminder -- native <select> options on iOS are rendered by the OS itself, completely exempt from the page's own font-size CSS, which is exactly why this component exists in the first place. Per her request to check for any other un-enhanced dropdowns: audited every single <select> in the entire app (both static HTML and any created dynamically via JS) -- confirmed all 9 existing static selects were already correctly using the component, and the QAB action picker (dynamically created, one per button row) was the only gap. CAUGHT A REAL BUG during implementation, not assumed correct on the first pass: the enhancement call initially threw a genuine error ("Cannot read properties of null, reading insertBefore") when placed immediately after creating the element, since the component needs the select already attached to a real DOM parent to insert its own trigger button next to it -- fixed by moving the call to after the row is actually appended to the settings dialog. Verified directly and thoroughly, not just checking an internal flag: confirmed the original select is correctly hidden and replaced by a real trigger button showing the current selection's label; confirmed clicking that trigger actually opens the real picker overlay with the correct title and populated options; confirmed the full round trip works end to end -- selecting Typing Mode through the picker correctly updates the underlying select's value, the saved settings.quickActionBar config, auto-fills the new action's default letter, updates the trigger's displayed label, and the overlay itself correctly closes afterward (an initial 100ms check that looked like a bug turned out to just be the close animation not yet finished, confirmed by waiting longer and seeing it close correctly, not a real problem); re-confirmed the Typing Mode light-up fix together with this in the same build; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1856 - FOUR changes, per Lynn's requests. (1) FIX: the QAB staying invisible if the collapse handle is disabled while the bar happens to be collapsed, with no way back -- per her report. Fixed at two levels: the settings toggle now resets handleCollapsed to false when the handle is turned off, AND found and fixed the actual root cause at the CSS level (the hiding rule only ever checked "is collapsed", never "is the handle even enabled", so a stale collapsed value could hide the bar regardless of the handle's own state) -- requiring both classes together directly encodes the correct invariant rather than relying solely on the JS reset to always run correctly. (2) NEW: two QAB actions, per Lynn's explicit request -- Typing Mode (letter T, mirrors the exact logic of the existing upper-toolbar view-mode-btn including persisting settings.lastViewMode, lights up while in typing mode, so this and the toolbar button behave identically rather than two subtly different implementations) and Settings (letter S, a simple one-shot action, not a toggle). (3) CHANGED: Floating Pill Header now defaults to off, per Lynn's explicit request. Confirmed a real duplicate-key bug caught DURING this exact change, not assumed correct on the first pass: found and fixed a pre-existing homeFloatingHeader default entry further down in the same DEFAULT_SETTINGS object that was silently overriding a first attempt at this fix (JavaScript resolves duplicate object-literal keys by taking the last one) -- traced with direct Node.js isolation testing after a real browser test came back unexpectedly true, confirmed exactly one definition remains after the fix. Also found and removed a stale, hard-coded checked attribute on the actual Settings-dialog checkbox markup itself (functionally harmless, since the dialog's own JS sync overwrites it every time it opens, but genuinely inconsistent, misleading source that contradicted the new default) -- caught after Lynn's own direct follow-up question about whether this was really fully verified. (4) MAJOR CHANGE: removed the older, non-'Next' Atkinson Hyperlegible font entirely, per Lynn's explicit request, and redirected every theme that referenced it to the newer variable-font 'Next' version instead. Found and updated six separate locations: the shared Google Fonts @import URL (carefully removing only the old font's own segment, confirmed the Next and Mono segments and surrounding syntax remain untouched and correctly joined), the font-picker quick-pick pill, six separate settings-dialog dropdown menus (general font, heading font, and others), the font weight-support registry, and four built-in sample themes (Task Master, Dyslexia Friendly -- the one Lynn specifically named -- Poetry Page, and Fun & Bright) that all specified the old font by name. Left two generic, still-accurate help-text prose mentions of "Atkinson Hyperlegible" untouched, since they describe the font conceptually rather than reference the specific removed CSS font-family string. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed zero old-font dropdown options or pills remain anywhere in the live DOM; confirmed the weight registry no longer has the old entry while the Next entry is intact; confirmed all four sample themes (including Dyslexia Friendly specifically) now resolve to the Next font; re-ran every test from earlier in this same build (QAB handle fix, both new actions, 2-character labels, the pill-header default) together to confirm nothing from the font work affected any of it; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1855 - QAB buttons can now be labeled with up to 2 characters instead of just 1, per Lynn's request. Genuinely small change: found exactly three places truncating to a single character (the render function, the settings-dialog input field's maxLength, and its input event handler) and raised all three consistently -- render and the input handler both now keep up to 2 characters (.slice(0, 2) instead of .charAt(0)), and the input field's maxLength raised from 1 to 2, widened slightly (44px to 56px) so two characters don't look cramped while typing. Added a smaller font size specifically for 2-character labels (scaled relative to the same --qab-size variable the default font-size already uses, so it stays proportional at any button size setting) since the 1-character size was already sized to nearly fill the button's own width and would have overflowed with two letters at the same size. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed the render function correctly keeps a 2-character label, correctly still allows a normal 1-character label unchanged, and correctly truncates a 3-character value down to 2 rather than accepting it in full; confirmed visually via screenshot at the default QAB size that mixed 1- and 2-character buttons sit together cleanly; separately confirmed visually at the actual minimum QAB size setting (40px, via the real settings.quickActionBar.size path, not a manual CSS override) that 2-character labels stay clearly legible even at the smallest button size. Re-ran the QAB scroll-arrow tests from the previous build and the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1854 - Hid the QAB scroll left/right arrow buttons specifically on touch/coarse-pointer devices, per Lynn's request -- she finds them unnecessary and a little odd-looking next to the docking handle on mobile, since a finger can just swipe directly, but wanted them kept available for a bigger, non-touch screen where they might genuinely help. Implemented as a CSS media query ((hover: none) and (pointer: coarse), the standard signal for "primarily touch-driven") rather than JS touch-event detection, since many laptops now have touchscreens but remain mouse-driven day to day -- pointer/hover capability is the more accurate signal here than device type alone. Deliberately CSS-only rather than removing the feature outright, so it costs nothing to bring back if she changes her mind, and confirmed this correctly overrides the JS's own overflow-detection logic (which would otherwise show the arrows whenever the bar genuinely overflows) since that logic only ever sets an explicit inline 'none' or clears it back to an empty string, never forces a visible value the CSS would have to fight against. On her question about adding a new setting instead: agreed a toggle would be overkill for something this narrow and cosmetic -- the touch/non-touch split already gives the right behavior automatically on both kinds of device without her needing to configure anything, and a dedicated Settings row for it would add clutter for a one-person stylistic preference. Verified directly and thoroughly, not just visually: confirmed under a real touch-capable browser context that the arrows stay hidden even when the QAB is GENUINELY overflowing (scrollWidth actually exceeds clientWidth, confirmed numerically, not just by having many buttons); separately confirmed under a non-touch context that the same genuinely-overflowing QAB still shows both arrows normally and functionally; confirmed the visual result via a real touch-context screenshot showing the docking handle and scrollable QAB buttons with no arrows present, matching what Lynn asked to see before deciding; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1853 - THREE changes, per Lynn's requests. (1) The toast message-counter number ("#1", "#2", ...) is now visually small and subtle, separated from the message text -- was rendering at the same size/weight as the message itself, reading like an error code rather than the debugging aid it's meant to be. Built via DOM elements (a styled span for the number, a text node for the message) rather than string-concatenated innerHTML, so message text can never be misinterpreted as HTML. CAUGHT BY VISUAL TESTING, not assumed correct: the number initially broke internally into two lines ("#" and "1" separately) at narrow widths, since the toast's own word-break:break-word applies to everything inside it -- fixed with white-space:nowrap scoped to the number specifically, confirmed correct via a second screenshot. (2) MAJOR CHANGE, per Lynn's explicit aesthetic preference: heading levels are now normalized during HTML-to-markdown paste conversion so the most prominent heading actually present in the source becomes markdown level 3 (###), never level 1 or 2 -- she finds those disproportionately huge next to body text and has been avoiding them entirely in her own writing for this reason. Scans once for the most prominent (smallest-numbered) heading level actually used in the source, computes a single offset so that level becomes 3, and shifts every other heading the same amount -- preserving relative hierarchy (a genuine subheading still renders smaller than its parent) whether that means demoting real H1/H2 content or promoting a document that only ever used H4/H5. Clamped to markdown's own maximum of 6. (3) Every resulting level-3 heading now gets two blank lines before it instead of one, per Lynn's separate explicit aesthetic request -- implemented as a placeholder resolved into a real extra blank line AFTER the existing newline-collapsing step runs (doing it any earlier would have the extra line flattened right back down to the standard single blank line by that same collapse rule), re-trimmed afterward so a level-3 heading that happens to open the whole paste doesn't end up with a stray leading blank line. Confirmed unaffected, as Lynn correctly assumed: table content never contains heading elements, so none of this touches the table feature at all. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed heading remapping across three real scenarios (a full H1/H2/H3 hierarchy demoted to 3/4/5, an H2-starting document promoted to 3/4, and an H4-only document promoted up to 3); separately confirmed the two-blank-line rule specifically -- three real newlines before a resulting H3, but still just the standard two before a resulting H4, confirming the rule is correctly scoped to level 3 only, not all headings; confirmed the full visual result in R&R mode via screenshot; re-ran all table and paste tests and confirmed tables remain completely unaffected while general paste conversion correctly picks up the new heading behavior; re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1852 - MAJOR NEW FEATURE: real markdown table rendering in R&R mode, per Lynn's explicit request to build this out. Investigated the renderer's actual architecture first rather than guessing at an approach: found that fenced code blocks already solve the exact same underlying problem (a multi-line block spanning several rendered lines), and used that as a direct template. Added computeTableBlocks() (detects a GFM-style table: a row containing at least one pipe, immediately followed by a valid separator row of only pipes/dashes/colons/whitespace with alignment markers, consuming subsequent pipe-containing non-blank rows as data) and renderTableBlock() (builds a real <table> with proper <thead>/<tbody>, per-column alignment from the separator row's colons, and cell content re-extracted from the ALREADY bold/italic/link-processed line text so inline formatting inside cells works correctly). A whole table renders as ONE self-contained block at its first line, deliberately simpler than spanning fragments across multiple line-loop iterations the way fenced code does -- also means move mode treats a table as one movable unit rather than letting someone accidentally split it apart by moving a single row. New .viewer-table CSS matches this app's existing theme system (font-size-editor scaling, fg/bg2/border variables) rather than hardcoded colors, so it correctly follows light/dark mode like everything else in the viewer. Also upgraded convertHtmlToNoteMarkdown() (the paste conversion) to generate real GFM pipe-table syntax for actual <table> elements, replacing the earlier, deliberately-deferred fallback-text-only approach from v0.1846 -- now that the renderer can display it, pasting a real table (from Google AI or anywhere else) produces an actual rendered table, not just readable plain text. Escapes any literal pipe character within cell text so it can't be mistaken for a column separator when re-parsed. Typing mode needed no changes at all, per Lynn's own correct expectation -- it only ever shows the raw textarea content directly, never routed through this renderer, so raw pipe characters display exactly as typed. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed full table rendering with real data (three columns, mixed left/center/right alignment, bold and a real link inside cells) via both a structural test and a visual screenshot; confirmed three important edge cases separately -- a table-looking block inside a fenced code block correctly stays literal text and is never rendered as a real table, an ordinary line containing a single stray pipe character is never mistaken for a table (no valid separator row follows it), and a header-only table with no data rows still renders correctly with no empty <tbody>; confirmed the complete round trip end to end -- pasting a real HTML <table> produces clean GFM markdown, which then renders as an actual table with formatting preserved, using the exact same HTML structure that matched Lynn's own real diagnostic data from earlier in this project; separately confirmed a literal pipe character within cell text is correctly escaped in storage and correctly restored on render. Re-ran the full existing test suite and confirmed nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1851 - Rich HTML-to-markdown paste now works in the "Add text to note" dialog too, per Lynn's explicit request -- important for anyone with typing mode turned off, who relies on this dialog as their primary way to add text and had been getting plain, unformatted paste with no blank lines. Investigated first: this dialog's existing paste listener (shared with Edit Paragraph) only ever did diagnostic logging and never called preventDefault(), so every paste here fell through to the browser's bare default plain-text behavior regardless of what was on the clipboard. Added a new, dedicated paste handler scoped ONLY to quick-add-input, deliberately NOT edit-block-input (Edit Paragraph), per Lynn's own explicit reasoning that paragraph editing doesn't need this and the "Add text to note" dialog is always available as the richer alternative. Adapted the exact same logic as the main note editor's own paste handler -- accidental-URL-encoding decode, tab/bullet conversion for plain text, and full HTML conversion via the same shared convertHtmlToNoteMarkdown() -- so this dialog's paste behavior is now fully consistent with the main editor's rather than a partial subset. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed bold, a list, and blank-line spacing all correctly convert when pasted into this dialog, matching the main editor's own output exactly; separately confirmed Edit Paragraph is genuinely unaffected (no listener intercepts its paste at all, confirmed via defaultPrevented staying false); re-ran the main-editor and toolbar-button paste tests plus the full remaining suite and confirmed nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1850 - Made the toolbar Paste button's error message platform-aware, per Lynn's question: it was hardcoded to say "iOS security sometimes requires it" regardless of the actual device, which would have been inaccurate and confusing if this app were ever used on Android. Now uses the existing _isIOSDevice() helper (already used elsewhere in this app for other iOS-specific behaviors, not a new detection mechanism) to say "iOS security" on an actual iOS device and the generic "your browser's security" everywhere else, since the exact wording of Android/Chrome's own equivalent clipboard security behavior hasn't been confirmed. Verified directly with two different simulated user agents (iOS Safari and Android Chrome) that the ACTUAL toast message shown reads correctly for each -- not just calling showToast() directly, but through the real click-and-fail path a person would actually hit. Re-ran the toolbar button and native-paste tests plus the full remaining suite and confirmed nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1849 - REVERTED the toolbar Paste button to plain-text-only clipboard reading (readText()), per Lynn's explicit report: an extra native iOS clipboard popup started appearing on every single use of this button, requiring an additional tap she didn't want -- new behavior that exactly coincides with when this button began calling navigator.clipboard.read() (the multi-type API, needed to also fetch HTML) instead of the simpler readText(). Her own hypothesis, and a reasonable one: reading the richer, multi-type clipboard specifically may be what triggers this extra native confirmation on iOS, distinct from a simple plain-text read. Given rich HTML paste via this specific button was never reliably working on her device to begin with (a genuine, still-unresolved iOS constraint, see v0.1847), trying it was costing a confirmed, real annoyance for no working benefit -- straightforward trade to make. The native paste-event handler (tap in the note content pane, use iOS's own paste popup), which she confirmed works beautifully and preserves rich formatting correctly, is completely untouched by this change -- this only affects the toolbar button's own separate, JS-driven clipboard read. Removed the now-fully-unused pasteFromClipboardAtCursor() function entirely (confirmed zero remaining callers after this revert, since the earlier new-note button that also used it was already deleted) rather than leave dead code behind. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed the toolbar button now reads only plain text even when the clipboard also has an HTML flavor available (previously it would have preferred and converted the HTML); confirmed the native paste-event handler is completely unaffected and still produces fully rich, correctly-converted markdown; re-ran the full remaining test suite and confirmed everything still passes. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1848 - Made the clipboard-read-failure error message itself actionable, per Lynn's request. Was just "Clipboard read not available" with no guidance; now reads "Clipboard read not available — tap in the text area and use the paste pop-up instead (iOS security sometimes requires it)" (and a matching variant for the separate "not available on this browser" case, when the clipboard API isn't present at all). Directs toward the one paste path already confirmed reliable on Lynn's own device -- native paste via the iOS pop-up -- right at the moment the unreliable, button-triggered path fails, rather than leaving her to already know (or guess) that native paste is the better option. Verified directly: confirmed the exact, real message a person actually sees (via a genuinely denied clipboard permission, not just calling showToast() directly) reads correctly with the new guidance included; re-ran all sixteen tests from the previous nine builds and confirmed every one still passes with identical output, nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1847 - MAJOR FIX: the "Clipboard read not available" error toast was destroying the very clipboard content Lynn was trying to paste, per her report that dismissing it replaced her clipboard. Root cause confirmed directly, not guessed: showToast() already has a noCopyOnTap parameter, built specifically for this exact class of problem -- its own existing comment states plainly that copying a toast's own text on tap "is actively harmful: it silently overwrites whatever the user meant to keep for the paste they were about to do." Every clipboard-related error toast in this app (six across the shared pasteFromClipboardAtCursor() and the older doPaste()) had simply never been passed this existing safeguard. All six now pass it. Investigated her second, related report -- that the error still occurs at all even after granting the permission prompt -- honestly rather than attempting a third unverified fix: two different technical approaches were already tried this project (v0.1842's single-clipboard-call fix, v0.1845's HTML-only-read optimization), and neither resolved it on her actual device. Given native paste (the iOS popup) is confirmed working perfectly, this now looks like a deeper, iOS-level constraint on programmatic button-initiated clipboard reads specifically, not something further code changes can be confidently expected to solve without real hardware to verify against -- flagged clearly as an open, unresolved limitation rather than papered over with another speculative attempt. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed tapping the error toast now dismisses it while leaving clipboard content completely untouched (previously the toast's own error text would silently overwrite it); re-ran all fifteen tests from the previous eight builds and confirmed every one still passes with identical output, nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1846 - Table-adjacent spacing and cell-fusion fix, per Lynn's real pasted example showing table content coming out as "FeaturelocalStorageIndexedDBe" -- cells with zero separator between them -- and no blank line around the table rows at all, including no space before the markdown header following the table. Root cause confirmed directly: TABLE/TD/TH had no handling whatsoever in the conversion function (their text just concatenated directly with nothing between it), and TR only ever got a single trailing newline, not the double-newline treatment already applied to paragraphs/headers/list items. NOT full table rendering -- that's still deliberately deferred as its own future project, per the earlier conversation about GFM table syntax needing real viewer support first. This is scoped purely to readability: TD/TH cells now get a trailing separator so adjacent cells don't fuse into unreadable run-together text, and TR now gets the same double-newline treatment as everything else, which also naturally separates the table as a whole from whatever precedes or follows it (a header, in Lynn's real example) without needing a table-specific spacing rule of its own. Verified directly with HTML matching her real, described table structure (three columns, three rows including a header row, bold text within cells, preceded by a bullet list and followed by a heading): confirmed every cell is now readable and clearly separated, confirmed every row sits on its own line with a blank line before and after, and confirmed the heading immediately following the table now correctly gets its own separation, matching the exact gap Lynn reported. Re-ran all fourteen tests from the previous seven builds and confirmed every one still passes with identical output, nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1845 - THREE changes, per Lynn's direct feedback after testing. (1) REMOVED: the creation-time new-note paste button entirely, per her explicit request that it's no longer needed -- all HTML, CSS, and JS wiring (visibility toggling, click handler, the setViewMode()/openNote() hooks) cleanly removed, confirmed zero remaining references. The underlying pasteFromClipboardAtCursor() function it shared with the toolbar Paste button is untouched and still fully in use there. (2) MAJOR CHANGE: universal blank-line spacing for the HTML-to-markdown conversion path specifically, per Lynn's explicit, broad request ("double space all items that end with a newline") -- headers now always get a blank line after them (was a single newline), and list items (bullets/numbered/checkbox-ish) now ALWAYS get a blank line after them, replacing the earlier conditional detection (P-wrapped item or inline margin/padding style) entirely, since it's no longer needed once this is unconditional. Also directly resolves her separate report of a sometimes-missing blank line BEFORE a following heading -- that gap was really about whatever preceded the heading not leaving one behind, so making every element's own trailing spacing consistent closes it as a natural side effect, not a separate fix. Deliberately scoped to rich-text-to-markdown conversion only (not general typing or plain-text paste), matching her own explicit scoping, and made default behavior rather than a new setting, consistent with her stated preference not to have to double-space manually. (3) INVESTIGATED AND OPTIMIZED (not fully solved, and said so honestly) her report that plain text usually pastes successfully via the toolbar button after allowing the permission prompt, but rich text often still fails. Best working hypothesis, consistent with everything already established about iOS's strict live-gesture window: the shared clipboard function was reading BOTH the HTML and plain-text clipboard blobs in parallel every time, even though only one is ever actually used -- and rich HTML (especially Google AI's, often many kilobytes with heavy inline styling) takes meaningfully longer to read and process than plain text, adding avoidable work during that narrow window. Now reads only the HTML blob when one is present, falling back to a plain-text read as a separate subsequent step (from the SAME already-obtained item list, not a second clipboard permission request) only if HTML conversion finds no usable richness. Explicitly flagged to Lynn as an optimization based on a reasonable hypothesis, not a confirmed, guaranteed fix, since this cannot be verified without her real device. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed zero remaining references to the deleted button anywhere in the file; confirmed headers/paragraphs/list items all correctly get blank lines after them together in one combined test, including confirming a header correctly gets space before it even when directly preceded by a list (previously a gap); confirmed nested lists don't produce runaway excessive blank-line stacking; re-confirmed the toolbar button's optimized clipboard logic still correctly handles rich HTML, plain-text-only clipboards, AND HTML-present-but-with-no-richness (the fallback path) all separately; removed two now-obsolete test files that specifically tested the deleted button (their failure, DOM elements correctly no longer found, confirmed complete removal rather than indicating a bug); re-ran the full remaining test suite and confirmed everything still passes. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1844 - Bullet marker character changed from hyphen to asterisk for both paste-related conversion paths (plain-text bullet-glyph conversion via convertBulletsToMarkdown(), and HTML <li> conversion in convertHtmlToNoteMarkdown()), per Lynn's explicit request. Root cause confirmed directly in the viewer's own rendering code, not guessed: a hyphen list marker specifically never gets the colored bullet glyph treatment -- a documented, intentional PAST design decision already in this file's own comments ("a hyphen's thin stroke doesn't carry a color well"), while asterisk and plus markers unconditionally do. Switching the generated marker to asterisk means pasted bullets now correctly pick up the same bullet color as manually-typed ones, with no changes needed to the rendering logic itself, which was already correct and working as designed. INVESTIGATED (not fixed yet) the separate multi-level-bullet hollow-circle report: confirmed directly, with both a DOM-level test and a real screenshot, that all four nesting levels (including the hollow circle specifically) render correctly in this sandbox's Chromium environment -- the underlying level-to-glyph logic itself appears correct. This points toward a device/font-specific rendering issue on Lynn's actual iOS device, plausibly the same class of bug already found and fixed for the square-bullet level (a missing glyph in the chosen font causing a fallback font substitution) -- flagged clearly as a finding to discuss and confirm before attempting a fix, rather than guessed at blind without being able to verify on real hardware. Updated 4 existing golden test cases (remember-test-goldens-v30.json, delivered alongside this build) that explicitly asserted the old hyphen-based bullet output -- an intentional behavior change, not a regression, confirmed by re-running the full suite against the corrected expectations. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed both conversion functions produce asterisk markers; confirmed the resulting bullet actually receives the colored-glyph styling in R&R mode (not just that the character itself changed); re-ran all fourteen tests from the previous six builds (after fixing several stale file-path references in the test scripts themselves, caught by inconsistent results rather than assumed correct) and confirmed every one still passes on its actual content assertions. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8, against the updated goldens file).
  v0.1843 - TWO fixes to the smart HTML paste, both traced to Lynn's own real, confirmed clipboard sample rather than guessed at. (1) MAJOR FIX: single-spaced paragraphs, per her report that pasting Google AI content came out single-spaced in Remember (and in Apple Notes) despite being visually double-spaced at the source, while Bear/Obsidian handled it better for headings specifically. Root cause confirmed directly from her own real diagnostic data: Google AI wraps paragraphs in <div>, not <p> -- and this app deliberately excluded <div> from blank-line treatment, reasoning it's usually just a generic layout wrapper. That caution was reasonable in principle but confirmed wrong for this real, recurring case. Rather than blanket-apply blank-line spacing to every <div> (risking doubled blank lines for a <div> that's genuinely just a structural wrapper around OTHER block elements), a <div> is now only treated as paragraph-like when its direct children are purely inline content -- text, spans, links, bold/italic -- with no nested block-level children. Verified this correctly distinguishes the two cases: a paragraph-like <div> gets a real blank line, while a <div> that only wraps a heading and another <div> does not get extra, incorrect spacing added by the outer wrapper itself. Applied as default behavior, not a new setting, per Lynn's explicit preference not to double-space manually every time. (2) FIX: odd, unexpected line breaks Lynn traced herself to non-breaking-space characters, confirmed visually via this app's own view-whitespace mode. Root cause confirmed directly from the exact same real clipboard sample: WebKit/Safari commonly wraps a copied space in <span class="Apple-converted-space">&nbsp;</span> to preserve it precisely during copy -- a real, documented browser behavior, not something Google does deliberately, and very likely why Apple Notes and Bear don't show the same artifact (they almost certainly normalize this during their own paste handling). Non-breaking spaces (U+00A0) are now converted to regular spaces during conversion. Verified directly and thoroughly with HTML matching Lynn's own real diagnostic structure exactly (DIV-wrapped paragraphs, Apple-converted-space non-breaking spaces, style-based bold, and a list, all together): confirmed a genuine blank line now separates the two paragraphs, confirmed zero non-breaking-space characters remain in the output, and confirmed bold/bullets still work correctly alongside both fixes; separately confirmed a purely structural outer DIV (wrapping a heading and another DIV, not itself paragraph content) does NOT get incorrect extra spacing added, confirming the fix is genuinely conditional and not over-applied; re-ran all eleven tests from the previous five builds and confirmed every one still passes, nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1842 - Rich-text paste now works through the pre-existing toolbar Paste button too, per Lynn's explicit report that it sometimes failed with permission errors specifically when pasting rich text. Investigated first rather than assumed: this button never actually attempted to read HTML at all, only ever plain text -- so rich formatting was always lost through this specific path regardless of any permission issue, a genuinely separate gap from the new-note button's earlier bug. Extracted a single, shared clipboard-read-and-insert function (pasteFromClipboardAtCursor()) used by both the toolbar button and the new-note button now, so they can't drift apart into two different behaviors again. Confirmed the actual, correct fix per direct technical verification this session (Apple's own developer forums, plus this app's own real diagnostic data): making exactly ONE clipboard call, decided upfront by API availability, never chaining .read() then a separate .readText() call as a fallback on failure -- that chaining, not "custom buttons can't read rich text" in general, was the actual root cause of the earlier failures. The single call now extracts BOTH the HTML and plain-text flavors from the same result, routing HTML through the same convertHtmlToNoteMarkdown() the native-paste handler already uses, falling back to plain text only when no usable HTML is present -- not a second, separate clipboard call. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed the toolbar button now correctly preserves bold and list formatting from a real HTML clipboard write (inserted at the correct cursor position within existing note content, not just at note start); confirmed it still correctly falls back to plain text when the clipboard has no HTML flavor at all; re-ran all nine tests from the previous four builds and confirmed every one still passes byte-for-byte, nothing regressed by sharing this logic between the two buttons. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1841 - FOUR fixes, per Lynn's report of "Clipboard read not available" and real diagnostic data from an actual Google AI paste. (1) ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED for the button error: it tried navigator.clipboard.read() first (for HTML), only falling back to readText() if that failed -- two separate, sequential async clipboard calls. Confirmed via Apple's own developer forums (a DTS engineer directly: the permission prompt only fires when an app accesses the pasteboard directly via JS, never for the native Paste menu, long-press, or keyboard shortcut) that iOS enforces clipboard access must happen within the live user-gesture window -- a failed first attempt very plausibly burns through that window, dooming the fallback too. This is exactly why the OLD, proven tap-based feature worked (only ever one clipboard call) while the new button didn't. Simplified to a single readText() call, matching the old, confirmed-working approach -- gives up on rich HTML via the button specifically (genuinely unreliable on iOS per Apple's own docs), while the toolbar Paste button and native-paste handler remain fully capable of rich conversion via a real paste gesture, which isn't subject to this constraint at all. (2) Applied Lynn's own real diagnostic data directly: her actual [PASTE LIST DIAG] log capture confirmed Google AI uses <span style="font-weight: bold"> for inline bold, not semantic <b>/<strong> tags -- extended both the richness-detection gate and the inline-formatting logic to recognize style-based bold (font-weight: bold or 600-900), not just tag names, with a guard against double-wrapping nested bold-styled elements. (3) Fixed the "extra empty bullets" issue Lynn confirmed after testing Google AI's own suggested HTML-to-markdown demo -- a common artifact of rich-text sources generally, per her own observation of the same thing happening in Bear and Pages. A list item whose content ends up empty after conversion is now skipped entirely rather than emitting a bare marker line; restructured so a numbered list's counter is only incremented for items that actually get emitted, so a skipped empty item can no longer cause numbers to jump (e.g. "1, 3" instead of the correct "1, 2"). (4) Investigated Google AI's own explanation of the error and its suggested code -- correctly identified the real underlying mechanism (paste-event-based access avoids the permission prompt entirely) but overstated the conclusion ("custom paste buttons always hard-fail" contradicts Lynn's own OLD, working tap-based code, which successfully read the clipboard from a click handler) -- verified against Apple's own developer documentation rather than accepted at face value. Verified directly and thoroughly: the style-bold fix confirmed against HTML matching the EXACT structure from Lynn's real diagnostic log; the empty-bullet fix confirmed both for an unordered list (skipped cleanly) and a numbered list (confirmed the counter correctly continues 1, 2 rather than skipping to 3); every test from the previous four builds re-run and confirmed still passing, nothing regressed by these changes. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1840 - MAJOR CHANGE: replaced the tap-triggered "want to paste?" card entirely with a creation-time paste button, per Lynn's explicit request after two real-device attempts confirmed iOS's own native popup wins the race regardless of event timing -- and confirmed that reading the clipboard just to decide whether to show anything was itself triggering iOS's own permission prompt before this app's own UI ever got a turn. Per her direct instruction: gave up completely on clipboard-checking. The new button just shows whenever a note is empty in typing mode -- no clipboard read, no "have we asked before" state, nothing tied to any tap at all, so there's no native-UI race to lose in the first place. Disappears the instant real content exists (typed or pasted), and the toolbar Paste button remains available afterward exactly as it always has. Tapping it reuses the exact same rich-HTML-aware paste logic already built for the toolbar button and native-paste handler (tries navigator.clipboard.read() for HTML first, falls back to plain text, handles an empty clipboard with a toast at the point of tapping rather than trying to know in advance). Removed the old card's HTML, CSS, and all of its tap-counting/clipboard-fingerprint-checking JS -- the underlying _hasAskedForClip()/_markClipAsked() infrastructure itself was left completely intact, since the toolbar Paste button still legitimately uses it and wasn't part of what Lynn asked to remove. CAUGHT A REAL BUG IN MY OWN FIRST TEST, not the actual code: initial testing showed the button never appearing on a fresh note at all -- traced to the test itself, not this feature, reusing Object.keys(notes)[0] as a stand-in note, which happened to BE the Debug Log, so the very act of calling openNote()/setViewMode() during the test kept re-filling the "empty" note with their own logged execution. Corrected by using a dedicated, guaranteed-fresh test note, which then confirmed the real feature works correctly. Verified directly and thoroughly against the corrected test: confirmed visible on a genuinely empty note, hidden immediately after typing, hidden in R&R mode and correctly reappearing when switching back to typing, hidden again after a successful paste; separately confirmed the button correctly reaches the rich HTML conversion (bold/lists) when the clipboard has an HTML flavor available, not just a plain-text fallback; confirmed visually via screenshot on a fresh note. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1839 - Three responses to Lynn's real-device testing of v0.1838, each handled honestly rather than assumed fixed. (1) The "Claude would like to paste from Google" permission dialog: almost certainly a testing-context artifact (that wording names the HOST app asking for clipboard access -- on the real deployed site it would say "Remember", and should only need to ask once, not every test) rather than a bug -- flagged clearly, not silently assumed away, since this genuinely needs confirmation on the real deployed site to know for sure. (2) EXPERIMENTAL, explicitly flagged as unverified: an attempt to suppress iOS's own native text-selection/paste popup while a note is still empty, since Lynn reported seeing iOS's own context menu instead of this app's own card -- meaning native UI may be winning a race against, or rendering on top of, this app's own prompt. Applies CSS user-select:none to the editor specifically while empty (not the textarea's readonly attribute, which would also block focus/cursor placement -- a much worse regression), restored the moment the offer is resolved either way or the note gets real content. This sandbox has no way to simulate iOS's actual native popup behavior, so while the CSS state change itself is confirmed correct here, whether it actually suppresses the native popup needs real on-device confirmation, not just a passing test. (3) The lost blank lines between numbered list items: confirmed the original double-spacing heuristic (each list item wrapped in its own <p>, the Google Docs/Word pattern) doesn't match Google AI's real output, since Lynn's actual bold/numbering survived but spacing didn't. Rather than guess again blind, added a second heuristic (an explicit inline margin-bottom/padding-bottom on the list item, a common alternative spacing signal, confirmed working with a direct test) AND diagnostic logging that captures the actual raw HTML structure the next time a real list paste happens, so any further fix is built from Google AI's real markup, not another guess. Verified directly: the new inline-style-based detection confirmed producing correctly blank-line-separated output; the user-select suppression mechanism's own state changes confirmed correct (applied while empty, restored after resolution, restored on typing); every existing paste test re-run and confirmed unaffected. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1838 - MAJOR NEW FEATURE: the "want to paste?" offer, per Lynn's explicit request and a real design goal from months earlier that had been dropped along the way -- discourage iOS's native paste (which can silently corrupt rich content, per her report elsewhere this version) by offering this app's own safe, smart paste instead, right at the moment iOS's own native context menu tends to appear. Triggered on the SECOND tap at the very start of a still-empty note; checks the clipboard is non-empty and that this exact content hasn't already been asked about (reusing the existing, already-working _hasAskedForClip()/_markClipAsked() fingerprint tracking, persisted to localStorage so it survives app restarts -- confirmed this infrastructure was already fully intact, just missing a UI that called it). Simple, neutral card (not an accent-colored banner) with a Paste button and an X to dismiss -- deliberately avoided the hardcoded-white-on-accent-color pattern already found and fixed elsewhere in this app, confirmed #update-banner still has this exact same risk (not touched, out of scope for this request). Accepting tries the newer, multi-flavor navigator.clipboard.read() API to get the clipboard's HTML flavor too, routed through the exact same smart conversion (bold/headings/lists/footnote-stripping, built this same version) the toolbar Paste button and native-paste handler use -- not a plainer, HTML-blind fallback -- falling back to plain text if HTML isn't available. Extracted the HTML-to-markdown conversion out of the native-paste event handler into a new, standalone convertHtmlToNoteMarkdown() function first, specifically so this new feature and the existing native-paste intercept share the exact same logic rather than risk drifting apart as two separate copies -- confirmed the refactor was completely behavior-preserving by re-running all four existing HTML-paste tests and getting byte-identical output before and after. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed the offer does NOT appear after only one qualifying tap, confirmed it DOES appear on the second; confirmed accepting correctly inserts the clipboard text and dismisses the card; confirmed dismissing (either via Paste or the X) correctly prevents the SAME clipboard content from triggering the offer again, even in a completely different note; confirmed the offer never appears at all on a note that already has real content, regardless of tap count. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1837 - Two blank-line/spacing preservation fixes to the smart HTML paste, per Lynn's explicit requirement: no newlines, blank lines, or indentation should be lost when pasting rich text. Confirmed a real, concrete bug by testing directly rather than assuming the v0.1836 rewrite already handled this correctly: two adjacent <p> paragraphs from HTML collapsed together with nothing separating them, even though the source explicitly had a blank line between them -- every block-level tag (P, DIV, BR, TR) was treated identically, a single trailing newline each. Fixed by giving <p> specifically (not the more ambiguous <div>, often just a generic layout wrapper, and not <br>, a soft line break within the same paragraph) its own double-newline treatment, so a real paragraph break now produces a real blank line. Also added: double-spaced list preservation. Rich text sources (Google Docs, Word HTML exports, Google AI's own output) commonly signal "this list has visible spacing between items" by wrapping each item's content in its own <p> tag -- detected directly and now adds a matching blank line after that specific item, while a normal list with no such wrapping stays correctly single-spaced. Verified directly and thoroughly: confirmed the exact bug first (two bold-containing paragraphs collapsing to a single newline), confirmed the fix produces a real blank line in the same test; confirmed a Google-Docs-style <p>-wrapped three-item list preserves blank lines between every item; separately confirmed a normal, non-wrapped list stays single-spaced, so the fix is genuinely conditional and doesn't over-apply; re-ran every test from the v0.1836 build (the combined heading/bold/footnote/list/link test, and the nested-list test) to confirm nothing regressed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1836 - MAJOR REWRITE: smart HTML-aware paste, per Lynn's report of copying Google AI results and getting 50 raw URLs with no bold, bullets, or headings. Found the exact, complete mechanism by reading the existing HTML-paste code directly, not guessing: (1) it only ever intercepted a paste at all if the clipboard HTML contained at least one link -- rich content with bold/headings/lists but zero links got none of this function's help and fell straight through to plain-text-only browser paste, since a plain <textarea> can never receive rich HTML on its own; (2) headings and list items only ever got a trailing newline, never an actual markdown marker (# for headings, - or N. for list items), so they silently flattened into unmarked plain lines even when the function DID run; (3) every single link -- including tiny superscript footnote citations, which is exactly how Google AI (and most citation systems) render references -- expanded into its full raw URL on its own line, with no way to distinguish a real link from a citation reference. All three fixed together: the intercept now triggers on ANY richness signal (links, bold, italic, headings, or lists), not just links specifically; headings now become real '#'-N markdown, list items become real '- ' or 'N. ' markers with correct per-level counters and 4-space nested indentation matching this app's own convention; and a link whose visible text is JUST a number is now recognized as a footnote citation and stripped entirely (neither the number nor the URL kept), while a genuine link converts to a real, clickable [text](url) markdown link (upgraded from the older "text\nurl" the original function used, since this app added real markdown link support in v0.1645, after this function was first written, and never went back to use it here). CAUGHT AND FIXED TWO REAL BUGS DURING TESTING, not assumed correct on the first pass: whitespace-only text nodes between block tags (common in real-world clipboard HTML, not just hand-formatted test markup) were leaking into the output as stray blank lines and unwanted leading spaces on otherwise-clean content lines -- fixed at the actual source (a text node that's pure whitespace AND contains a newline is dropped; a single meaningful space between inline elements is left completely untouched); and a nested list inside a list item ran directly into whatever text came before it with no line break at all (e.g. "Second    - Nested bullet A" all as one broken line) -- fixed by ensuring a newline precedes a nested UL/OL specifically. Also added a small, safe polish: a space left directly before punctuation when a footnote citation right before it gets stripped ("claim ." from "claim [1].") now collapses cleanly.
