Verified directly and thoroughly with a real simulated paste event, not just calling the function in isolation: a combined test covering a heading, bold, italic, two adjacent footnote citations, a bulleted list, a numbered list, and a genuine link all together produced exactly the correct markdown output with nothing left to clean up by hand; a separate nested-list test (ordered list containing a nested unordered list inside one of its items) confirmed correct indentation and correct separation onto its own line, re-verified after the fix. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1835 - TWO Find-in-Note issues, per Lynn. (1) MAJOR FIX: the typing-mode scroll landing under the lower toolbar, per her report and her own suggested approach (verify actual visibility and correct for it, rather than continuing to hand-tune a fixed offset). Root cause found by reading pinToolbars()'s own comment directly ("Position find bar just below the lower toolbar"): the find bar sits BELOW the header and regular toolbar, both of which stay visible and stacked above it -- but the scroll-clearance calculation only ever accounted for the find bar's own height, never the header/toolbar height sitting above it, confirmed directly to be missing over 90px of real clearance in a typical case. Corrected to the exact same total-clearance formula pinToolbars() itself already uses for the content area's own padding-top, so the two can never drift out of sync with each other again. Also implements Lynn's own suggested robustness layer on top of the corrected formula: after scrolling, re-measures on the next frame and verifies the match is ACTUALLY visible, correcting further if it still isn't -- catches any remaining edge case without needing to enumerate every possible cause of covering in advance. (2) Investigated the Debug Log wrong-match report thoroughly, but could not reproduce it with realistic test content (tried both a typical multi-line log format and one containing a real URL, searching for a common term in each -- neither reproduced a mismatch). Did find a genuine, real architectural risk while investigating: R&R-mode Find highlighting works by embedding raw-text placeholder markers directly into the note's content BEFORE running it through the full markdown rendering pipeline (urlsToLinks()) -- which itself performs several TEXT-SHRINKING passes (images, code spans, links, each replacing a matched span with a much shorter placeholder) on that same raw string. If a search query ever sits near or overlaps one of those other markdown constructs, an embedded marker could in principle survive but end up wrapping different text than intended, since it has no way to "know" what it's supposed to wrap, only where it was inserted -- this is a plausible mechanism for exactly what Lynn described, even though it didn't reproduce with the specific content tried. Rather than guess at a fix blind, added the diagnostic logging she herself suggested: every rendered match now gets sanity-checked against the actual search query, and any real mismatch will now log a clear, specific message (which note, which match index, expected vs actual text) automatically the next time this happens, rather than needing her to notice and describe it after the fact. Verified directly: the Find scroll fix confirmed with real header/toolbar/find-bar heights showing the match correctly clearing all three, and separately confirmed what the OLD formula would have produced for direct comparison (90px short); the new diagnostic-logging code's own detection logic confirmed correct via an artificially-forced mismatch, since a naturally-occurring one couldn't be reproduced. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1833-0.1834 - FIVE things from Lynn's to-do list, tackled while she was multitasking on something else. (1) FIX, and an apology owed: the Typing/R&R mode button's wrong accent color, which Lynn had already reported once this session -- my earlier investigation only checked the R&R-mode .active CSS class (genuinely correct) and never examined the actual culprit: a completely separate inline-style code path, applied specifically when switching TO typing mode, that used the general accent directly (bypassing the note-accent fallback) plus a hardcoded white foreground with no contrast checking. Fixed to match the established pattern, confirmed directly with distinct accent colors this time. (2) FIX: the QAB letter-reset bug Lynn described (an E left over from a previous action, mistaken for a currently-different button) -- changing an already-configured slot's action now always resets its letter to match the new action, not just when the slot was previously empty. (3) FIX: the quiet-mode back+speak pill's two segments visibly mismatching in size at larger icon settings -- root cause turned out deeper than expected: neither button's own container size was ever tied to the icon-size setting in quiet mode at all, both were separately hardcoded to a fixed 40px, and since that's a floor rather than a ceiling, each button grew past it to fit its own icon at large sizes -- unevenly, since the back button's chevron is deliberately 30% larger than standard (an existing, intentional design choice, not something removed). Fixed by extending the existing JS-driven icon-sizing mechanism so both segments share one computed minimum based on the larger (chevron-inclusive) footprint, scoped to quiet mode only. (4) FIX: the rename-note pencil icon overlapping cursive note names, per Lynn's report -- small, deliberate increase to the gap between title and icon (2px to 7px), enough clearance for a slanted font's trailing flourishes without losing the intended snug feel. (5) NEW: a prototype QAB collapse handle, per Lynn's explicit request -- a large, thick, obvious tab at the bottom-left of the note editor (56x72px, deliberately not styled like the small, mouse-only scratch-pad edge tab, since low-vision touch accessibility was the explicit goal here) that shows or hides the QAB bar with one tap, letting it stay permanently enabled without always being visible. Opt-in via its own new Settings toggle (off by default, explicitly experimental), with a chevron that flips direction to match state. CAUGHT A REAL BUG BEFORE SHIPPING: the handle's own initial HTML had a redundant inline style="display:none", which -- being an inline style -- unconditionally overrode the CSS class-based visibility rule regardless of specificity, meaning the handle would never have actually appeared even with its setting correctly enabled. Found by testing the actual visibility, not just the click behavior (which worked regardless, since .click() fires on hidden elements too) -- fixed by removing the redundant inline style, since the CSS already had its own correct default-hidden state. Verified directly and thoroughly throughout: the view-mode button's fix confirmed with real distinct note/general accent colors; the QAB letter reset confirmed changing from a real previous value to a real new one; the quiet-mode pill fix confirmed with both segments measuring identically at the largest icon size, where the mismatch was most visible; the collapse handle confirmed with the actual computed visibility (not just that clicking did something) for both enabled and disabled states, and confirmed the full expand-collapse-expand cycle with real screenshots of both states, not just DOM state alone. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8), including the structural nesting check, and a scoped div-balance count specifically for the new Settings row confirming it's genuinely self-contained (4 opens, 4 closes) rather than trusting the whole-file delta alone.
  v0.1832 - FOUR things, per Lynn. (1) MOVED: Headings & Note Name Font Face, now below Advanced Typography instead of right after Note Font Face -- a rarely-used setting she didn't want appearing before the typography settings. (2) NEW: an explicit Select All button in the Edit Paragraph dialog, grouped with Paste on the right with a deliberate gap between them -- there won't always be a physical keyboard for Ctrl+A, and dragging to select text can be difficult for senior citizens. (3) NEW: a third, optional "Keep Editing" button on the Settings unsaved-changes prompt, per Lynn's report of accidentally clicking outside the dialog and only being offered Save or Discard when she actually just wanted to keep working. Confirmed technically feasible before building it, not assumed: the Settings dialog itself is still fully intact underneath the confirm popup at that point -- nothing has torn it down yet, the popup is purely a layered overlay -- so dismissing it without triggering either Save or Discard genuinely does mean "nothing changed, keep going," no callback even needed. Extended the SHARED confirm-dialog component (used throughout this app for many other prompts) with a new optional third button, defaulting to hidden unless a caller explicitly provides a label -- every other existing call site is completely unaffected. (4) FIX, likely root cause found for a real bug: Lynn's report of the PWA appearing "half updated" twice in one day -- the version number and update banner correctly showing the new version, while actual behavior still ran old, stale code, until a full force-quit and reopen. Found by reading the actual service worker fetch handler directly: fetch(event.request) passed the original Request object with no explicit cache directive, meaning this "network-first" HTML fetch -- despite the surrounding code's clear intent -- could still be silently satisfied by the BROWSER's own HTTP cache layer (entirely separate from the Cache API used everywhere else in this file) if GitHub Pages' response headers permitted it within their cache window. Fixed with an explicit cache:'no-store' on that one fetch call, in both the stable and dev service worker files (delivered separately, versioned _v2, replacing the existing _v1 files) -- guarantees a genuinely fresh network fetch every time, matching what this code already believed it was doing. Verified directly and thoroughly: the third button confirmed staying completely hidden for an ordinary, unrelated confirm call (e.g. a delete confirmation), confirmed correctly appearing with the right label only for the Settings unsaved-changes case, and confirmed that clicking it closes the popup while Settings stays open with the unsaved change still fully intact, not reverted; Select All confirmed actually selecting the real full text range in the textarea, not just clicked without effect; the Settings row reorder confirmed via real measured position relative to its new neighbors (caught and fixed a timing issue in the test itself along the way -- Settings wasn't actually open yet the first time it was checked). Suite: 332/332 (harness v8), including the structural nesting check.
  v0.1831 - NEW: Headings & Note Name Font Face, per Lynn's request -- a cursive/handwriting body font can look great for note text but distracting for markdown headings and the note title, so this lets those two use a different, calmer font face while leaving the note body untouched. Font FACE only, deliberately no size/weight controls -- headings already correctly vary size by level and the title has its own fixed size/weight, matching Lynn's explicit request to keep this simple. Defaults to "Default (match note text font)" as an actual dropdown option (matching the exact pattern already used for Note & Folder Name / Note Preview font settings), not a separate toggle, and is themeable (SETTING_BUCKETS_NOTE_APPEARANCE) per her request. Answers her direct question about typing mode: confirmed technically possible for the note title specifically (a genuine, separate DOM element, not embedded in the note's own textarea) -- and built it to work in both modes. Markdown headings remain R&R-mode-only, correctly matching her own guess about a real technical limitation: a plain textarea cannot selectively font-style just the heading LINES within otherwise-uniform text. Deliberately reuses the exact same full font list (Sans/Serif/Handwriting/Mono) as Note Content Text Font Face, including Handwriting, since the whole point is choosing something calmer than an otherwise-cursive body font. CAUGHT A SERIOUS BUG BEFORE SHIPPING, via the regression suite's own structural check, not missed: an earlier edit's old_str/new_str pair silently deleted the opening wrapper of the adjacent "Preview — tap to edit" row while leaving its content behind -- exactly the "expanding one section also expands a sibling section" bug class this project has hit before, confirmed by the harness's own targeted structural check (which flagged Note Behavior and Debugging nesting one level too deep) before a single functional test was even run. Found and fixed at the exact source (the missing wrapper + label), confirmed with a scoped div-balance count before and after (279/279 balanced after, vs 277/278 before), and reconfirmed the harness's full structural check passes clean with no warning. Verified directly and thoroughly, re-run against the corrected file rather than just the pre-fix version: headings and note title both default to matching the note's current font; explicitly overriding switches both correctly while the note BODY stays completely unaffected; heading size (confirmed H1 still computes to exactly double the base size) and weight (700) both confirmed completely untouched by this setting; the note title confirmed picking up the override in BOTH typing and R&R mode. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8), including its structural nesting check specifically.
  v0.1830 - Documentation only, per Lynn's explicit request -- no behavior change, confirmed by the regression suite passing identically. Added a clear, deliberate-decision comment at both places someone would naturally look when touching the notes-list preview (the CSS rule in .note-item-preview, and the JS source of truth in applySettings()'s "Notes list fonts" block), recording the architecture settled in discussion this session: every note's preview intentionally uses ONE SHARED font/size/weight/variation/style/stretch/feature/color, not each note's own individually pinned style, even once per-note themes exist and could technically override these properties. Lynn considered per-note matching directly and decided against it -- a list where every entry renders in a visibly different font would likely be harder to scan at a glance, not easier, the same reasoning already behind the existing global (not per-note) preview color setting. Explicitly recorded so this isn't mistaken for an oversight and "fixed" into per-note matching by a future session (or by Lynn's own future self, per her own stated worry about forgetting and re-raising it) -- and clarifies what must still hold true: the shared value must never go stale against the current/live note-font setting, which was the actual real bug already fixed in v0.1829. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8), confirming zero functional change.
  v0.1829 - THREE things, per Lynn. (1) FIX: the notes-list home-screen preview (and each individual note entry's preview) never reflecting stylistic-set changes, or any variable-axis change beyond weight -- confirmed the exact same category of gap already found once for R&R mode: font-feature-settings, font-variation-settings, font-stretch, and font-style were completely missing from this rule. Falls back to the note editor's own current values, so it now correctly matches live via CSS variable inheritance rather than needing a separate change-listener. IMPORTANT ARCHITECTURAL FINDING, flagged rather than silently worked around: confirmed the current system uses ONE SHARED set of CSS variables for every note's preview simultaneously -- meaning even the properties that already worked (font/size/weight) cannot currently reflect a per-note theme override on the home screen, since nothing there resolves each note's own theme individually. This fix is correct and complete for the common case (no per-note override, true for virtually every note today), but genuine per-note-aware preview rendering -- computing each entry's own resolved style rather than one shared value -- would need a larger restructuring of renderNotesList() itself, not attempted here pending Lynn's direction on scope. (2) Per Lynn's decision after comparing both new fonts: Playwrite Peru fully removed -- @font-face, dropdown entry, pill, weight registry, and stylistic-set registry all confirmed cleanly gone, GB J covers what she needs. (3) NEW: GB J's italic, per Lynn's request -- a genuinely separate font file (confirmed via direct fvar inspection: no italic/slant axis, upright and italic are two distinct exports), downloaded from TypeTogether's own GitHub and confirmed via direct GSUB inspection to share the exact same 10-set stylistic structure as the upright version. Registered under the same font-family with font-style:italic, the standard CSS mechanism for an upright/italic pair -- the browser selects it automatically wherever font-style:italic is applied. New Italic toggle in Settings, shown only for fonts registered in a new FONT_HAS_ITALIC registry (so it's invisible for every other font, which has no italic file to select). CAUGHT AND FIXED A REAL BUG before it shipped: the new toggle's own conditional show/hide only worked the FIRST time Settings opened -- clearSettingsSearch() (called on every subsequent open, to undo search-hiding) unconditionally resets every .settings-row's display style, which also silently wiped out this row's OWN conditional hide, since it's a static, always-present element rather than something rebuilt fresh each time like the stylistic-set checkboxes (which aren't affected by this). Fixed inside clearSettingsSearch() itself rather than at just one of its 5 call sites, so every path is covered automatically. Also found and fixed a related gap on the way: the toggle's effect was only wired into Settings-dialog-specific sync functions at first, not the general applySettings() path that runs on every note open -- fixed there directly, guarded so italic can never leak onto a font with no italic file even from a stale saved value. Verified directly and thoroughly: the notes-list preview confirmed showing the exact right font-family and feature-settings with real stylistic-set values; Peru confirmed fully absent from every registry; both GB J @font-face rules (upright and italic) confirmed present with correct paths; the toggle confirmed hidden for a non-italic font and visible for GB J -- including a second, explicit re-check after actually calling openSettings() a full time through, which is what caught the clearSettingsSearch() bug in the first place, not just checking the function in isolation; toggling on confirmed updating both the setting and the live computed font-style on the real editor element; switching away from GB J confirmed correctly reverting to normal, with no italic leaking onto the new font. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1828 - THREE things, per Lynn. (1) FIX: the Quick Action Bar bouncing up and down during Ctrl+wheel zoom, per her report right after confirming last build's ballooning fix worked. Root cause: applyZoomSizeCompensation() hardcoded transform-origin as 'top left', correct for the header/toolbar (both top-anchored) but wrong for the QAB (bottom-anchored, bottom:0) -- scaling from the top pulls a bottom-anchored element's bottom edge upward as zoom changes, which is exactly the bounce described. Added an optional transformOrigin parameter (defaults to 'top left', so the two existing callers are completely unaffected) and passed 'bottom left' for the QAB specifically. Verified directly: under a real simulated devicePixelRatio change, the QAB's actual rendered bottom edge now measures exactly flush with the viewport bottom, not just that a transform value looks plausible. The separate, long-standing toolbar-collapse-handle bounce Lynn also mentioned was NOT touched -- flagged honestly rather than guessed at: this codebase's own history shows a prior attempt at a related fix for this exact element was reverted after a genuine regression, and the likely mechanism (its position is freshly recalculated from a live measurement of the header on every single pinToolbars() call, unlike the QAB's simpler static anchor) isn't something this sandbox can properly reproduce or verify -- a real, continuous mouse-wheel gesture on Lynn's own desktop is what this actually needs. (2) NEW: two more Playwrite country variants, per Lynn's request after Ireland worked well and she wanted an upright (non-slanted) option to compare -- Playwrite GB J (England Joined) and Playwrite PE (Peru). Both downloaded directly from TypeTogether's own GitHub (the same source Ireland came from), converted to woff2, and self-hosted from the very first version this time -- Ireland's original mistake (ever routing through Google's feature-stripping CDN) isn't repeated. Confirmed via direct GSUB/fvar table inspection of the real files, not assumed from Ireland's own mapping: both share the exact same weight-only 100-400 range and the exact same 10-set stylistic structure (embedded feature names read directly from each file independently matched Ireland's confirmed mapping exactly). Both wired into every location Ireland already has: the note-font dropdown, quick-pick pills, the weight-support registry, and the stylistic-sets registry. The two new woff2 files are delivered as separate files alongside this build, versioned per the project's own file-naming rule (PlaywriteGBJ-v1.woff2 / PlaywritePE-v1.woff2) -- unlike Ireland's original, unversioned file. (3) Per Lynn's own direct question about whether these stylistic sets should really be a slider instead of on/off toggles: confirmed, not guessed, that they can't be -- OpenType stylistic sets are discrete glyph substitutions with no continuous "amount," so a slider isn't technically meaningful for them regardless of design preference. Separately confirmed that Playwrite's general design engine does have a genuinely continuous axis for curve shape ("speed"), validating her instinct that such a parameter could exist -- but also reconfirmed, from this project's own earlier direct fvar inspection of the Ireland file, that individual exported country variants only expose weight; the other three engine-level axes are pinned to fixed values per country, not left adjustable. No code changes from this discussion -- informational only, so the checkbox-vs-radio-button question (still open, pending Lynn's own visual testing of set combinations) isn't affected by it either way. Verified directly and thoroughly: the QAB zoom-position fix confirmed via real measurement, not just plausible-looking style values; both new @font-face rules, both dropdown entries, both weight-registry entries, both stylistic-set-registry entries (all 10 tags each), and both quick-pick pills confirmed present and correctly wired -- nothing assumed to have copied correctly without checking. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1827 - MAJOR FIX: Playwrite Ireland's stylistic sets, per Lynn's report that they still had no visible effect at all even after self-hosting the font file exactly as instructed. Root cause found by fetching and reading the LIVE deployed site's actual source directly (not assumed from memory): there was no @font-face rule anywhere in the file -- the app was still exclusively loading Playwrite IE through the Google Fonts @import, completely ignoring Lynn's correctly-placed self-hosted copy. This was a real gap in this project's own process, not anything wrong on her end: a standalone preview file was built and delivered to prove the font itself works when self-hosted, but the actual integration into the live app -- making it USE that file instead of Google's -- was never built at all. Also confirmed directly: the file exists and is genuinely reachable on GitHub, and GitHub Pages requires no special permission or configuration to serve a .woff2 file -- nothing needed on Lynn's end beyond what she'd already done. Fixed: (1) removed Playwrite+IE from the Google Fonts @import entirely; (2) added a real @font-face pointing to PlaywriteIE.woff2 via a relative URL (resolves correctly since the file sits next to index.html), with a 100-400 weight range matching what the removed Google import segment previously requested. FOUND AND FIXED A SECOND, BROADER BUG while investigating: R&R/view mode (.note-viewer) was missing font-feature-settings, font-variation-settings, font-stretch, AND font-style entirely -- not just the one property Lynn's report was specifically about. Confirmed by direct comparison against #note-editor and the other three locations that already had all four -- meaning variable font axes and stylistic sets never had any visible effect in R&R mode at all, for any font, not just Playwrite Ireland's stylistic sets specifically. All four added. Verified directly and thoroughly: the @font-face rule confirmed present with the correct family name, relative URL, and weight range; confirmed zero remaining references to Playwrite+IE anywhere in the file after the import edit; the full pipeline confirmed end to end in both modes with real checkbox values (ss01 and ss19 both on) -- typing mode already worked, and R&R mode now correctly shows the exact same font-family and feature-settings it was silently missing before. Genuinely can't verify the final visual rendering myself (this sandbox has no path to actually download and render Lynn's real font file), so this needs a real check on her own device once deployed -- flagged honestly rather than assumed. The checkbox-vs-radio-button design question deliberately left open until she can see real stylistic-set combinations rendering, rather than guessed at further from documentation alone. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1826 - THREE things, per Lynn. (1) FIX: the "+" FAB, covered by the Quick Action Bar when both are visible. First attempt (a CSS calc() rule) turned out to be dead code -- confirmed directly that the FAB's position is actually controlled by applyZoomPositionCompensation() (called from pinToolbars(), which runs far more often than any QAB-specific trigger), which always sets bottom as a direct inline style, unconditionally overriding any CSS rule regardless of specificity. Rebuilt to fold the QAB's actual rendered height into that real, authoritative mechanism instead. A further subtlety: the offset is a hybrid of a fixed intended distance (28px, which needs zoom scaling) and a live measurement (the QAB's real height, already correctly expressed in the current coordinate frame, which must NOT be divided by zoom factor again) -- passing a hybrid like that through the generic anchor/zf division would double-compensate the live portion, so this follows the same "set explicitly, layer scale only" pattern already established for the Settings dialog's own dynamically-positioned FAB rather than the simpler path used for note-fab's own plain (pre-QAB) case. A single ResizeObserver on the QAB bar itself (catching every way its visibility can change -- enabled/disabled, real-keyboard-open, quiet-mode-conditional -- without a separate hook for each) triggers pinToolbars() to keep this current. (2) NEW: lazy-loaded font previews in the custom picker, per Lynn's question about whether showing every option in its own real font forces loading every font upfront -- confirmed yes: showing live text in a font genuinely forces the browser to download it, so a 30+ font list would trigger 30+ simultaneous downloads the instant the picker opened. Deferred via a per-picker IntersectionObserver, scoped to the picker's own scrollable list as the observer root (not the page), so "visible" correctly means within this dialog, not the whole browser viewport. Kept fully generic -- the observer itself has no notion of "font" at all, just a deferred style string -- consistent with the component's portability goal. (3) FIX, caught by Lynn before it ever reached her: the QAB bar is a new position:fixed, left:0/right:0 full-width bar -- architecturally identical to the note header/toolbar in this specific respect -- and had never been given the zoom-size-compensation treatment those two already have (would have ballooned wider than the viewport on Ctrl+wheel zoom-out, the exact failure mode already fixed for header/toolbar back in v0.1716). Simply didn't exist yet when that original work was done. Added the same applyZoomSizeCompensation() call already used for header/toolbar. Verified directly and thoroughly, not just structurally: the FAB's computed bottom confirmed at exactly 28px normally and 125px (28 + real QAB height + 12px gap) with the QAB visible, correctly back to 28px after disabling; the lazy preview confirmed with a real 30-item list -- only 9 near-visible options had their style applied initially, the far-offscreen last option had none until actually scrolled into view, at which point it correctly picked it up, with the real in-app font picker confirmed visually unchanged by screenshot; the QAB's zoom compensation confirmed engaging correctly under a real simulated devicePixelRatio change (scale and width computed exactly per the established formula), AND confirmed correctly leaving the bar completely untouched on a genuine touch/no-mouse context, using Playwright's real touch-device emulation rather than only checking the mouse-present path. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1825 - FOUR more fixes, per Lynn's follow-up testing. (1) FIX: the font weight nudge buttons' toast was misleading at the boundary -- it fired the same "Font weight: X" message on every tap regardless of whether the weight actually changed, so repeatedly tapping past a font's real max/min (e.g. Comic Relief's 700) looked like something was still happening when nothing was. Now detects whether the value genuinely changed and says so honestly ("Already at heaviest/lightest weight this font supports") when it didn't. (2) FIX, more robustly: Find in Note's keyboard overlap, per Lynn's report that the v0.1823 fix was insufficient -- still overlapped with a larger font size or an expanded (non-collapsed) toolbar. Root cause of the INSUFFICIENCY: that fix added scroll room but the scroll TARGET itself still used a fixed "2 lines below the find bar" offset that doesn't scale down when the actually-available visible band (keyboard-shrunk viewport) is itself smaller -- exactly matching her report that a taller font (taller lines) or more UI chrome eating into the same space made it worse, and a collapsed toolbar (more room) made it better. Replaced with the same "check if the match is already inside the real, keyboard-aware visible band; only scroll if it isn't; land it just inside the band's edge" pattern this codebase already trusts elsewhere for this exact class of problem, rather than an arbitrary fixed offset. (3) FIX: Quick Action Bar's "lit up" toggle-button color, per Lynn's report -- it was using the general accent color instead of the note-specific one, the established convention roughly 18 other selectors in this app already follow. New, separate _noteAccentContrastColor() added rather than changing the existing _accentContrastColor(), since that one is also correctly used by the Settings-dialog slider buttons, which should stay tied to the GENERAL accent regardless of note. Directly investigated Lynn's related report that the upper toolbar's Typing/R&R mode button (the old "view/edit" button) had the same problem -- confirmed, with real distinct test colors, that it was ALREADY correct; the misleading result in an early check traced to reading the computed color before its own 150ms CSS transition had settled, not a real bug. (4) FIX: Quick Action Bar's Tap to Edit action, per Lynn's explicit architectural correction -- there's no such thing as tap-to-edit in typing mode (it's purely an R&R-mode concept). Tapping this QAB button from typing mode now switches to R&R mode first and then turns tap-to-edit on, rather than calling enterEditBlockMode() in a context where it doesn't make sense. New, generic availableWhen capability added to the QUICK_ACTION_BAR_ACTIONS registry (matching how isActive was added) -- Tap to Edit is now excluded from the QAB action dropdown entirely when R&R mode is fully disabled (noteEditingMode === 'typing'), and a previously-configured slot referencing it now quietly disappears from a rendered bar rather than showing a button that would behave incorrectly if tapped, if R&R gets disabled after the fact. Verified directly and thoroughly throughout: weight toast confirmed showing the honest boundary message at both 700 and 400 for a real static font, and the normal message for a real in-range change; Find's scroll fix confirmed with a real simulated keyboard AND a large font size together, matching Lynn's exact reported combination; the view-mode-btn's color confirmed correct with real distinct accent colors after eliminating the transition-timing artifact; QAB's active-state background confirmed showing the distinct note-accent red, not the general blue; Tap to Edit confirmed switching to R&R mode and turning on correctly from typing mode, confirmed excluded from the dropdown and correctly disappearing from a rendered bar when R&R is disabled, and confirmed still available/included normally when R&R is enabled. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1824 - Quick Add ("Add text to note") dialog reordered, per Lynn's explicit request: the collapsible "More options" section (blank lines, dividing line, hyphen, checkbox marker, tag-sort) now sits BELOW the Cancel/Add button row instead of above it. She uses this dialog dozens of times a day and the buttons kept ending up underneath the virtual keyboard, meaning she had to dismiss the keyboard before she could even tap Add -- moving More options out of the way means the buttons themselves now sit higher on screen, reachable without that extra step. Explicitly a deliberate departure from the usual "primary actions go last" convention, made for a concrete, named friction reason rather than by default. Since More options no longer lives inside the dialog's own already-scrollable middle region (which is itself capped and scrollable specifically so nothing can get clipped by .confirm-box's 80vh limit), it was given its own bounded scroll (max-height:35vh, overflow-y:auto) as a safety net -- if its content ever got tall enough to matter when expanded, it scrolls within its own space instead of being silently clipped or pushing the dialog past its cap. Verified directly: the button row's own vertical position now measures above More options' position (not just assumed from the HTML order); the section still opens/closes correctly in its new spot; all 6 checkboxes present and independently confirmed toggleable, not just rendered; screenshot confirmation of the full expanded state showing Cancel/Add sitting right below the text box with the checkboxes correctly laid out beneath. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1823 - FOUR fixes, per Lynn. (1) NEW setting: Quick Action Bar "Show in Quiet Mode", off by default (preserves the existing hide-in-quiet-mode behavior exactly). Directly motivated by v0.1820's toggle-type QAB buttons -- a toggle-type button like Quiet mode itself does no good if the whole bar disappears the instant quiet mode turns on. (2) FIX: font weight increase/decrease buttons now jump directly to the next weight the current font actually supports, rather than a flat +/-10 that could silently do nothing for several taps in a row on a font with sparse discrete weights (e.g. a font supporting only 400 and 700 needed 30 taps of the old flat step to visibly change at all, since every intermediate value the browser can't render just displays identically to whichever real weight is closest). New getNextSupportedWeight() reuses the existing FONT_WEIGHT_SUPPORT registry -- exact same underlying data the sliders already use for their own variable-step-size fix, just never applied to these buttons until now. Variable fonts keep their existing fine flat step (every value is genuinely valid), now correctly clamped to that specific font's real range instead of the generic 100-900 bounds. (3) FIX: the "Show 'Back' label on large screens" text now uses the accent color instead of inheriting the toolbar's plain foreground -- confirmed exact color match against --accent directly. (4) FIX: Find in Note's keyboard overlap in typing mode, per Lynn's report -- the match's scroll-to position never called this app's own keyboard-aware scroll-room function, unlike every other similar scroll-to-position call site in this codebase (the exact bug class already documented and fixed everywhere else back in v0.1666: "a perfectly correct target could never actually be reached... with little or no room below"), just never applied to this one path. CAUGHT A SERIOUS BUG IN THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT THIS, via testing rather than assuming it worked: the keyboard-scroll-room function is closure-scoped inside init(), but the Find code calling it lives at the top level with no access to it -- the naive fix would have thrown a real ReferenceError and broken Find in Note's next-match navigation entirely, a materially worse regression than the cosmetic keyboard-overlap issue being fixed. Caught immediately by testing before shipping, fixed by exporting the function on window, matching this codebase's own established convention for exactly this cross-scope need. Also flagged, not chased down: a small, pre-existing HTML/text count discrepancy was noticed while double-checking balance, confirmed to already exist in the prior delivered build (not introduced by this one) and confirmed not to be affecting the regression suite, which has passed cleanly throughout -- most likely changelog prose text containing the literal characters "div" rather than a real unclosed tag, the same category of false positive already found and fixed once this session for a different tag. Verified directly, not assumed: weight-jump confirmed with a real static font (400->700 in one tap each direction) and a real variable font (clamped correctly at its own range boundary, not the generic one); the quiet-mode setting confirmed hiding/showing correctly in both states, end to end, not just checking the class toggle in isolation; the BACK label's computed color confirmed to exactly match the accent CSS variable; the Find keyboard fix confirmed twice -- first that the ReferenceError is genuinely gone, then separately, with a real simulated 300px keyboard shrink, that the resulting scroll padding computes to exactly the expected value using the app's own established formula. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1822 - Accessibility hardening on two fronts, per Lynn's request. (1) The new custom picker component (v0.1821) audited against common checker-tool and human-reviewer expectations, not just left as originally built: options switched from <button> to <div role="option" tabindex="-1"> with fully JS-managed roving focus, matching the W3C APG reference implementation exactly rather than the button-with-overridden-role pattern some automated checkers flag as a semantic mismatch; groups now properly wrapped in role="group" with a real aria-label, not just a sibling label with role="presentation", so a screen reader actually announces group membership while navigating; a visually-hidden aria-live region added so a screen reader user filtering the list hears the result count or "No matches", not just silence; type-ahead added (a single keypress jumps to the next option starting with that letter), matching native <select> behavior and especially valuable on a picker with no search box, where arrow-key-only navigation through a long list is slow; prefers-reduced-motion respected for the open/close transition; the close button enlarged to meet the standard 44x44px minimum touch target guideline. (2) The pre-existing toolbar-placement dropdown (from v0.1584, unrelated to the new component) given the keyboard navigation it never had -- confirmed by reading its code directly that it had no keydown handling at all beyond default Tab order, meaning using it required a pointer. Added arrow-key/Home/End navigation, Enter/Space to select, and Escape to close with focus correctly returned to the trigger button -- matching the same roving-focus convention just established for the new component. TESTING NOTE, disclosed rather than glossed over: several rounds of this session's own testing initially produced confusing, seemingly-contradictory results (focus silently landing on <body> instead of an option) that traced entirely to a testing artifact already learned once before in this project -- elements inside a collapsed Settings section are genuinely unfocusable (0x0, not rendered) regardless of correct code, not a real bug -- confirmed by directly checking getBoundingClientRect() before concluding anything, rather than trusting the first (misleading) result. Verified directly, with sections properly expanded this time: the custom picker's core mechanism, keyboard nav, groups, and live region all still work correctly after the hardening changes; the toolbar-placement dropdown's full lifecycle (open with correct initial focus on the current selection, arrow-navigate, Escape-close-and-return-focus, reopen, arrow-navigate, Enter-select) confirmed end to end with real values, not assumed. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1821 - MAJOR: custom picker component, per Lynn's request -- a reusable, accessible replacement for native <select> dropdowns, built specifically portable to future Claude PWA projects (a standalone reference file, custom-picker-component.html, ships alongside this build with the exact same component code, zero Remember dependencies, and two working live demos). Follows the W3C ARIA "listbox in a dialog" pattern rather than an invented one: focus trap, arrow-key navigation, Escape to cancel, Enter/Space to select, focus returned to the trigger on close. Modeled visually after the More menu per Lynn's own suggestion, but built as a centered/bottom overlay rather than an anchored dropdown, since it needs to work for long lists (30+ fonts) without running off-screen. CAUGHT AND FIXED SEVERAL REAL BUGS DURING DEVELOPMENT, not glossed over: (1) a scary near-miss where an early CSS edit accidentally dropped the .modal-sheet selector line entirely, caught by direct file inspection before any testing built on top of it; (2) Enter-to-select silently didn't work at all -- relied on native button-activation instead of explicit handling, which isn't actually correct for role="option" elements under the accessibility pattern being followed anyway; (3) a related bug caught before shipping -- treating Space as a universal select key would have blocked typing an actual space character into the search box; (4) a genuine architectural issue -- the first draft listened for keydown on document, which turned out fragile against Remember's own existing global keyboard shortcuts (confirmed at least two other document-level listeners already exist); one of them silently ate the Enter key before the picker ever saw it, fixed by scoping the listener to the dialog itself, which is also just more correct for a modal component; (5) the test harness caught a genuine false positive -- literally wrote the tag name for the page's own style element, spelled out with angle brackets, inside a JS comment as prose, which fooled the harness's own comment-balance checker into merging unrelated content into a bogus block, fixed by rewording. INTEGRATED into 8 real dropdowns, not just the 5 font ones originally planned -- Lynn's follow-up specifically named + Button Inserts At, the speech Voice picker, and Quick Action Bar Button Colors, found by auditing every <select> in the file rather than assuming the font ones were the only ones that mattered. While wiring these up, found and fixed SIX separate instances (across all 8 selects) of a real pre-existing pattern: code that silently syncs a select's value via a plain assignment (deliberately not dispatching 'change', to avoid triggering that select's full side effects just for a display sync) -- which would have left the new trigger button's label permanently stale after each one. Exposed an explicit _refreshPickerTriggerLabel() specifically so those exact spots can refresh just the label, with none of a full change event's side effects. Confirmed the Voice picker's async, browser-populated option list (fundamentally different from every other select's static HTML options) is still handled safely, since item-reading happens fresh every time the trigger is clicked rather than being cached once at setup. Also relaxed .settings-select from a select-only CSS rule to a general one, needed so the new trigger buttons could inherit the exact same visual styling -- confirmed this incidentally fixes a small, real, pre-existing gap too: the Quick Action Bar's own "Add another button" control already used this class but never actually got the styling, since the rule was select-tag-scoped. Verified directly and extensively throughout: core mechanism (selection, checkmarks, pre-selected state), keyboard navigation and Enter-select, Escape-cancel with proper cleanup, search/filter narrowing correctly, focus trap wrapping correctly in both directions, all 8 real integrations opening/selecting/updating settings correctly end to end, the async voice-population edge case specifically, font-preview styling rendering each option in its own actual typeface, and the standalone reference file working completely independently in total isolation from the rest of the app. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1820 - NEW: toggle-type Quick Action Bar buttons now "light up" (solid accent-color background, contrast-checked foreground) while their underlying state is active, per Lynn's request -- same visual language as the slider default/reset buttons, reusing the exact same contrast-picking helper rather than a new one. Systematically reviewed every action in the registry for toggle-ness rather than guessing, per Lynn's own follow-up catches: confirmed THREE genuine toggles -- Tap to Edit, Quiet mode, and (caught on Lynn's second follow-up) Show/hide toolbar, each wired to refresh at its real underlying state-change point (enterEditBlockMode/exitEditBlockMode, toggleReadingMode/clearReadingMode, applyToolbarCollapsed -- the last one deliberately chosen as a single hook since every toolbar-collapse code path, including on load, already routes through it). Also explicitly considered and deliberately excluded two borderline cases rather than silently guessing either way: Speak and Find both have an underlying active state, but neither of their actions actually TOGGLES it on repeat press (Speak restarts rather than stops; Find re-opens rather than closes) -- lighting them up would misrepresent how tapping them actually behaves, and changing that behavior would be a real design change beyond what was asked. Confirmed there's no existing toggle-classification data structure anywhere in the codebase to reuse (checked directly, per Lynn's own question, rather than assumed) -- DUAL_MODE_BUTTON_IDS is a different, unrelated concept (toolbar vs. More-menu placement). Extensible design: any action can now optionally provide an isActive() function; only genuine toggles need one. ALSO FIXED, a real pre-existing bug found while extending this same mechanism: the accent-contrast color (introduced for the slider buttons) was cached but never invalidated when the accent color itself changed, meaning changing your theme's accent would leave a stale, potentially illegible contrast color in place for the rest of the session -- fixed at the one place accent actually gets applied. Verified directly and thoroughly: all three toggles correctly light up on entry and correctly un-light on exit; a non-toggle action (Print) confirmed never receiving the active class under any condition; actual computed background colors confirmed switching between accent-green and the normal scheme's white; the accent-cache fix confirmed producing correct black-on-light-accent and white-on-dark-accent contrast after changing accent color twice; screenshot confirmation of two simultaneously-active toggle buttons alongside two inactive ones, visually correct. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1819 - MAJOR: Quick Action Bar redesigned from a fixed 4-button row into a scrollable bar supporting any number of buttons, per Lynn's request (motivated by her father specifically needing large Undo/Redo alongside his existing buttons). Also added: Tap to Edit, Undo, and Redo as new available actions, reusing the exact same underlying functions the real toolbar buttons already call. ARCHITECTURE: deliberately mirrors the lower toolbar's own proven scroll-arrow pattern rather than inventing a new one, per Lynn's own suggestion -- a genuinely scrollable inner container (which gets native swipe scrolling for free, not something needing separate implementation) plus explicit arrow buttons for a precise, discoverable, big alternative to swiping; same show-only-on-overflow, dim-and-disable-at-limits, hold-to-repeat behavior as the toolbar's arrows, for both mouse and touch. Buttons are now created/destroyed dynamically based on however many are actually configured, not a fixed loop of 4 -- confirmed fully backward compatible with existing saved 4-button configs (a plain array of 4 already worked correctly under the old fixed-loop code; the new forEach-based render produces an identical result for that same array, no migration needed). Settings UI: the old 4 static rows replaced with a dynamically-rendered list (each row's own listeners wired inline at creation, since rows now come and go rather than being fixed elements wired once) plus an explicit "+ Add another button" control and a per-row Remove button. Verified directly and extensively: 7 configured buttons correctly overflow a 400px viewport with both scroll arrows appearing; arrow dim/enable state confirmed correct at both the start and end of the scrollable range (with real settle-time verification, not just an immediate check); clicking an arrow correctly scrolls and updates the opposite arrow's state; the full Settings add/configure/remove lifecycle confirmed correct end to end (adding a 5th slot, picking an action for it with the letter auto-filling correctly, then removing a different row, correctly shrinks both the rendered rows and the underlying settings array back to the right length); a pre-existing plain 4-button saved config confirmed rendering identically under the new code. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1818 - CRITICAL FIX: crash when switching to R&R mode with an active Find in Note search, per Lynn's exact report ("undefined is not an object (evaluating 'el.classList.remove')"). Root cause confirmed precisely: _findMatches has a completely different shape depending on mode -- plain {start,end} position objects in typing mode (used with the textarea's own native selection), real DOM <mark> elements in R&R mode (used for click-to-jump and highlight styling) -- and nothing ever converted between the two shapes on a mode switch. Switching to R&R mode left the stale typing-mode position-objects in place; the very next 'next match' press then tried to call .classList on a plain object instead of a real element. Per Lynn's explicit request that this actually work rather than just avoid crashing (typing mode's keyboard popping back up on every 'next' press being the specific annoyance driving the request) -- rather than just guard against the crash, the search now genuinely survives a mode switch in EITHER direction: reuses runFind()'s own existing per-mode match-building logic (rather than duplicating it) to correctly rebuild _findMatches in whichever shape the new mode needs, then restores the same match INDEX she was on (clamped, in case the match count somehow changed) rather than resetting to the first match -- fixed a related bug found while implementing this: runFind()'s R&R-mode branch never actually respected its own skipJump parameter, unlike the typing-mode branch, which would have silently discarded her position even with a naive fix. Previously, leaving R&R mode always silently cancelled an active search entirely (never crashed, but never explained why); now both directions behave the same, consistent way. Verified directly, end to end: the exact reported crash scenario (search 'apple' in a 4-match note, advance to the third match, switch to R&R mode) produces zero errors, correctly rebuilds all 4 matches as real DOM elements, and correctly marks the SAME third match as current in the new mode; switching back to typing mode correctly restores the exact right text selection (confirmed character-precise); separately verified three edge cases produce no errors: a mode switch with no active search at all, an active search with zero matches, and that explicitly closing Find still works normally afterward. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1817 - NEW: Copy for Speech and Share for Speech added to the Quick Action Bar's available actions, per Lynn's request (for routing note text into external, more natural-sounding text-to-speech apps like ElevenReader). Reuses the exact same copyNoteForSpeech()/shareNoteForSpeech() functions the existing toolbar buttons and More menu items already call, rather than reimplementing the behavior -- guarantees identical behavior with zero risk of the two versions drifting apart. Since the Settings dropdown for each of the 4 Quick Action Bar slots is already dynamically generated from the same central QUICK_ACTION_BAR_ACTIONS registry the actual click-handling reads from, this was a pure data addition -- no separate UI wiring needed, confirmed directly rather than assumed. Verified directly: both new actions correctly call their real underlying functions (not placeholder or duplicated logic), and both correctly appear as selectable options in the actual rendered Settings dropdown. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1816 - FIX: 11 sliders the original survey genuinely missed, per Lynn's report -- bold cursor width, ruler thickness/transparency, all four "line position" sliders (ruled/dot-grid/checkbox-line/margin-line), and four bubble-mode sliders (padding/spacing/radius/shadow) found while re-auditing. Confirmed root cause directly rather than guessed: every one of these uses a plain range input with no settings-range class at all, an exact-match gap in the original class-based search, not something ambiguous. Also confirmed and fixed a second, genuinely separate bug Lynn flagged as a possibility: bold cursor width's slider and value sit inline together in one flex row, competing for space -- a new button there really could push off-screen. Fixed generally (not specific to this one row) by having the button-insertion logic set flex-wrap on whatever row it lands in; verified directly with a real bounding-box measurement (button's right edge lands at 480px in a 500px viewport, not clipped) plus a screenshot confirming a clean wrap. Caught a related third issue while wiring these: several rows put their unit (px, %) as separate text AFTER the value span rather than inside it, which would have wedged the new button between the number and its unit -- fixed by attaching to the row generally instead of directly after the number, confirmed backward-compatible with every slider from the last build too. NEW: a genuine Bold Cursor preview, per Lynn's report that one never existed. Deliberately reuses the app's actual, hard-won caret-measurement system (the same one powering the real note editor, Quick Add, and Edit Paragraph) on a real, focusable preview textarea registered into the same instance list, rather than building a simplified mockup that could end up looking different from the real thing. Verified directly via screenshot: the real colored cursor renders correctly mid-word in sample text, and a width change correctly shows once the preview is refocused -- confirmed this refocus requirement matches the real feature's own authentic behavior (a cursor only shows while its text box is actually focused) rather than being a bug. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1815 - MAJOR: default/reset buttons extended to all 23 remaining sliders in Settings, per Lynn's request, plus a much stronger visual treatment applied everywhere this pattern now exists. (1) STRONGER STYLING: per Lynn's explicit feedback that the previous text-color-only version didn't "light up" enough -- changed/non-default state is now solid accent-color BACKGROUND with a properly contrast-checked foreground, reusing the exact same contrast-picking approach already established for the move-line indicator (_pickHighContrastBW) rather than guessing at a color that might not read clearly against every theme's accent. Applied consistently to all three places this pattern now lives: the 23 sliders below, the axis sliders from v0.1814, and the original letter/word-spacing rows. (2) NEW: a general, reusable _wireSliderDefaultButton() helper wired to all 23 remaining sliders (UI/note/notes-list font sizes and weights, tooltip size, line heights, margins, editor font size and weight, view indent width, chars per line, speech rate/pause, quick-action-bar size). Deliberately non-invasive: adds a SEPARATE 'input' listener purely for button styling rather than touching any of the 23 existing, already-tested value-setting listeners, and inserts the button via generic DOM placement right after each slider's existing value-display span so it works regardless of each row's specific surrounding layout. Handles three real complications found while surveying every slider rather than assumed away: settings that default to null ("inherit contextually" -- several font-size/weight settings), a nested settings path (quickActionBar.size), and a genuine type mismatch (fontWeight's literal default is stored as the STRING '400' while the slider always writes a number, which a strict comparison would have misread as "changed" for anyone who'd never touched the slider at all -- caught before shipping and fixed with a loose-equality comparison). Verified directly across five representative, deliberately varied cases: a plain numeric default, a null/contextual default, the nested quick-action-bar path, the string-vs-number fontWeight edge case (confirmed correctly muted on a fresh, untouched load), and a float value with custom formatting -- plus three callback-dependent sliders specifically (tooltip preview sync, chars-per-line's "Off" label, speech rate's distinct apply path), all confirmed working correctly end to end, not just assumed from the shared mechanism. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1814 - NEW: default-value indicator and reset button for variable-font axis sliders, per Lynn's request -- the first landing spot for a general mechanism she wants extended to other sliders next, starting here specifically because she flagged axes as what she's most anxious about not knowing a default for. Deliberately did NOT invent a new UI pattern -- checked the existing letter/word-spacing rows first (which Lynn already said she likes) and extended that exact same established design instead: an always-present small pill button reading "Default", muted gray when the axis is already at its default, accent-colored the moment it's changed, tap to reset. CAUGHT AND FIXED a real regression before shipping: the settings search specifically queries for the .settings-row-label class on each row, which the first draft of this restructuring omitted entirely (would have made every axis row permanently unfindable by search) -- fixed by placing the class on the correct element, matching exactly where the letter/word-spacing precedent puts it. Verified directly: moving a slider correctly turns both the value and the Default button accent-colored; clicking Default correctly resets the slider position, the displayed value, and the underlying setting (confirmed the stored override is genuinely deleted, not just set to the default number, matching the null-based precedent); searching an axis name by itself (e.g. "bounce") still correctly finds and shows only that row. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1813 - TWO fixes, per Lynn's report. (1) FIX: font dropdown menus weren't honoring UI Font Size at all -- looked tiny even with a large size saved. Root cause: the closed select box itself was already correctly sized, but individual option/optgroup elements had no font-size rule of their own, so the open dropdown popup fell back to the browser's native default -- a well-known cross-browser quirk where that popup is partially rendered by an OS-level layer that doesn't always inherit page CSS the way regular content does. Added explicit font-size/font-family to both, the standard remedy. Verified directly: an option element's own computed font-size now correctly matches a large saved UI Font Size (confirmed 32px on both), though the actual native popup's on-device rendering is something only real-device testing can fully confirm, given its partially OS-native nature. (2) NEW: every font-CHOICE label now consistently says "Font Face", per Lynn's request for searchability -- "Note & Folder Name Font" and "Note Preview Font" renamed to end in "Font Face" (the other three -- UI Font Face, Note Font Face, and the home-item-meta Font Face row -- already said it correctly). Confirmed the settings search reads labels live from the DOM rather than a separate maintained index, so no other change was needed for the renamed rows to become findable. Verified directly: searching "font face" now surfaces all four font-choice rows correctly. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1812 - Full audit of every variable font already in the app, per Lynn's request, to check for unhandled axes beyond weight. Checked all 23 fonts registered as genuinely variable (confirmed each individually rather than assumed, mostly via Fontsource's per-font axis listings, cross-checked against official sources for the more consequential ones). FOUND AND FIXED TWO REAL GAPS: IBM Plex Sans has a Width axis (75-100) that was never requested in the import or exposed as a control -- added to both. Literata has an Optical Size axis (7-72) that was actually already being requested in the app's own import string from earlier work, but had never been turned into an actual slider -- added the missing registry entry to finally expose it. CONFIRMED WEIGHT-ONLY, no gaps found: Atkinson Hyperlegible Next (confirmed directly, "1 axis"), Atkinson Hyperlegible Mono, Lexend (confirmed directly from its own official GitHub repo history), Mulish, Roboto Mono (confirmed -- Fontsource notes its no-extra-axes "full" variant doesn't even exist), Playwrite Ireland (consistent with the same single-axis pattern independently confirmed for every other individual Playwrite country variant checked this session), plus Nunito, Rubik, Noto Sans, Afacad, Lora, Playpen Sans, and Source Sans 3 via the same established pattern. Fonts already correctly handled from earlier sessions, reconfirmed rather than re-researched from scratch: Shantell Sans, Inter, Open Sans, Google Sans Flex, Roboto Flex, Merriweather, Inconsolata, Noto Sans Mono. Verified directly: selecting IBM Plex Sans now generates exactly one correctly-labeled Width slider; selecting Literata now generates exactly one correctly-labeled Optical Size slider with the exact 7-72 range already present in the import. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1811 - NEW: Open Sans's width axis added, per Lynn's request (75-100, confirmed as its only axis beyond weight). DELETED: Comic Neue, per Lynn's decision it wasn't worth keeping -- while removing it, found and cleaned up a genuine duplicate entry in the weight registry left over from an earlier edit (both a sans-serif and a stray cursive key existed for it simultaneously), now moot since the font is gone entirely. Verified directly: Open Sans generates exactly one correctly-labeled Width slider; Comic Neue confirmed completely absent from the dropdown's actual rendered options. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1810 - Smooth-weight audit, per Lynn's request. Researched every font's actual variable status directly rather than assume, since the app had been registering several genuinely variable fonts with only a handful of discrete weight stops instead of their real, full continuous range -- confirmed each one specifically (mostly via Fontsource, whose page titles reliably say "variable" only when a font genuinely has a continuous-range release, cross-checked against official sources for the more consequential ones). WIDENED to true continuous ranges, confirmed variable: Lexend (100-900), Roboto Mono (100-700, both specifically requested), Nunito (200-1000), Rubik (300-900), Open Sans (300-800), Noto Sans (100-900), IBM Plex Sans (100-700), Source Sans 3 (200-900, confirmed directly from a live CDN CSS file), Lora (400-700), Afacad (400-700), Literata (widened from just 400-600 to its full 200-900). CONFIRMED GENUINELY STATIC (Lynn's requested list, verified rather than guessed): Atkinson Hyperlegible (original), Andika, Alegreya Sans (notably NOT its serif sibling Alegreya, which is variable), Comic Relief, Spectral (7 fixed weights, no variable release exists), Georgia (system font). Also fixed a small pre-existing bug found while touching this same code: Comic Relief's weight-registry key said cursive while every actual dropdown/pill entry for it said sans-serif, meaning the two never matched and its weight registry silently never applied at all -- corrected to match. DELETED: Edu SA Beginner, per Lynn's decision that more weights didn't change her mind about the font itself. ADDED: Comic Neue (300/400/700, genuinely static, added purely to see it against real notes per Lynn's own explicit request), placed alongside Comic Relief in the Sans-serif group across all three dropdowns and the pill row, matching how visually similar the two fonts are. Verified directly: every widened font's registry entry confirmed holding its real min/max range, not just the old discrete stops; Edu SA Beginner confirmed absent from the actual dropdown; Comic Neue confirmed present and correctly registered. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1809 - FIX: Playwrite Ireland's stylistic-set registry corrected against the font's own real, confirmed data, per Lynn's request after investigating why the sets weren't working. Downloaded Playwrite Ireland's actual variable font file directly from TypeTogether's own GitHub (bypassing Google Fonts entirely, since GitHub is reachable from this sandbox when Google's font CDN currently isn't) and inspected its embedded GSUB feature table directly -- found the general nine-set Playwrite documentation (which the original registry was built from) does not match this specific country variant's file past ss07. Confirmed: ss08 is not "Cursive Uppercase" as documented, it's a set called simply "Print" with no further embedded description; ss09 does not exist in this file at all; "Cursive Uppercase" and "Decorative Uppercase" are both real, but live at ss19 and ss20, not ss08/ss09. Also definitively confirmed, by actually rendering all ten real sets from the original font file in a real browser (not just inspecting metadata), that every single one produces a genuinely different, visible letterform change -- ss20 in particular gives capital letters a real decorative flourish. This settles the open question from the last several versions: the feature itself is completely real and working in the font; it simply does not survive Google Fonts' specific packaging of this file, confirmed by the exact same CSS producing zero effect through Google's CDN and a clear, visible effect from the original source. Registry now reflects Playwrite Ireland's own confirmed mapping specifically, with a note that any future Playwrite country variant needs its own mapping confirmed the same way rather than assumed to match. Verified directly: selecting Playwrite Ireland now generates exactly 10 checkboxes (up from 9) with the corrected labels, confirmed exactly matching the font's own embedded names. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1808 - MAJOR font library update, per Lynn's request. DELETED (removed from every dropdown, pill row, and the weight registry, confirmed zero remaining references anywhere in the file): Caveat, Playwrite NZ Basic, Patrick Hand SC. ADDED: Playpen Sans (wght 100-800, same TypeTogether family as Playwrite, Handwriting group), Roboto Flex (wght 100-1000 plus 12 additional axes -- confirms Lynn's own memory of "13 variables" for this font exactly), Inconsolata (wdth 50-200 + wght 200-900, confirmed directly from the font's own official dev repo), Noto Sans Mono (wdth 62.5-100 + wght 100-900, confirmed via three independent sources including Google's own METADATA.pb directly), Atkinson Hyperlegible Mono (wght 200-800). EXTENDED with axis support: Google Sans Flex gained its other 5 axes (Optical Size, Slant, Width, Grade, Roundness) on top of its existing weight support; Merriweather gained Width support, confirmed as a genuinely new axis this font only gained in a 2024-2025 update (and its weight upgraded from disconnected stops to the continuous range the font now fully supports, since leaving it awkward while adding continuous width would have been inconsistent). FIXED, per Lynn's own correct diagnosis: Edu SA Beginner was registered as just two disconnected weight stops (400 and 700) despite the font's real, confirmed range being the full continuous 400-700 band -- exactly the missing-intermediate-weights problem Lynn identified by eye. ALSO CAUGHT while auditing: Google Sans Flex's weight registry entry was simply wrong (said 300-700, confirmed actual range is 1-1000) -- fixed alongside everything else. ARCHITECTURAL FIX applied proactively rather than waiting to be rediscovered: width (wdth) is, like slant, a "standard" OpenType axis with its own dedicated CSS property (font-stretch) that browsers can favor over the generic mechanism -- applied the exact same belt-and-suspenders treatment learned from the slant investigation before Lynn could hit the same silent-no-op bug a second time, for all three newly width-enabled fonts. On the remembered "dense weights 300-700, sparse outside" pattern: searched thoroughly and found no trace of it anywhere in the file or its import URLs -- every variable-weight font already uses a simple continuous range, which gives full smooth access to literally every integer weight already; continued that same approach for Roboto Flex and Google Sans Flex rather than inventing a discrete-stops scheme with no established precedent. Verified directly, not assumed: Roboto Flex generates exactly 12 correctly-labeled sliders; Google Sans Flex generates exactly 5; Merriweather's width slider produces both "95%" on the real font-stretch property and the belt-and-suspenders font-variation-settings value simultaneously; Noto Sans Mono's slider correctly starts at its true 62.5 minimum with a 0.5 step (its range isn't a whole number); every deleted font confirmed absent from the actual rendered dropdown's options, not just the source; the app's own settings-bucket validator confirms 191 settings, all classified, no drift. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1807 - NEW: Playwrite Ireland font added, per Lynn's request, plus OpenType stylistic-set toggles for it -- her earlier-researched, independently reconfirmed nine sets (Modern Cursive, Joined Modern Cursive, Full Cursive, Speed Loops, Curved Entry Stroke, Mirrored Loops, Micro Loops, Cursive Uppercase, Decorative Uppercase), confirmed again this session across TypeTogether's own official Playwrite article and Grokipedia, matching what Lynn found independently. Font added following the established recipe (import with a continuous 100-400 weight range rather than the existing Playwrite NZ Basic entry's two fixed stops, giving the weight slider full fine control; Handwriting-group dropdown option; quick-pick pill; weight registry entry). Stylistic sets are a DIFFERENT mechanism from the variable-font axes built two versions ago -- font-feature-settings, not font-variation-settings -- so this is new machinery: a FONT_STYLISTIC_SETS registry (keyed to work for any future Playwrite country variant, not just Ireland), a new per-context setting (noteFontStylisticSets, note-context only since handwriting fonts only live in that one dropdown), and a new dynamically-generated checkbox UI (not sliders, since these are independent on/off toggles) placed right after the axis sliders. Deliberately independent, freely-combinable toggles rather than a mutually-exclusive group -- OpenType stylistic sets can be combined by spec, and documentation alone doesn't make clear which of these 9 are meant as alternative base styles versus layerable refinements, so this lets Lynn discover working combinations herself rather than have that guessed at and enforced. Applied at the same five locations already established for the axis work (both note-content textareas, the content-area container, and both preview boxes). UNVERIFIED, flagged clearly rather than assumed: found a real, repeatedly-reported community pattern (including an open issue on Google's own font repository) of font-feature-settings simply not working for some fonts served via the Google Fonts CDN specifically, even when the original font has the features -- this sandbox is currently blocked from reaching Google Fonts (confirmed via a direct 403 test) so this could not be verified end-to-end. Everything checkable without an actual font file downloading was verified directly: selecting Playwrite Ireland generates exactly 9 correctly-labeled checkboxes; toggling one produces the correct font-feature-settings value and persists to settings; switching to a font with no registered sets correctly clears them. The one open question -- whether these toggles produce any visible effect at all -- needs a real check on Lynn's own device before further investment here. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1806 - Attempted fix for Inter's Slant slider, per Lynn's iOS report that -1 and -10 looked visually identical despite v0.1804's fix already confirmed producing genuinely different CSS values for each position. Researched rather than guessed at cause: found real, documented evidence this is a genuinely unsettled area of browser support, not clearly "our bug" or "the font's bug" -- a WebKit bug report titled exactly "Variable fonts with 'slnt' or 'ital' axes are not rendered correctly with font-style:italic, etc." (WebKit being exactly what iOS uses), a separate Firefox bug about browsers historically getting the slant DIRECTION backwards when mapping between font-style and the raw slnt axis, and an open discussion on Inter's own GitHub about its slant sign convention being inconsistent with the OpenType spec. Given v0.1804's switch to font-style specifically addressed a CONFIRMED issue (raw font-variation-settings being silently ignored in favor of font-style) but WebKit may have its own separate, incomplete handling of the font-style side of that exact interaction -- now sets slnt through BOTH mechanisms simultaneously rather than font-style alone, so whichever one a given engine actually implements correctly for this specific, historically inconsistent combination gets a chance to take effect. Verified directly: Inter at slnt=-8 now correctly shows both "oblique -8deg" AND "slnt -8" simultaneously in its computed style (previously only the former); Shantell Sans confirmed completely unaffected, still correctly using only font-variation-settings with font-style staying neutral, since it has no slnt axis at all. Flagged honestly: this is a genuine attempt at a real, researched hypothesis, not a confirmed fix -- Safari/WebKit's exact behavior can't be reproduced from this sandbox (Chromium only), so whether this actually resolves what Lynn is seeing on iOS needs her own next test to confirm either way. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1805 - FIX: a third preview box -- the larger, editable one sitting directly above Note Text Size -- never reflected any variable-font axis change either, per Lynn's report. Same root cause as the textareas and the smaller font-face preview fixed the last two versions: this element sets font-family/size/weight explicitly (via an inline style attribute this time, not even a CSS class rule) and was simply never given the two new properties. Checked directly whether any OTHER similar element had been missed rather than assume this was the last one -- confirmed by searching the whole file that this is the only contenteditable preview box that exists, so no further instances of this same gap remain. Verified directly: this box now correctly shows the exact live Bounce value for Shantell Sans, and correctly shows "oblique -6deg" (not a raw font-variation-settings string) for Inter's Slant, confirming both the custom-axis and standard-axis (font-style) code paths both correctly reach this element now. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1804 - TWO fixes to the variable-font axis prototype, per Lynn's real-device testing. (1) FIX: Inter's Slant slider had no visible effect at all, even with italic toggled on. Root cause: slnt is a REGISTERED (standard) OpenType axis with its own dedicated CSS property (font-style: oblique Xdeg) -- confirmed directly against this same investigation's own earlier research that browsers can and do silently ignore a raw font-variation-settings "slnt" value in favor of whatever font-style already resolves to, which is exactly why toggling italic didn't help either -- that sets font-style: italic, a completely different value than the oblique-angle form slnt actually needs. _buildFontVariationCSS now returns the variation and style pieces separately: genuine custom axes with no dedicated property of their own (Shantell Sans's Bounce/Informality/Spacing) still go through font-variation-settings exactly as before; slnt specifically now goes through font-style instead, applied via a new pair of CSS custom properties (--font-style-ui/-editor) alongside the existing variation ones. Checked before applying broadly that this couldn't conflict with the app's own existing italic text-formatting feature -- confirmed every existing font-style: italic rule in this file is scoped to a specific, more specific selector (e.g. .note-viewer em), which correctly wins over this new, broader base-level rule via normal CSS specificity, so real italic formatting inside notes is unaffected. (2) FIX: the font preview boxes in Settings never reflected any axis change at all, per Lynn's report. These preview elements sit inside the Settings dialog, entirely outside the note-content-area/body-scoped inheritance chain the sliders' live-update already targeted -- same root cause as the earlier textarea fix, same resolution: added both new properties explicitly to both preview elements, matching the exact pattern already established there for font-weight. Verified directly: Inter's editor and CSS variable both now correctly show "oblique -8deg" (not a font-variation-settings string) for a -8 slant value; Shantell Sans's preview box now correctly reflects a live Bounce change (previously confirmed NOT updating); Shantell Sans itself confirmed unaffected by this change, still correctly routing entirely through font-variation-settings with font-style staying neutral, since it has no slnt axis. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1803 - NEW (prototype): variable-font axis sliders, per Lynn's request, scoped to the two fonts discussed -- Shantell Sans (Bounce/Informality/Spacing) and Inter (Slant).
