Inline placement, as chosen: sliders appear directly under each context's existing weight slider (UI Font Weight / Note Font Weight), only for a font that actually has registered extra axes, invisible for every other font. Manual per-font registry (FONT_EXTRA_AXES), matching the exact shape of the existing FONT_WEIGHT_SUPPORT registry -- ranges confirmed directly rather than assumed: Shantell Sans's Bounce/Informality/Spacing from the font's own documentation, Inter's Slant range (-10 to 0) confirmed against a real, working Google Fonts API URL rather than a secondary source. Storage is per-context (fontExtraAxes for the UI font, noteFontExtraAxes for the note font), mirroring the existing uiFontWeight/fontWeight split rather than one global value per font family, so the same font can be tuned differently in each role. Applied via font-variation-settings, inherited from a single body-level (UI) and content-area-level (note) declaration rather than touching the many individual selectors that already set font-weight explicitly -- font-variation-settings is a naturally inherited CSS property, so one declaration point correctly cascades everywhere. CAUGHT AND FIXED two real bugs before shipping, both found through direct verification rather than assumed correct: (1) the note editor and edit-block textareas don't inherit ambient font properties from ancestors the way normal elements do -- confirmed directly (computed style showed the CSS variable correctly cascading all the way to the content-area container, but not reaching the textarea itself) and fixed by adding the same property explicitly to both textareas, exactly matching how this codebase already handles font-family/size/weight there for the identical reason; (2) the app's own SETTING_BUCKETS validator (built specifically to catch exactly this) correctly flagged both new settings as unclassified -- fixed by placing fontExtraAxes in GENERAL (matching its counterpart 'font') and noteFontExtraAxes in NOTE_APPEARANCE (matching noteFont/fontWeight). Verified directly: selecting Shantell Sans generates exactly 3 correctly-labeled sliders ("Bounce"/"Informality"/"Spacing"), Inter generates 1 ("Slant"); moving a slider updates the live CSS variable, persists into settings, and correctly disappears when switching to a font with no registered axes; the fixed textarea now correctly shows the exact axis values in its own computed style, confirmed directly (previously confirmed reaching the container but not the textarea, now confirmed reaching the textarea itself); the SETTING_BUCKETS validator confirms 190 settings, all classified, no drift. ONE CAVEAT, disclosed rather than hidden: could not get a final visual (screenshot) confirmation that the axis values visibly change the rendered glyphs, since a direct test confirmed Google Fonts is currently returning HTTP 403 to this sandbox (almost certainly from the sheer volume of repeated automated requests this session has made, not a code issue -- earlier tests this same session did show correct, distinctive font rendering before this). Every other layer has been verified structurally correct; the final visual step needs confirming on a real device with normal network access, which Lynn's own testing will settle directly. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1802 - CRITICAL HOTFIX: the real cause of Lynn's PWA crash, traced directly from her own debug log rather than inferred -- a genuine, self-sustaining cross-tab infinite loop, confirmed by thousands of identical log entries firing 5-10 milliseconds apart for about 7 straight seconds. Root cause: renderNotesList()'s own internal diagnostic log call runs on every single invocation and previously always mirrored into the persisted debug-log note -- which means it silently triggered its own saveNotes() call every time. That save bumps the shared generation counter unconditionally (confirmed: v0.1797's earlier throttle only ever guarded the expensive MERGE comparison, never the underlying write+bump), which the real-time-sync listener in any OTHER open tab reacts to by calling renderNotesList() there too -- which logs, which saves, which bumps the counter again, forever, with zero user involvement, entirely independent of anything Lynn was actually doing. Fixed at the root (that specific log call no longer mirrors into the note, only the ephemeral syslog ring buffer, fully sufficient for diagnostics and where it always belonged for a trace line firing on every single render) plus a defensive rate-limit on the real-time-sync listener's own re-render call, as depth against the same class of loop recurring via any other not-yet-audited path. Verified directly with two genuinely separate tabs: a single real save in one tab now produces exactly one reaction in the other (confirmed before the fix, this same single-save test already showed the other tab both rendering AND saving in response -- the exact mechanism, caught red-handed); ten realistic saves spread over three seconds produced only three throttled renders and, critically, zero cascading saves in the other tab, down from what was an unbounded, self-sustaining loop. (2) NEW: Shantell Sans added, per Lynn's request, to the Handwriting group specifically (confirmed via research it's a genuine 5-axis variable font, 300-800 weight range on the standard axis, marker-style and built for creative/handwriting-style expression) -- added to the ONE dropdown (Note Content Text) that actually has a Handwriting optgroup, deliberately not the other four (three of which don't offer handwriting fonts at all, being UI-chrome-adjacent contexts; the fourth is a curated abbreviated list that already only includes one of the four existing handwriting fonts, so a new fifth wasn't added there either, matching that same established curation). Verified directly: both weight extremes (300 and 800) confirmed actually downloaded via the browser's own font-loading API, and a live screenshot confirms its distinctive marker-style letterforms rendering correctly. (3) CONFIRMED, no change needed: Atkinson Hyperlegible Next is already the default font for both UI and note content on a fresh install, verified directly against a genuinely clean browser context with zero prior storage -- this was already correctly done at v0.1768, Lynn's request is already fully satisfied. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1801 - NEW: Andika font added, per Lynn's request (400 and 700 weights) -- a real Google Font designed by SIL for literacy, beginning readers, dyslexia support, and broad international-language coverage. Followed the exact existing recipe for adding a font (established at v0.1656 for Atkinson Hyperlegible Next/Mulish) rather than improvising: added to the single shared Google Fonts @import every other font in this app already loads from; a quick-pick pill placed alongside the other literacy/accessibility-focused fonts (Atkinson family); an <option> in all five font dropdowns (confirmed the exact count directly by search, not assumed -- one more than the four the v0.1656 changelog mentioned, since a fifth was added since then); and a weight-support registry entry using the plain [400, 700] fixed-array form already used by other exactly-two-weight fonts (matching plain Atkinson Hyperlegible itself), not the {min,max} variable-range form used for genuinely variable fonts like Inter or Atkinson Hyperlegible Next. Verified directly: selecting Andika correctly updates the live font CSS variable; both weights are confirmed actually downloaded and available via the browser's own font-loading API (document.fonts.check), not just referenced; a live screenshot confirms Andika's distinctive open, rounded letterforms rendering correctly in a real note. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1800 - FIX: the active window's claim never expired, per Lynn's report that the PWA (opened first) kept blocking the Chrome tab indefinitely, even after she'd switched entirely to using only the tab. Also directly addresses her follow-up questions: whether both windows freezing until manual closing would be safer (concluded no -- risks a worse failure mode, being unable to find/close a forgotten window and getting fully locked out), and whether an inactivity timeout should apply (yes). Heartbeat renewal is now gated on actual visibility, not just "the tab is open": a window only keeps renewing its claim while genuinely visible, so a window that's been backgrounded and not returned to naturally lets its claim expire. Staleness threshold increased from 8 seconds to 60 (matching Lynn's own suggested timeout) since visibility is now the real gate -- an actively-used window renews every 3 seconds regardless and can never go stale while someone's actually looking at it; the longer window only governs how long a genuinely abandoned window's claim lingers before another window may take over, generous enough to avoid disruptive flapping from brief window-switching. Also added an immediate re-check the moment a blocked window becomes visible again, rather than waiting out whatever's left of the next scheduled check, so switching TO the other window feels responsive rather than laggy. This design guarantees whichever window is actually in front of the person eventually wins automatically, with no manual closing ever required -- directly addressing the "could get stuck" risk Lynn flagged with the alternative both-freeze approach. Verified directly: a window whose heartbeat has gone stale (simulating an abandoned, backgrounded PWA) correctly and automatically hands off to the previously-blocked window the moment it rechecks; heartbeat renewal is confirmed to be genuinely skipped while a window is hidden, using real visibility-state manipulation, not just inference; the full original two-window detection and every UI-level block (opening notes, creating notes, creating folders) are all reconfirmed working correctly on top of these changes. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1799 - FIX: concurrent-instance detection only blocked the eventual SAVE, per Lynn's direct report that both windows still let her open and edit notes while the banner was showing. Real gap: someone could type into a note believing it was being saved while saveNotes() silently discarded every one of those edits underneath them -- recreating the exact silent-data-loss problem this whole feature exists to prevent, just wearing a different disguise. Blocked at the interaction level now, not just the save: openNote(), createNewNote(), and createFolder() all refuse outright while blocked (with a toast explaining why), and a backstop makes the note editor read-only immediately if someone is already mid-edit at the exact moment a conflict is detected (deliberately not navigating them away, so whatever they'd already typed stays visible to copy out by hand if they want to) -- restored automatically the instant the block clears. Also found and fixed a completely unguarded function while auditing this: saveFolders() had no protection at all, notes/settings/backups aside -- this is the exact function whose gap explained Lynn's very first symptom in this whole investigation (a new folder appeared correctly while the note created inside it did not, since folders and notes save independently). Verified directly: opening a note, creating a note, and creating a folder are all confirmed refused while blocked (with folders/notes counts unchanged and openNote() confirmed to never navigate); a direct test of the mid-edit backstop confirms the editor goes read-only the instant a block is triggered and correctly restores when cleared; solo, single-instance use (opening, creating notes, creating folders) is confirmed completely unaffected and works exactly as before. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1798 - MAJOR: concurrent-instance detection, plus a storage-exhaustion hotfix, per Lynn's decision after an extensive debugging session (a toast flood, real crashes, and a save-quota failure, each traced and fixed in turn) that trying to fully support two genuinely concurrent, actively-used instances sharing one storage was the wrong target -- the more robust fix is detecting the dangerous situation directly and refusing to proceed. (1) HOTFIX: found and fixed the exact cause of Lynn's "storage may be full" report -- the per-conflict backup mechanism wrote a NEW, uniquely-timestamped key on every conflict with nothing ever cleaning old ones up, each a full serialization of the entire notes collection (including the ever-growing debug log note); over an extended session this can exhaust localStorage's quota entirely, which then makes EVERY subsequent save fail, and since a failed save showed its own untouched, unthrottled toast, directly explained the reported flood past #600. Now deletes existing backup keys before writing a new one (bounded to exactly one at any time), PLUS a startup cleanup pass that sweeps up any already-accumulated backup keys from before this fix existed -- fixing future growth alone wouldn't have helped Lynn's already-full storage. Also added a matching throttle to the previously-untouched SAVE FAILED toast itself, as defensive depth against any future cause of repeated failures. (2) NEW: concurrent-instance detection. A lightweight heartbeat -- a single, fixed key overwritten in place every ~3 seconds, deliberately never a growing set of keys, and deliberately bypassing saveNotes()/saveSettings() entirely so it can never itself contribute to a save cascade -- lets each instance detect whether another is already active. A newly-opening instance that finds an existing, recently-active heartbeat immediately pauses itself: a prominent, persistent top banner (deliberately a dedicated element, not a toast, since toasts proved losable/overwritable during this exact investigation) explains that another window is open and must be closed, and — as a hard guarantee independent of the banner or any other UI state — saveNotes() and saveSettings() both refuse to write anything at all while blocked, checked as the very first thing either function does. The earlier merge-on-save protection (v0.1794/1795) stays in place as defense-in-depth for the brief window before detection completes, not removed. Recovery is automatic: a blocked instance rechecks periodically, and the active instance releases its claim on page close (pagehide) for a fast handoff rather than making the waiting instance sit out the full staleness timeout. A brief claim-then-verify pattern (tentative write, short randomized delay, re-check) handles the rare case of two instances loading at nearly the exact same moment, resolving deterministically to exactly one active instance with no flapping between them. Verified directly and extensively with genuinely separate browser tabs (not simulated): a second tab opened while a first is active correctly detects and blocks itself while the first remains completely unaffected; a forced save attempt from the blocked tab is confirmed to never actually reach storage even though it succeeds in memory; closing the active tab correctly and automatically restores full, genuine save capability in the previously-blocked one within a few seconds; and a near-simultaneous open of two tabs resolves cleanly to exactly one active instance. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1797 - CRITICAL HOTFIX: the actual root cause of the crashes, per Lynn's precise report that having both the PWA and a Chrome tab genuinely active together made the app unusable, crashing before she could even open a note. v0.1796's toast throttle helped but didn't address the real mechanism, confirmed directly this time via real call-stack capture rather than inference: saveNotes() <- logDebugAction() <- logSys(). The debug log is itself an ordinary note, and saveNotes() serializes the WHOLE notes object every time it's called -- meaning every single routine logSys() call anywhere in this file (there are dozens during perfectly normal use, almost none of which pass skipMirror) triggers its own saveNotes() call, which as of v0.1795 always ran the full conflict-check-and-merge machinery regardless of how trivial or automatic the underlying save was. With two tabs both actively logging routine activity (confirmed directly: opening a single note triggered 12 separate saveNotes() calls, not the 1 a person would expect), each tab's own incidental logging-triggered save looked like a genuine conflict to the other -- a self-sustaining storm with nothing to do with any actual user action, exactly matching Lynn's report of being unable to even open a note. Fixed by throttling the conflict CHECK itself (not just its notification, which v0.1796 already throttled): a real, meaningful conflict -- actual content genuinely changed in another tab -- is naturally spaced far more than a couple of seconds apart from routine internal logging, so skipping the check when one just ran within the last 1.5 seconds costs essentially nothing in real protection (the underlying write and generation-counter bump still happen every time regardless, so other tabs still learn about real changes promptly) while completely breaking this specific cascade. Applied identically to both the notes and settings conflict paths. Verified directly: the exact reproduction that previously showed 12 saveNotes() calls still shows 12 (the routine logging itself is unchanged, as expected), but the actual expensive merge operations and toasts both dropped to zero in that same scenario -- confirmed by instrumenting the merge function and toast calls directly, not just inferring from log counts. Separately re-confirmed a genuine cross-tab conflict (real, different notes created in each of two instances) still merges correctly and loses nothing once the brief throttle window has passed, so the actual data-safety protection Lynn's original report needed remains fully intact. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1796 - URGENT HOTFIX: undismissable repeating toast and real tab/PWA crashes, per Lynn's report right after updating multiple tabs to v0.1795 at once. Investigated with direct measurement rather than guessing: reproduced with 4 tabs opened together and found 63 genuine conflict-merge events firing within about one second -- confirmed bounded (stabilizes, does not keep growing indefinitely -- ruled out a true infinite loop) but still a large burst of synchronous work (JSON parse/stringify, localStorage reads/writes, DOM manipulation) crammed into a very short window, on top of showToast()'s single, un-queued toast element: each of those 63 calls immediately overwrote whatever the previous one was still showing, so dismissing one just cleared the way for the next to appear a moment later -- exactly matching Lynn's precise description ("I was clicking to dismiss them but another one kept coming up") and her separate observation that crashes correlated with backgrounding/foregrounding (each tab switch, with several tabs open, triggers its own flush-on-hide save, capable of triggering its own fresh burst). The underlying merge protection was never the problem -- it was resolving every one of those 63 events correctly, confirmed by the syslog's own full count staying at 63 throughout. Fixed by rate-limiting the NOTIFICATION only, never the underlying safety merge: a 6-second grace period after the page's own load (exactly when a multi-tab startup race is expected and low-stakes, since nothing has actually been edited by a person yet) plus an 8-second cooldown between notifications afterward -- the merge and its full diagnostic log entry still happen every single time, unconditionally; only the user-facing toast is throttled. Verified directly: the exact 4-tab reproduction that produced 63 toast calls before now produces at most 1, while the underlying merge count stays exactly the same (53 in this specific measured run), confirming data safety is completely unaffected and only the notification flood was fixed. Also fixed a related, lower-severity false-positive found during this same investigation: the settings conflict check compared a defaults-merged baseline against raw, un-merged live storage, meaning any setting that only existed in DEFAULT_SETTINGS (never explicitly saved before) always looked like "the other instance changed this," triggering an unnecessary (if silent, non-toast) merge pass on literally every single settings save, even with zero other tabs involved -- now compares apples to apples. Also added, per Lynn's own request: a running message number prefixed to every toast ("#1 ...", "#2 ..."), so it's possible to tell directly whether toasts are genuinely new events firing versus a display glitch reshowing the same one, for this investigation and any future one. NOT YET CONFIRMED: whether this fully explains the crashes themselves (a large, bursty pile of synchronous work is a very plausible contributor, especially on real Chromebook hardware, but was not directly reproduced as an actual browser crash in this sandbox) -- Lynn's next real-device test will be the actual confirmation. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1795 - TWO more data-safety fixes, per Lynn's continued Chromebook investigation. Confirmed via chrome://version (Profile Path byte-for-byte identical on both her out-of-sync tabs) that this was never a separate-profile or separate-storage issue -- two tabs genuinely sharing one storage, one of them just sitting on a stale in-memory snapshot until it saved and silently overwrote the other, exactly the failure mode v0.1794 started addressing for notes. (1) FIX: settings changes (specifically Quick Action Bar, confirmed by Lynn) reverting the same way notes did. saveSettings() had the identical unprotected unconditional-overwrite vulnerability v0.1794 already fixed for saveNotes() -- confirmed by reading it directly, still completely unpatched. Settings has no per-field modification timestamp the way notes have editedAt, so "newer wins" doesn't apply here; used a 3-way baseline-diff merge instead (the same general strategy version control systems use): a snapshot of settings as of this instance's last load/save (the baseline) is compared against this instance's current in-memory value AND the live stored value, per key -- a key unchanged since baseline on this side defers entirely to storage (adopts the other instance's change); a key changed only here keeps this instance's value; a key changed identically on both sides is a non-issue; a key changed to genuinely different values on both sides is a real per-key conflict, resolved in favor of this instance (the one actively saving right now) and logged loudly, never silently. Verified directly: the exact scenario Lynn described (Quick Action Bar enabled here, an unrelated theme change saved elsewhere) merges correctly with both changes intact and no unnecessary toast, since there's no genuine per-key conflict; a real same-key conflict (uiSize set differently on both sides) correctly resolves to this instance's value with a toast and a syslog entry naming the specific conflicting key. (2) NEW: real-time cross-tab sync, using the browser's native storage event (fires in every OTHER same-origin tab the instant one of them writes to localStorage, never in the tab that made the write). The merge-on-save protection above only ever runs at save time, so a tab could still sit arbitrarily stale for as long as it happened to go without saving anything -- this closes that gap by keeping notes continuously current in the background: on the home screen, a live save elsewhere is adopted and the list re-renders immediately, with nothing to disrupt; with a note open, the SAME live refresh only happens if this tab has no unsaved edit of its own in progress (checked by comparing the editor's live DOM value against what this tab itself last knew as saved) -- if the user has typed something not yet saved, the editor is left completely untouched, and the merge-on-save protection remains the backstop for that case instead of risking a live overwrite mid-edit. Settings deliberately gets no equivalent live-sync handling: saveSettings() already does its own fresh, live read of storage at actual save time, so proactively touching the baseline here would only risk breaking the merge's correctness for keys the user hasn't touched, for no real benefit. Verified with two genuinely separate browser tabs sharing one context (required for real storage events, which never fire in the same tab that made the change): a new note created in tab A appears in tab B automatically with no reload; a tab B mid-typing an unsaved edit is completely undisturbed when tab A saves something unrelated, while that unrelated note is still correctly adopted into tab B's memory in the background. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1794 - Two DATA-SAFETY fixes (the priority of this build) plus a Move Lines improvement, per Lynn. (1) FIX: a note completely disappearing on a Chromebook after switching between a Chrome tab and the installed PWA. Investigated thoroughly before writing any code -- ruled out Chrome tab groups directly touching storage (confirmed: purely a UI/organizational feature), ruled out the three-deployment storage-prefix architecture (Lynn confirmed she'd only ever used the main remember/ path on this device), then found and closed two real, independent gaps. First: saveCurrentNote() runs on a 600ms debounce with no flush on backgrounding, so a tab discarded by Chrome's Memory Saver (a well-documented, RAM-sensitive behavior particularly aggressive on Chromebooks) between a keystroke and that 600ms window could lose the most recent edit -- added an immediate flush on visibilitychange-to-hidden (fires reliably before any possible discard, since Chrome only discards hidden tabs) and on pagehide as defense-in-depth, reusing the exact flush pattern already proven safe elsewhere in this file (setViewMode()). Second, and the more likely explanation for a note being COMPLETELY gone rather than just missing recent edits (confirmed createNewNote() itself saves synchronously and immediately, ruling out "never saved in the first place"): saveNotes() always unconditionally overwrote remember_notes with whatever was in THIS instance's memory, with no way to know if another tab/window/PWA instance -- holding its own, potentially staler, in-memory copy -- had saved more recently. Classic multi-instance "last write wins" data loss, and confirmed structurally invisible to the existing after-the-fact loss detector (remember_note_census): an overwriting save immediately updates its own census to match the new, already-corrupted state, leaving nothing for the next load to compare against. Fixed with a generation counter checked at the moment of writing (not after the fact) plus a conservative, per-note automatic merge -- never resolved by picking one side's entire note set wholesale, since this data model has no deletion tombstones and treating absence as intentional deletion would risk silently resurrecting exactly the class of permanent loss this mechanism exists to prevent: a note present on either side is always kept, and a note edited on both sides keeps whichever copy has the more recent editedAt/createdAt. A full pre-merge backup of this instance's own view is written regardless, as a last-resort safety net in case the merge logic itself has an edge case not yet discovered. Verified extensively and directly: a normal single-instance save still increments the generation counter and stays in sync with no false conflicts; a simulated genuine conflict (a note created in "this" instance, a different note saved by a simulated "other" instance) correctly merges and preserves BOTH notes with full content intact -- precisely the scenario Lynn described; the same-note-edited-on-both-sides case correctly keeps the newer edit; the pre-merge backup key and an always-on syslog entry (deliberately not mirrored into the visible debug-log note from inside saveNotes() itself, to avoid recursion -- logDebugAction indirectly calls back into saveNotes()) are both confirmed written on conflict. (2) NEW: arrow-key stepping in Move Lines is now bubble-mode-aware, per Lynn's report that it took multiple presses to skip an invisible blank line. In bubble mode, one press now hops over the next VISIBLE bubble as a single step, per her explicit design: implemented as pure DOM node manipulation (moving the actual blank-line elements along with everything else), never raw-text splicing, deliberately consistent with this exact function's own established, hard-learned safety rule (v0.1516, real data loss) that content must only ever be reconstructed by reading DOM order after genuine DOM moves. The underlying swap-only-the-two-content-spans algorithm leaves every blank-line run exactly where it already sat relative to whichever element it was adjacent to, so no separator is ever gained or lost -- an unconditional guarantee, not a best-effort cleanup pass, exceeding Lynn's stated requirement (a boundary that started with >=1 blank line always keeps >=1) by preserving exact counts rather than just a minimum. Plain (non-bubble) mode's existing per-raw-line stepping is completely unchanged. Verified directly: single bubble hopping up and down across a blank-line gap, a multi-line group with an interior blank line hopping as one unit, and both document-boundary edge cases (nothing above/below to hop over) all produce exactly the hand-derived expected output. NOT YET RESOLVED: Lynn's separate report that the move-lines indicator is still not visible during an actual live drag (as opposed to the arrow-key buttons) -- the underlying class-application and CSS mechanism was verified correct via real HTML5 dragover events, real touch-drag events, single- and multi-bubble selections, and both drop positions, in every scenario constructible in this sandbox; suspected but unconfirmed cause is the browser's own native drag-preview image visually covering the target during a genuine live drag gesture, which cannot be reproduced through synthetic event dispatch -- flagged honestly as needing more specific information (mouse vs. touch, exact device) rather than a guessed fix. Suite: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1793 - FIX: move-lines drag indicator color, per Lynn's report that it was "almost invisible" in bubble mode, then her further pushback that plain (non-bubble) mode needed the same contrast-safety, then her correction that a rough luminance threshold isn't the same as genuinely picking the higher-contrast option. All three addressed. Root cause of the original report: the indicator reused var(--note-accent), the SAME color source the bubble's own border/fill is styled with -- no amount of thickness (already fixed in v0.1791) could fix a genuine color collision. First attempt computed contrast using a >0.45 luminance threshold (matching this file's existing solidText pattern) -- verified numerically, per Lynn's direct correction, that this is measurably wrong for a real range of backgrounds: at luminance 0.3 or 0.45 (plausible greys), a >0.45-style threshold picks white when black actually gives 2-3x better contrast (7:1 or 10:1 vs only 3:1 or 2.1:1) -- the true crossover point is luminance ~0.179, not ~0.45. Replaced with a new shared function (_pickHighContrastBW) that computes actual WCAG contrast ratios against both pure black and white and picks whichever is genuinely higher, for any background -- guarantees a worst-case contrast of ~4.58:1 against literally any possible color, well above WCAG's 3:1 minimum for graphical/UI elements. Applied in both modes, per Lynn's follow-up that plain mode needed the same treatment: computed against the note's own resolved background there (--bg, what an indicator on a plain .viewer-line -- confirmed via its CSS to have no background of its own -- actually sits on top of); against whichever bubble fill is actually active in bubble mode (Tinted/Solid's own real computed fill, Outline/Stripe's interior color). Also found and fixed a second, independent bug while verifying visually: Outline and Stripe bubbles draw their own border via the `border` shorthand at higher specificity (#note-viewer in the selector), silently overriding the indicator's border-top/border-bottom entirely -- the exact same specificity pattern already proven and fixed once before for .block-selected -- confirmed directly: computed borderTop read the bubble's own 2px accent border, not the indicator's intended 5px high-contrast one, until a matching higher-specificity override (scoped to bubbles-outline/bubbles-stripe specifically; Tinted/Solid only ever set background, never border, so don't conflict and need no override) was added. Verified extensively: the contrast function tested across a full grey range (luminance 0.01 to 0.91) achieves at least 5.3:1 in every case, comfortably clearing the proven worst-case guarantee; all four bubble variants confirmed showing a correct 5px high-contrast border (black on Tinted/Outline/Stripe, white on Solid's dark fill); plain mode confirmed showing a bold black bar against the light page background via direct screenshot; the specific scenario that started this (Outline bubble with interior and accent both set to the same blue) confirmed via screenshot showing an unmistakable black bar cutting across the bubble, where the old accent-matched indicator would have been invisible. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1792 - DIAGNOSTIC build, per Lynn's report: bubbles stopped rendering at all, with any style, on any note, despite settings.bubbleStyle correctly showing a non-default value ('outline') in her own "Show Non-Default Settings" export. Investigated two concrete leads before adding logging: (1) reproduced directly with Lynn's exact settings snapshot (bubbleStyle/matchNoteAccentForBubbles/bubbleAccentColor/bubbleInteriorColor all copied verbatim) in a clean load -- bubbles rendered correctly (confirmed via screenshot: outline style, correct accent border, correct custom interior fill color, matching her settings exactly) -- meaning applyBubbleAppearance() itself is not structurally broken, and whatever's happening is specific to her real, live session rather than something a fresh reload of this exact settings state reproduces. (2) considered whether a saved per-note or global Theme (which only overwrites whatever keys it explicitly captured, confirmed by reading applyThemeValues() directly) could be silently resetting bubbleStyle back to 'none' after her own change -- ruled out by Lynn directly: the problem affects every note, not one specific note, which a per-note theme override could explain but a systemic problem couldn't be dismissed as easily. Rather than keep guessing blind, added targeted debug logging directly to applyBubbleAppearance() itself: logs the raw settings.bubbleStyle value actually read (and its full call stack) the instant the function runs, an explicit line when it early-returns for style='none', and on completion, the resulting body classes plus the actual computed --bubble-accent/--bubble-fill-tinted/--bubble-interior CSS values -- covers both "is the JS logic even reading what I think it's reading" and "did the right CSS custom properties actually get set" in one pass, without needing a second round-trip if the first hypothesis is wrong. Verified the logging itself fires correctly and produces exactly the intended detail (confirmed via a live test: settings.bubbleStyle, resolved style, full call chain, final body classes, and final CSS values all appear correctly in the debug log). No behavior change to bubble rendering itself in this build -- purely additive logging, so this build's own bubble behavior should be identical to v0.1791's, which the next real debug log from Lynn's actual session will help confirm or contradict. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1791 - THREE Move Lines fixes, per Lynn, addressing a bug from the handoff notes (Bubble/Cloud Paragraphs' documented "known incompatibility with Move Lines"). (1) FIX: selecting two adjacent bubbles with a blank line between them made Move Lines completely unusable -- bubble mode intentionally never renders blank lines as selectable bubbles at all, so there was no way to satisfy the old strict adjacency check by manually including the blank line, unlike in plain (non-bubble) R&R mode. Fixed in the single shared function both Move Lines and Code Fence already use (_contiguousSelection()): a gap between two selected indices is now tolerated if every raw line inside it is blank/whitespace-only, checked directly against the note's own raw content rather than the DOM (works correctly whether bubble mode is on or not) -- those blank lines are automatically folded into the result, so the existing move logic (unchanged) carries them along verbatim, preserving them exactly as Lynn asked. Deliberately unconditional rather than bubble-mode-gated: a pure relaxation that can never change behavior for a selection that was already contiguous. Updated two now-stale toast messages (Move Lines and Code Fence) that used to instruct manually including blank lines, since that's automatic now. (2) FIX: no insertion-point indicator visible during a move while bubbles are active. Investigated directly rather than assumed a CSS-specificity conflict (the established root cause for two earlier, similar-sounding bubble-conflict bugs) -- confirmed instead that the border was already computing and applying correctly even with a bubble style on, just visually too subtle (2.5px) against a bubble's own background, shape, and shadow to actually notice. (3) TWEAK: the same indicator "has always been a bit thin," per Lynn, even before bubble mode existed -- turned out to be the SAME root cause as (2), not a second one: a single, uniformly bolder indicator (2.5px -> 5px, plus a matching colored glow) fixes both reports at once, verified directly with a real screenshot showing it now clearly visible sitting on top of an active bubble, with no bubble-specific CSS override needed. Verified extensively: selecting bubbles across a blank line now correctly returns the filled index list and successfully enters move mode; a genuine gap with real content in between still correctly blocks the move, unchanged; an already-contiguous selection is completely unaffected; a full move-and-commit end-to-end test confirmed the blank line survives byte-for-byte inside the moved group; a baseline comparison against the pre-existing, unmodified no-blank-lines case confirmed the boundary behavior (no line added where the moved group lands) is long-established, existing behavior, not a side effect of this fix. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1790 - NEW: Quick Action Bar's Message button now supports Google Messages for Web, per Lynn's Chromebook plan for her father. Researched first rather than assuming: confirmed Google documents no public URL scheme to pre-address a conversation or pre-fill its text box on messages.google.com/web -- unlike sms: or WhatsApp's wa.me, the only such mechanism Google documents (a body= parameter) is for verified RCS Business Messaging agents, not personal use. Best available alternative built instead: on a real desktop/Chromebook, the button now copies the note's text to the clipboard (reusing the existing writeClipboardSafe() helper Copy already uses) and opens Messages for Web in a new tab, so the only remaining step is picking the conversation and pasting. Device branching reuses (hover: hover) -- the exact signal this app already relies on elsewhere (_mouseZoomCompensationActive() etc) to mean "a real mouse/trackpad is attached" -- rather than inventing new detection: iOS is unaffected (still sms:, exactly as before); a genuine Android phone (non-iOS, no hover) also still gets its own native sms: intent, not redirected to the web version, since a phone that already has the Messages app installed has no reason to be routed to a page meant for a keyboard-and-mouse device; only a real desktop/Chromebook gets the new Messages-for-Web path. Also independently confirmed via research (not assumed) that an Android phone texting an iPhone lands directly in the recipient's normal Messages app with zero setup on their end -- SMS/MMS or RCS, not technically "iMessage" (a green bubble rather than blue, Apple's own protocol name specifically meaning Apple-to-Apple), but functionally identical to receive and reply to, which is what actually matters for Lynn's father's kids not needing to install anything. Verified all three device paths directly: desktop/Chromebook (hover present, non-iOS) correctly opens Messages for Web and copies the note text to the clipboard; a simulated Android phone (no hover, non-iOS) correctly falls through to its own native sms: path rather than being redirected to the web version; iOS is confirmed completely unaffected. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1789 - FIX: false-positive DEV WARNING, per Lynn's report (renaming a note triggered "Home Screen elements will NOT be positioned correctly"). Reproduced directly and traced exactly: applyRename() deliberately calls renderNotesList() while still inside the note editor (note-screen-active still true), specifically to keep the home screen's underlying list data fresh in the background for whenever the user navigates back -- a legitimate, intentional call, not the anomaly the v0.1749 warning system exists to catch. Root cause: that warning fires purely based on whether note-screen-active happens to be true when renderNotesList() reaches one of its own 3 internal endpoints, with no way to distinguish WHO called it or WHY -- its own comment already anticipated "other callers... can be completely legitimate" but never actually implemented a way to tell them apart. Fixed with a new optional suppressActiveScreenWarning parameter on renderNotesList(), defaulting to false so all ~44 other existing call sites are completely unaffected; applyRename()'s call now explicitly opts out. Found and fixed one more confirmed instance of the identical pattern while investigating: the "Remove" button next to each note in Settings' view-only-notes list, which -- like the rename dialog -- can be open while a note is active behind it. Did not attempt to blindly audit all ~44 other call sites without specific evidence each is actually reachable while a note screen is active; flagged honestly that other instances of this same shape may exist elsewhere and would need the same explicit, evidence-based fix if reported. Verified directly: the exact reported scenario (create a note, set its name) no longer shows any toast; a genuine anomaly (note-screen-active forced true, renderNotesList() called with no suppress flag) still correctly triggers the warning, confirming the guard itself wasn't weakened, only given two legitimate, confirmed exceptions. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1788 - THREE more Quick Action Bar pieces, per Lynn. (1) TWEAK: a few trailing blank lines added to the Mail body -- Gmail's on-screen keyboard leaves very little room to edit or add a signature otherwise; added before the blank-line-preservation pass so they get the same anti-stripping treatment as the note's own content. (2) REAL FIX: the Quick Action Bar was disappearing on Lynn's Chromebook (physical keyboard) the instant she clicked into a note, with no huge button left to get back out -- precisely the "can't see small toolbar buttons" problem this whole feature exists to solve, just relocated to the escape hatch instead of actually fixed. Root cause: the bar's hide-while-typing rule keyed off #note-screen.keyboard-open, which (confirmed by reading its own trigger code) fires on EVERY tap into the editor regardless of platform -- correct for a touch device (a real on-screen keyboard genuinely needs the space), completely wrong for a physical keyboard (nothing ever appears to make room for). Fixed with a new, separate, precise check (_isRealKeyboardOpen()) that measures actual visualViewport shrink -- the same technique _applyKeyboardScrollRoom() and pinToolbars() already trust elsewhere in this file -- rather than widening or removing the existing, much broader keyboard-open class, which stays completely untouched for everything else that correctly depends on it (note-fab, content padding, the main toolbar's own kb-hide/kb-only rules). Verified directly, both directions: a simulated 300px real keyboard shrink correctly hides the bar; no shrink at all (the physical-keyboard case) correctly leaves it visible. (3) NEW, general (not just for Lynn's father): a small "Done typing" escape button added to the note header -- the one bar that never collapses -- shown ONLY in the specific scenario that can genuinely trap someone (toolbar collapsed AND a real keyboard open), for anyone without the Quick Action Bar enabled at all. Root cause of THAT trap, found while investigating: the existing dismiss-keyboard-btn (and its More-menu equivalent) both live inside #note-bottom-bar, which the collapse rule hides with !important -- no CSS on a descendant can override a non-rendered ancestor, so neither was ever actually reachable once collapsed, regardless of R&R mode availability. The existing view-mode-btn only ever seemed to provide an escape route by accident, being structurally outside that container. Verified directly: hidden normally, appears only when both conditions are true simultaneously, disappears again the instant either one isn't. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1787 - TWO Quick Action Bar fixes, per Lynn's first real device testing (iOS). (1) FIX: Print threw "Can't find variable doPrintNote". Root cause confirmed by checking function scope directly: every other action in the registry (copyNoteAsPlainText, shareNote, speakNote, etc.) is a top-level/global function, but doPrintNote is uniquely declared INSIDE init(), a closure-scoped local function invisible to the top-level QUICK_ACTION_BAR_ACTIONS registry -- exactly why Print alone failed while Copy/Mail/Message all worked. Fixed by exposing it on window at its own existing definition site; its existing call sites (print-btn/more-print-btn, also inside init()) are completely unaffected, since they already reference the local closure variable directly. Verified directly: the button's onclick now reaches doPrintNote with no error. (2) ATTEMPTED FIX: Mail losing all blank lines specifically on iOS's Gmail app (confirmed by Lynn NOT an issue with iOS Apple Mail or Android Gmail -- narrowing it to that one specific app). No authoritative source found describing that app's exact internal behavior -- this is a different issue from the separate, well-documented, since-patched Apple Mail iOS 14.6 bug that turned %0D%0A into a literal "<BR>". Applied two independent, broadly-recommended mailto: techniques together as a best effort, flagged honestly as unconfirmed rather than promised: explicit CRLF line endings (the most widely-cited general fix across mail clients) plus a zero-width non-breaking marker (U+FEFF) on genuinely blank lines, so nothing can treat them as purely-empty whitespace to collapse away -- targets the specific "blank lines vanish" symptom, which plain CRLF alone may not fix if Gmail normalizes the body through an HTML-rendering step internally (where consecutive blank lines commonly collapse by default). Scoped to Mail only -- Message/sms: was confirmed already working correctly and left untouched. Verified the transform itself produces exactly the intended output (CRLF between all lines, U+FEFF on blank ones); the actual fix for the Gmail app itself still needs Lynn's real on-device retest to confirm one way or the other. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1786 - NEW FEATURE, per Lynn: the Quick Action Bar, for her father (advanced macular degeneration). An optional docked row of up to 4 huge, single-letter buttons at the true bottom of the note editor screen (not the confusingly-named #note-bottom-bar, which actually renders near the top) -- each permanently wired to ONE fixed action, deliberately NOT a cycling/long-press design: after discussion, a hidden mode someone has to remember (and verify by re-reading a tiny letter) was judged a worse fit for very low vision than simply configuring 1-4 separate buttons. New single object-valued setting, settings.quickActionBar (enabled/size/shape/colorScheme/buttons), Note Behavior bucket. A central QUICK_ACTION_BAR_ACTIONS registry (Print, Copy, Mail, Message, Share, Speak, Find in note, Toggle quiet mode, Back to notes list, Show/hide toolbar) is the single source of truth for both the Settings dropdown and the actual click wiring, so they can never drift apart -- and so a future action (a microphone/dictation toggle, per Lynn's stated plan once dictation support exists) is a one-line addition, not a redesign. Mail and Message deliberately go STRAIGHT to compose (mailto:/sms:), bypassing navigator.share()'s native picker entirely -- that picker is exactly the kind of small-icon obstacle this feature exists to route around; generic Share remains available as its own separate action for anyone who wants the picker. sms: reliably opens Messages with a recipient (confirmed against Apple's own developer docs) but pre-filling the body is NOT officially supported by Apple and is known to be inconsistent across iOS versions -- documented honestly in the code rather than overpromising; even a bare launch-into-Messages is real standalone value given how Lynn describes her father's current copy/paste workflow. Bottom placement was a deliberate, discussed decision: this project's own past attempts at any bottom-docked toolbar broke on iOS PWA because the on-screen keyboard's height can't be reliably measured -- rather than re-attempt keeping the bar correctly positioned WHILE the keyboard is up (the part that actually broke before), it's hidden outright the instant the keyboard opens (reusing the exact #note-screen.keyboard-open selector pattern #note-fab already relies on) and restored the instant it closes -- costing nothing in practice, since Print/Copy/Mail/Message are all things done when NOT actively typing. Deliberately DOCKED, not floating over note text like #note-fab -- #note-content-area gets a real padding-bottom reservation measured from the bar's own actual rendered height (matching pinToolbars()'s established measure-don't-guess approach for the top toolbars), re-synced on every resize/zoom event via the same listeners pinToolbars() already has (split into a lightweight space-only re-measure for that high-frequency path, versus the full button/handler rebuild only needed on actual settings changes). Locked, theme-INDEPENDENT high-contrast color pairs (black-on-white / white-on-black), plus an explicit opt-in 'match app theme' third choice -- a general-purpose accent color has no guaranteed contrast level, which matters more here than usual. Settings UI: enable toggle, a size slider (40-140px), a square/circle shape picker, the 3-way color picker, and 4 action-dropdown-plus-single-letter-box rows, added as a new subsection at the end of Note Behavior. Verified end to end: default four (Print/P, Copy/C, Mail/M, Message/T) render correctly with real screenshots at both default (64px) and a much larger (110px) size; the Copy button genuinely writes real note content to the clipboard (confirmed with clipboard permissions granted); the Mail button's mailto: URL is correctly subject/body-encoded; the Print button's click handler correctly maps to the same, already-shipped doPrintNote() the regular toolbar button uses; keyboard-open correctly hides and un-hides the bar; settings changes (shape, color, action, enabled) correctly save to localStorage, survive a reload, and correctly apply live -- including confirming a per-slot letter customization is preserved when only the ACTION is changed, not silently overwritten back to that action's own default; Settings search correctly surfaces the new rows. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1785 - FOUR fixes, per Lynn's continued testing. (1) FIX (real bug, introduced by v0.1784): Import/Export stopped scrolling entirely. Root cause confirmed directly: unlike Settings/Help/Export-Format (genuinely DEFINITE-height dialogs from fixed top+bottom offsets), Import/Export deliberately uses an auto, shrink-to-fit-content height capped by max-height (top:auto + a bottom offset + max-height:420px), so it hugs short content tightly instead of always being a large box. v0.1784's shared inner-scroll-wrapper fix used position:absolute, which removes a child from normal flow -- the OUTER's own auto-height calculation had nothing left to measure and collapsed to 0 (confirmed directly: computed height read exactly "0px"). Settings/Help/Export-Format were unaffected since their height doesn't depend on their children at all. Fixed by giving Import/Export's inner wrapper its own different treatment: a normal in-flow block (not absolutely positioned) with its own matching max-height, so short content still shrinks the outer tightly (verified: 71px for minimal content, not always 420px) while long content hits the inner's own cap and scrolls internally, landing the outer at essentially the same value as its own independent cap. Verified directly: outer/inner heights now match at 420px for full content, scrollTop genuinely moves and a scrolled screenshot shows different content in view. Per Lynn's specific request, also checked the Export Format dialog (the other one restructured in v0.1783) for the same latent bug -- it wasn't actually broken, since it has a genuinely definite height like Settings/Help, but it's using the same shared, now-corrected class regardless. (2) FIX: Help dialog showing two overlapping 'x' clear icons in Chrome (desktop and Android), not in Firefox, not reliably in Safari/iOS -- and occasionally two magnifying-glass icons in either dialog, not reliably reproducible. Root cause: input[type=search] gets native, non-stylable Chrome/WebKit decorations (a search icon and a clear button) rendered in the browser's own accent color, on top of the app's own custom versions of both. Settings had already suppressed the native clear button specifically (an existing, undocumented-here rule found during investigation) but never the search-icon decoration; Help had neither suppressed at all. Firefox doesn't render these WebKit-specific pseudo-elements, explaining why it was never seen there. Added the same suppression (::-webkit-search-cancel-button and the three related ::-webkit-search-* pseudo-elements) to BOTH search inputs, closing the gap completely rather than patching one browser's one icon at a time. Verified directly with real Chromium screenshots: both search bars now show exactly one magnifying glass and one clear button with text entered. (3) TWEAK: Help's "Collapse all sections" button nudged down 1px further (5px/1px padding split, was 4px/2px), per Lynn's follow-up that it could still scoot down a tiny bit more -- Settings' wasn't mentioned this time so left alone. Verified live: row height unchanged, still a pure redistribution. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1784 - THREE fixes, per Lynn's continued testing after v0.1783. (1) FIX: the Import/Export dialog still had the rounded-corner-vs-scrollbar problem -- it was missed in v0.1783 because it's a third, separate CSS class (.importexport-sheet, not .modal-sheet or .help-sheet) with the identical underlying issue. Same fix applied: outer element now only positions/rounds/clips, a new inner #importexport-sheet-scroll wrapper (reusing the existing shared .modal-sheet-scroll class) does the actual scrolling. This dialog had no scrollTop/scrollTo JS to redirect (only open/close class toggles), so the fix was purely structural. Verified directly: outer overflow:hidden, inner overflow-y:auto, border-radius intact. (2) FIX: the Help dialog's search bar (and its Next/Previous match arrows) was clipping/landing off-screen -- same root cause as the Settings search bar fixed in v0.1780, just never applied here: applyZoomSizeCompensation() was called with no targetWidth for both the sticky header and the search bar, silently defaulting to the full window.innerWidth instead of the dialog's own bounded width. Fixed the same way, passing #help-sheet-scroll's own current width. Also added the matching visualViewport resize/scroll listeners here (previously only plain window 'resize', the same gap Settings had before v0.1780), so both recompute reliably during an actual Ctrl+wheel zoom gesture, not only on a genuine window-size change. Verified directly with simulated zoom: search bar's right edge now lands exactly at the dialog's right edge (was overflowing past it). (3) TWEAK: "Collapse all sections" button in both Settings and Help nudged down 2px, per Lynn's report that it sat too high in its row and could clip at the top under some zoom levels. Fixed by swapping each row's own top/bottom padding (2px/4px -> 4px/2px) -- a redistribution, not an addition, so the row's total height and padding budget are unchanged; verified live that this shifts the button down 2px with the row's rendered height identical before and after. Suite unchanged: 332/332 (harness v8).
  v0.1783 - THE real Firefox/Chrome rounded-corner fix, plus a tweak, per Lynn. (1) FIX: Settings and Help dialogs showing square (not rounded) corners on the side the scrollbar sits, confirmed by Lynn to affect Chrome too once v0.1782's scrollbar-width:thin mitigation was tried (that mitigation is reverted here, since it made things worse rather than better). Root cause confirmed: .modal-sheet/.help-sheet were BOTH the rounded/positioned box AND the actual scrolling container on the same element -- neither browser reliably clips a native scrollbar to its own element's border-radius. Restructured properly this time: each dialog's outer element (#settings-sheet, #help-sheet, #export-format-sheet -- fixed all three sharing this pattern, not just the one reported, per the project's reuse/consolidate philosophy) now only positions, rounds, and clips (overflow:hidden, never scrolls itself); a new inner wrapper (#settings-sheet-scroll, #help-sheet-scroll, #export-format-sheet-scroll -- shared .modal-sheet-scroll class) does the actual overflow-y:auto scrolling. Clipping a scrollable descendant's native scrollbar via an ANCESTOR's overflow:hidden + border-radius is the standard, reliable cross-browser pattern for this. Every one of the 16 existing call sites that read/wrote #settings-sheet or #help-sheet directly was individually reviewed and classified: open/close class toggles, the FAB's own position measurement, and the change-event delegation listener correctly stay on the OUTER element (still the visible/positioned box, and delegation still works via normal event bubbling through the new wrapper); every scrollTop/scrollTo/scrollHeight usage and the sticky search bar's jump-to-section math were redirected to the new INNER scrolling element, since that's where real scrolling now actually happens. Each dialog's own bottom padding (extra breathing room after the last scrolled item, per Lynn's standing preference) moved from the old outer box to the new inner one, so it now correctly shows up as trailing space after the actual scrolled content instead of just unused space in a box that no longer scrolls. Verified directly: Settings opens, renders with intact rounded corners, and genuinely scrolls via the new inner element (confirmed scrollTop actually moves and clamps correctly at true scrollHeight-clientHeight). The regression suite's own settings-section nesting-depth check needed updating for this -- see Test Harness v8 below -- since every section is now, correctly and uniformly, one DOM level deeper. (2) TWEAK: "Back" label nudged further left again (margin-left 0px -> -3px, pulling it into the chevron SVG's own built-in whitespace without touching the drawn glyph), per Lynn's follow-up request, verified visually. Suite: 332/332 on harness v8 (v7 would report a false failure here -- see that file's own header comment).
  v0.1782 - ONE tweak, one mitigation, per Lynn. (1) TWEAK: "Back" label nudged closer to the chevron again (margin-left 2px -> 0px), per Lynn's follow-up request, verified visually. (2) PARTIAL MITIGATION for the Firefox rounded-corner/scrollbar issue flagged in v0.1781: added scrollbar-width:thin (a Firefox-originated, now-standard property, harmlessly ignored elsewhere) to both #settings-sheet and .help-sheet -- narrows Firefox's native scrollbar substantially, reducing how much straight edge clashes with the border-radius. Explicitly NOT the complete fix: that requires separating the rounding/clipping container from the actual scrolling container, a larger restructuring deliberately deferred as its own dedicated build (16+ existing call sites read/write #settings-sheet directly as both the dialog box and the live scroll container) -- discussed directly with Lynn rather than restructured unilaterally. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1781 - TWO fixes plus a tweak, per Lynn's continued desktop testing (Windows/Chrome and Firefox). (1) FIX: the Settings dialog's floating Save/Cancel/Scroll pill was still escaping the dialog under Ctrl+wheel zoom even after v0.1780's listener fix, worse in Firefox and escaping LEFT specifically on zoom-out. Root cause was a genuine math bug, confirmed by direct measurement with simulated zoom via CDP device-metrics override: positionSettingsFab() divided its right/bottom offsets by the zoom factor (right = desiredRight / zf), but desiredRight is derived from sheetRect, a LIVE getBoundingClientRect() measurement taken in that same call -- already correctly expressed in the current coordinate frame, unlike a hardcoded CSS constant (e.g. the note editor's own "+" FAB, whose static anchor genuinely does need that division). Dividing an already-live value by zf a second time double-compensated: measured directly, the pill's right edge landed 49px PAST the dialog's own right edge when zoomed in, and 100px INSIDE it (a large leftward jump) when zoomed out -- versus a stable, correct 16px inset in BOTH directions once the redundant /zf on right/bottom was removed. The scale(1/zf) transform itself is kept (still needed to hold the pill's own rendered size constant under zoom) since transform-origin sits at the same anchored corner, so removing the redundant division doesn't disturb it. Verified directly with simulated zoom-in and zoom-out: overhang past the dialog's edge is now a constant, correct -16px in both directions (was +49px / -100px). Not separately re-verified in real Firefox (unavailable in this sandbox and not downloadable here), but this is a pure position-math bug, not browser-specific rendering, so the fix applies the same way there. (2) INVESTIGATED, not yet fixed: the Settings/Help dialogs showing rounded corners on the left but not the right in Firefox on Windows. Likely root cause identified: .modal-sheet has both border-radius and overflow-y:auto on the SAME element -- a well-documented cross-browser inconsistency where Firefox's native (non-overlay) scrollbar isn't clipped to the element's own border-radius the way Chrome's typically is, leaving the corner nearest the scrollbar looking square. The standard fix (separate an outer non-scrolling wrapper that owns the border-radius/overflow:hidden from an inner element that owns the actual overflow-y:auto scrolling) is a genuinely invasive restructuring here -- 16+ separate JS call sites read/write #settings-sheet directly as both the positioned dialog box AND the active scroll container (scrollTop, jump-nav, sticky search bar, FAB positioning, scroll-position memory). Flagged for discussion with Lynn before attempting, rather than restructuring unilaterally. (3) TWEAK: "Back" label moved closer to the chevron (margin-left 4px -> 2px) with added separation before the next toolbar button (Speak), via a new margin-right: 10px on the label -- per Lynn's explicit request, verified visually. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1780 - THREE fixes, per Lynn's desktop/Windows testing report. (1) FIX: "Show 'Back' label" mode never benefited from the earlier taller-chevron fix (v0.1771) -- root cause: applyToolbarIconSizes()'s JS-injected, !important-tagged style block sizes all '#note-top-bar .icon-btn svg' elements identically, which also matches #back-btn svg and silently overrode the dedicated 1.3x CSS rule every time it ran (confirmed directly: computed chevron size was 20px, matching siblings, instead of the intended 26px). #back-btn is now excluded from the generic rule and given its own 1.3x rule in the same injected block, computed from the same _iconPx the rest of the function already uses. Also gave the 'Back' label its own inline-flex + slightly looser line-height (was inline with line-height:1 inherited from .icon-btn, leaving uppercase text's vertical position dependent on the platform's own default UI font metrics) so it self-centers consistently rather than only happening to look right on some platforms. (2) FIX: the Settings dialog's floating Save/Cancel/Scroll control could escape the dialog horizontally on Ctrl+wheel zoom (Windows/Chrome) -- its position-recompute was wired only to plain window 'resize', unlike the note editor's own equivalent (pinToolbars()), which has always also listened on visualViewport resize/scroll since Ctrl+wheel zoom reliably fires those and not always a plain resize. Added the same two listeners here. (3) FIX: the Settings dialog's search bar was clipping/extending off-screen horizontally under zoom -- applyZoomSizeCompensation() always compensated against window.innerWidth, correct for genuinely full-viewport-width elements (the note header/toolbar it was built for) but wrong for this search bar, which lives inside the dialog's own narrower, capped width. Added an optional targetWidth parameter (default unchanged, window.innerWidth, so every other existing caller is unaffected) and the Settings search bar now passes the dialog's own current rendered width. Verified directly: chevron computed size now 26px (was 20px) with the label's own centering independently confirmed; both Settings zoom listeners now fire on visualViewport events, matching pinToolbars()'s wiring exactly; the search bar's compensated width now derives from the dialog's own bounding rect, not the viewport. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1771 - THREE things, per Lynn. (1) FIX: back button chevron made taller -- found that even at its existing 1.3x SVG scale, the chevron shape itself only filled half its own 24x24 viewBox vertically (12 of 24 units), noticeably less proportionally than sibling icons like Undo (14 of 24); scaling the whole SVG box up doesn't fix that underlying proportion, so the shape's own points were changed to span 16 of 24 units instead, same width as before per Lynn's request to try taller without necessarily wider first. (2) FIX: back button vertical misalignment relative to sibling buttons -- found a concrete, confirmed difference from every correctly-aligned sibling: min-height/min-width had been fully removed (unset) rather than matching the shared var(--icon-btn-min) every other icon-btn uses, breaking box-model consistency with the row it sits in. Restored to standard, keeping the button's own deliberate tighter/asymmetric padding and larger ink scale intact, both clearly intentional per the existing code comment. Flagged honestly that fine vertical alignment may still need one more round of visual confirmation, since this can't be rendered and checked directly in this environment. (3) NEW setting: "Show 'Back' label on large screens" -- adds the word "Back" next to the chevron, per Lynn's report that alpha testers have gotten stuck in a note not realizing how to get out. Styled to match the existing folder-breadcrumb-back-label. Gated behind BOTH the new setting (off by default -- purely an opt-in aid, not a behavior change) AND a 700px-wide CSS media query, chosen specifically to exclude even large phones in landscape, not just portrait phones -- confirmed directly that no existing width-breakpoint convention already existed in this app to reuse, so this establishes a new one specifically for this purpose. Verified directly: the chevron's updated points, the setting's off-by-default state and its class-toggle wiring, and the label element's correct text all confirmed working. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1770 - REFINE: made the v0.1769 theme-color fix genuinely future-proof, per Lynn's direct follow-up question -- correctly pointed out that the fix as shipped hardcoded '#1e1c1a'/'#f5f0e8' as a second, separate copy of the palette values, meaning a future change to --bg would need someone to remember to also update this specific line, or the exact same staleness bug (the old dark value having silently drifted out of sync with the app's real background) would eventually repeat itself. Replaced the hardcoded pair with a live read via the existing resolveCSSVarToHex('--bg') utility (already built for the Bubble feature) -- confirmed directly that its probe element is appended to document.body specifically, making it a correct descendant of body.dark and therefore able to see that override properly (the opposite of an unrelated bug found earlier this session in a standalone prototype, where a probe was incorrectly scoped relative to a dark-mode override). Now any future change to --bg's actual value is automatically reflected here with no separate step to remember. Verified the full flow runs correctly end to end with no errors and confirmed the live-read function is genuinely being called and applied, not skipped. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1769 - FIX: re-diagnosed and fixed the regressed "Android dark mode PWA" bug Lynn recalled from a prior chat, predating this file's detailed changelog -- the system status bar (and other OS chrome outside the app's own content) staying the wrong color in dark mode. Root cause, found by direct inspection rather than guessing: theme-color was set via two static &lt;meta&gt; tags gated by CSS media="(prefers-color-scheme: ...)", which only ever reflects the OS's raw light/dark preference -- never this app's own actual current theme, which can be explicitly overridden independent of the OS via settings.theme. If a user's OS preference and their own app-level override disagreed (a real, fully supported case here), the browser picked whichever static tag matched the OS preference regardless of what the app was actually rendering -- producing a status bar that didn't match the app's own background at all, exactly the reported symptom. A second, related bug found while verifying the fix: the old dark value (#1a1a1a) had also silently drifted out of sync with this app's actual current dark background (#1e1c1a) at some point, a smaller but related staleness problem confirming this whole mechanism had been disconnected from the live theme system for a while. Fixed by replacing both static tags with a single JS-controlled one, updated from the exact same useDark computation (accounting for OS preference, explicit override, and 'auto') the rest of this app's dark-mode logic already uses, with the current --bg hex values directly so it can't drift out of sync with the actual palette again. Verified directly by reproducing the precise mismatch scenario that would have triggered the bug: OS reporting a light preference while the app is explicitly overridden to dark -- confirmed the meta tag correctly shows the app's real dark background rather than the OS's light preference. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1768 - CHANGE: default UI font and note font switched from Atkinson Hyperlegible to Atkinson Hyperlegible Next, per Lynn's request -- confirmed directly this had never actually happened: v0.1656 added the Next variant as a selectable option alongside the original specifically so it could be compared and the default switched over later, but that follow-up never occurred, exactly matching Lynn's own recollection. Updated the two CSS :root fallback variables and the two JS default-settings values (fresh-install only -- existing users' own saved settings are unaffected, same as any other default-value change), plus three third-level JS fallback expressions for consistency. Deliberately left untouched: the font-picker's own UI (pill button, all four dropdown <option> lists), which correctly needs to keep offering both variants as real choices; the weight-range registry entry describing the plain font's own supported weights, unrelated to which one is the default; and four named sample theme presets (Dyslexia Friendly, Poetry Page, Task Master, Fun & Bright) that specifically chose the plain variant for their own themed presentation, not simply because it happened to be the app's default at the time. Verified directly: a fresh install's DEFAULT_SETTINGS.font/noteFont and the live settings.font/noteFont both correctly resolve to the Next variant. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1767 - TWO things, per Lynn. (1) NEW: the note title now gets its own bubble too, mirroring all four variants exactly the way .viewer-line already does -- previously left entirely outside the bubble system, so its text color never matched the rest of the note (most visibly wrong in Solid mode, where the body turns white-on-color but the title stayed the ordinary page color). Its own full-width divider is suppressed once it has a bubble boundary instead. Caught a real scoping gap before shipping: the title row is shared between Typing and R&R mode, unlike .viewer-line's own rules (naturally R&R-only via #note-viewer), so applying this unconditionally would have made the title bubble incorrectly show up while actively typing too. No existing hook tracked current view/edit mode at the body level (confirmed directly: setViewMode() only ever toggled the button's own active state) -- added a new body.note-in-view-mode class specifically for this, and scoped every new selector to require it. (2) NEW setting: "Reopen last note on launch" (App Theme/General), per Lynn's report that always landing on the last-open note -- previously unconditional, no setting existed at all, confirmed directly by reading the actual launch code -- confuses some users who expect to start on the Home Screen every time. Default true, exactly matching the app's existing behavior, so this is purely additive. Verified directly: the note-in-view-mode class correctly toggles false while typing and true in R&R mode, the actual mechanism the new CSS scoping depends on; the new setting's default correctly matches prior behavior, and toggling it off in Settings correctly updates the underlying value. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1766 - FIX: custom colors for Bubble Accent Color / Bubble Interior Color silently never applied, per Lynn's report that presets worked but a custom color didn't. This codebase already has a documented precedent for exactly this bug class (v0.1541, light/dark-preview-color): the custom color picker's Apply handler looks up which actual setting to write to via a keyMap keyed by row name, and these two rows -- both added this session (v0.1761/v0.1765) -- were never added to that map at all. The picker's own button still visually updated (it always does, regardless of the lookup), giving no indication anything had failed, while settings.bubbleAccentColor/bubbleInteriorColor were silently never written. Added both missing entries. Verified through the real, complete flow rather than testing the fix in isolation: opened the actual custom color dialog via its real button, typed a hex value into the real input, clicked the real Apply button, and confirmed settings.bubbleAccentColor correctly ends up with that exact value afterward. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1765 - TWO things, per Lynn. (1) Paragraph rules / List item rules now correctly suppressed while a bubble style is active, with a notice explaining why in Settings -- both draw a border-bottom/border-top marking paragraph or list-item boundaries, which would visually leak through underneath a bubble's own rounded shape (most obviously on Tinted/Solid, which have no competing border to mask it). Unlike Ruled Lines & Grid, these two toggles deliberately stay fully interactive -- per Lynn's request, just a notice this time, not disabling the controls -- CSS suppression alone makes them harmlessly inert while bubbles are active, resuming automatically the moment bubbles are turned back off with no separate action needed. Uses the same higher-specificity-override technique (an #note-viewer id selector outweighing the base rule's plain class selector) already proven correct for the Select Lines highlight fix a few builds back. (2) NEW setting: Bubble Interior Color, for Outline/Stripe specifically -- previously just hardcoded to --bg2 in the CSS with no way to adjust it, confirmed directly with Lynn this was never actually built despite being discussed. Row visibility is tied to the currently selected bubble STYLE (shown only for Outline/Stripe, since Tinted/Solid derive their own fill from the accent color instead), separate from the existing accent-color row's own match-toggle-based visibility. When left unset (the default), the CSS custom property is cleared entirely rather than resolved to a concrete hex in JS -- the stylesheet's own var(--bubble-interior, var(--bg2)) fallback then applies natively, staying correctly theme-aware with no computation needed for the common, non-customized case. Verified directly: both new notices correctly show/hide with bubble state while their toggles remain fully interactive; the interior color CSS property correctly sets when customized and correctly clears (letting the fallback apply) when reset to default; the new settings row correctly shows only for Outline/Stripe and hides for Tinted/Solid. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1764 - TWO things, per Lynn. (1) Confirmed directly: the discussed "separate interior color for Outline/Stripe bubbles" setting was never actually built -- only the existing --bg2 (this app's card tone) was reused directly in the CSS, addressing whether it should differ from the rear background, but never exposed as its own adjustable setting. Logged as a known, honest gap on the existing bubble-mode to-do note rather than silently claimed as done; not fixed in this build. (2) NEW: diagnostic logging for the "scroll to last position" Settings button, per Lynn's report that it's regressed. Investigated directly rather than re-patching blind: verified the underlying save/restore of settings.settingsScrollPosition still works correctly on both the Save and Cancel close paths (tested directly); verified getDialogSectionKey()'s section-identity scheme is stable (id-based, not positional, so new sections added elsewhere -- including this session's own Bubble feature and device-aware-sizing work -- shouldn't have disrupted existing sections' saved open/closed state). Neither line of investigation found the actual root cause, so rather than guess further, the button's click handler now logs the saved target position, the sheet's actual current scrollHeight at the moment of the click (if a relevant section isn't expanded, this will be far shorter than the target expects, which would directly explain the symptom), and which sections are currently open -- real evidence from the next actual occurrence, rather than more theorizing from code alone. Verified the logging itself fires correctly and captures the right data. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1763 - FIVE things, per Lynn.
