(1) Extended the note-specific accent color to view-mode-btn, check-mode-btn, and add-blank-line-btn -- Lynn correctly identified the original v0.1544 scoping (some toggle buttons follow the note's own accent, some don't) as an unexplainable inconsistency rather than a deliberate design worth keeping; agreed, found no good rationale for it either. speak-btn and toggle-checkbox-btn were confirmed to have no comparable persistent glow state to convert at all (the former swaps to a separate stop-btn element while speaking; the latter is a momentary action, not a toggled mode) -- until Lynn specifically pushed back on speak-btn, which turned up a second, genuine miss: its own BASE/default color (not a .active state, just its normal always-visible background) was still plain --accent, missed by only checking .active-style rules the first time through. Fixed. Also found and fixed two more genuine misses from a comprehensive sweep of the actual current CSS rather than trusting the changelog summary alone: #tag-filter-indicator and #hide-completed-indicator (the pill badges, not the toolbar buttons) were also still on plain --accent. (2) NEW setting: "Match note editor accent" for the Bubble accent color, following the exact pattern of the existing note-accent toggle, per Lynn's report that she'd set an explicit bubble color override without meaning to and it kept silently applying afterward. Default on -- while on, bubbleAccentColor is bypassed ENTIRELY (not just used as a null fallback), so an old override can't reassert itself; the color swatch row stays hidden the same way the existing note-accent-color-own row does. (3) FIX: the tag-filter-indicator long-press menu "won't stay open" bug, which Lynn correctly remembered being fixed twice before (v0.1659 built it, v0.1694 patched a regression) yet kept recurring. Root cause, confirmed directly by testing rather than re-patching blind: v0.1694's fix only guarded the 'click' event against iOS's delayed synthetic click after a long-press -- but the menu's own "tap outside closes it" logic runs on SEPARATE 'touchstart' and 'mousedown' listeners that were never covered by that guard at all. iOS's synthetic touch sequence can include a touchstart landing outside the menu (which isn't positioned exactly at the original touch point), closing it through this completely unguarded path regardless of the click guard being active -- meaning v0.1694 was a real but incomplete fix, not something that later got lost. Extended the same eat-clicks guard to both listeners. Verified through the real long-press mechanism end to end, matching v0.1694's own verification rigor: a genuine 500ms long-press correctly opens the menu; an outside touchstart landing during the guard window now correctly leaves it open (confirmed this reproduced the bug precisely before the fix, and confirmed it's fixed after); a genuine outside tap after the guard window expires still correctly closes it, so normal dismissal isn't broken. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1762 - TWO fixes to the new Bubble/Cloud Paragraphs feature, per Lynn's first-round testing. (1) Markdown headings inside a Solid bubble kept the normal page text color instead of the computed light/dark text that variant uses for contrast against its own colored fill. Root cause: .viewer-h1 through .viewer-h6 explicitly set their own color rather than inheriting the parent .viewer-line's, so the solid-variant override never reached them -- fixed with an explicit, higher-specificity override for headings specifically. Confirmed the other three variants (Tinted/Outline/Stripe) don't need this, since their interiors stay page-like enough that the normal text color already has correct contrast. (2) The Select Lines tool's blue highlight was invisible while a bubble style was active, per Lynn's report -- considered and explicitly recommended AGAINST temporarily disabling bubble mode during selection (would be more work to get right and more visually jarring than the actual fix) after finding the real cause: the highlight was never broken or needing a redesign, just losing a CSS specificity fight, since the bubble variant rules include #note-viewer in their selector and so out-specify the plain .viewer-line.block-selected rule, silently replacing its background entirely rather than blending with it (CSS doesn't layer separate rules' background declarations). Restored with a higher-specificity override re-asserting the exact same existing translucent blue -- not a new design, just making the already-correct rule win the cascade again. Verified directly: heading color confirmed correctly picking up the new override rule rather than the old one; the selection highlight confirmed correctly computing to its intended translucent blue rather than the bubble's own fill color. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1761 - NEW: Bubble/Cloud Paragraph appearance for R&R mode, per Lynn's multi-turn design discussion this session. Each raw non-blank line becomes its own visually distinct card -- confirmed directly with Lynn this means one bubble per raw line, not merging multiple lines into one paragraph -- styled entirely via CSS on the EXISTING .viewer-line elements with zero DOM restructuring, so select-lines, move-lines, checkboxes, and every other line-based feature keep working exactly as before. Four style variants (Tinted/Solid/Outline/Stripe), all derived from a SINGLE new color setting (Bubble Accent Color, defaulting to the note's current accent) rather than four separate colors -- resolved through direct back-and-forth with Lynn: outline/stripe reuse the existing card tone (--bg2) for their interior, matching what she confirmed she wanted after questioning the original "just reuses the page background" design; solid's fill is programmatically darkened from the active accent (not pulled from the other theme's palette, which would often make contrast WORSE, not better -- dark mode's accent is lighter than light mode's, the opposite of what a dark solid fill needs) with its text color computed via the same WCAG luminance formula already used elsewhere in this app, not a hardcoded white that would fail for light custom accent colors. Fixed the stripe variant's fuzzy top edge (flagged from the standalone prototype) with a subtle uniform border, overridden to a thicker accent stripe on the left only. Shadow angle fixed at 35 degrees per Lynn's explicit choice, not a user-facing setting. All four numeric settings (padding, spacing, corner radius, shadow intensity) are stored as reference/touch-tier pixel values and scaled through var(--ui-scale) at render time, exactly matching the Stage 1/2 device-aware-sizing convention Lynn specifically asked to reuse. Ruled Lines & Grid is force-disabled (not just visually greyed out -- genuinely non-interactive) the moment a bubble style is active, with an explicit notice explaining why, per Lynn's request; does not auto-restore when bubbles are turned back off. New global color utilities added (mixHex, darkenHex, relativeLuminanceHex, resolveCSSVarToHex), following this app's own established convention of plain JS hex math over CSS color-mix() for broader browser support. Two real bugs caught and fixed before shipping, both found by testing rather than assumed away: (1) a stray extra closing div in the new Settings HTML, caught by the existing structural harness; (2) a genuine cross-scope bug where the ruled-lines conflict sync function was invisible to the Settings-open initialization code (confirmed directly by testing, not assumed) -- the exact class of mistake this codebase's own v0.1543 fix already documented once for markRow() -- fixed by making the function truly global. Also caught and fixed a harness-flagged unclosed-CSS-comment warning that turned out to be an accidental literal "less-than style greater-than" string inside an unrelated JS comment, confusing the file's tag-boundary detection. Verified extensively: DOM structure confirmed unchanged (same element count, same blank-line detection) with bubbles on; all four variant body classes confirmed mutually exclusive; the color derivation math confirmed correct once verified independent of a known, pre-existing jsdom limitation (already confirmed present in this app's own existing, shipped code) that prevents resolving CSS custom properties in this specific test environment; the ruled-lines force-disable and notice confirmed correctly appearing on Settings open, not just after a change event. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1760 - NEW: Ctrl/Cmd+F now opens the Home Screen's own search bar, per Lynn's confirmed request -- previously fell through to the browser's native find there, inconsistent with the note editor, where the same shortcut has always opened this app's own find bar. Wired directly to the existing openHomeSearch()/closeHomeSearch() pair rather than building anything new -- press again to close, matching the note editor's own toggle behavior exactly. Guarded against firing while some other dialog (Settings, Help, etc.) happens to be open on top of the Home Screen, so the shortcut still reaches whatever's actually on screen -- every one of the app's modal overlays follows the same "*-overlay" id + .open class convention, confirmed directly, so one generic check covers all of them without needing to name each individually. Verified directly: Ctrl+F on a bare Home Screen correctly suppresses the browser's native find and opens the app's own search bar; pressing it again correctly closes it; pressing it while Settings is open correctly does neither, leaving native find available for Settings' own content instead. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1759 - NEW: closed three remaining Ctrl+wheel zoom-compensation gaps, per Lynn's report -- all three were explicitly flagged as known, deliberate gaps back in the changelogs when this whole system was first built (v0.1716), never addressed since. (1) The Settings dialog's own floating bottom control (checkmark/scroll/X) never had compensation at all. Caught a real conflict before shipping: it also has separate wide-window logic recomputing its position based on the sheet's current width, and the shared compensation helper's own "no mouse present" behavior (clearing the anchor back to an empty string, relying on a static CSS default) would have silently broken that wide-window centering the moment no mouse was present, since this element's correct value is dynamic, not a static default. Fixed properly: computes the current correct position first, always, then layers the zoom-scale correction on top only when actually needed. (2) The Settings dialog's own floating search bar (position:sticky) never had compensation. (3) The Help dialog's floating header and search bar (also position:sticky, stacked -- the search bar's position depends on the header's own height) never had compensation; applied it before the existing height-measurement code runs, since a compensated element's getBoundingClientRect() correctly reflects its true, corrected size, so measuring after compensation gives the search bar the right offset instead of one based on an inflated, uncompensated height. All three use applyZoomSizeCompensation(), the same function the Home Screen's own sticky header ended up using after its own earlier back-and-forth (v0.1727-v0.1729) -- reused for consistency with what was actually kept, rather than trying yet another scale-only variant already tried once and abandoned. Verified directly with a simulated zoomed-in mouse device: all four elements (Settings search bar, Settings floating control, Help header, Help search bar) correctly receive the compensating scale transform; the floating control's position correctly reflects the scaled-down DYNAMIC wide-window value, not the static default, confirming the two systems compose correctly rather than fighting each other. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1758 - FIX: find-in-note losing track of matches in R&R mode, per Lynn's report of hitting "next" and landing on unrelated highlighted text -- happened specifically while searching the debug log, which was the key clue. Root cause: runFind() marks match boundaries by inserting null-byte (\x00) placeholder tokens into the raw text before it's passed through urlsToLinks() for rendering, then swaps those placeholders for real <mark> elements afterward. The plain-URL-detection regex in urlsToLinks() excluded whitespace, angle brackets, and quotes from a URL's body, but not the null byte -- so if a placeholder landed directly adjacent to a URL with no whitespace between them, the greedy URL match swallowed the placeholder whole, burying it inside the generated <a> tag where the later placeholder-to-<mark> replacement could never find it. That silently dropped one match from the list without erroring, shifting every later match's index out of alignment with what was actually highlighted on screen -- exactly the reported symptom. The debug log is especially prone to this since it's dense with repeated stack-trace URLs from diagnostic logging. Fixed by adding \x00 to the regex's excluded characters, so a URL match always stops cleanly at a placeholder boundary rather than consuming it. Verified directly: reproduced the exact scenario (a stack-trace URL immediately followed by a find-match placeholder, no whitespace between them) and confirmed the placeholder now survives the render fully intact instead of being absorbed into the link. Two other bugs Lynn reported in the same session -- an export-reminder nag appearing despite a JSON export the day before, and an iOS text-selection copy landing two lines off from what was visually selected -- were investigated directly (the JSON export path does correctly call markFullExportDone(); the shared download function is fully synchronous with nothing that should throw) but not conclusively reproduced from code alone; no fix attempted for either without stronger evidence. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1757 - NEW: Stage 2 of device-aware sizing, per Lynn's request to proceed using Stage 1's numbers as a working model rather than waiting to finalize the exact relative sizes first -- everything here is driven through --ui-scale (already defined per size tier AND touch/mouse input mode, per Stage 1), so a future adjustment to those numbers automatically propagates everywhere touched here with no further per-component changes needed, exactly as requested. Covers all six items from the original Stage 2 list: (1) note editor header's own padding and internal gap; (2) both FABs (note-fab, quiet-more-fab) -- their own circle size didn't even vary by size tier before this, a gap beyond just touch/mouse; (3) the floating Home Screen pill and logo's internal padding -- deliberately left their outer position (top/left/right) untouched, since applyZoomPositionCompensation() hardcodes that exact anchor (confirmed directly in its call site) and would desync if the position itself moved with the tier/input mode, so only padding scales there; (4) folder breadcrumb, home search bar, and select-notes bar padding -- the search bar's was in an inline style attribute, moved into a proper CSS rule since inline styles can't be overridden by any selector; (5) the toolbar row's own inter-button gap; (6) note list and folder list entry padding/gap/margin. Every value uses the exact prior touch/medium pixel number as its base, so --ui-scale=1 there reproduces the original layout exactly -- confirmed by direct arithmetic, not just by construction. Verified structurally (the same jsdom limitation noted in Stage 1 applies here too: calc()/var() chains can't be resolved into rendered pixels in this environment) -- CSS brace balance confirmed unchanged (1038/1038), and every touched rule confirmed to correctly reference var(--ui-scale) in place of its old hardcoded value. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1756 - TWO refinements to the new font-size buttons, per Lynn's first-round testing feedback. (1) Both glyphs made significantly larger and equal in size (0.9x icon size, up from 0.62x/0.42x) -- the smaller 'a' had deliberately echoed "smaller font" the same way the bottom sheet's own icon does, but these buttons are specifically for low-vision users, so a small, hard-to-see glyph was actively counterproductive regardless of what it was meant to symbolize. (2) Added hold-to-repeat, reusing the exact same pattern already established by the caret nudge buttons (wireCaretNudge): Pointer Events, immediate first step on press, 450ms delay, then repeats at ~11/s until released -- same alarm-clock-style behavior Lynn asked for. saveSettings() is deferred to fire exactly once when the hold ends, rather than on every rapid repeat step, to avoid unnecessary repeated writes; the visible size change and live toast still update on every single step throughout the hold. Verified directly: a quick tap still increments once and saves once; holding continues past the initial step and saves exactly once on release, not per repeat; both glyphs now render at the same, larger size. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1755 - THREE pieces, per Lynn. (1) FIX: the entire zoom-cap system never once checked the separate "Disable Pinch to Zoom" setting -- capping was harmless to leave as-is with that setting on, but every release path was unconditional, meaning the system would keep re-enabling actual zoom capability at the OS level even after the user explicitly turned pinch-zoom off. Gated the 350ms timer's restore and the force-release function behind the setting; capping itself untouched. (2) FIX: repeated pinch attempts in EMNI became progressively harder (an unusual ~2-second hold needed) even though the very first attempt worked cleanly after v0.1754. Theory, flagged honestly since it involves iOS's own undocumented gesture-recognition timing, invisible to JS logging: v0.1754 still capped the first finger's touch immediately then un-capped it a moment later once a second finger confirmed a pinch -- a real, if brief, maximum-scale=1.0 moment right as the gesture begins, which may be enough for iOS's recognizer to decide up front "no zoom for this gesture" and not reconsider mid-gesture, only on a fresh touch sequence (matching the ~2-second-hold pattern). Redesigned so the cap is delayed 60ms and fully cancelable -- if a second finger arrives within that window, the cap is never applied at all for that gesture, not applied-then-undone. A genuine tap still caps correctly (60ms is well within a normal tap's hold time). (3) NEW: two one-touch lower toolbar buttons in the note editor, per Lynn's own testing with elderly users showing even the existing text-size bottom sheet is too much friction for some. Capital 'A' increases note text size, lowercase 'a' decreases -- reuses the exact same increment/bounds (12-72px) as the sheet's own buttons, saves immediately, and shows a brief toast confirming the new size. Fully registered alongside every other toolbar button: hideable/reorderable (HIDEABLE_BUTTONS, DEFAULT_TOOLBAR_ORDER), paired as one settings row like undo/redo (SETTINGS_ROW_PAIRS), and documented in the Toolbar Help panel. Verified all three directly: the setting-respecting release paths correctly stay capped when Disable Pinch to Zoom is on; the delayed cap correctly never touches the capped state at all during a simulated two-finger pinch start while still correctly capping a genuine single tap after the delay; the new buttons correctly adjust, clamp at both bounds, update the live CSS variable, and show the confirmation toast. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1754 - FIX: found the actual, final root cause -- every prior fix correctly restored zoom at the MOMENT of transition into EMNI, but Lynn's log showed it breaking again barely half a second later, with no focus or typing event anywhere in between: zoom correctly freed at timestamp .079, then capped again at .641, both from the exact same touchstart listener. Root cause: the editor remains the visible, on-screen element throughout EMNI (until switching back to R&R) -- and the touchstart listener caps on ANY touch landing on it, with no way to distinguish a genuine tap-to-type gesture from the first finger of a pinch-to-zoom gesture. The very act of touching the screen to BEGIN pinching was itself immediately re-capping the zoom before the gesture could ever happen, which is exactly why the bug persisted despite every transition-point fix working correctly in isolation. A pinch's first finger necessarily touches slightly before its second, and that first touch alone still reports touches.length===1 -- so simply skipping multi-touch touchstarts doesn't help, since the cap is already applied by the first finger before the second one ever arrives. Fixed instead by detecting the moment a SECOND finger lands and immediately undoing whatever the first finger's touch may have just capped, since that retroactively proves it was never a single tap-to-type gesture -- the brief cap-then-uncap for the first finger alone happens in a sub-frame window before any real gesture is underway. Verified directly: a genuine single-finger tap still correctly caps (the actual intended trade-off, unaffected); a second finger arriving immediately and correctly releases the cap, confirmed in a clean instance with no state carried over from a prior test. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1753 - FIX: v0.1752 only fixed R&R mode -- EMNI (Typing mode with the keyboard NOT showing) was still broken, per Lynn's own precise correction, which also caught a real gap in the fix being planned: EMNI can be reached two genuinely different ways -- fresh entry into Typing mode from R&R (never having been in EMTIP at all this session), and dismissing the keyboard while already in Typing mode -- and a fix aimed only at the second path would have completely missed the first, exactly the scenario she flagged before it was built. Root cause of the fresh-entry case: setViewMode()'s switch-to-Typing branch calls editor.focus({preventScroll:true}) to prepare the cursor for typing -- necessary and correct -- but the zoom-cap system's focusin listener can't tell "editor is programmatically focused" apart from "the keyboard is genuinely showing," and caps regardless, landing fresh EMNI entry incorrectly zoom-locked despite no keyboard ever appearing. Fixed by force-releasing the cap immediately after that focus() call -- it fires synchronously during focus(), so by the time execution resumes the cap is already wrongly applied and needs undoing right there. If the user then genuinely taps into the text, the normal touchstart/focusin path still correctly re-caps at that point, unchanged. Also added the same force-release to the EMTIP-exit blur handler (the actual keyboard-dismiss-while-staying-in-Typing-mode path), as belt and suspenders alongside the existing timer-based restore, which has already been shown unreliable twice. Verified directly, covering all three real states: fresh EMNI entry now correctly stays zoom-free despite the programmatic focus; genuinely tapping into the text still correctly caps (preserving the actual intended trade-off); dismissing the keyboard while remaining in Typing mode now correctly releases. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1752 - FIX: found the ACTUAL root cause of the pinch-zoom bug, directly from Lynn's own diagnostic log. At the exact moment setViewMode(true) (switching to R&R mode) was entered, activeElement was ALREADY "BODY" -- focus had already left the editor -- yet the viewport was STILL capped at maximum-scale=1.0, and no "focusout from text field" line appears anywhere in the several preceding seconds. Something moved focus away from the editor (very likely the toolbar view-mode button itself, tapped while the keyboard was still open) WITHOUT a real, catchable focusout event ever firing. This meant v0.1750's explicit editor.blur() call -- which only guarantees a focusout event fires -- was calling blur() on an element that had already lost focus through some other, untraceable path: a genuine no-op, explaining precisely why it made zero improvement despite being technically sound. Fixed by no longer depending on that event at all: exposed a new, directly-callable window._forceReleaseZoomCap(), reusing the exact same unconditional release logic the existing visibilitychange handler already used, and call it directly from both setViewMode(true) and goHome() -- both are the app's own authoritative signal that editing has ended, and shouldn't need to wait for or depend on any DOM event to know that. The original explicit editor.blur() calls are kept in place too, for the normal case where focus genuinely is still on the editor. Verified directly by reproducing Lynn's EXACT scenario: focus the editor (caps the viewport), then simulate focus silently moving to body with NO focusout event ever dispatched (precisely what her log showed), then call setViewMode(true) -- confirmed the cap now correctly releases regardless, since the new mechanism never depends on that event firing at all. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1751 - NEW: full diagnostic logging for the pinch-zoom bug, per Lynn's report that v0.1750's explicit-blur fix made no improvement -- meaning the root-cause theory was likely wrong, not just incomplete, since an explicit blur() call is about as reliable as JS gets for triggering a real focusout. Rather than theorize further, added real evidence-gathering, same approach that solved the toolbar-overflow and breadcrumb bugs: (1) the zoom-cap system's own private _cap()/_scheduleFree() functions now log every cap/restore decision directly, including a caller stack for _cap() (called from multiple event listeners) and the exact reason a restore attempt does or doesn't succeed; (2) the focusin/focusout listeners themselves now log every time they fire on a text field, so it's directly visible whether these events are actually firing at all when they should be, not assumed; (3) a new shared diagnostic snapshot function logs the FULL picture at once -- viewport meta content, activeElement, touch-action on both the editor and content area, and the separate "Disable Pinch to Zoom" setting's state (a completely different, CSS-based mechanism that could independently block pinch-zoom and would explain why a fix aimed only at the viewport-cap system had zero effect) -- called at setViewMode's entry, immediately after the blur() call, and again 500ms later once the real restore window has passed. Verified directly: the full logging chain fires correctly end to end and correctly captures every field. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1750 - FIX: pinch-zoom getting stuck disabled after leaving Typing mode, per Lynn's report -- worse specifically via the toolbar view/edit button than double-tap. Found the existing iOS zoom-cap system (an intentional, documented mechanism: while a text field is focused, the viewport's max-zoom is capped to 1.0 to prevent iOS's own jarring auto-zoom-on-focus, restored ~350ms after a genuine focusout). Clarified directly with Lynn: the accepted trade-off was specifically EMTIP (Typing mode with the keyboard actually open) losing pinch -- not the whole of Typing mode, and R&R mode should always have it back. Root cause: setViewMode()'s switch-to-R&R branch hid the editor via display:none without ever explicitly blurring it first -- and whether hiding a still-focused element via CSS reliably fires a real focusout on its own is inconsistent and known to be unreliable on iOS Safari specifically, rather than something to depend on implicitly. Confirmed directly that the app's own EMTIP/EMNI keyboard-open tracking depends on this exact same focusout event too (not a separate, disagreeing signal), and that the two existing "Done typing" handlers already blur explicitly and correctly -- narrowing the actual gap to specifically the Typing-to-R&R transition. Fixed by calling editor.blur() explicitly before hiding it in setViewMode(), guaranteeing a real focusout fires regardless of which code path triggered the mode switch, rather than depending on the browser's implicit behavior. Also added the same defensive blur to goHome(), for the separate case of exiting a note directly from Typing mode without ever switching to R&R first -- the editor could still be focused when the whole note screen gets hidden via a CSS class toggle, the identical underlying risk. Verified end to end with iOS mocked: focusing the editor correctly caps the viewport; switching to R&R via setViewMode() correctly blurs it; after the real 350ms restore delay, the viewport is correctly restored to allow pinch-zoom again; exiting a note directly from Typing mode via goHome() also correctly blurs. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1749 - THREE pieces, per Lynn. (1) FIX: export reminder nagged on every single app launch once its thresholds were crossed, per Lynn's report -- confirmed by reading the original v0.1683 design directly: it only had a ONE-TIME 3-day grace period before the reminder could first fire, never a repeating cooldown between separate showings, so once crossed, the same check re-fired on every subsequent launch with nothing tracking when it had last actually been shown. Added a genuine cooldown (2 days, matching Lynn's own recollection of the intended design) via a new tracked timestamp, lastExportNagShownAt, recorded only when the notice is actually shown. (2) NEW: consolidated the "switch to Home Screen + render its list" pair into one shared helper, goToHomeScreen() -- direct response to Lynn's question of how to prevent bugs like the breadcrumb one from recurring. The correct order between these two calls (showScreen must run first) was found to have been gotten wrong independently in FIVE separate places throughout the file. Replaced all five with the new helper, so the correct order is now structurally guaranteed in one place instead of five places each needing to remember it correctly on their own. (3) NEW: a loud, non-transient toast warning for the exact failure mode just fixed, per Lynn's explicit request that a debug-log entry alone isn't enough during alpha testing -- it's exactly what let this bug go unnoticed through several rounds of testing. renderNotesList()'s own three internal calls to pinHomeScreenElements() now route through a wrapper that checks for the specific anomaly (note-screen-active still set when it shouldn't be) and immediately shows a persistent, must-dismiss toast if so -- deliberately NOT applied to pinHomeScreenElements()'s other callers (like a settings-change handler), where hitting this same guard can be completely legitimate. Verified directly: the cooldown correctly blocks a repeat nag within 2 days and correctly allows one again after; the new helper correctly performs both steps in the right order; the loud toast correctly fires for the exact anomaly scenario and correctly does NOT fire during normal, correct operation (no false positives). Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1748 - FIX: found the ACTUAL root cause for the remaining breadcrumb-missing case, from Lynn re-running the exact same scenario on the v0.1747 build and getting the identical symptom -- since the folder involved clearly wasn't empty (it had a note in it, confirmed by the log), the previous build's fix genuinely didn't apply here, exactly as flagged. The distinguishing pattern across both logs was unmistakable: every render triggered by directly tapping a folder from the list worked correctly; only the one triggered by goHome() (returning from a note back into its folder) consistently failed. Traced goHome() directly and found it: renderNotesList() was called BEFORE showScreen('home-screen') -- but showScreen() is the only thing that clears the note-screen-active class, and pinHomeScreenElements() (called at the very end of renderNotesList()) begins with a guard that returns immediately while that class is still present. Calling renderNotesList() first meant that guard was still seeing the note screen's own, not-yet-cleared state, silently exiting before ever reaching the breadcrumb margin code -- every single time a note was exited this way. A plain, deterministic ordering mistake, not timing, and it explains precisely why direct folder-tap navigation always worked (that path never went through this wrong order) while this one never did. Fixed by swapping the order in goHome() (both its normal exit and its auto-delete-empty-note early return), then searched specifically for the same exact wrong-order pattern anywhere else in the file and found two more genuine instances -- the app's initial-load path and the Siri Shortcut/URL-action "note not found" path -- fixed identically. Verified directly: reproduced Lynn's exact scenario end to end (open a folder, open a note inside it, exit via goHome()) and confirmed the breadcrumb now correctly receives a real margin value clearing the header, where before it would have silently stayed empty. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1747 - FIX: found a genuine, confirmed structural gap in renderNotesList(), from directly reading Lynn's own log. Her log showed the breadcrumb correctly created (confirmed present in the DOM) right after returning from a note back into its folder -- but, unlike every other successful case earlier in that same log, no pinHomeScreenElements() margin-decision entry ever followed it. Traced the actual code and found two early `return` statements in renderNotesList() -- one for an empty folder, one for zero search results -- both of which exit the function completely before ever reaching pinHomeScreenElements() at the very end. Since the breadcrumb is created earlier in the SAME function call, either path leaves it correctly present in the DOM but with no margin ever set to clear the fixed header, making it invisible behind it. A plain, deterministic structural gap, not timing: whenever a folder happens to render as empty (or a search inside one finds nothing) while the breadcrumb is showing, this happens every single time, guaranteed. Fixed by calling pinHomeScreenElements() before both early returns. One honest caveat: the specific folder in Lynn's log did have at least one note in it, so this exact path isn't confirmed as the trigger for that particular instance -- but it's a real, separately-confirmed bug regardless, and would produce exactly this symptom under the right conditions. Verified directly: an empty folder, and a folder with an active search matching nothing, both now correctly set the breadcrumb's margin before returning. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1746 - NEW: diagnostic logging for the sometimes-missing folder breadcrumb on iOS PWA, per Lynn's request -- starting the same evidence-first approach that just solved the toolbar-overflow bug, rather than guessing further. Confirmed first that nothing had been added for this specific bug yet (only pre-existing, narrower "FOLDER ANOMALY" logging that covers just one failure mode -- currentFolderId pointing at a folder that no longer exists). Three additions: (1) renderNotesList() now logs its breadcrumb creation decision every time it runs -- currentFolderId, whether the folder actually exists, and whether it decided to create the element -- plus a direct verification that the element genuinely landed in the DOM right after insertBefore, rather than assuming it did. (2) pinHomeScreenElements() now logs its own decision whenever a breadcrumb element exists -- which element was determined to be first-visible, the computed header height, and the actual marginTop value set, with a call stack, since this function has multiple trigger points. (3) A new, separate visibilitychange listener (kept independent from the two existing ones in this file, matching this app's own established convention for these) logs the breadcrumb's real DOM state -- exists, current marginTop -- on every background/foreground transition. This exact app already has a precedented, documented bug class here (v0.1657, pinch-zoom getting stuck on iOS PWA) where backgrounding can leave stale state that predates a suspension without firing expected events, so this specifically captures facts across that transition rather than assuming it's the cause. Verified directly: navigating into a folder correctly logs the decision and creation; simulated background/foreground events correctly log the breadcrumb's real state on each; the margin-decision logging correctly fires and captures the right values once realistic layout measurements are present. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1745 - FIX: found and fixed the actual root cause, from Lynn's own precise comparison of two logged runs -- her second test deliberately skipped opening the More menu specifically to keep the data simple, and it worked. Same note, same view-mode transition, identical window width logged in every line -- one measurement read 6px (correctly not wrapped), another read 48px (wrapped), and the ONLY difference between their two visible-button lists (added in the last build specifically to catch this) was select-blocks-btn ("Select Lines"). Root cause, confirmed by reading the exact code order: applySmallScreenOverflow() ran PARTWAY through updateViewOnlyBtns() -- specifically, before select-blocks-btn (and everything else set after it, all the way to the end of the function) had its own visibility finalized for the CURRENT call. The check was measuring select-blocks-btn's STALE value left over from the PREVIOUS call, not what it was about to become moments later in the very same invocation -- a plain, deterministic ordering bug inside one function, never a timing issue of any kind, exactly matching what Lynn asked to rule out from the very start. Fixed by moving the overflow check to the true end of updateViewOnlyBtns(), after every single button it sets has been finalized for that call -- not just past select-blocks-btn specifically, since other buttons set later in the same function could plausibly have had the identical problem. Verified directly: engineered the exact stale-state scenario from the log (select-blocks-btn left hidden from a prior call, then switching to view mode) and confirmed the overflow check's own visible-button list now correctly includes select-blocks-btn in the SAME measurement that turns it visible, rather than one call behind. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1744 - NEW: full visible-button-list logging, found necessary by actually tracing through Lynn's newest log line by line with the added stack traces. Real progress this round: the log clearly showed the SAME note, in the SAME view mode, with window.innerWidth logged as exactly 402 in every single line (the viewport genuinely never changed) -- yet one measurement read 6px (correctly not wrapped) and another, three seconds later, read 48px (wrapped), with only opening the More menu in between. Checked two plausible CSS-driven causes directly and ruled both out: neither the view-only-mode class nor the toolbar-collapsed class touch anything in the upper toolbar row at all (confirmed by reading their actual CSS rules, not assumed). Since the viewport didn't change, the row's own CONTENT must have -- but the logging so far only ever recorded the two measured positions, never which specific buttons were actually visible at each moment, so there was no way to see WHICH one differed. Added exactly that: every overflow check now lists every currently-visible button in the row by id at the moment of measurement, since updateViewOnlyBtns() (which runs immediately before every check) sets visibility for several other buttons based on note state, any of which is now the leading suspect for the real, additional cause. Verified directly: the new field correctly lists the actual visible buttons by id. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1743 - NEW: verification-focused logging, per Lynn's specific, pointed questions -- not just logging the DECISION, but verifying the actual DOM state independently rather than trusting the decision's own claim, and investigating a concrete new hypothesis she raised (the More menu being rebuilt without the moved items) rather than only adding more logging blindly. Checked directly: the overflow items are NOT registered in MORE_MENU_ITEMS (so that table's own rebuild loop can't silently overwrite them), and confirmed structurally (not just by test) that #toolbar-more-hidden-items -- the container that DOES get its innerHTML wiped on every menu open -- is a completely separate, initially-empty element from where the overflow buttons actually live in the HTML, so that rebuild mechanism cannot be destroying them either. Along the way, the new call-stack logging surfaced something not previously known: opening the More menu itself triggers a full fresh re-check of its own, via openMoreMenu -> rebuildMoreMenuItems -> updateViewOnlyBtns -> applySmallScreenOverflow -- a real, additional trigger point beyond the three already known. Added exactly what Lynn asked for: showInMainBar() (the function that resets copy/cut/paste back to visible) now logs every single time it runs, with its own caller stack; openMoreMenu() now reads and logs the ACTUAL current DOM display values of both the overflow items and the main-bar buttons at the exact moment the menu is opened and looked at, independent of what any earlier "corrected" message claimed -- if these two ever disagree in a real log, that's direct, undeniable proof something changed them in between without going through the overflow-check code at all. Verified directly: triggered the real click handler (not a synthetic function call, since openMoreMenu isn't exposed globally) and confirmed the full logging chain fires correctly end to end, including correctly naming the newly-discovered rebuildMoreMenuItems call path. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1742 - NEW: further diagnostic logging, per Lynn's continued report that the bug still occurs after v0.1741 and her own suggestion to add logging in the deciding functions and capture the call stack. Re-searched by button ID rather than variable name this time (broader net than the earlier search) -- found two more references to paste-btn, but both only toggle a CSS class for the paste-mode indicator, not visibility, so ruled out. Re-examined updateViewOnlyBtns() (28 separate callers throughout the code) in full: it unconditionally resets cut/paste to visible and calls applySmallScreenOverflow() right after, every single time -- nothing found there that would skip or bypass the correction. Since inference from surrounding log lines hasn't been enough to find what's actually happening, added exactly what Lynn asked for: applySmallScreenOverflow()'s own log entry now captures and includes the real call stack (just the function names from the first few frames, not a full raw trace, which would mostly be noise) so every entry shows precisely which of the many call paths triggered it, rather than guessing from context; updateViewOnlyBtns() itself also now logs the moment it resets these buttons, including whether it's in view or edit mode at that exact point. Verified directly: the stack capture correctly names the actual calling function when tested through a wrapper. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1741 - FIX: found the ACTUAL root cause of the toolbar-overflow intermittency, from reading Lynn's own debug log with her precise clarification -- every logged correction had claimed success, but she confirmed several of them did not actually stick. That mismatch was the real clue: a SEPARATE, completely independent window.resize listener, unrelated to the one removed in v0.1739, was still calling applySmallScreenOverflow() on every resize -- discovered sitting right next to a second, older, mostly-dead function (checkTopBarOverflow) from an earlier, abandoned attempt at this exact same problem, whose own button-moving logic was already commented out and whose only remaining output (_topBarOverflow) was confirmed unused anywhere in the file. v0.1739 could only remove the resize listener it knew about -- the one it had added itself -- with no way to know this second, pre-existing one was also there. That's exactly why corrections kept logging as successful in the moment: they genuinely were, right up until some later resize event (iOS fires these for non-width changes like the address bar or keyboard) silently reran the whole check and undid it, with nothing left to catch and re-correct that second pass. Removed both the dead checkTopBarOverflow function entirely and its own resize listener, along with the second, still-live one -- confirmed no other callers of checkTopBarOverflow exist anywhere, cleaned up its now-vestigial stub declaration, and corrected a stale comment that still pointed to it. Verified directly: after a correction is applied, firing a resize event (waited past the old 150ms debounce window, to be thorough) no longer undoes it -- the buttons correctly stay in the overflow menu. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1740 - NEW: full diagnostic logging for applySmallScreenOverflow(), per Lynn's explicit request. Every call now logs the current note id, window width, UI size setting, whether Speak is icon-only, the actual measured offsetTop of both the More button and the first toolbar button, and the resulting wrapped/not-wrapped determination -- not just when something moves, so entries from different opens of the SAME note can be directly compared to see exactly what differed between a call that worked correctly and one that didn't, rather than guessing. If a correction is applied, a second entry logs whether the single re-check confirmed the wrap actually resolved. Also corrected the record on the previous build's own tests: they used artificial, hand-set offsetTop values to verify the JS logic responds correctly to an ASSUMED wrapped state -- they never verified whether real wrapping actually occurs under real conditions, since this environment has no real browser layout engine and cannot compute CSS flexbox wrapping at all. That distinction wasn't stated clearly before and should have been. Verified only what's actually verifiable here: the logging mechanism itself correctly captures every intended field, tested explicitly with Speak set to NOT icon-only (Lynn's exact repro condition) and an iPhone-width viewport -- confirming the diagnostic captures what's needed, not confirming real-world wrapping behavior, which only Lynn's own device can settle. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1739 - FIX: v0.1738's toolbar-overflow fix was intermittent -- sometimes correctly moving copy/cut/paste to the More menu, sometimes not, even for the exact same note, per Lynn's report. Per Lynn's own diagnosis and explicit guidance (not a timing/race-condition theory, which she specifically asked to rule out first): v0.1738 also re-ran the full check on every window resize event, but phones and tablets don't get resized by the user, and iOS is known to fire resize for things that aren't a real width change at all -- the address bar showing/hiding, the keyboard appearing. Re-running "reset to visible, then measure, then possibly hide again" on every one of those spurious events is very likely the actual cause of the repeated back-and-forth flipping Lynn recalled from earlier attempts at this same problem -- not a race condition, just this function being triggered far more often than the real toolbar width could ever change. Rewritten per her exact guidance: the resize listener is removed entirely; the function now runs only from genuine recalculation moments (opening/re-entering a note, a settings change like button size) -- the same three call sites already in place, none of them resize-driven. Within one call, it does at most ONE corrective pass: measure, and only if wrapped, move copy/cut/paste to the menu and measure exactly once more to confirm the wrap actually resolved -- never loops, never re-triggers itself. Verified directly, including the specific case Lynn was concerned about: a scenario engineered so the wrap can never resolve confirms the function still returns immediately rather than looping or hanging; a second, independent call (simulating reopening the note) is confirmed to be a fresh, correct evaluation rather than compounding on the previous one. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1738 - FIX: the upper toolbar's More button wrapped to a second line at Medium icon size instead of correctly moving cut/copy/paste into the More menu first, per Lynn's report -- reproducible specifically when the Speak button isn't set to icon-only, working correctly at Large. A genuinely pre-existing bug (confirmed by reading the code, not something the recent sizing work introduced): the check was `settings.uiSize === 'large' || 'xlarge'` -- a hardcoded GUESS based on the size setting's name, never an actual measurement of whether the toolbar's real content genuinely overflows. At Medium this guess was always false, so cut/copy/paste never moved to the menu no matter how wide the toolbar's actual content got -- exactly why it broke specifically when Speak's label added real width the old check had no way to know about, while working at Large only because the hardcoded guess happened to already be true there, for an unrelated reason. Replaced with a genuine measurement: does the More button actually sit on a different line than the first button in the row (comparing their real offsetTop, not a proxy setting)? Resets cut/copy/paste to their natural, fully-visible state before every measurement, so a stale "already moved" state from an earlier, narrower measurement can't understate the toolbar's current true width and prevent items correctly returning to the main bar once there's room again. Also added a resize listener, since a real measurement (unlike the old static guess) needs to be re-checked whenever the available width actually changes. Verified directly across all three real states: fits on one line (nothing moves), wraps (cut/copy/paste correctly move to the overflow menu, exactly the reported bug), and widening back out (items correctly return rather than staying stuck in the menu). Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1737 - FIX: note editor toolbar icons (both bars) stayed at their old touch size in hover mode, per Lynn's precise report -- padding visibly reduced, but the drawn icon itself unchanged, while the Home Screen pill and list icons correctly shrank. Root cause: applyToolbarIconSizes() injects a dynamic, !important-tagged style element that sets these SVGs' width/height directly in JS pixels -- a deliberate, pre-existing mechanism (documented in its own comment) working around CSS var() being unreliable in SVG width/height attributes in some browsers. Being a completely separate, JS-only computation from the CSS --icon-size variable, it never had any awareness of the new hover-mode override from the last build at all, and kept using its own hardcoded, touch-only lookup table regardless of input device -- exactly matching Lynn's observation, since the pill and list icons use var(--icon-size) directly with no JS involved, while these specific toolbars route through this separate path. Fixed by giving this lookup its own hover-aware touch/mouse tables, using the EXACT same numeric values already defined in the CSS override, so both mechanisms now agree. Verified directly: touch mode still produces the original 28px (medium); mouse mode now correctly produces 20px, matching the CSS side exactly, with the button's own minimum click-target floor (also computed in this same function) correctly shrinking in proportion. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1736 - NEW: Stage 1 of device-aware sizing, per Lynn's discussion -- the core mechanism, plus one proof-of-concept spot (.icon-btn's own internal padding, Lynn's top priority). Icon size and button padding were calibrated as fixed pixel values tuned for touch targets -- correct for phones, but noticeably oversized once a mouse is doing the clicking, since mouse precision and desktop conventions call for tighter controls, not bigger ones. Added a full set of @media(hover: hover) overrides for the existing --icon-size/--btn-pad/--btn-icon/--ui-scale variables (already used 98 times throughout the CSS for icon sizing, just never previously varied by input type), separately calibrated per size tier at roughly 70-75% of each touch dimension -- a reasonable starting point grounded in common touch-vs-mouse target-size guidance, explicitly NOT visually tuned by eye the way the original four touch tiers were, since nothing in this environment can render and look at it; expect these specific numbers to want adjustment once actually seen. Font size deliberately left unchanged in every tier -- legibility wasn't part of what read as oversized. Also added two new, dedicated variables (--icon-btn-pad, --icon-btn-min) specifically for .icon-btn's own internal padding and minimum click-target size, defaulting to its exact previous hardcoded values (5px, 36px) so touch behavior is completely unchanged, with a separate, smaller hover-mode override (3px, 28px) -- proof of concept for the "padding within a button" priority Lynn flagged specifically. Confirmed structurally (jsdom cannot fully resolve calc()/var() chains into rendered pixel values, a genuine limitation of this test environment, not something worked around): the full four-tier hover override block is correctly present and cascades after the touch tiers; icon-btn correctly references the new variables in place of the old hardcoded ones; :root's default (touch) values for the new variables exactly match what was there before. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1735 - NEW: "Floating Pill Header" is now a genuine on/off setting, per Lynn's request, rather than the pill redesign being the only option. Defaults to on, keeping the just-shipped look as the default rather than silently reverting existing users to the old bar on upgrade. Off restores the classic single header bar -- specifically the version from right before the pill redesign (v0.1730/1731: position:fixed, not sticky), deliberately not the original, older sticky version with its own since-fixed bugs. Implemented with a shared wrapper around the logo and pill: display:contents by default (invisible to the box model, so the two children behave exactly as independent position:fixed islands, unchanged from current behavior), becoming a real, single, full-width flex bar when Classic mode is selected, with both children dropping their independent positioning and pill-specific background/border/shadow to become ordinary flex items sharing the bar's own background instead. Zoom compensation branches accordingly: Classic mode reuses the exact same width-override size compensation the note editor's own header already uses (since it's now the same kind of element); Pill mode keeps the current independent-islands approach. Per Lynn's explicit instruction, the logo is now ALWAYS hidden while Pill mode is on, regardless of the separate "Hide Remember Logo" setting's own value -- "the logo looks silly in this mode" -- while Classic mode continues to respect that setting normally. Verified directly: default state correctly has the setting on with the logo hidden; toggling to Classic mode correctly restores the single bar (confirmed fixed positioning, flex display, and pill styling stripped from both children), respects the separate hide-logo setting again, and its own zoom compensation and breadcrumb/search/select-bar clearing logic all work correctly measuring the single bar instead of the two independent pieces; toggling back to Pill mode correctly re-hides the logo. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1734 - TWO fixes, per Lynn. (1) The Select Notes bar landed hidden behind the header pill unless the search bar was also open, or when navigating within a folder -- the exact same underlying bug class as the folder breadcrumb and search bar from two builds ago (an ordinary, in-flow element that was never given any header-clearing margin at all), just never previously recognized as also affecting this specific element. Confirmed via direct DOM inspection (not assumed) that these are all direct children of the same scrollable #home-screen container, in this fixed order: search bar, select bar, breadcrumb, then the list. Rewrote the clearing logic as an explicit priority chain rather than a two-way branch, since the old logic never knew the select bar existed at all, and would have picked the wrong element (breadcrumb instead of select bar) in the real combination of being in select mode while also inside a folder. (2) LONG-STANDING BUG, per Lynn's own correct read that this predates any recent header work: selecting multiple notes (or Select All) and trying to move them to Top level never offered that option in the Move sheet, even though moving items one at a time worked fine. Root cause: the multi-select Move button's call to openFolderPicker() was missing its 4th argument (startId) entirely, unlike the single-note and single-folder move calls right above it in the same file, which both pass it correctly. Omitted, it silently defaulted to null (top level) regardless of where the selected items actually lived -- so the picker's own "are we already at the starting location" check went true the instant it reached top level, hiding the Move button exactly when it was needed. Fixed by passing currentFolderId, matching the exact pattern the working single-item paths already use (all selected items necessarily live in the currently-viewed folder, since select mode only operates within one folder view at a time). Verified directly end to end: selecting multiple items inside a real folder, opening the move picker, and navigating up to top level within it now correctly shows an enabled "Move to Top level" button, where before it would have stayed hidden. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1733 - FIX: the logo was fully transparent floating text, per Lynn's request it now matches the button pill -- same background, border, border-radius, and shadow, reading as a matched pair rather than one solid pill and one bare label. Adjusted its top offset from 14px to 12px to match the pill's own, since both now have similar padding/border structure. Verified directly: background color, border-radius, and shadow all confirmed matching between the two elements; top offsets confirmed identical. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1732 - NEW: Home Screen header redesign, per Lynn -- first piece of the floating-pill experiment. Replaced the single solid header bar with two independently-floating pieces: the logo top-left, and a new pill-shaped container (rounded, its own background/border/shadow) holding the 4 buttons (sort, help, more, settings) top-right. Both are position:fixed on their own now, not sharing a layout container the way the old single bar did -- genuinely separate floating islands rather than sections of one continuous strip. The old <header class="home-header"> wrapper is removed entirely. Zoom compensation updated to match: both pieces are now small, anchored islands (the same kind of element as the note editor's own FABs), so they use position compensation instead of the old width-override size compensation a full-width bar needed; the notes list's own top-clearing padding (and the folder breadcrumb / search bar clearing logic from the last two builds) now uses whichever of the two pieces extends further down, since logo text and a row of icon buttons can have genuinely different natural heights. The "Hide Remember Logo" setting also improved as a side effect: the logo used to need visibility:hidden specifically because it acted as a flex spacer positioning the buttons; now that it's independently positioned, there's nothing to preserve space for anymore, so it correctly uses display:none instead. One known gap, flagged rather than solved here: the old "large font wraps buttons to a second line" accommodation depended entirely on the old shared-container layout and doesn't map onto two independent pieces -- removed for now; revisit only if large-font users actually report the logo and pill visually overlapping. Verified directly: both pieces exist with correct fixed positioning and pill styling; Hide Remember Logo confirmed using display:none; zoom position compensation confirmed working on both; the list's padding math confirmed using the taller of the two, not a fixed assumption about which one that would be. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1731 - FIX: navigating into a folder made the back-chevron/"Back"/folder-name breadcrumb row missing or invisible, per Lynn's report -- a direct casualty of v0.1730's header switching to position:fixed. Root cause: the breadcrumb is an ordinary, in-flow sibling inserted directly before #notes-list, which used to rely on the header (when position:sticky) still reserving its own space in normal document flow. With the header now fixed and removed from flow entirely, the breadcrumb rendered right at the very top of the page, landing directly underneath the header's own opaque background. The Home Screen's search bar is the exact same kind of element -- also ordinary, in-flow, also previously relying on the same sticky behavior -- so it was fixed here too rather than waiting for a second report of the identical underlying problem the moment it's opened. pinHomeScreenElements() now checks the known, fixed DOM order after the header (search bar, then breadcrumb, then the list) and gives whichever of these actually renders FIRST a top margin that clears the header directly, while the list's own padding only ever needs to handle its own small, original gap beneath whatever now precedes it. Verified directly across all four real combinations: root level (list gets the full header-clearing padding, unchanged from v0.1730), inside a folder (breadcrumb correctly clears the header, list drops to its small original gap), search open (search bar clears the header the same way), and the edge case of both search open and inside a folder at once (search bar -- first in DOM order -- correctly gets the margin, breadcrumb correctly gets reset rather than double-clearing). Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1730 - NEW: switched the Home Screen header from position:sticky to position:fixed, per Lynn's request, after she found the real, more precise symptom underneath all of this -- at extreme zoom-out, the topmost notes in the list ended up permanently hidden underneath the header with no way to scroll far enough to reveal them (something the note editor's own header never exhibited). Root cause, confirmed by checking the actual CSS directly: .notes-list had only a static 16px top padding, with no dynamic reservation for the header's actual height at all -- unlike the note editor, which recalculates this every single time via pinToolbars(). Since you can't scroll above scrollTop:0, any notes already past that point when the header covers more space than the static padding accounts for are trapped there permanently, not just temporarily obscured. Fixed by switching to position:fixed, which removes the whole "is it currently stuck" question this header never really needed anyway (it's the very first thing on the page, so it was effectively always stuck from the moment the page loaded), and now dynamically reserves exactly the right amount of top padding on the notes list for the header's actual, current rendered height -- the same proven approach the note editor's own content area already uses. Critically, this padding reservation now runs unconditionally, for every user, called directly from renderNotesList() itself rather than only from the mouse-gated zoom-compensation function -- the header is now position:fixed for everyone, not just mouse users, so relying on the mouse-only compensation path alone to set it would have left touch/mobile users with the exact same problem from the very first render. Also restored the header's box-shadow, confirmed uninvolved in the earlier growing-band problem by Lynn's own direct test. Verified directly: the header is confirmed fixed at left:0/right:0; the notes list's padding is correctly set even with no mouse present at all (the critical gap this build closes); the existing mouse-zoom compensation and FAB positioning both continue working correctly on top of this. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1729 - REVERT + experiment, per Lynn's explicit request: both v0.1727 (scale-only, no width override) and v0.1728 (added height-locking on top of that) are backed out entirely -- neither improved the Home Screen header's growing-band problem, and v0.1728 was reported as bad too. Back to exactly v0.1726's approach: applyZoomSizeCompensation() (the width-override version, same formula the note editor's own header uses), which Lynn confirmed was an acceptable starting point despite the original issue. applyZoomScaleOnlyCompensation() and its baseline-height variable are removed entirely rather than just left unused. Also, per Lynn's request, temporarily commented out the Home Screen header's box-shadow, to test whether that effect is involved in whatever's actually happening -- easy to restore by uncommenting the single line once this experiment has an answer. Verified directly: the header's compensation now behaves identically to v0.1726 (scale and width both set, height untouched); the removed function no longer exists at all; the shadow is confirmed absent via computed style. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1728 - FIX: v0.1727 made the growing-band problem worse, per Lynn's report -- her precise description ("scaling applied to the header inner elements but its surrounding area still grows") pointed straight at the real mechanism: transform:scale() only affects how an element is PAINTED, never its actual LAYOUT size. v0.1727 correctly stopped fighting the header's width, but never addressed its actual, underlying HEIGHT, which kept ballooning untouched by the scale transform -- exactly matching what she saw, content that looked right sitting inside a box that was still the wrong size, still covering the list beneath it and still blocking mouse scroll. Fixed by explicitly locking the header's real layout height too, using the same normalized-baseline math already proven for the header's width on the note editor's own version: a baseline height is captured once, lazily, on first use, and corrected back to what it would measure at true 100% zoom regardless of what zoom level happens to be active the first time it runs -- then every subsequent call sets the pre-scale layout height to baseline*zoomFactor, so after the compensating scale is applied, the rendered result always lands back on that same stable baseline, in either zoom direction. Deliberately captured ONCE and reused, rather than re-measured on every call -- re-measuring on every call is the exact mistake that caused v0.1718's runaway compounding-growth regression (each call measuring the previous call's already-scaled result instead of the element's true natural size), and this build specifically re-ran that same multi-call stress test to confirm it doesn't recur here: four successive calls at the same zoom level now produce byte-for-byte identical results, not compounding growth. Also verified the rendered height stays visually stable at its true baseline value across three different zoom levels in both directions. This height-locking is isolated to the new Home Screen function only -- the note editor's own, already-working header/toolbar compensation is completely untouched. Also directly addressed Lynn's question of why this header is sticky rather than fixed: it's the very first element on the page with nothing above it, so in practice it's continuously in its "stuck" state from the moment the page loads -- sticky's whole distinguishing behavior (only activating past a scroll threshold) barely applies here. Switching it to fixed outright would simplify this compensation logic further, but is a bigger structural change deliberately left for its own separate discussion rather than bundled into this fix. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1727 - FIX: a growing horizontal band under the Home Screen's header, hiding list text and blocking mouse scroll over that area, per Lynn's report and screenshot. Root cause traced by reasoning through what's actually different about this header versus the note editor's own: her screenshot showed the search bar (display:none when not open, so it couldn't be the visible thing growing) and confirmed via inspector that a container near it was ballooning -- but the real culprit was .home-header itself growing in HEIGHT, something never explicitly addressed before now. v0.1726's fix reused applyZoomSizeCompensation() wholesale, including its width-override formula -- but that formula was specifically built for the note editor's header/toolbar, which are position:fixed with left:0;right:0 (a genuine 100%-of-viewport rule that needs correcting under zoom). The Home Screen's header is position:sticky, and its width comes from ordinary block-level layout instead, already correctly tracking its parent with no help needed -- forcing the same artificial width formula onto it anyway meant its flex-wrapping row of buttons could wrap differently at that artificial width than they actually render at, inflating the header's own natural height. Since it's sticky (overlaying content once stuck, not pushing content down the way normal flow does), a taller header simply covers more of the list beneath it and intercepts mouse wheel events meant for that list -- matching the screenshot and the scroll-blocking symptom precisely. Fixed with a new, simpler scale-only compensation variant that never touches width at all, used for this header specifically; the note editor's own header/toolbar are completely unaffected, still using the original width-override version they actually need. Verified directly: the Home Screen header's width is now correctly left untouched while still compensating for scale; the note editor's header still gets the full width-override treatment, unchanged. Flagged honestly: the underlying wrapping-at-the-wrong-width theory is well-reasoned from the actual CSS, not something that can be fully confirmed without real layout, which nothing in this testing environment can compute -- worth specifically re-checking that horizontal band is fully gone. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1726 - NEW: started the Home Screen's own Ctrl+wheel zoom compensation, per Lynn -- first two pieces, the sticky header and the "+" FAB button (#new-note-btn, class .fab), extending the same mouse-only approach already proven in the note editor. The header required one genuinely new piece of care nothing covered so far needed: it's position:sticky, not fixed, and a sticky element only behaves like (and therefore only balloons like) a fixed one while it's actually STUCK at its own scroll threshold (top:0 here) -- while still in normal, unstuck document flow, it's ordinary in-flow content that already scales correctly with the rest of the page, and forcing the same compensation onto it in that state would incorrectly resize perfectly normal content. Detected via getBoundingClientRect().top: at (or very near) 0 means currently stuck and gets full size compensation, same as the note editor's own header; anything meaningfully greater means still in normal flow, compensation skipped entirely and any stale compensation from a moment earlier cleanly reset. Reacts to both resize (zoom changes) and scroll (since scrolling itself, independent of any zoom, is what actually crosses the stuck/unstuck threshold) -- the scroll listener uses the capture phase specifically, since scroll events don't bubble on their own and the Home Screen's own scrollable region isn't the window itself. Entire function gated on the Home Screen actually being the active screen (checked via the same note-screen-active body class the rest of the app already uses), so none of this runs at all while a note is open. Verified directly: currently-stuck state correctly applies the same width/scale formula already proven for the note editor's own header; not-stuck state correctly leaves the header completely untouched, including cleanly resetting any compensation left over from a moment ago; the FAB's position scales in the correct direction; and the whole function correctly does nothing at all while the note editor is the active screen. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1725 - TWO fixes, per Lynn. (1) Extended Disable Pinch to Zoom to also cover the Home Screen, Help dialog, and Settings dialog -- it previously only reached the note editor and Toolbar Help panel. None of these three had any touch-action set before at all (default auto, which allows pinch-zoom natively), so this is a genuinely new restriction on each rather than an override of an existing allowance the way the earlier two were. (2) FIX: expanding the Note Behavior settings section also expanded Debugging below it, with Debugging's own chevron then stuck showing the wrong (collapsed) state despite its content being visible -- a bug class Lynn correctly recognized as familiar even without remembering the exact prior sections involved, and one the project's own instructions already document by name ("Settings HTML div nesting") with a ready-made verification script, specifically because it has recurred multiple times before. Ran that exact script rather than guessing: confirmed settings-section-debugging sitting at DOM depth 3 instead of the required 2, meaning it was nested one level too deep inside Note Behavior's own body. Traced the precise cause by tracking the running open/close tag depth through Note Behavior's own content line by line: the "Speak button: icon only" row was missing its own closing </div>, silently pushing every row after it (including the entirety of Debugging) one level deeper for the rest of the section. Added the missing tag; re-ran the verification script and confirmed every section, including Debugging, now correctly sits at depth 2. Verified directly end to end: clicking Note Behavior's header now opens only Note Behavior, Debugging stays closed, and Debugging is confirmed no longer a DOM descendant of Note Behavior at all; the three newly-covered pinch-zoom areas all correctly pick up the disabled override. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1724 - FOUR pieces, per Lynn. (1) NEW: "Reset Zoom Size" button at the bottom of Settings -- clears the persisted zoom baseline and immediately re-captures the CURRENT devicePixelRatio as the new "100%" reference, with pinToolbars() re-run right away so an open note reflects it immediately rather than needing a reload. Mainly useful for the one-time case where the very first-ever load of the app happened to already be at a non-100% zoom for unrelated reasons. (2) NEW App Theme setting: "Disable Pinch to Zoom" -- turns off pinch-zoom in the note editor and Toolbar Help panel for anyone who prefers it off. Implemented as a body-class CSS override dropping pinch-zoom from the touch-action whitelist on all four elements that grant it (#note-content-area, #note-editor, .note-viewer, #toolbar-help-body) -- the last of these had its touch-action set inline, which no stylesheet rule can override regardless of specificity, so it was moved into a proper CSS rule first so the override could actually reach it. (3) NEW App Theme setting: "Hide Remember Logo" -- hides the "Remember" wordmark on the Home Screen. Uses visibility:hidden rather than display:none, since .home-title has flex:1 and acts as a spacer positioning the header's other buttons; hiding it that way keeps the header's layout completely unchanged rather than risking the buttons shifting once the spacer's space collapses. (4) FIX: the Settings dialog's floating Save/Cancel/Scroll control landing outside the dialog on wide desktop windows -- it's a true HTML sibling of the dialog (moved there in v0.1713 to sidestep an iOS WebKit bug), so its right offset was measured from the full browser viewport rather than the dialog's own edge, which only matched on phones where the dialog already spans nearly the full width. Fixed by computing its position from the dialog's own actual rendered bounding rect instead of a static value, reacting to the dialog opening (a MutationObserver on its own open/close class, the same pattern the scratch-pad panel already uses) and to the window being resized while it's still open. All four pieces verified directly: the reset button correctly overwrites both the in-memory baseline and persisted storage; both new toggles correctly set their setting, apply their body class, and the resulting computed touch-action/visibility CSS is confirmed correct; the FAB's position was verified against two different simulated window widths, both matching the expected math exactly. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1723 - FIX: folder entries on the Home Screen had no mouse-hover highlight, per Lynn's report -- note entries have always had one. Found a leftover comment ("folder-item hover already wrapped") sitting exactly where the rule should have been, suggesting this was intended at some point but never actually added. Added the same hover/active pair note-item already has -- background highlight on hover, slight press-down scale on active -- wrapped in the same (hover: hover) media query used throughout the app to mean a real mouse/trackpad is attached. Folder entries don't use the customizable "Notes list entry background" setting notes do, so only the simple version was needed, not a separate light/dark-mode variant. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1722 - TWO fixes, per Lynn. (1) Zoom compensation for the quiet-mode/view-only More menu FAB (#quiet-more-fab) -- position:fixed, right/top anchored, ballooning under mouse zoom the same way the note FAB, collapse tab, and scratch-pad tab did before their own fixes. Its real CSS also adds env(safe-area-inset-right) to the right offset for notch clearance on mobile, but since this compensation is gated to (hover: hover) -- desktop/mouse devices only -- that safe-area component is effectively 0 on every device this code actually runs on, so the static 12px/6px alone is the correct desired position here. (2) Toolbar Help panel (the one with a sentence or two about each note editor button) now supports pinch-to-zoom -- its scrollable body had touch-action:pan-y alone, which allows vertical scrolling but explicitly excludes pinch-zoom by spec. This is the identical root cause already fixed once for the note editor itself, all the way back in v0.1498 (touch-action pan-y -> pan-y pinch-zoom) -- just never applied to this panel, added separately later. Verified directly: the FAB's right/top offsets scale in the correct direction under zoom, its size stays steady, and it cleanly falls back to untouched CSS with no mouse present, matching every other fixed element already covered; the Toolbar Help panel's touch-action is confirmed set to the correct value in the DOM. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1721 - FIX: the note editor's toolbar rendering huge or tiny after closing and reopening the tab, when the browser remembers a non-100% zoom level for the app's origin -- the big feature this whole session's zoom-compensation work has been building toward, now landed. Diagnosed carefully in the isolated test page first, with Lynn's own real-device data: devicePixelRatio was confirmed completely stable from the earliest possible reading through 1.5 seconds later on a reopened tab, ruling out any settling/timing issue.
