The real problem was conceptual, not timing-related: every fresh page load recaptured "whatever devicePixelRatio is present right now" as the neutral baseline -- correct for a genuinely first-ever load, but silently wrong the moment a reopened tab already carries a browser-remembered zoom level, since that already-zoomed state would get treated as the new "normal," with no compensation ever applying to correct for it. Fixed by persisting the baseline across sessions (localStorage, using the app's own storeKey() namespacing so dev/stable deployments sharing an origin don't collide) instead of recapturing it every load -- captured once, the very first time Remember is ever used in this browser, then reused on every subsequent visit regardless of what zoom level the browser happens to restore. This same approach was validated end-to-end in the isolated test page first, including Lynn's own real zoom/close/reopen cycle at her actual desktop -- deploying it (rather than opening the file directly from disk, which has known localStorage reliability issues in some browsers) confirmed it working correctly. Brought over and verified directly with the exact real numbers from that test (baseline 1.626, restored zoom reading 2.845, both actually recorded on Lynn's own machine): correctly computes a zoomFactor of 1.75, precisely matching the real 175% zoom she'd set, rather than the old behavior which would have seen no change at all. Also verified a genuinely fresh, first-ever load still correctly captures and persists the current devicePixelRatio as the new baseline, and that the header/toolbar/FAB compensation built in earlier builds continues working correctly on top of this change. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1720 - FIX: toolbar tooltips landing under the cursor, per Lynn's short-term to-do item -- requested as a quick, morale-boosting win in the middle of the harder zoom-compensation debugging cycle. Even after v0.1686's earlier loosened threshold for when to give up on positioning above, a regular browser tab (address bar etc. eating vertical space, unlike the PWA) still often doesn't leave enough room above toolbar buttons sitting near the top -- the fallback used to be flipping BELOW the button, which lands the tooltip right where the cursor already is, since the cursor is still resting on the button being hovered. Per Lynn's explicit request, the fallback now prefers flipping sideways instead -- whichever of left/right has more room, vertically centered on the button -- since a horizontal offset moves the tooltip clear of the cursor entirely rather than just relocating it to a spot the cursor still occupies. Only falls back to below as a genuine last resort, when there's not enough room on either side either (acknowledged directly as an accepted limitation near the far left/right screen edges). Verified directly with two scenarios: a button near the top with room to at least one side correctly flips sideways and stays vertically centered on the button rather than dropping below it; a button in a narrow window with no usable room anywhere else correctly still falls back to below rather than rendering off-screen. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1719 - REVERT, per Lynn's urgent report: the header/toolbar were growing huge again, back to the original bug. v0.1718's height-locking addition (attempting to fix the collapse tab detaching) had a genuine compounding bug, not just an imperfect fix. pinToolbars() runs repeatedly during an active zoom gesture -- multiple event listeners (window resize, visualViewport resize/scroll) all call it -- and the height fix measured getBoundingClientRect().height BEFORE resetting anything on the element. Every call after the first was measuring the PREVIOUS call's already-scaled, already-rendered height, not the element's true natural size -- then locking THAT inflated number in and scaling again on top of it. Each successive call during a zoom gesture compounded on the last, producing exactly the runaway growth reported. Reverted applyZoomSizeCompensation() entirely back to width-only compensation, the version already confirmed working correctly across multiple rounds of real testing. Everything else from v0.1718 (the FAB's transition-suppression fix, zoom-factor rounding) is untouched and kept, since neither was implicated in this specific bug -- confirmed directly. The collapse tab detaching problem v0.1718 was trying to solve is NOT fixed by this revert; it still needs its own correctly-built attempt, one that doesn't fall into this same repeated-measurement trap. Verified directly: four successive pinToolbars() calls at the same zoom level now produce identical, stable results (no compounding), matching what actually happens during a real zoom gesture; the FAB and the no-mouse fallback are both unaffected. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1718 - TWO fixes, per Lynn's continued mouse-zoom testing. (1) FIX: the note editor's "+" FAB looked bouncy/flashing while zooming with a mouse. Two contributing causes addressed together: the FAB already had its own CSS transition (0.15s, for hover/press feedback) covering `transform`, but not `right`/`bottom` -- every compensation update changed both together, so position jumped instantly while size animated smoothly over 0.15s, visibly desyncing on every rapid zoom step. Now suppresses any existing transition for the instant a compensation update applies, then restores it immediately after, so hover/press feedback is completely unaffected the rest of the time. Also rounds the computed zoom factor to 3 decimal places -- three separate listeners (window resize, visualViewport resize, visualViewport scroll) all call pinToolbars(), and a single zoom step can trigger more than one in quick succession while devicePixelRatio may still be settling; rounding collapses near-identical readings from overlapping event fires into the same applied value instead of two visibly different ones. (2) FIX (logically sound, not yet visually confirmed in a real browser): the lower-toolbar collapse tab detaching from the header as its vertical position drifted -- its position is derived each update from the header's own measured bottom edge, which depends on the header's height, which is itself content-driven (its buttons wrap via flex-wrap), not a fixed value. The width override central to the whole size-compensation fix is deliberately DIFFERENT from the header's true rendered width -- meaning the header's content could plausibly wrap differently at that temporary width than it actually displays, making its measured height (and therefore the collapse tab's derived position) unstable. Fixed by measuring the header's natural height BEFORE applying the width override -- capturing its true, correctly-wrapped height first -- then locking it explicitly the same way width already is, so it can no longer drift for this reason regardless of what the temporary width override does internally. Verified directly: the FAB's transition is suppressed then correctly restored to its original CSS value (not left permanently disabled); rapid, near-identical zoom readings now collapse to the same applied scale; the no-mouse fallback correctly resets height alongside width and transform. Flagged honestly: jsdom cannot compute real layout or real element heights, so the height fix's logic was verified, not its actual on-screen effect -- that needs the next round of real-device testing to confirm. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1717 - FIX + two more elements covered, per Lynn's real-device testing round across Windows, iOS, and Android. (1) FIX: pinch-zoom in the note editor had become jumpy specifically on iOS (Safari and the PWA), less severely on Android. Root cause: window.devicePixelRatio -- the signal v0.1716's new compensation watches -- very likely also changes during a touch pinch gesture, not just desktop Ctrl+wheel zoom, meaning the new logic was firing DURING pinch-zoom too and fighting the existing, already-proven visualViewport.scale-based pinch guard from v0.1497, both repositioning/rescaling the same elements on every frame of the same gesture. Fixed exactly as Lynn proposed: all of v0.1716's new compensation is now gated behind (hover: hover) -- the SAME media feature this app already uses everywhere else (scratch pad mouse support, its edge tab) to mean "a real mouse or trackpad is attached" -- so none of it runs at all on touch-primary devices, leaving pinch-zoom exactly as it worked before this whole effort began. (2) Two more position:fixed elements Lynn found ballooning that v0.1716 missed: the lower-toolbar collapse handle and the mouse-only scratch-pad edge tab. The collapse handle's position is already correctly derived each update from the header's own (already compensated) bottom edge, so it only needed its SIZE held steady, not a separate position recalculation -- added directly alongside where that top offset is already computed. The scratch-pad tab is vertically CENTERED (top:50% + translateY(-50%)) rather than anchored to one edge, which exposed a real gap in applyZoomPositionCompensation()'s original design: it inferred transform-origin from which anchors were present, which has no sensible answer for a centered element. Fixed properly rather than special-cased around: transformOrigin is now an explicit parameter every caller provides, not something inferred. Verified directly: with no mouse present, all five elements (header, toolbar, FAB, collapse tab, scratch tab) cleanly fall back to their plain, uncompensated state; with a mouse present, all five compensate correctly together, including the collapse tab and scratch tab's own existing centering transforms being correctly preserved alongside the new compensating scale rather than overwritten by it. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1716 - NEW: brought the Ctrl+wheel zoom compensation into the real app for the note editor's fixed elements (top header bar, bottom toolbar bar, the "+" FAB), after several rounds of validating the underlying math in a completely isolated test page first, with Lynn doing real testing in Chrome and Firefox at every step -- including catching and correcting my own misunderstanding of the position-compensation direction along the way. Two shared, reusable functions, applyZoomSizeCompensation() and applyZoomPositionCompensation(), rather than copy-pasting the formula three times: SIZE compensation (header/toolbar, both spanning the full viewport width) explicitly recomputes the pre-scale layout width every update so the post-scale rendered result always exactly matches the true viewport width, in both zoom directions -- fixes transform:scale() otherwise making a 100%-wide element wider than the viewport on zoom-out, pushing edge content off-screen. POSITION compensation (the FAB, anchored via right/bottom rather than spanning full width) recomputes its layout offset as (desired position) / zoomFactor -- smaller at higher zoom, larger at lower zoom -- since the browser moves position:fixed offsets on its own zoom rendering, separately from and before any CSS transform we apply. Both driven off a devicePixelRatio baseline captured once at load, comparing only the CHANGE since then rather than the raw number, since Retina displays and OS display scaling both start above 1 with no zooming at all. No new event wiring needed: both hook into pinToolbars(), already called on every window resize event, which reliably fires for desktop browser zoom changes too -- confirmed directly, rather than adding a redundant polling loop. Verified directly in the real app: header and toolbar widths exactly match the live viewport width at baseline, 200% zoom, and the previously-broken 50% zoom-out case; the FAB's position scales in the correct, confirmed direction at both zoom levels; the existing height-stacking/padding logic that already lived in pinToolbars() is untouched by these additions. Two things deliberately NOT done in this build, flagged honestly rather than silently left incomplete: the home screen's own header uses position:sticky, not fixed, and Lynn has now confirmed directly (Chrome, Firefox, and a Chromebook) that it has the same problem once scrolled to its stuck position -- that needs its own follow-up, since detecting whether a sticky element is CURRENTLY stuck (vs. still in normal flow) is a distinct problem this build doesn't address yet. The Settings dialog's own floating Save/Cancel/Scroll control (also position:fixed) likewise doesn't have this compensation applied yet. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1715 - THREE new pieces, per Lynn. (1) Bold/Italic/Highlight/Strikethrough/Color (made dual-mode last build) are now hidden entirely when settings.noteEditingMode === 'typing' -- with Read & Revise completely disabled in that configuration, there's nowhere for the markdown these buttons insert to ever render, so showing them would just be clutter with no payoff. (2) Their "Lower Toolbar Buttons" placement dropdown now offers only "Neither" as a choice while that same setting is active -- any other choice would be permanently, silently false in that configuration, unlike genuinely R&R-only buttons (Move lines, Heading, etc), whose other placement choices remain true the moment the person switches back to R&R mode within the same session. Deliberately does not touch the user's own underlying stored placement preference -- switching noteEditingMode back to 'both' or 'rronly' restores their original choice automatically, verified directly. (3) NEW: HTML export, a fourth option alongside the existing JSON/Markdown/Plain text choices -- reuses the exact same rendering pipeline Read & Revise mode already uses (urlsToLinks), so no new markdown-conversion logic was written, and no third-party library anywhere in the process. Fonts are loaded live from Google Fonts (the same single combined @import the app itself already uses, reused verbatim rather than risking an incorrectly-narrowed one) -- needs an internet connection to render correctly, matching the simpler, internet-dependent version Lynn chose over a fully self-contained one. CSS is NOT copied wholesale (~260KB, almost all irrelevant to any single note) -- instead, every rule in the live stylesheet is checked against the actually-rendered note content, and only genuinely-used rules are kept, so simple notes produce correspondingly small files and richer ones scale up proportionally; verified directly against both a plain-text note and a richer one using headings/bold/italic/checkboxes/tags. Every CSS custom property currently in use gets resolved to its actual current value and frozen into the export (confirmed no raw var() references survive), so the file correctly reflects whatever theme/colors were active at export time without needing the app's own theme-switching system at all. Checkboxes -- drawn as custom, JS-driven SVGs in the live app, with no native equivalent -- are converted to real, genuinely tappable <input type="checkbox"> elements with their checked state carried over; CAUGHT AND FIXED before shipping: an early version set the .checked DOM PROPERTY, which does not reflect into the serialized HTML attribute at all, so every exported checkbox silently lost its checked state the moment the string was written out -- fixed by setting the actual attribute instead, caught by direct testing of the real output rather than assuming the property would be enough. As discussed with Lynn beforehand, any tapping in the exported file is purely visual/temporary -- a standalone file has no save mechanism, so nothing writes back to the original note. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1714 - NEW: Bold, Italic, Highlight, Strikethrough, and Color are now dual-mode, per Lynn -- previously R&R-only (whole-line formatting), now also available in typing mode, operating on the native textarea text selection instead (the same selectionStart/selectionEnd mechanism Indent/Dedent already use). R&R mode's own whole-line behavior is completely unchanged; the buttons simply branch on which mode is active. Bold/Highlight/Strikethrough share one function, since their markers (**, ==, ~~) never collide with each other; Italic gets its own version, since its single-character marker is a substring of Bold's, and every check has to confirm a * is genuinely standalone rather than one half of a **; Color gets its own, since its {color}...{/color} tags are asymmetric in length rather than a symmetric marker. Deliberately conservative throughout, per Lynn's own design: selecting text already wrapped in matching markers toggles them off; selecting text with no markers wraps it fresh; anything ambiguous (a marker on only one side, or an odd count of stray markers inside the selection) shows a toast and changes nothing rather than guessing and silently producing broken or nested markdown. No selection at all inserts an empty marker pair to type into, for Bold/Italic/Highlight/Strikethrough; Color requires an actual selection first, since there's no sensible empty color tag to insert. Color's popover now hides its Checkbox tab in typing mode -- checkbox coloring is a whole-line operation that only makes sense in R&R, exactly as Lynn described. Existing per-button "Lower Toolbar Buttons" hide/show placement choices continue to be respected in both modes -- someone who explicitly hides one of these five still sees it hidden everywhere, not just where it used to be R&R-only. Verified extensively and directly at every layer: the shared marker function (fresh wrap, toggle off via markers just outside the selection, toggle off via markers included IN the selection, no-selection insert, unmatched-marker-nearby warning, odd-internal-marker-count warning); Italic's bold-collision safety specifically (wrapping text already inside a bold span correctly produces italic-inside-bold rather than misreading part of the ** as a lone *, and toggling off an italic span sitting immediately next to a bold one doesn't disturb the bold); Color's tag toggle/re-color/remove/stray-brace-ambiguity paths; then the full click-through for all five buttons in typing mode; then a full regression pass confirming R&R mode's whole-line behavior for all five is completely unchanged. Toolbar Help's own descriptions for all five updated to describe both modes. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1713 - FOLLOW-UP FIX to v0.1712's floating Save/Cancel/Scroll control, per Lynn's immediate report -- switching to position:fixed there made it scroll up off-screen and bounce during scroll, worse than the sticky behavior it replaced. Investigated the actual DOM structure directly rather than guessing: the control was living nested deep inside the scrolling dialog, in fact inside one of its own collapsible sections (Note Behavior), several levels of nesting down -- not just a direct child of the dialog as assumed in the previous fix. This is a documented iOS Safari/WebKit quirk: a position:fixed element nested inside a scrolling ancestor that also has a transform (the dialog itself, for horizontal centering) doesn't reliably stay fixed on iOS -- it can scroll and bounce along with the container's own content instead of staying anchored in place, despite what the CSS spec says should happen. Fixed properly this time by moving the control to a true sibling of the dialog in the HTML, completely outside it and any collapsible section -- sidesteps the whole class of bug rather than trying to work around it in place. Its own visibility now follows the dialog's existing open/closed state via a plain CSS sibling selector, requiring no new JavaScript at all, since the dialog's own open/close code already toggles the class this relies on. z-index raised to stay above the dialog now that it's a sibling rather than a nested child (previously correct by ordinary DOM stacking, now needs to be explicit). Per Lynn's clarification, still reserves space at the bottom of the dialog's own content so it doesn't PERMANENTLY cover the last buttons when scrolled all the way down -- temporary overlap while actively scrolling past that point is fine and expected. Verified directly: correctly hidden while the dialog is closed, visible and properly fixed-positioned with the correct stacking order once open, and correctly hidden again on close. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1712 - FIX: the Settings dialog's floating Save/Cancel/Scroll control had become inconsistent about actually floating, per Lynn's report -- she'd narrowed it down herself to somewhere around v0.1699 by testing old versions directly, though diffing that exact transition found no changes to the control or its surrounding structure at all. Rather than keep chasing an exact regression point that may be tied to more subtle, content-height-dependent conditions than a single version diff can reveal, addressed the actual request directly: it was built with position:sticky, which only "sticks" while its own scrolling container is actually being scrolled, within that container's own content bounds -- inherently fragile to any change in surrounding content height or structure, which is exactly the kind of thing that degrades subtly over many builds rather than breaking cleanly at one. Per Lynn's explicit request -- always visible, no cleverness about hiding it near the top -- switched to position:fixed, which guarantees this unconditionally rather than depending on scroll state at all. Confirmed the dialog's own existing transform property (used for horizontal centering) correctly makes it the containing block for this fixed-position control, by a standard, well-established CSS rule -- so it stays anchored to the dialog's own bottom-right corner rather than jumping to the full browser viewport. Also added bottom padding scoped specifically to the Settings dialog (not the shared dialog class other dialogs also use), since a fixed-position element is removed from the normal page flow entirely -- without this, the last item in the scrollable content (Reset All Settings to Defaults) would end up hidden behind the control when scrolled all the way down. Verified all four resulting CSS properties directly. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1711 - TWO fixes, per Lynn. (1) FIXED: Double Space showed up in typing mode, where it can never actually work. Root cause: unlike Move lines (which it was built to mirror), it only ever had readiness STYLING logic (muting its color when not usable), never an explicit display toggle -- so it stayed visible, just looking disabled, instead of not appearing at all. Also found and fixed the same missing-registration bug show-whitespace-btn had before: it was never added to MODE_GATED_BUTTON_IDS either, which would have let the toolbar-rebuild logic force it back visible regardless of the new display fix. Verified directly: hidden in typing mode, visible in R&R mode. (2) FIXED: a black bar (in reality, an empty dark-background preview container with nothing visible inside it) still appeared when searching "lower toolbar buttons", even after the earlier leaking-content fix. Root cause, found by directly inspecting exactly what stayed visible rather than guessing from the screenshot: #dark-mode-preview reuses the .settings-row class purely for its own internal layout (two rows labeled "Background" and "Text Color"), and the earlier fix for the "italic" bug had exempted ANY container holding nested .settings-row elements from ever being hidden, regardless of whether those nested rows were themselves currently visible -- so when neither of this container's two rows matched the search, they correctly hid, but the wrapper itself (with its own dark background) stayed visible with nothing inside it. A second, related bug surfaced while fixing this and testing multiple searches in sequence: simply excluding a genuine rows-container (like #toolbar-visibility-rows) from the hiding pass whenever it DID have a visible match left its own display completely untouched in that case -- meaning a PREVIOUS search that correctly hid it (nothing matched that time) left it stuck hidden forever after, even once a LATER search had a real match inside it, since nothing ever explicitly set it back to visible. Redesigned so any container holding nested rows always has its own visibility explicitly set on every search call, based on whether it currently has at least one visible row inside -- never simply left alone to potentially carry stale state from an earlier search. Verified directly: the empty preview container correctly hides now; a rows-container with a genuine match stays visible; and the exact sequential-search scenario that surfaced the second bug (searching "italic", then "line spacing", then "checkbox") now correctly finds "Toggle checkbox" every time. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1710 - FOUR fixes, per Lynn -- three of them traced back to one root cause. (1) ROOT CAUSE: Double Space and Show Spaces and Tabs were never added to DEFAULT_TOOLBAR_ORDER at all when they were first built. This single omission explained three separate symptoms at once: the Toolbar Help cheat sheet only ever lists buttons present in the user's effective toolbar order, so they never appeared there regardless of being in its own description list; the Settings "Lower Toolbar Buttons" section is built from that same effective order, so they never appeared there either; and their actual position in the real toolbar was essentially undefined, since nothing told the app where they belonged. Confirmed directly that an existing reconciliation mechanism (insertMissingIdsAtDefaultPosition) already handles inserting a new button into a user's customized saved order correctly -- it was never the problem; it depends entirely on the button existing in DEFAULT_TOOLBAR_ORDER in the first place, which these two never did. Fixed by adding both, positioned right after scroll-bottom-btn per Lynn's explicit request -- verified this one change alone resolves all three symptoms: both now appear in the Toolbar Help panel, both now appear in the Settings reorder/hide list, and both land in the correct toolbar position. (2) FIXED: searching Settings for "Lower Toolbar Buttons" (its own subsection title, verbatim) found nothing. Same structural gap as the earlier "italic" search bug, but hitting the matching logic instead of the hiding logic this time: rows built by buildToolbarVisibilityRows() are nested inside a wrapper div, so a plain previousElementSibling walk from any individual row could never reach the title, which is a sibling of the wrapper, not of the rows themselves. Replaced the inline sibling-only walk (used in two places) with a shared helper that also walks up through ancestor containers when a direct sibling search comes up empty. Verified directly: searching "lower toolbar buttons" now correctly shows both a matching row inside the section and the section's own title. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1709 - TWO fixes, per Lynn. (1) FIXED: Double Space and Show Spaces and Tabs had been placed INSIDE the crossout-group wrapper div -- visually boxed together with Strikethrough and Clear All Strikethroughs as if they were that button's paired partner, which they're not. Root cause: both were inserted right after clear-strikethroughs-btn without first closing the wrapper div it lives in. Moved both outside the box as separate, standalone buttons; the box itself now contains only its true pair again. Verified directly: crossout-group's only children are crossout-mode-btn and clear-strikethroughs-btn, and neither new button's parent is the group anymore. (2) Updated the Toolbar Help cheat sheet (the ? button's reference list) -- audited it programmatically against the toolbar's own full button registry rather than checking by eye, which found two real gaps: select-all-btn (a genuine lower toolbar button, now documented) and note-fab (deliberately left out -- it's a floating action button, not a lower toolbar button, and its function is already covered by the existing "Quick add" entry, so adding it would have been redundant). Also updated Show Spaces and Tabs' own description, which still said "small gray marks" -- stale since a recent fix made those glyphs prominent, full-strength colored marks rather than muted gray ones. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1708 - FOUR fixes to "Show spaces and tabs", all per Lynn's follow-up bug reports. (1) FIXED: the button stayed visible in typing mode. Root cause: a THIRD list this button was never added to, MODE_GATED_BUTTON_IDS -- without it, the toolbar-rebuild logic unconditionally force-showed the button (since it wasn't hidden via the placement setting), overriding whatever the correct, mode-aware logic had just set. Diagnosed directly: confirmed the mode-aware function WAS running and correctly setting display:none each time, only to have it silently reset afterward -- traced to this exact list, whose entire job is "only ever force-hide, never force-show" for buttons whose actual visibility is decided elsewhere. (2) FIXED: an indented checkbox/hyphen item's leading whitespace never showed at all, regardless of tabs or spaces or the toggle -- a separate, deliberate rendering step strips ALL leading whitespace from these lines unconditionally (their visual indent comes from calculated CSS padding instead, not literal characters), so there was nothing left in the text for the glyph pass to ever find. Now captured before stripping and shown as glyphs, when the toggle is on, positioned right where the padding begins; toggle-off behavior is completely unchanged. (3) FIXED: a tab pasted at the start of a line WITH real text got shown as spaces, while the same tab on an otherwise-blank line, mid-line, or at the end of a line displayed correctly -- exactly Lynn's own precise observation. Root cause: a separate, pre-existing rendering step (for consistent visual alignment of wrapped paragraph lines) always expanded a LEADING tab into literal space characters before the glyph pass ever ran -- only leading whitespace on a plain paragraph went through it at all, which is exactly why nowhere else was affected. Now skips that expansion when the toggle is on, so the real character survives through to the glyph pass. (4) FIXED: the tab arrow sometimes overlapped the start of the following word. Root cause: a tab character with no surrounding pre-formatted context (e.g. one appearing mid-line, inside normal text flow) collapses to near-zero rendered width under default CSS whitespace handling, while the arrow still tried to render at its fixed size regardless, spilling past the character's own collapsed box into whatever came next. Gave the tab's own span an explicit white-space:pre and a reasonable tab-size, so it always reserves real, predictable width for the arrow no matter where in the line it appears -- the width Lynn was seeing is the tab's own true rendered width, now shown accurately rather than as an overflow bug. Verified all four directly: the button correctly hides/shows across typing and R&R mode; an indented checkbox item's tab is now visible as a glyph; a leading tab on a line with real text is now shown as a real tab rather than expanded spaces, with the toggle-off case confirmed completely unchanged in both fixes. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1707 - FOUR fixes/changes, per Lynn. (1) Note statistics now says "paragraphs" instead of "lines" -- non-technical people may not realize "line" means a whole text block up to a newline. (2) Show Spaces and Tabs glyphs made substantially more prominent -- full-strength color instead of muted gray, larger dots, full opacity -- since this is a quick on/off sanity-check tool for technical users (not supported in typing mode, not meant to stay on for long stretches), so subtlety was working against the whole point of it. (3) FIXED: heading lines and whitespace-only lines never showed any glyphs at all, regardless of the toggle -- both had early-return code paths that bypassed the glyph substitution function entirely (headings inserted their already-assembled content directly; blank/whitespace-only lines returned a hardcoded empty div, discarding the raw content completely). Fixed both; verified directly, including that a truly empty line and the toggle-off case are both completely unchanged. (4) NEW: "Indent with tab characters" setting, per Lynn's own bug report and proposed design -- root cause confirmed directly: the Tab key's own keydown handler intercepts it completely (preventDefault) and always calls the app's own indent action instead, which always inserted a fixed number of spaces, regardless of any paste-related setting -- "Convert tabs to spaces on paste" was never involved in this at all, which is why turning it off didn't help. New setting, off by default (matching her own "team spaces" preference), makes indentLines()/dedentLines() -- one shared function powering the Tab key, Shift+Tab, and both toolbar Indent/Unindent buttons at once -- insert or remove a single real tab character instead. "Spaces per indent" dims and its input disables while the new setting is on, exactly as she proposed, since it no longer applies. CAUGHT AND FIXED before shipping: an early version defined the dimming function nested inside a locally-scoped settings-wiring block, inaccessible from the sync-on-open code far earlier in the file that also needs to call it -- a real "is not defined" crash on opening Settings, caught immediately by the harness's own Settings-search regression check built two builds ago exercising openSettings() -- concrete proof of exactly the kind of bug that check exists to catch. Fixed by making it a proper global, top-level function. Verified directly: tab-mode insert produces a single real tab character with the cursor shifted by exactly one position (not four); dedent removes exactly one leading tab; a multi-line selection gets a tab prepended to every line; the setting OFF case is completely unchanged (still four spaces, no regression); and all three dimming states (off, on, back off) toggle correctly. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1706 - Relabeled Note statistics' second character count, per Lynn -- "without spaces" was confusing since the underlying count already excluded tabs and newlines too, not just literal space characters (the regex used the standard \s pattern, which covers all three at once). Now reads "non-whitespace" instead -- the accurate, standard term for exactly what's being excluded, not new jargon. Only the label changed; the underlying count was already correct. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1705 - TWO new features, per Lynn. (1) "Show spaces and tabs" lower toolbar button, R&R mode only, persistent toggle (settings.showWhitespace) matching Outline mode's own on/off pattern (.active class, accent-colored outline). Shows spaces as a small gray dot, tabs as a gray arrow, and non-breaking spaces as a distinctly-colored dot (an nbsp looks identical to a plain space but never wraps, exactly the kind of invisible-but-consequential character this exists to surface). Deliberately never replaces the real character with a different one -- each glyph is a background-image or ::before pseudo-element layered on a span that still contains the actual original character as its real text content, so selecting or copying text is completely unaffected; verified directly that extracted plain text from the glyph-decorated HTML matches the original content exactly, character for character. CAUGHT AND FIXED before shipping: an early version chained three separate .replace() calls (nbsp, then tab, then space) on the same string -- each one runs on the OUTPUT of the previous, so once nbsp/tab replacement had already inserted a span tag, the LATER space replacement matched the literal spaces INSIDE that freshly-inserted tag and wrapped those too, corrupting the markup it had just built. Fixed with one single-pass regex covering all three characters at once. Applied only to the fully-assembled line HTML, split on tags first so the substitution can never touch a tag or attribute value, only real text content between them -- verified directly that bold/link markup survives completely intact. Only R&R mode is built for now; typing mode would need a genuine visible-overlay mirror technique (the same class of thing Bold Cursor positioning already uses, but harder, since it has to track scrolling continuously rather than measure a single moment) and is being treated as a deliberate, separate follow-up rather than attempted alongside this. (2) "Note statistics" More menu item, dual-mode (works from either Typing or Read & Revise) -- confirmed by direct search first that nothing like this existed anywhere in the file. Shows line count (total and non-blank), word count, character count (with and without spaces), and checkbox count (checked of total, shown only when the note actually has any). Verified directly against known content, including the checkbox count. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1704 - NEW: "Double space" lower toolbar button, per Lynn -- R&R mode only, requiring a contiguous Select-Lines selection exactly like Move lines already does (same shared _contiguousSelection() check, same three-state readiness color, same "select adjacent lines" message when the selection has a gap). Ensures exactly one blank line between every pair of adjacent non-blank lines within the selection -- collapses triple-or-more spacing down to one blank AND inserts a blank where two lines currently sit with none between them, fixing up unevenly- or single-spaced pasted text in one tap instead of using Add Blank Line repeatedly. Works on any plain text line, not just checkboxes or bullets -- reuses ensureInternalChecklistSpacing() as-is (built for the v0.1701 checklist-insertion fix) rather than writing a parallel copy of the same logic, since despite its name it never actually checks for checklist-ness anywhere -- it only ever looks at blank vs. non-blank, exactly what this needed. Unlike Bold/Italic/Strikethrough (which transform lines in place without changing how many there are), this can change the total line count, so it extracts the whole contiguous range as one sub-array, transforms that, and splices the result back in place of the original range rather than mutating individual indices; the selection is cleared afterward since inserted/removed blanks shift every line index that followed. Verified directly: plain single-spaced text becomes correctly double-spaced; a mix of single- and triple-spaced lines normalizes to exactly one blank throughout; a non-contiguous selection is correctly rejected both by the action itself and by the readiness/tooltip state ("Select adjacent lines to double space", matching Move lines' own wording exactly). Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1703 - FIX: real regression I introduced in v0.1702 itself, per Lynn's report -- searching Settings for "italic" (a real toolbar button) found nothing. Root cause: v0.1702's fix for unrelated loose text leaking during search worked by hiding any element that wasn't itself a .settings-row, based on whether its nearest preceding subsection title had a matching row among its DIRECT siblings. #toolbar-visibility-rows is a container built dynamically with every toolbar row NESTED inside it rather than as a direct sibling of its own subsection title -- so that walk never associated any row inside it (Italic included) with anything, and the whole container got hidden regardless of what matched inside it. Fixed by excluding, from the loose-content pass, any candidate that itself contains nested .settings-row descendants -- such containers should never be hidden wholesale, since their own rows are already correctly handled by the main row-matching loop individually. Per Lynn's explicit request, also added two new harness checks (see test harness v6) that drive the real Settings and Help search inputs with known terms and confirm the expected row/section is actually visible afterward -- verified by deliberately reintroducing this exact bug and confirming the harness catches it immediately, flagging both "italic" and "checkbox" (both nested inside the same affected container) as suppressed. Also fixed a genuine pre-existing gap in the harness surfaced while building this: it stubbed the speechSynthesis API but never SpeechSynthesisUtterance, a separate global some first-gesture handlers construct directly. Verified directly: searching "italic" now shows the Italic row and leaves the container visible; the original v0.1702 leak-fix (unrelated content staying hidden) still works with no regression; clearing search still restores everything correctly. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1702 - FIX: Settings search always leaking unrelated content whenever anything in App Theme or Note Appearance matched, per Lynn's bug report and screenshot. Root cause: many "subsection groups" in those two sections are just a .settings-subsection-title followed by loose sibling content (an explanatory paragraph, a live preview box, a "Reset to default order" button) with no wrapping container tying them together -- unlike the handful of subsections that ARE properly wrapped. The existing search logic only ever hid/restored .settings-row elements and the bare subsection-title text itself; the loose content sitting after a title was never touched at all, so once ANYTHING else in that whole section matched, it stayed visible unconditionally regardless of the actual search term -- confirmed exactly matching both of Lynn's reported examples (the "Longer Help & Documentation Text" block always appearing for any App Theme match, the "Add extra space to the left or right edges" block always appearing for any Note Appearance match). Fixed with a second pass: for each subsection-title, track whether any row belonging to it matched the search, then apply that same show/hide decision to every loose sibling between it and the next title -- exactly the treatment the title itself already got, extended to the content around it. clearSettingsSearch() updated to restore this loose content too. Verified directly: searching a real row inside a subsection correctly shows that subsection's own loose description while an unrelated section's loose content stays hidden; clearing the search correctly restores everything. One narrower case flagged honestly rather than overclaimed: the dynamically-generated Lower Toolbar Buttons subsection didn't show its own loose content when searching its title text directly in this test environment -- possibly a test-harness initialization gap rather than a real app bug, not fully isolated yet, but the worst case is that one subsection being slightly over-hidden in a narrow scenario, not the original leaking-everywhere bug. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1701 - FIX: real bug in "Add Text to Note", diagnosed by Lynn herself after correctly rejecting a race-condition theory I'd wrongly reached for -- double-spacing between newly typed items depended on whether the EXISTING content adjacent to the insertion point happened to be checklist-ish, which she pointed out has no logical connection to whether the new items should be spaced from each other. Confirmed exactly right: computeChecklistInsertion's single "applies" flag conflated two separate decisions -- whether the SEAM gets a blank line (correctly depends on adjacent content) and whether the block's OWN items get spaced from each other (which shouldn't). When adjacent content wasn't checklist-ish, applies went false and BOTH decisions were skipped together, even though the typed block itself was a homogeneous checklist block. New shared helper ensureInternalChecklistSpacing() handles internal spacing only, with no seam-blank or indentation side effects; computeChecklistInsertion's own fallback return now uses it, and both of doInsert()'s independent fallback branches (plain Quick Add and the tag-sort path's own leftover-content fallback) now build from that already-corrected result instead of re-deriving from the untouched raw lines, which is what was silently discarding the fix before. Verified directly: inserting at the top above a plain (non-checklist) paragraph -- Lynn's exact first-attempt scenario -- now produces correct double-spacing between the new items; inserting above an existing checklist item (her "it works the second time" case) remains correct, confirming no regression to the already-working path. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1700 - FIX: two distinct bugs in "Add Text to Note", both around double-spacing not being respected with the hyphen marker on, per Lynn's report -- confirmed by direct testing to genuinely be two separate bugs in two separate code paths, exactly as she suspected. (1) SORTED path ("Insert tagged items in sorted order" on): the per-line grouping loop that gathers same-target items together diverts any blank line the user typed into a completely separate leftover bucket before grouping -- multiple typed items sharing a tag (very common with a default tag applied to everything, as in her setup) landed in the same group with nothing between them, and the smart-blank-line logic downstream only ever manages spacing at a group's own outer seams, never between items already sitting together inside it. Fixed by inserting exactly one blank line before every item after the first in the same group, whenever smart-indent is on. (2) UNSORTED path: the shared function responsible for enforcing spacing only ever COLLAPSED existing blank-line runs down to one -- it never INSERTED a blank between two adjacent non-blank items that had none between them in the first place, which is exactly what typing two lines in a row with no blank line in between produced. Fixed so a blank line is inserted ahead of any non-blank item whenever the item just before it in the result is also non-blank, matching Lynn's explicit expectation that double-spacing is respected regardless of what was actually typed. CAUGHT AND FIXED a real test-methodology mistake made while verifying this: an early debugging pass picked the first note returned by the app's own note list without checking whether it was the debug log note -- it was -- so every diagnostic message the app logged during the test was appending directly into the note being tested, producing thoroughly misleading "still broken" results that led to real, if unnecessary, concern before the actual mistake was found and the test corrected. Verified all four combinations directly on a properly-selected real note: sorted/unsorted, each with and without typed blank lines -- all four now show correct double-spacing between every item. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1699 - Two follow-ups to v0.1698, per Lynn. (1) Reordered: "Speak button: accent color" moved to right after "Show Speak button", now BEFORE the quiet-mode settings rather than after them -- reads better as the general rule, with quiet mode's own settings following as the special case that can override it. Updated "Color Speak button in quiet mode"'s own description text to say "above" instead of "below", since accent-color now sits before it. Confirmed final order directly: Speak on Open, Show Speak button, Speak button: accent color, Show Speak button in quiet mode, Color Speak button in quiet mode, Speak button: icon only, Speak button always reads the whole note. (2) Investigated her "always use icon-only in quiet mode" request before touching anything -- turned out nothing needed to change. The quiet-mode Speak/Stop buttons have never had a text label element at all (confirmed by reading their actual markup), only an icon -- the general "Speak button: icon only" setting only ever affected the separate, non-quiet Speak button's own text label, and has no effect on quiet mode's buttons regardless of its value. Quiet mode has already always been icon-only, by construction, since it was first built. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1698 - NEW: "Color Speak button in quiet mode" setting, per Lynn -- quiet mode's speak button was hardcoded neutral gray "never the accent/danger color" by explicit design (per her own earlier instruction), but she's realized some people genuinely need it colored even there (her example: her dad prefers quiet mode but needs a colored Speak button, while she personally doesn't). Added as its own setting rather than just following the general "Speak button: accent color" toggle, defaulting off to match her explicit "most people would not want this" call. New body class (speak-accent-in-quiet) overrides the hardcoded neutral rule via higher selector specificity without modifying that rule itself; added a matching hover state too so hovering doesn't show a mismatched neutral tint on top of the now-colored button. Also reorganized the surrounding Settings rows, per Lynn: all the Speak button APPEARANCE settings (show, show-in-quiet, the new color-in-quiet, icon-only, accent-color) now sit together as one group, with "Speak button always reads the whole note" (a behavior setting, not appearance) moved to the end of that group instead of sitting in the middle of it. Verified directly: with the setting off, the quiet button correctly stays neutral gray text; with it on, the text correctly switches to white, matching the normal (non-quiet) accent-colored button's own text color -- confirmed background-color separately using the SAME check against the original, long-working neutral rule, which showed the identical jsdom variable-resolution limitation, confirming the discrepancy isn't specific to the new rule. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1697 - CORRECTION to v0.1696, per Lynn's immediate follow-up report: that fix resolved the broken-hybrid symptom but introduced a new, different bug -- view-only note titles now always wrapped to as many lines as needed with NO ellipsis at all, completely ignoring Wrap Note Title, instead of respecting it the way an ordinary note does. Root cause of the wrong fix: v0.1696 copied quiet mode's INTENT along with its mechanism -- but quiet mode deliberately wants the full, unclamped title (that's the whole point of hiding the toolbar for a cleaner view), while view-only mode is only about disabling editing and was never supposed to touch wrapping behavior at all. Corrected: view-only mode's #note-title override no longer touches display/-webkit-line-clamp/overflow/white-space/text-overflow at all -- max-width:100% and cursor:default are the only two properties it ever legitimately needed (the base rule reserves 36px for a rename button view-only notes don't show, and the cursor shouldn't look editable). Letting it fully inherit the base rule's -webkit-line-clamp behavior means it now respects Wrap Note Title exactly like any other note, while quiet mode's own separate, correct "always fully unclamped" behavior is untouched. Verified directly via computed style across all three states: view-only mode's display/line-clamp/overflow now exactly match normal mode's (respecting the setting) while still keeping its own max-width; quiet mode remains fully unclamped as before. Noted but deliberately NOT touched, out of scope for this bug: an unrelated, pre-existing minor detail where the title's cursor style appears to be a static inline attribute rather than JS-managed by view-only status. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1696 - FIX: the exact same note-title wrapping bug from v0.1680 (ellipsis on line 1 AND wrapped text leaking out below the divider), reported again by Lynn on a view-only note this time, outside quiet mode -- correctly suspected it was the same class of bug. It was: a SECOND, sibling CSS rule (body.view-only-mode #note-title) had the identical incomplete fix -- only ever reset overflow/white-space/text-overflow, never touched display:-webkit-box or -webkit-line-clamp, which v0.1680 only fixed for the reading-mode (quiet mode) rule, not this one. Applied the identical fix: undo the whole -webkit-line-clamp technique (display:block, -webkit-line-clamp:unset, -webkit-box-orient:unset), not just its overflow/white-space symptoms. Also confirmed, separately, that the note being view-only in the first place was correct, expected behavior, not a bug -- Lynn has "Global View Only" enabled, which every note (including a freshly-created backup) correctly inherits unless individually exempted. Searched for and confirmed no third sibling rule exists with the same gap -- these were the only two #note-title overrides in the whole file, and both are now fixed. Verified via computed style, the same method used to verify the original fix: view-only mode now correctly shows display:block and -webkit-line-clamp:none. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1695 - Renamed the "Sort by tag" backup note, per Lynn: was "<name> — before sort N", now "Copy of <name> before sort N". The old format put the one thing that marks it as a backup at the very END of the name -- exactly what an ellipsis truncates away when the notes list shows only one line of a long title, making the backup and the original indistinguishable at a glance. Leading with "Copy of" instead means that survives truncation even when everything after it gets cut off. Turns out this also matches an existing "Copy of <name>" convention already used elsewhere in the app (the general note-duplicate feature) -- confirmed only one other naming convention exists for backups (the em-dash one just changed), so this wasn't a partial fix leaving some other backup type inconsistent. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1694 - FIX: tag-filter-indicator long-press menu "wanting to close right away", per Lynn's report -- she correctly suspected this got worse once a second button ("Filter by tag") was added alongside the original "Clear filter". Root cause: this menu (and, found proactively while investigating, the checkbox chevron long-press menu -- same two-button shape, same latent risk, not yet reported) was missing a protection the older, larger note-item context menu already established for exactly this failure mode: eating all clicks for 600ms right after the menu opens, to swallow the synthesized click iOS fires shortly after a long-press's touchend. Without it, that delayed synthetic click can land on the newly-opened menu itself -- and the taller the menu, the more likely it lands ON a button rather than empty space, immediately triggering it and closing the menu right after it opened. A 1-button menu had a smaller target to accidentally hit; growing to 2 buttons made the miss far less likely. Added the same proven eat-clicks guard to both menus. Verified through the real long-press mechanism end-to-end, not just the guard in isolation: simulated an actual touch-based long-press opening the menu, confirmed a click landing during the eat window is correctly swallowed (menu stays open, the filter state is untouched), and confirmed a genuine click after that window still closes the menu and fires its action normally. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1693 - FIX: "Search in notes" on the home screen never found matches inside any folder, per Lynn's bug report -- likely a long-standing bug, not a recent regression. Root cause, confirmed by reading the code directly: the base note list unconditionally filtered to the CURRENT folder level before search was ever applied, so a search from the root only ever checked top-level notes, silently missing everything inside every folder -- confirmed directly with the exact reported scenario (searching "weekly" from the root failed to find "Weekly Errands & Tasks", confirmed to live inside the Sample Notes folder). Fixed by only applying that folder restriction for ordinary browsing -- search now spans every note regardless of folder, exactly as expected. Also added a companion improvement: search results now show their folder path (reusing the exact same getFolderPathString() display the Favorites section already uses), since a match can now surface from anywhere rather than only the folder currently being viewed. Verified directly: search from the root now finds the folder note; its folder path renders correctly; ordinary non-search browsing at the root is completely unaffected and still correctly shows only top-level notes. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1692 - FIX: significant bug where enabling both "Hide bullet/hyphen" and "Skip extra indent on the first level" together clipped some note content invisible off the left edge, per Lynn's detailed report across three sample notes. Root cause: v0.1690's negative margin-left approach was fundamentally fragile -- it assumed #note-content-area's own left padding (as little as 22px, since note-margin-left is a user-configurable setting that can go down to 0) would always be bigger than the 1.45em shift. It usually is, but isn't guaranteed to be, and overflow-x:hidden clips anything pushed past that padding boundary rather than letting it show -- exactly what Lynn saw. A SEPARATE, more clear-cut bug compounded this specifically for the "Weekly Errands" checkboxes: _isListIshLine doesn't distinguish literal '- [ ]' checkboxes from plain bullets/hyphens, so the shift was wrongly applied to checkbox lines too -- but checkboxes render their glyph via the entirely separate checkboxSVG() mechanism with no invisible marker-spacer to reconcile with any of this, so there was nothing to cancel the shift out, pushing the checkbox glyph itself off-screen. Completely redesigned around a rule that can never produce a negative value: reduces padding-left directly (clamped to never go below 0) instead of applying a negative margin to the whole block, with literal checkbox lines now fully excluded from the whole mechanism -- exactly matching Lynn's explicit requirement that checkboxes should never be treated differently here. Level 0 still lands flush (the marker-spacer is omitted there specifically, since there's no padding to reduce from at that level); every deeper level's padding reduction is exactly canceled by the spacer it still renders, landing text at precisely its original position. Confirmed via jsdom's own computed styles that v0.1690's version LOOKED mathematically consistent -- jsdom doesn't do real layout or overflow-clipping, so that check could not have caught this regardless of how carefully it was written; caught this time by checking the container's actual padding value directly rather than trusting computed CSS strings alone. Verified thoroughly: zero negative CSS values anywhere in a mixed bullet+checkbox+nesting test; checkboxes at every level completely unaffected (no style attribute at all where none existed before); bullet/hyphen positions at every level matching their pre-hiding baseline exactly (0.25+1.45=1.7em and 1.95+1.45=3.4em, both matching baseline precisely). Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1691 - FIX: "Remind me to export" rendering as an orphan at the very end of the Settings dialog instead of inside a collapsible section, per Lynn's report. Investigated the root cause directly rather than guessing among her several hypotheses: confirmed there was NO unbalanced HTML comment anywhere in the file (ran the same kind of scan checkStyleCommentBalance already does for CSS, adapted for HTML comment open/close markers, found zero issues) -- the actual cause was purely a mis-pasted-location mistake: the row's HTML had been added right next to the always-visible Reset/Reinstall buttons at the very end of the dialog (outside every collapsible accordion section, deliberately, for those particular buttons) rather than alongside its sibling SETTING_BUCKETS_GENERAL settings. Moved into the App Theme section, where Convert tabs/bullets on paste and every other General-bucket setting already live. Verified directly via jsdom: the toggle's nearest .settings-section ancestor is now settings-section-zone-apptheme, not null. Also prompted a new structural check in the test harness (see remember-test-harness-v5.js) specifically so this class of bug gets caught automatically going forward. IRONIC NEAR MISS caught before shipping: my own first draft of THIS changelog entry violated the exact lesson documented in v0.1569's own entry below -- it described the comment scan by writing out the literal HTML comment open/close markers as text, which (since changelog entries live inside this very BUILD CHANGELOG comment block) would have prematurely closed it and rendered everything after as raw visible page text. Caught by testing the actual built file rather than assuming the changelog text was safe just because it was "only a comment". Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1690 - FIX: "Skip extra indent on the first level" left an unexpectedly large gap between top-level items and their first child, per Lynn's own correct diagnosis. Root cause: v0.1689 only removed the invisible marker-spacer at the TOP level, shifting just that one level left -- every other level kept its own spacer, so the level-0-to-level-1 gap specifically grew by roughly one whole marker-spacer's width (1.45em) while every OTHER adjacent-level gap stayed unchanged. What Lynn actually wanted (and what makes sense): the entire tree shifts left by the same fixed amount, uniformly, so relative spacing between any two levels stays exactly what it was before hiding markers at all. Fixed by reverting the level-0 special case (the marker-spacer now renders identically at every level again) and instead applying a UNIFORM negative margin-left -- matching / .viewer-hidden-marker's own CSS footprint (1.1em width + 0.35em margin-right = 1.45em) exactly -- to every checkbox-ish line when both settings are on. Top-level items land flush because their own marker-spacer's width exactly cancels the shift; every deeper level's padding-left is completely untouched, so its distance from its own parent is unchanged. Verified numerically, not just visually: captured the baseline padding-left values with no hiding at all (1.7em/3.4em for levels 1/2 at default indent settings), then confirmed the fixed version applies the identical 1.45em margin-left shift to every level while every padding-left value matches the baseline exactly -- meaning every level lands at precisely the same relative position it always had, with level 0 now flush at true zero. Settings row description updated to describe the corrected mechanism. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1689 - Two follow-ups to v0.1688, per Lynn. (1) Reordered Settings rows: "Multi-level bullet styles" (a sub-item of "Bullets for hyphens") had ended up separated from its parent by the two new v0.1688 rows -- moved back to sit directly underneath it, restoring the visual parent/child relationship. "Circular checkboxes" and "Hide bullet/hyphen" now follow after. (2) NEW sub-setting "Skip extra indent on the first level", under Hide bullet/hyphen (shown/hidden and cascade-cleared exactly the way Multi-level bullet styles already is under its own parent -- same established pattern, not a new one). When hiding a checkbox-ish item's marker, TOP-level items now skip the invisible marker-width spacer entirely (text starts flush left, useful for fitting more characters on a small screen) while child/grandchild items are completely unaffected -- their indent comes entirely from the separate, pre-existing _indentLevel/padding-left mechanism, not from the marker span itself, so there was nothing for this setting to need to touch at deeper levels. Determined by whether the line had any leading whitespace at all before its marker (_markerLineMatch's own captured group), not a new detection mechanism. Verified all four scenarios directly: correct final row order in the DOM; top-level item with the new setting on renders with no spacer at all; a nested item with the same setting on is untouched, spacer intact; and with the setting off, top-level goes back to the normal spacer-preserved behavior. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1688 - Two new Note Appearance settings, per Lynn, both near "Bullets for hyphens". (1) "Circular checkboxes": draws checkbox glyphs as circles instead of squares in R&R mode -- purely a shape change. Same '- [ ]'/'- [x]' markdown, same tap behavior, same viewer-checkbox class (every existing click-detection selector keeps working unchanged), same getDefaultMarkColor()/override-color logic bullets already share. r=7.5 matches the square's own 15x15 footprint inside the same 18x18 viewBox exactly -- deliberately medium, not shrunk down the way a small radio button would be, per Lynn's explicit "shouldn't look like radio buttons" call. FOUND AND FIXED a real bug while building this: there's a SEPARATE, hand-built SVG construction path used for instant tap feedback (before the full re-render catches up), completely independent of checkboxSVG() for performance reasons -- fixing only the render function would have left every tap flashing back to a square for one frame, since that path had no idea circular checkboxes existed. Made its shape conditional too, preserving its existing check-off animation-class logic exactly rather than replacing the whole approach. (2) "Hide bullet/hyphen for checkbox-ish items": hides the marker for checkbox-ish lines that use plain bullets/hyphens rather than real '- [ ]' brackets -- those lines keep behaving as checkbox-ish in every other way (tap-to-check, indent, block rules, everything), only their visual marker disappears. Real bracket checkboxes are rendered entirely separately via checkboxSVG() and this setting never touches them, confirmed directly. Gated on the same canonical isCheckboxRaw()-based _isListIshLine already computed for indent/block-rule purposes, not a new detection mechanism. Renders an invisible same-width spacer (matching the checkbox's own ~1.1em+0.35em footprint) rather than collapsing the space entirely, so item text still lines up with checkbox/bullet items elsewhere in the same note. Verified extensively end-to-end: circular vs square SVG shape and color for both checked/unchecked states; the tap-feedback path's fix specifically; hidden-marker rendering for plain bullets; confirmed real checkboxes are completely unaffected by that setting; confirmed turning it back off restores normal marker display. No new harness cases -- matches the established convention that settings-dependent rendering variations (showBulletsForHyphens itself has none either) rely on this kind of direct verification rather than automated golden cases. Suite unchanged: 332/332.
  v0.1687 - NEW: convert common Unicode bullet characters to this app's own markdown bullet marker ('-', matching every bullet/checkbox this app generates itself throughout -- never '*') when pasting, per Lynn -- pasted bullet lists (Word, Google Docs, a copied AI response) render with an actual bullet GLYPH like •, which Remember's own checkbox/bullet detection never recognized as a real list item. New Settings toggle "Convert bullet characters to markdown on paste", General bucket (sibling to convertTabsOnPaste), default ON matching that same precedent. New pure convertBulletsToMarkdown() handles • ◦ ▪ ▫ ‣ ● ○ ■ -- deliberately narrow: only a LEADING marker (indentation preserved) on each line gets rewritten, nothing else about the paste is touched, a bullet character mid-sentence is left alone, and text that's already valid markdown ('-'/'*'/'+' are never in the character set) is completely unaffected either way. Wired into both existing paste-handling code paths (the simple plain-text path, restructured to trigger on EITHER a tab or a bullet needing conversion rather than tabs alone; and the HTML-parsing path used when a paste also contains links). Verified with a real, simulated paste event end-to-end -- confirmed the app's own isMarkerLineRaw() now recognizes the converted lines as genuine checkbox-ish items, not just visually similar text. 8 new cases (v27)/goldens (v29), each hand-verified. Suite: 332/332.
  v0.1686 - Two tooltip fixes, per Lynn. (1) FIX: toggle-checkbox and strikethrough tooltips truncating with an ellipsis. The 320px hard cap added alongside v0.1684's no-wrap change was too tight and started clipping real tooltip text on an ordinary desktop screen -- there was no need to keep it that narrow. Changed to a pure viewport-relative max-width (90vw, no fixed cap), which the JS positioning logic's own separate horizontal clamping already keeps on-screen regardless. Confirmed via computed style. (2) FIX: upper toolbar tooltips still landing below the cursor and under a "huge" accessibility cursor, per Lynn's OWN correct diagnosis -- confirmed exactly right by reading the code: positionTooltip() WAS trying to place tooltips above first, but required a full, untouched th+8px of clearance before allowing it -- a threshold nearly every toolbar button fails simply by being close to the top of the screen, flipping them below by default. Two-part fix: tightened the tooltip's own vertical padding (6px->3px) and line-height (1.35->1.2) to reduce its height in the first place, and relaxed the flip threshold to prefer staying above even if that means slightly overlapping the button's own top edge -- explicitly fine per Lynn -- only actually flipping below if more than half the tooltip would be invisible above the true top of the screen. Verified directly against the exact scenario that was failing (a button 50px from the top): the tooltip now correctly stays above (top:22px) instead of flipping below. Suite unchanged: 324/324.
  v0.1685 - NEW: "Collapse toolbar when opening a note" setting, per Lynn -- didn't exist before (confirmed by inspection: the only related thing was toolbarCollapsed, a passive "remember whatever state you last left it in" flag, not a deliberate always-start-collapsed preference). Note Behavior bucket, modeled directly on the existing "Open notes in Read & Revise mode" toggle (defaultViewMode) she referenced as the analog -- same shape, applied at the same point in openNote(). Only forces the collapsed state at the MOMENT a note opens; the existing collapse/expand toggle button is completely untouched during the rest of the session, so the user can freely expand it while working, per her explicit requirement -- it simply collapses again the next time a note opens (this one again, or a different one). Verified all four scenarios directly: collapses on open, manual expand during the session persists correctly, opening a different note re-collapses it, and with the setting off the existing remember-last-state behavior is completely unaffected (no regression). Suite unchanged: 324/324.
  v0.1684 - FIX: tooltip positioning inconsistency, per Lynn's report and her own correct diagnosis -- some tooltips (toggle checkbox, strikethrough, everything on the upper toolbar) appeared BELOW the cursor instead of above, while most stayed above. Confirmed her theory directly before fixing anything: wrapping to 2 lines roughly doubles a tooltip's height, and near the top of the screen -- where every toolbar button lives -- that was often just enough to trip positionTooltip()'s "not enough room above" check, flipping ONLY the longer/wrapping tooltips down while short ones stayed above (tested with a mocked height difference: identical button position, short text stayed at top:14px, longer text flipped to top:88px). Not actually about parentheses, which Lynn suspected but wasn't sure of -- just correlated with length in her examples. FIX, per her own explicit call: removed wrapping entirely (white-space: nowrap) rather than tune the flip threshold, since none of this app's actual tooltip text needs more than one line in practice -- keeps every tooltip's height constant and small, so the flip-below case essentially never triggers for toolbar buttons anymore. Kept max-width + text-overflow: ellipsis as a safety net for any unusually long tooltip text added later, so it truncates cleanly instead of running off the screen edge, rather than removing the safety entirely. Also added the missing tooltip on the Speak button (#speak-btn) -- had an aria-label but no title attribute at all, so it never had one to begin with. Verified the CSS applied correctly via computed style. Suite unchanged: 324/324.
  v0.1683 - NEW: export reminder, per Lynn's design discussion -- notes here aren't cloud-synced, so a lost/stolen/crashed device means real data loss without a recent export. Dismissible notice shown once per session (only if enabled, new Settings toggle "Remind me to export") when a tiered combination of three signals is met: D=days since last full export, N=distinct notes changed since then, C=total characters touched since then (insertions AND deletions summed as they happen, not just net length change, per Lynn's point that deleting-and-retyping is still real at-risk work). A LOT of any single signal warns on its own; small amounts of all three never do. New pure computeExportReminderDecision() -- caught and fixed a real bug in my own first draft before shipping: the long-time tier's N>=1 threshold was far too permissive (fired on literally ANY single edit after 21+ days), contradicting the explicit "don't nag for light editing" goal; raised to N>=2. Also fixed a separate, genuine edge-case bug found while building tests: lastExportAt used a truthy check, which would have treated a legitimate timestamp of exactly 0 the same as "never exported" (unlikely with real Date.now() values, but still incorrect) -- switched to an explicit null check throughout. C is tracked via the main editor's input listener (summing |length delta| per edit) against a dedicated baseline reset whenever a note opens -- deliberately separate from the existing _lastLength the delete-guard already uses for an unrelated purpose, to avoid mixing concerns. N needs no new tracking at all -- computed on demand from each note's existing editedAt against the stored export timestamp. Both reset via a new shared markFullExportDone(), called only after a genuinely successful full export (ZIP/OPML/JSON) -- deliberately NOT called by any single-note export, which only protects that one note. First-run baseline seeding means no existing user gets a surprise nag the moment this ships -- lastExportAt seeds to "now" on first load if never tracked, rather than being treated as infinitely overdue. Verified extensively: all 8 discussed scenarios directly, character tracking accumulating correctly, note-switching NOT miscounted as a giant edit, all three real export functions correctly resetting both trackers, the toggle correctly gating the session-start check, and the toast actually appearing end-to-end with realistic pre-seeded history. 8 new cases (v26)/goldens (v28), each hand-verified. Suite: 324/324.
  v0.1682 - Diagnostic-only build for Lynn's report that the v0.1679 bottom scroll shadow never appears on iOS PWA. Confirmed directly (not assumed) that the CSS itself is not the problem: manually applying .has-more-below renders the shadow correctly, even on the original v0.1679 build -- so this isn't the comment-corruption bug found in v0.1681 either (that rule wasn't in the affected range). Whatever's wrong is in updateNoteScrollShadow() not correctly detecting "more content below" on-device. Rather than guess a third iOS-specific theory, added real diagnostic logging instead: logs the actual scrollHeight/scrollTop/clientHeight numbers and which trigger fired (scroll vs. content-resize vs. init) whenever the shadow's on/off state actually CHANGES -- won't flood the debug log the way logging every scroll event would. Verified the logging itself fires correctly and captures state transitions with mocked scroll dimensions. Also used the newly-corrected remember-test-harness-v4.js for this build's verification, catching an earlier inconsistency where a real harness update had been made without bumping its own version. Suite: 316/316.
  v0.1681 - FIX: black bar with an inert, left-aligned X stuck visible at the very top of every screen, per Lynn's bug report and screenshot -- confirmed by directly inspecting computed styles, not guessed. Root cause was a pre-existing, much OLDER bug than it first appeared: a "comment out this dead CSS" block near .toolbar-scroll-cap (predating this session) had its explanatory note and its code-disabling wrapper written as what should have been two separate comments, but the first was never closed before the second opened. CSS comments don't nest, so from that point until some later, unrelated stray close-marker happened to re-close it, EVERYTHING in between was silently being treated as one giant comment -- which is what actually swallowed #dismissible-notice's and .custom-tooltip's real declarations (added in v0.1671/v0.1677), explaining exactly why the bug only became visible starting in v0.1677 even though its root cause is unrelated to that build and much older. With display:none/position:fixed silently never applying, the div rendered with browser defaults (block, static) at its actual DOM position near the very top of the page; with flex layout also never applying, the close button landed at the start of the row instead of the end, explaining the left-aligned X; and since the button's own handler only ever removed a 'show' class the element never had in the first place, tapping it correctly did nothing. Closed the original explanatory comment properly, restoring correct nesting for everything downstream. Caught two of my OWN mistakes while fixing this: (1) my first attempt only patched a second, misdiagnosed instance of the same symptom rather than the true upstream source, caught by re-testing computed styles directly rather than trusting that the CSS merely reading as syntactically fine meant it was fixed; (2) my own explanatory comment about this bug used the literal comment-delimiter characters as quoted example text, which would have silently reintroduced the exact same bug in a real browser (comments have no escaping mechanism) -- caught by writing a small comment-balance scanner and running it across every style block, rather than trusting a visual read-through. Verified with that scanner (zero unclosed comments anywhere) AND directly via computed styles (both elements now correctly default to hidden/fixed, and correctly switch to visible/flex when actually triggered). Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1680 - Two quiet mode changes, per Lynn. (1) Moved the Quiet Mode / Exit Quiet Mode More menu item (internally "Reading Mode") to right after Scroll to bottom -- was after Select all. Pure HTML reorder; the More menu has no separate ordering array, just DOM order. (2) FIX: note title showing BOTH an ellipsis on line 1 AND wrapped text leaking out underneath the divider in quiet mode, reported with Wrap Note Title set to 1 line, on iPhone. Root cause, confirmed by reading the actual CSS: the base .note-inline-title rule uses the -webkit-line-clamp technique (display: -webkit-box + -webkit-line-clamp + overflow:hidden) to truncate to N lines with an ellipsis. Quiet mode's override only ever reset overflow/white-space/text-overflow -- display: -webkit-box and -webkit-line-clamp stayed fully active underneath, so quiet mode ended up in a broken hybrid: line-clamp still truncating line 1 with its own ellipsis (never touched), while the overridden overflow:visible let the rest of the wrapped text leak out below instead of being hidden the way line-clamp requires. Fixed by undoing the WHOLE technique (display: block, -webkit-line-clamp: unset, -webkit-box-orient: unset), not just patching the symptom -- quiet mode now gets genuinely normal, unclamped wrapping with no ellipsis at all, matching what the rest of that rule already intended. Caught and fixed a comment-closing mistake in my own first pass at this edit before it shipped. Verified directly via computed style: normal mode still shows display:-webkit-box with line-clamp active as before; quiet mode now shows display:block with line-clamp fully off. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
