  v0.1679 - NEW: bottom scroll shadow on the note editor, per Lynn -- shown only when there's more note content below to scroll to, gone entirely once truly at the end. Corrected mid-build after Lynn clarified: NOT the gradient-fade-to-background-color technique .qa-scroll-fade/#more-menu-fade use elsewhere in this app (she was explicit she doesn't want that here) -- a genuine plain box-shadow instead, the same kind .note-bottom-bar's own shadow already uses (which she'd said she liked in the previous build). inset + negative spread confines it to a thin band right at the edge rather than washing over the whole scroll area. Same shape as the existing horizontal TOOLBAR SCROLL ARROWS block: a scroll listener recomputes on every scroll, and a ResizeObserver on whichever of #note-editor/#note-viewer is currently rendering catches content-height changes (switching Typing/R&R mode, opening a different note, font size changes, typing itself) automatically, rather than needing to hook this into every individual render call site by hand. window._updateNoteScrollShadow exported the same way window._updateToolbarArrows already is, for any future call site that wants to force a recheck. Verified all four scenarios directly: shadow shown at the top with more below, correctly gone at the exact scrolled-to-bottom position, shown again when scrolled partway, and correctly never shown at all for a note short enough that no scrolling is possible in the first place. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1678 - Two toolbar fixes, per Lynn's Chromebook report. (1) FIX (probable, not visually confirmed): thin sliver of scrolled note text visible in the gap between the two stacked toolbar bars. pinToolbars() positions the lower bar using the header's measured height (offsetHeight), which rounds to whole pixels -- a well-known source of hairline gaps when precisely stacking two fixed-position elements, if the actual rendered layout lands on a fractional pixel. Switched both measurements to getBoundingClientRect().height (sub-pixel-precise) instead -- confirmed safe everywhere else these two variables are used (all px-string concatenation or plain arithmetic, both fine with fractional values). Flagged honestly: a strong, well-supported hypothesis given the symptom, not something visually confirmable without a real screen. (2) FIX (confirmed): the top header bar and the lower toolbar each had their OWN separate box-shadow -- correct for the header when it's the sole visible bar (toolbar collapsed), but when both bars are visible the header's shadow lands at the seam BETWEEN them rather than at the toolbar block's true bottom edge, where the lower bar's own (the one Lynn likes) already does the job. Header's shadow now only applies when toolbar-collapsed. Verified directly: header box-shadow is 'none' with both bars visible, correctly restored to its normal value once collapsed. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1677 - NEW: custom tooltips app-wide, per Lynn's Chromebook accessibility report -- REPLACE native browser title-attribute tooltips entirely (confirmed by inspection: 322 title attributes, zero custom tooltip system before this). Native tooltips are positioned by the OS relative to the cursor (so a large accessibility cursor sits right on top), use a fixed OS font size, and offer no wrap control -- none of which a web page can touch at all; that's why a dedicated setting couldn't have worked before this. New system: a single shared tooltip element + one pair of delegated mouseover/mouseout listeners on document (not per-element -- automatically covers every current and future title-bearing element with no extra wiring), mouse-only via the same (hover: hover) gate used throughout this app, so touch devices are completely unaffected. Anchored to the hovered ELEMENT (above it, flipping below if there's no room, clamped to viewport edges) rather than the cursor -- solves the large-cursor-covers-it problem at the root, not just by moving it a bit. Native tooltip suppressed for the hover's duration (title moved to data-tooltip-pending and restored on mouseout) so the two can never show simultaneously. Real CSS font-size tied to a new Tooltip Size setting (independent of every other size setting, with its own live preview bubble in Settings, right under UI Font Weight) plus real word-wrap with a sane max-width instead of one long strip on a big screen. Colors deliberately theme-matched rather than the common "always dark" convention several major design systems use -- reuses --bg3/--fg/--border, the same tokens every other subtle elevated surface in this app already uses, per Lynn's explicit preference against a black tooltip in light mode and the discussion of common conventions (always-dark, theme-matched, classic pale-yellow) beforehand. Verified the complete lifecycle directly: native tooltip correctly suppressed on hover, custom tooltip correctly appears after the delay with correct text and clamped position, title correctly restored and tooltip correctly hidden on mouseout; Settings slider and its live preview bubble both correctly reflect the configured size. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1676 - NEW: "Read Last Opened Note" action/shortcut, per Lynn -- Read Last Note has always meant most recently EDITED, not most recently viewed; these can genuinely differ (view one note, then edit a different one). Generalized the ?action=open/speak handler to also accept ?action=speakopened, differing only in which timestamp field the "no name given" fallback sorts by (viewedAt vs editedAt, falling back to editedAt if viewedAt is unset, same convention the notes-list's own sort-by-opened already uses) -- everything else (name/id lookup, opening the note, showing the Speak banner) is identical to the existing speak action. Shares v0.1675's Debug Log exclusion. Also updated the manifest.json family (all three variants -- base/stable/dev, which only differ in branding/version) with launch_handler: navigate-existing, per Lynn's report that using the "Open Remember" app shortcut while the PWA was already open spawned a second, independent window whose changes didn't sync with the first -- confirmed this is real, well-documented PWA/browser behavior (each window is its own separate JS process with its own in-memory notes object, only reconciled through shared localStorage at load time, not live between windows) rather than anything specific this app was doing wrong, and confirmed launch_handler is the standards-track fix for exactly this: an existing window now gets navigated to the shortcut's target URL instead of a new one being opened, which naturally re-runs this app's existing ?action= handling with zero other code changes needed, since it's a real navigation/reload rather than requiring a new window.launchQueue consumer. Help text also updated: corrected terminology (this is called App Shortcuts, not an Android-specific feature) and documented actual current platform support (Android/ChromeOS long-press, Windows/Mac right-click via Chrome or Edge, not supported on iOS/Safari), plus the new Read Last Opened Note entry. Verified directly: engineered edited-time and viewed-time to point at two DIFFERENT notes and confirmed speak/speakopened each correctly picked their own, both still correctly excluding the debug log. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1675 - FIX: "Read Last Note" (?action=speak/open with no name) always opened the Debug Log instead of a real note, per Lynn's bug report. Root cause: the Debug Log is stored as a genuine note (findOrCreateDebugLogNote()) whose editedAt updates on every single logged line -- far more often than any real note gets edited -- so it permanently won the "most recently edited" sort this fallback used. It's already deliberately excluded from the home list, search, and export-all via its own isDebugLog flag (see that function's own comment) -- this call site was simply missed when that exclusion list was built. Fixed the same way (filter out isDebugLog before sorting), plus two related spots with the identical gap found while fixing this one: the sibling ?action=copy Siri Shortcut's own "no name given" fallback, and findNoteByName() (used whenever a name IS given, e.g. if a Shortcut happened to pass "Debug Log" as the search term). Confirms for the record: Read Last Note has always meant most recently EDITED, not most recently opened/viewed -- unchanged, just no longer shadowed by the Debug Log. Verified directly: engineered the exact bug trigger (debug log's editedAt set higher than any real note) and confirmed the fix now correctly picks a real note instead; confirmed findNoteByName('Debug Log') now correctly returns null. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1674 - Folder entry vertical padding tightened further, per Lynn's iteration feedback: 8px -> 6px top/bottom (now matching note items exactly). Horizontal padding and note items untouched. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1673 - Reduced vertical padding on the Home Screen notes list entries, per Lynn, to test alongside v0.1672 -- explicitly a first pass, expecting iteration once she can see it. Note items: 8px -> 6px top/bottom. Folder items: 13px -> 8px top/bottom -- confirmed by inspection they had noticeably more vertical padding than note items to begin with (13px vs 8px), matching her own observation, so tightened proportionally more to close that gap rather than an equal reduction on both. Horizontal padding on both left untouched. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1672 - NEW: two font weight settings, per Lynn's accessibility pass -- "Note & Folder Name Weight" and "Note Preview Weight", App Theme bucket, each placed directly under its corresponding font SIZE setting as requested. Neither existed before (confirmed by inspection -- note/folder names were HARDCODED font-weight: 600/700 respectively, with no setting at all; the preview line just inherited the note editor's own weight with no independent control). Reused the existing variable-font weight-snapping infrastructure (getNearestSupportedWeight/isVariableWeightFont/FONT_WEIGHT_SUPPORT, same system added for Inter/Atkinson Hyperlegible Next/Mulish) rather than building parallel logic -- new updateNotesListNameFontWeightStep()/updateNotesListPreviewFontWeightStep() mirror the existing updateNoteFontWeightStep() exactly, switching each slider's step between fine (10, variable fonts) and coarse (100, static fonts) based on whichever font that slider actually applies to, wired into both the notes-list-specific font selects AND the general UI/note font selects (since these sliders fall back to the general font when their own override is unset). CAUGHT AND FIXED a real default-regression risk before shipping: Note & Folder Name Weight's natural fallback (matching its font/size siblings) would have been settings.uiFontWeight, but note/folder names are deliberately semibold today as a visual-hierarchy choice independent of body text weight -- falling back to the general UI weight (usually 400) would have visibly lightened everyone's note names the moment this shipped. Fallback is 600 instead, exactly matching today's unchanged default. Folder names now share the SAME weight variable as note names (matching how Font and Size already treat both identically, and Lynn's own "note & folder" combined naming) -- flagged clearly in comments that this collapses their previously-different hardcoded defaults (700 vs 600) into one shared value unless the new setting is changed. Verified end-to-end: default values exactly match the old hardcoded look (no regression); setting values apply correctly to the CSS variables; Settings dialog sliders correctly sync to saved values on open; live preview boxes correctly reflect configured weights. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1671 - NEW: "Get fresh copy" for sample notes, per Lynn -- creates a brand-new, independent note from a sample's CURRENT content, without ever touching an existing edited/renamed copy, so she can compare the two side by side (the thing she used to try doing by renaming a folder, which wouldn't actually have worked -- Reinstall's own "already exists" check looks anywhere, not just inside the Sample Notes folder). Two entry points: a "Get fresh copy of this sample" More menu item (shown only when the CURRENTLY OPEN note's id is a recognized sample -- new SAMPLE_NOTE_IDS registry, same manual-sync discipline as FONT_WEIGHT_SUPPORT/SETTING_BUCKETS), and a "Get Fresh Copy of a Sample Note..." picker in Settings next to Reinstall, for when you're not currently viewing one. The fresh copy lands in the exact same folder situation the sample is CURRENTLY in (top level or Sample Notes), per Lynn's explicit design call -- mirrors wherever you're looking at it now rather than a hardcoded original location. Content-sourcing needed no refactor of createInitialNotes()'s ~20 individual sample definitions: getFreshSampleCopy() momentarily deletes the target from `notes` (in memory only), calls the EXISTING createInitialNotes() unmodified (its own if-not-exists guard now sees it "missing" and recreates it fresh), captures the result, then immediately restores whatever was there before -- doubling up on a safety net that function already had (its own v0.1630 snapshot/restore pass). New name includes the app version (e.g. "A Quiet Evening (v0.1671)") so copies taken over time stay distinguishable. Confirmation is a new #dismissible-notice element -- deliberately separate from the existing toast per Lynn's explicit request for something requiring an actual tap on an X to close, not an auto-timeout or a stray tap anywhere on it, since a folder path needs a moment to actually read. Verified directly: fresh content correct while the user's edited original is completely untouched (both name and content); the no-folder and in-folder notice wording; the notice's close button; new note gets a distinct id and the same folderId as the original; SAMPLE_NOTE_IDS correctly distinguishes samples from ordinary notes. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1670 - NEW: numbered-list child levels now display as a full decimal path (1, 1.1, 1.1.1, ...) instead of a bare local count repeating "1, 2, 3" at every depth -- per Lynn's explicit choice after discussing the common conventions (legal/classic outline a/i lettering, alternating case, decimal). Decimal was chosen specifically to avoid Roman numerals entirely, since those would have needed new pronunciation logic in the speech feature; digits and periods need none. R&R mode only, as always -- typing mode still shows exactly whatever digits were typed, unchanged. computeOrderedListNumbers()'s `number` field is now always the full joined path string rather than just the current level's local count -- the per-depth counter stack the function already tracked for the "resume, don't restart" behavior turned out to hold exactly the data a decimal path needs, so this was really just "join the whole stack" instead of "use only its last entry", not a new mechanism. A nice side effect on the two existing bug-fix scenarios (indenting an item with a leftover digit, and renesting after a return to a shallower level): they now show something MORE informative than before (3.1, 2.1) instead of a bare, context-free 1. Updated 8 existing test goldens to reflect this intentional behavior change, each hand-verified against the design (including the exact 1.1.1 grandchild example this was built around) before acceptance. Also updated the Numbered List Sampler's own descriptive text to explain decimal paths instead of the old local-count wording -- note this only affects fresh installs/reinstalls-after-deletion, since the sample note's own reinstall-safe guard (if a note with this id doesn't already exist) intentionally never touches an existing copy someone may have edited. Suite: 316/316.
  v0.1669 - Applied the v0.1668 Android scrollbar-width fix to Add Text to Note (#quick-add-input) and Edit Paragraph (#edit-block-input), per Lynn's explicit request -- both are self-scrolling textareas (overflow-y: auto, fixed/max height) whose scrollbar toggles on/off as content crosses that threshold, the same mechanism that clipped the main editor's last lines. scrollbar-gutter: stable added to both, permanently reserving the scrollbar's width so its presence can never change the available width mid-measurement -- relevant to these dialogs' own Bold Cursor mirror positioning (v0.1601/v0.1634 already fixed a related but distinct width bug here: the mirror using offsetWidth instead of clientWidth). Confirmed via grep this is a clean, additive change -- no existing rule on either selector previously set scrollbar-gutter. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1668 - REAL FIX for the Android missing-lines bug, per Lynn's explicit instruction not to assume a timing/race issue -- her second log proved it wasn't one: byte-identical numbers (ed.offsetHeight=5880, ed.scrollHeight=6030) across two completely separate occurrences, even after v0.1667's 6 retries over ~900ms changed nothing at all. A deterministic mismatch that never moves means something is computing the SAME wrong answer every time, not still settling. Root cause, found by recognizing this codebase already diagnosed the identical bug class once before (v0.1634, a different pair of dialogs): "Android/desktop classic scrollbars consume interior width" -- iOS's overlay scrollbar doesn't, which is exactly why this never reproduced there. Applied to resizeEditor() specifically: its shrink-to-1px-then-grow trick can momentarily make note-content-area's scrollbar (overflow-y: auto) disappear while the textarea is briefly 1px tall, widening the space available to it right as scrollHeight gets measured -- text wraps into fewer lines than it truly needs at that artificially wider width, the textarea's height gets pinned to that too-small number, and once content is tall again the scrollbar reappears, narrowing the true width back down -- so the real rendering needs MORE room than the pinned height allows, and the excess is clipped. Identical every time resizeEditor() runs, exactly why retrying it changed nothing. FIX: scrollbar-gutter: stable on #note-content-area permanently reserves the scrollbar's width whether or not it's currently shown, so the available width can never change mid-measurement again. The v0.1667 retry loop and diagnostic logging are left in place -- no longer needed as a blind guess-and-retry, but now a useful confirmation signal: if this fix is right, the next Android log should show attempt=0, OK. Honest limitation, same as the iOS keyboard fixes: jsdom cannot render real CSS layout or scrollbars, so this cannot be verified end-to-end in the sandbox -- confidence here comes from the mechanism matching a previously-confirmed bug in this exact codebase and explaining every detail of Lynn's evidence precisely, not from a passing test. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1667 - Android missing-lines bug: Lynn's actual debug log (not another guess) pinpointed the real mechanism precisely. area.scrollTop WAS correctly at its max (5545.6 of 5545) -- the outer container's scrolling was never the problem. But ed.offsetHeight (5880) was 150px SHORTER than ed.scrollHeight (6030): the textarea itself was rendered too short for its own content, so even scrolling the outer container all the way down couldn't reveal text clipped inside an inner element sized wrong. This confirms resizeEditor() (height='1px' then height=scrollHeight) was the right idea all along -- the v0.1664/v0.1665 attempts just didn't retry it for long enough: her log shows the mismatch STILL present at exactly the 150ms mark her single retry fired at, meaning Android's post-keyboard-dismiss reflow (text rewrapping as the viewport settles) was still in progress past that point. FIX: replaced the single fixed-delay retry with a self-correcting loop that keeps calling resizeEditor() every 150ms, re-measuring fresh each time, until offsetHeight actually catches up to scrollHeight -- capped at 6 attempts (~900ms) so it can't run forever if something else keeps scrollHeight a moving target. The final attempt logs once, tagged OK or STILL-SHORT with the attempt count, so the next log shows directly whether this actually closed the gap rather than needing another guess. Verified the loop's safety property directly in the sandbox with a mismatch engineered to never resolve: terminates at exactly attempt 6, logs exactly once (no spam), correctly tagged STILL-SHORT -- confirmed no infinite-loop risk either way this turns out on Lynn's next test. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1666 - FIX for the iOS tap-near-bottom issue, per Lynn's own on-device diagnosis (correct): v0.1665 computed the right scroll target via _tpadEnsureCaretVisible(), but its final `area.scrollTop = newScroll` assignment gets silently clamped by the browser to whatever's actually scrollable -- with little or no room below the note's last line, a perfectly correct target could never actually be reached, no matter how right the math was. Fixed with a two-layer approach, per her exact framing ("add padding" + "maybe you're not measuring the keyboard height correctly"): (1) a CSS fallback, #note-screen.keyboard-open #note-content-area { padding-bottom: 320px }, scoped to keyboard-open only so it adds no space at any other time and never touches the note's actual saved content; (2) a new shared function, _applyKeyboardScrollRoom(), measures the REAL keyboard height (window.innerHeight - visualViewport.height) and overrides the CSS guess with the actual number, called immediately before every _tpadEnsureCaretVisible() scroll attempt (all 3 call sites: tap-to-edit, caret-nudge buttons, trackpad-drag-heal). The inline override is explicitly cleared (paddingBottom = '') in the EMTIP-exit blur handler, since an inline style would otherwise persist regardless of the keyboard-open class once the keyboard actually closes. Verified precisely in the sandbox with a mocked keyboard height: computed padding matched the expected formula exactly (340px for an 800/500 viewport split), and confirmed it correctly clears on a genuine .blur() (not just a synthetic blur event, which doesn't itself move document.activeElement -- caught and corrected that exact test-setup mistake before trusting the result). Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1665 - Two things from Lynn. (1) FIX (higher confidence than usual, since this reuses an existing, proven mechanism rather than a new theory): tapping one of the last lines of a long note to start editing left that line covered by the keyboard, requiring a dismiss-and-retap to actually see and type on it -- a long-standing issue. Root cause: the tap-to-edit handler already had multiple delayed pinToolbars() calls to handle the keyboard settling at the TOP of the screen, but nothing ever scrolled the tapped CURSOR clear of the keyboard at the BOTTOM. _tpadEnsureCaretVisible() -- the keyboard-aware, visualViewport-based scroll function already used successfully elsewhere for trackpad-drag caret healing -- was simply never wired up to this call site. Added at the same 100/350/600ms delayed timing already used for pinToolbars(), for the same reason: visualViewport.height doesn't yet reflect the keyboard's real height at the instant of the tap, so only the delayed calls (once the keyboard has actually finished animating in) compute against trustworthy numbers. Verified the code path resolves and executes without error at all three delays; the actual on-screen scroll positioning still needs on-device confirmation, since jsdom can't compute real layout. (2) Android missing-lines bug: v0.1664's resizeEditor() attempt did NOT fix it, confirmed on-device. Rather than guess a third time, replaced it with real diagnostic logging (kept the resizeEditor() calls themselves, harmless either way) -- captures area.scrollTop/scrollHeight/clientHeight, computed maxScroll, the textarea's own offsetHeight/scrollHeight, and visualViewport's height/offsetTop, all in one line 150ms after the keyboard closes. The actual numbers from Lynn's next Android repro will show directly which measurement is wrong, rather than more speculation. Suite unchanged: 316/316.
  v0.1664 - Two things from Lynn. (1) NEW: a little extra bottom padding in R&R mode, so the last couple of lines aren't hard to interact with near the iPhone's Siri/edge-swipe zone or the FAB. Dynamic, not flat: new pure functions countTrailingBlankLines() and computeExtraBottomPaddingPx() only top a note UP to ~2 blank lines' worth of bottom space -- a note that already ends in several blank lines gets little or nothing extra, so this never piles more empty space on space that's already there. Applied as an inline padding-bottom on #note-viewer, recomputed on every renderViewer() call; typing mode (#note-editor, a separate element) is untouched by design, matching her request being R&R-specific. (2) FIX ATTEMPT (Android): for her report that the final ~3 lines of a long note could go completely missing after dismissing the keyboard (EMTIP -> EMNI) -- not reproducible on iOS, reappearing only by switching to R&R and back. Found a real, curious gap: resizeEditor() (which re-measures the textarea's height AND re-derives the scroll container's position from CURRENT viewport conditions) is called at roughly 30 other places in this file whenever layout or content changes, but was never called in the keyboard-dismiss blur handler -- only pinToolbars() was. Best-supported hypothesis: Android actually shrinks the visual viewport while the keyboard is up (unlike iOS's overlay-style keyboard), so measurements taken during that shrunk state go stale once the keyboard closes and the viewport grows back, with nothing to refresh them. Added resizeEditor() calls at the same timing as the existing pinToolbars() re-checks (immediate + 150ms). Flagged honestly: this is a plausible fix based on how the surrounding code already behaves, not a confirmed root cause -- there is no way to reproduce a real Android keyboard resize event in this sandbox, so on-device testing of this exact scenario is what actually confirms or refutes it. 9 new cases (v25)/goldens (v26) for the padding functions, hand-verified. Suite: 316/316.
  v0.1663 - REPLACED v0.1662's hover-peek entirely, per Lynn's on-device feedback: even a 40px proximity zone along the full height of the note's right edge interfered with normal typing and clicking there. New approach: a small, ALWAYS-VISIBLE edge tab (#scratch-pad-edge-tab) -- no proximity detection of any kind, so there's nothing left to accidentally trigger while editing. Positioned at right:10px (clear of the app's global 6px thin scrollbar, which sits flush at the true edge), and deliberately NOT a plain bar/pill shape, per Lynn's explicit concern about it reading as a scrollbar drag handle -- uses the common bordered-rectangle-with-divider "toggle side panel" icon instead (same one VS Code and others use), which no scrollbar styling convention resembles. Independent of #note-fab's existing hide-while-typing rule (#note-screen.keyboard-open #note-fab {display:none}) -- confirmed by direct test that the tab stays visible while actively typing, unlike the FAB, satisfying Lynn's explicit "both Typing and R&R mode" requirement. Visibility is reactive via two mechanisms rather than a poll/mousemove loop: a body.scratch-pad-disabled class toggled in applySettings() (which already runs on load and after every settings save) for the disable-scratch-pad setting, and a MutationObserver on the panel's own class attribute to hide the tab while the panel is open (nothing to invite a click toward at that point) and restore it when closed. Cleaned up several now-stale .peek references left over in openScratchPad()/closeScratchPad()/the handle's click handler from the mechanism this replaced -- including reverting the handle's click from an open/close toggle back to close-only, since without a peek state the handle is fully off-screen (and therefore unclickable) whenever the panel is closed, so the toggle's open branch could never actually fire. Verified directly: hover-capable gating, click-to-open, the MutationObserver hiding/restoring the tab around the panel's open state, the disabled-setting reactive hide, and continued visibility while actively typing. Suite unchanged: 307/307.
  v0.1662 - REPLACED v0.1651's mouse click-zone for opening the scratch pad with a hover-peek, per Lynn's on-device feedback: the 12px click zone was "extremely hard to find." Now, getting the mouse within 40px of the note content pane's right edge slides just the handle into view (Windows auto-hide-taskbar style, her own reference) via a new .peek CSS class -- a big, easy-to-notice sliver to aim for instead of a precise hidden target. Moving away without clicking hides it again; clicking the peeking handle opens the pad the rest of the way (the handle's click handler is now a toggle -- open if not fully open, close if it is -- rather than v0.1651's close-only). Both proximity-to-edge and hovering the peeking sliver itself are checked off the SAME mousemove event, so no separate enter/leave listeners were needed on the panel. Gated on disableScratchPad and (hover: hover), same as before. FOUND AND FIXED a real risk while building this: .peek's CSS rule is declared after .open's, so if both classes were ever present simultaneously .peek would incorrectly win the cascade (equal specificity, later rule wins), leaving the pad looking stuck mid-peek instead of fully open -- fixed by having openScratchPad() (the one function every open path already goes through) explicitly clear .peek, rather than trusting cascade order or patching every call site individually. Verified the full interaction in the sandbox with mocked element geometry (jsdom can't compute real CSS transforms): approaching the edge triggers peek, moving away cancels it, clicking while peeking opens fully with .peek correctly cleared, clicking again closes. Suite unchanged: 307/307.
  v0.1661 - FIX: crash using Add Text to Note's "Insert tagged items in sorted order" checkbox in Typing mode, per Lynn's bug report -- "TypeError: undefined is not an object (evaluating 'newLinesArr.slice')". Root cause: newLinesArr is only ever assigned inside the "if (!_tagSortApplied)" branch of doInsert(); when the tag-sort insert path ran instead (exactly when that checkbox is on), it stayed undefined, and the Typing-mode scroll-into-view code further down referenced it unconditionally, outside that branch -- var's function-scoping meant the reference didn't throw a ReferenceError, just silently stayed undefined until .slice() was called on it. Only hit in Typing mode because R&R mode's own scroll-into-view code path doesn't reference newLinesArr at all. FIX: derive the lines array from newContent instead (split on newline) -- both the tag-sort and non-tag-sort branches populate newContent, so this works correctly regardless of which path ran, and is exactly equivalent to the old behavior for the non-tag-sort case since newContent was always just newLinesArr.join('\n') there anyway. Verified two ways: confirmed no other unsafe references to newLinesArr remain outside its own declaration block; and reproduced the exact bug through the real UI (Quick Add dialog, tag-sort checkbox, save) -- no crash, and the new item correctly sorted into place next to the existing item sharing its tag. Suite unchanged: 307/307.
  v0.1660 - Added "Filter by tag" as a second item in the v0.1659 tag-filter-indicator long-press menu, per Lynn -- just another entry point to the identical bottom sheet the More menu's own "Filter by tag" already opens. Extracted the shared open logic into a new openTagFilterSheet(), now used by all three entry points (More menu item, toolbar button, and this new long-press menu item) instead of two near-duplicate inline copies, so they can't drift apart from each other. Verified directly: clicking the new menu item opens the bottom sheet and closes the long-press menu correctly. Suite unchanged: 307/307.
  v0.1659 - NEW: long-press the tag-filter-indicator (upper toolbar, shown when a tag filter is active) to open a small "Clear filter" menu, per Lynn -- she often sets a filter by mistake tapping a tag in the note editor and wanted a faster way to clear it than opening the full filter dialog. Mirrors the app's existing #cb-longpress-menu (checkbox chevron long-press) shape exactly -- own id/CSS block rather than a shared class, matching how #note-item-context-menu and #cb-longpress-menu already each keep their own near-identical block -- and reuses attachLongPress() plus the same iOS synthesized-click-swallow pattern used for Select Lines range-select. Only shows when a filter is actually active (same guard the existing single-tap pause-toggle handler already used), positioned near the touch point and clamped to the viewport, dismissed by tapping outside (consolidated into the SAME existing outside-tap listeners the checkbox menu already used, rather than adding two more document-level listeners). Extracted the clearing logic itself into a new clearTagFilterState() function, shared by both the long-press menu and the existing Clear filter button in the full filter dialog, so they can't drift out of sync. Verified directly: clicking the new menu's Clear button correctly empties _tagFilterState; long-press with no active filter correctly shows nothing; long-press with an active filter correctly shows the menu at the right position. Suite unchanged: 307/307.
  v0.1658 - NEW: the delete guard dialog now reads differently when the deletion was actually a cut, per Lynn -- the old wording ("You deleted a lot of text" / "Go ahead and delete" / "Undo deletion") was scary and misleading when the text was already safely on the clipboard. Root cause of the inconsistent "Cut to clipboard" toast she noticed: the app's own scissor button always wrote to the clipboard explicitly, but there was no listener anywhere for the browser's NATIVE cut event, which fires for the OS/browser popup-menu Cut and any keyboard shortcut -- those cuts were completely invisible to the app. Added a native 'cut' listener on note-editor (sets a shared, short-lived _lastCutAt timestamp) alongside the scissor button now setting the same flag directly (its own primary path uses writeClipboardSafe + setRangeText, not the native cut command, so the listener alone wouldn't have seen it). The delete guard's existing 600ms-delayed check consumes this flag (2-second window, generous enough to survive that delay, tight enough not to misattribute a later unrelated delete) and swaps in reassuring wording when it applies: "You cut a lot of text" / "...it's on your clipboard, ready to paste..." / "Go ahead and remove it" / "Undo the cut" -- same button roles as before, still avoiding the earlier "keep the deletion" oxymoron trap. Verified end-to-end in the sandbox: plain deletion still shows the original wording unchanged; both the native-cut path and the scissor-button path correctly show the new wording. Suite unchanged: 307/307.
  v0.1657 - FIX (probable): pinch-zoom getting stuck disabled on iOS PWA, per Lynn's bug report -- confirmed root cause by inspection of the existing v0.1580 dynamic-viewport-cap mechanism (caps maximum-scale to 1 while a text field is focused, to suppress iOS's auto-zoom-on-small-font-focus, restoring it ~350ms after focusout). iOS can suspend a backgrounded PWA WHILE a text field is focused -- backgrounding mid-typing is completely normal -- without ever firing focusout for it; document.activeElement can still report that field focused once the app resumes even though the keyboard is gone, so the cap-restore logic (which only listens for focusout) never runs and stays stuck capped indefinitely. Matches Lynn's report closely: fixed by entering/exiting Typing mode (produces a fresh, genuine focusout), described as happening after backgrounding, no periodic timer anywhere in the file that would explain a spontaneous ~10-second self-fix (more likely how long it took to notice a fix from something she'd just done). FIX: a new visibilitychange listener inside the same IIFE (kept separate from the existing, unrelated v0.1507 visibilitychange handler, which only fires on 'hidden' for select-mode/speech and doesn't share this one's private state) re-derives the truth directly from document.activeElement on resume, instead of trusting whatever stale cap state predates the suspension. Verified the logic in the sandbox (stuck-capped state + focus genuinely moved away -> visibilitychange correctly force-restores immediately; focus still genuinely on a field -> correctly stays capped, unchanged behavior) -- but flagged as "probable", not confirmed, since an actual iOS suspend-without-focusout cycle can't be simulated here; this is the one thing only an on-device test after real backgrounding can fully confirm. Suite unchanged: 307/307.
  v0.1656 - NEW: two fonts added, per Lynn -- Atkinson Hyperlegible Next (added alongside the existing default Atkinson Hyperlegible, not replacing it, so she can compare and remove the original later if she decides to) and Mulish, both requested by name. Both are genuine Google Fonts variable fonts, confirmed exact weight-axis ranges before adding (Next: 200-800, ExtraLight-ExtraBold; Mulish: 200-1000) rather than guessing -- added to the single shared Google Fonts @import URL every other font in this app already loads from (one more family= clause each, same variable-range syntax Inter already uses), plus a quick-pick pill, an <option> in all 4 font dropdowns, and a {min,max} entry in the weight-range registry (the same object shape Inter/Google Sans Flex already use, since both are real variable fonts, not discrete static weights). No structural changes -- this followed the exact existing per-font list pattern throughout. Suite unchanged: 307/307.
  v0.1655 - NEW: "Slashed zero" checkbox added to Settings > Number Style, per Lynn. Implemented as an independent checkbox, not a 5th segmented-control option, because slashed-zero is genuinely a separate CSS font-variant-numeric keyword that COMBINES with Tabular/Old Style/Lining rather than being mutually exclusive with them (e.g. "tabular-nums slashed-zero" is one valid, different value from either alone) -- confirmed this is how the spec actually works before building it as a single 5-way exclusive choice, which would have silently prevented combining tabular numbers with a slashed zero, a completely reasonable thing to want together. settings.fontVariantNumeric now stores a space-separated combination; new helpers _dtiNumericMainToken()/_dtiNumericHasSlashedZero()/_dtiSetNumericValue() parse and rebuild it from the two independent UI controls. Verified end-to-end: enabling Tabular + slashed zero, switching the main style back to Default, confirms slashed-zero survives the switch (value correctly becomes "slashed-zero" alone rather than being lost), then disabling it fully clears the setting. Purely cosmetic when the current font has no slashed-zero alternate glyph -- flagged clearly in the row's own description, not a bug when nothing visibly changes. No new setting key added (reused the existing fontVariantNumeric slot), so no SETTING_BUCKETS change needed. Suite unchanged: 307/307.
  v0.1654 - Added 3 informal US date styles to Insert Date/Time, per Lynn: usInformalNoYear (7/23), usInformalYear2 (7/23/26), usInformalYear4 (7/23/2026) -- no leading zeros on month/day, distinct from the existing padded usYear/usNoYear (07/23/2026 / 07/23), which stay as their own separate options rather than being replaced (both offered side by side). Matches what people commonly type or handwrite when given free choice, vs. the padded style being more of a forms/spreadsheet-sorting convention. Did NOT add international (day-first) informal equivalents this build -- flagged genuine uncertainty about exactly how universal no-leading-zero informal writing is across non-US regions (day-first order and dropped-leading-zeros both do occur elsewhere, e.g. the UK, but confidence is lower than for the well-established US convention); worth confirming with more certainty or actual regional feedback before adding those as their own options. 4 new cases (v24)/goldens (v25), including a single-digit-month-and-day case. Suite: 307/307.
  v0.1653 - NEW: Insert Date/Time toolbar button, per Lynn. Typing mode inserts at the cursor; R&R mode appends to the end of every selected line (her explicit choice, matching how Indent/Dedent already treat multi-line selections). New pure functions formatDateOnly() (9 styles -- Jul 23 2026 / July 23 2026 / 07-23-2026 / 23-07-2026 / 2026-07-23, each with and without the year except ISO), formatTimeOnly() (reuses the existing 12h/24h setting, adds optional seconds), and formatInsertDateTime() (combines both, handles an optional day-of-week prefix -- full or abbreviated only, no medium form: no ambiguity exists among 3-letter day abbreviations so there's nothing a longer form would disambiguate). 4 new Note Behavior settings (per Lynn's explicit call -- this generates note CONTENT, not a visual choice, and is ready for a future Note Style): insertDateTimeMode/DateStyle/DayOfWeek/Seconds. Settings UI deliberately has NO format-string field anywhere -- every option's own label IS a live example computed from today's actual date, refreshing instantly as any control changes, using the app's existing accessible radio-group component (same one Ruled Lines/Note Editing Mode already use). Rows hide themselves when irrelevant (Date style/Day of week vanish for Time only; Include seconds vanishes for Date only). 20 new cases (v23)/goldens (v24), all hand-verified against a fixed reference date (including the 12am/12pm midnight/noon edge cases). Suite: 303/303.
  v0.1652 - FIX: default tags for new checkbox-ish items weren't reliably applied in Typing mode, per Lynn's bug report -- she correctly narrowed it to "Double-space smart indent items" being on. Root cause: the Enter-keydown handler that appends default tags assumed the just-completed item was always exactly ONE line above the cursor's new line -- true when double-spacing is off, but false when it's on, since smart indent then inserts an EXTRA blank line between the completed item and the new one. "One line up" landed on that blank line instead, and appending tags to an empty line is always a no-op. Explains her exact "occasionally it did work" report too: only on presses where smart indent didn't fire at all that time (e.g. the previous line wasn't checkbox-ish), never when double-spacing actually inserted its blank line. Fixed by extracting the position-finding logic into a new pure function, computeDefaultTagInsertion (previously inline in the keydown handler and untestable -- exactly how this went unnoticed), which walks backward past ANY number of blank lines instead of assuming exactly one. Verified directly against the exact bug scenario plus already-tagged (no-op), non-checkbox-ish (no-op), and multiple-pre-existing-blank-lines cases. 6 new cases (v22)/goldens (v23), all hand-verified (caught and fixed an off-by-one in my own first draft of the test positions before shipping). Suite: 283/283.
  v0.1651 - Two things, both per Lynn. (1) NEW setting: Disable scratch pad -- finally implements `disableScratchPad` from the spreadsheet's original planned-settings list (explicitly deferred since the SETTING_BUCKETS comment that first listed it). App Theme (General) bucket, per profiles-note-styles-plan-v2.md's own classification ("the pad is an app surface, not a note") -- ready to fold into a named App Theme once that save/apply UI exists, e.g. a senior-citizen-oriented theme that hides the pad since it uses a smaller font and lacks the note pane's full feature set. New Settings toggle in the App Theme section, right after the edge-swipe-home row. openScratchPad() itself refuses to open when disabled (defense in depth beyond just gating the gesture/click handlers), and turning the setting on force-closes the pad immediately if it happens to be open, via a new closeScratchPadHook (same late-assigned-hook pattern as the existing refreshScratchPad). (2) NEW: mouse support for the scratch pad, which previously only had touch-swipe open/close gestures with no mouse equivalent. A plain click within 12px of the note content pane's right edge (measured against #note-content-area's own bounding rect, not the whole window) opens it; a plain click on the pad's handle closes it. Both gated on `(hover: hover)` -- the same media feature this app already uses everywhere else to mean "a real mouse/trackpad is attached" -- so neither adds any new tap target on touch-only devices; the existing swipe gestures are completely unchanged and coexist safely (a simple tap's touchend never reaches the swipe-distance threshold, so it does nothing extra there). Suite unchanged: 277/277.
  v0.1650 - REVERTED v0.1649 (dictation-newline smart indent), per Lynn's on-device test: it occasionally worked, but when it did, dictation got stuck silently buffering words until she manually toggled the mic off/on -- an active regression to dictation itself, worse than not having the feature at all. Likely cause: this app already recognizes 'insertFromSpeech' as a real inputType (see the beforeinput handler that predates this feature), confirming dictation keeps its own live session state tied to the field while listening -- the feature's raw `ed.value = ...` reassignment is a fundamentally different operation from a real keystroke and likely desynced that session. Not something to keep guessing at blind in the sandbox; a future attempt, if wanted, should try document.execCommand('insertText', ...) instead, which goes through the same insertion path a real keystroke uses -- but that too would need an on-device test, not just code existing. Suite unchanged: 277/277.
  v0.1649 - NEW (experimental): smart indent / auto continue / continueList now also fires for DICTATED "new line" pauses, per Lynn -- the existing keydown-based smart indent only ever saw a real Enter keypress, and iOS dictation inserts a newline directly with no corresponding keydown, so it was invisible to that path. Separate, narrowly-scoped 'input' listener on the main note editor (not the Edit Paragraph dialog, at least for now): only continues the list if (a) exactly one bare newline appeared with nothing else changed in the same edit, and (b) nothing further arrives during a 500ms pause window afterward, per Lynn's explicit request (she pauses after saying "new line"; continuous dictation through it should not trigger this). Two independent guards against the real risk this design is built around -- corrupting a multi-line PASTE by splicing list markers into the middle of it: inputType exclusion (insertFromPaste/insertFromDrop/delete*/history*/insertReplacementText all skip outright) plus a strict value-diff requiring the ENTIRE change to be exactly one inserted '\n', which any real paste fails regardless of inputType. Verified in the sandbox with synthetic input events covering all three cases (isolated newline continues correctly; a dispatched insertFromPaste multi-line block is completely untouched; a newline immediately followed by more input cancels the pending continuation) -- but this is the one feature this session that could NOT be verified end-to-end, since genuine iOS dictation event timing/shape can't be simulated here; on-device testing is what actually confirms it works, not this code existing. Suite unchanged: 277/277 (no new pure-function cases -- this is DOM-event-driven, not a testable pure function, same as the existing keydown smart indent).
  v0.1648 - FIX: numbered-list child items weren't restarting at 1, per Lynn's bug report -- confirmed via both the Indent/Unindent toolbar buttons (applyIndentToSelectedBlocks(), which just prepends/removes literal leading spaces -- no bug found there) and manually typing leading spaces in the textarea, both hitting the same root cause: computeOrderedListNumbers() honored whatever digit was already typed when starting ANY new nesting level, not just a genuinely fresh run -- so indenting an existing "4. Fourth" (digit left unchanged) kept showing "4." instead of restarting at "1." like every other outliner/word processor. Fixed: the typed start number is now honored ONLY when the stack was completely empty before the push (a real fresh run, e.g. "5. 5. 5." at the very top of a list, or the first item after a paragraph break); a new level nested under an existing parent always starts at 1, full stop. 3 new cases (v21) covering Lynn's exact scenario, the still-working fresh-run start-number case, and a return-then-renest case; existing 274 cases didn't happen to cover this gap. Suite: 277/277.
  v0.1647 - NEW: "Numbered List Sampler" built-in sample note (fixed id note-ordered-list-sampler, lives in the Sample Notes folder), demonstrating v0.1646's numbered-list auto-renumbering: basic sequential renumbering, typed-digits-after-the-first-are-ignored, three-level nesting with resume-not-restart, both '.'/')' punctuation styles, a paragraph breaking one list into two, bold/italic/link formatting inside list items, and a live spot to try smart indent (auto continue). Installed both on fresh install and via Settings > Reinstall Sample Notes (createInitialNotes() is the same function behind both) -- follows the if (!notes[id]) guard pattern the most recently added samples (poem/fun/dyslexia/why-remember) already use, which is inherently reinstall-safe: since it never reassigns an id that already exists, a rename or edit survives Reinstall automatically with no dependency on the separate snapshot/restore wrapper older fixed-id samples need. Verified directly: created once, renamed, reinstalled -- rename survived, no duplicate. Suite unchanged: 274/274 on cases v20 / goldens v21.
  v0.1646 - NEW: numbered (ordered) markdown lists, "Option 2" per Lynn's own framing: 1./1) markers now auto-renumber in R&R view (only the first item of a run sets the start number, matching standard markdown/Word/Google Docs behavior -- "5. 5. 5." displays "5. 6. 7.", and deleting/reordering a middle item just works without retyping everything below it), WITH nesting (independent counter per indent depth, resuming -- not restarting -- a shallower level after a deeper sub-list ends). Deliberately NOT wired into isMarkerLineRaw/isCheckboxRaw -- the shared classifier Collapsible Outline/Move mode/indent-dedent/sort-by-tag/tag-sort-insert all depend on -- so numbered items don't collapse/move/tag yet ("Option 3", deferred by Lynn's explicit choice, not forgotten: computeOrderedListNumbers()/getOrderedListMarker() are their own single-source-of-truth pair, mirroring the isMarkerLineRaw/findTagOrderLine pattern, specifically so teaching isMarkerLineRaw to recognize these lines later is a small addition, not a rewrite). Rendering reuses getDefaultMarkColor() for the marker color (same default-color philosophy as bullets/checkboxes) and inherits inline bold/link formatting for free (the marker-replace happens on the already-formatted line, same technique bullets use). ALSO: smart indent (aka auto continue / continueList / continue list -- see the extensive alias comments added to getSmartIndentPrefix() and both its keydown call sites, since a prior build in this same chat incorrectly claimed this feature didn't exist at all -- it does, for bullets/checkboxes, and now for numbers too) now continues numbered lists on Enter, incrementing THIS line's own typed number by 1 -- deliberately independent of the view-mode cross-line renumbering, since typing mode always shows literal typed digits. Same empty-item-ends-the-list behavior as bullets (an empty "9. " does not continue), and _siCheckAndClear() needed zero changes -- it already tracked prefix length generically, not specific characters. New cases v20 (+18: getOrderedListMarker, computeOrderedListNumbers, and the numbered branch of getSmartIndentPrefix) / goldens v21, all hand-verified against the design before acceptance. Suite: 274/274.
  v0.1645 - NEW: [text](url) markdown links now render as a clean clickable link showing just the text, in R&R (view) mode only -- per Lynn, following up on the paste-bold fix. Typing mode is untouched (it's just the raw textarea, never routed through urlsToLinks). Implemented as a placeholder-extraction pass in urlsToLinks(), the SAME established technique already used for markdown images and inline code spans: runs after code-span extraction (so link-looking syntax typed literally inside a code span stays literal) and before bold/italic (link text isn't re-processed for nested markdown, matching how image alt text is already handled). No lookbehind used (unsupported before Safari 16.4) -- unneeded anyway since image syntax is already consumed into placeholders by the time this pass runs, so it can't double-match. Display-text character class excludes both [ and ] (not just ]) -- caught during testing against Lynn's actual pasted citation-list text, where a doubled bracket like "[[1](url), [2](url)]" (a common AI-citation wrapping convention, not standard markdown) would otherwise swallow the stray outer bracket into the first link's visible text; the fix leaves that outer bracket as harmless literal punctuation next to a clean link instead. Bare https:// URLs and markdown images are unaffected (verified: bare-URL autolinking, image rendering, checkbox lines, and [tag order] lines all produce identical output to before). Suite unchanged: 256/256 on cases v19 / goldens v20.
  v0.1644 - FIX: pasting rich HTML (e.g. an AI-generated response or webpage) that contains both formatting AND at least one link silently lost ALL bold/italic, reported by Lynn. Root cause, confirmed by direct testing (not the leading-# theory she suspected -- verified that's unrelated: isHeadingRaw/getHeadingLevel are evaluated fresh per line with no shared state, so a heading or bare # cannot affect unrelated bold elsewhere): the paste handler's extractText() -- which only engages at all when the pasted HTML contains a <a href> link -- walked the DOM and kept plain textContent for every element except <a>, discarding <b>/<strong>/<i>/<em> entirely rather than re-encoding them as the app's own **bold**/*italic* markup. Fixed by mapping those four tags to their markup wrapper (nested tags compose naturally: <strong><em> -> ***text***); everything else extractText already did (link handling, block-tag newlines) is unchanged. Note: [text](url)-style markdown links are still NOT specially rendered -- urlsToLinks only auto-links bare URLs it finds in text, so a citation like "[1](https://...)" still shows the raw URL next to literal bracket punctuation rather than a clean "1" link; that's a separate, larger ask Lynn raised but didn't request yet. Suite unchanged: 256/256 on cases v19 / goldens v20.
  v0.1643 - Removed the "PASTE DIAG" toast + logSys block from the main note editor's native paste handler, per Lynn: pasting text containing links (e.g. from a webpage) popped a persistent, tap-to-copy toast listing every link found, with no remaining purpose. It dated to v0.1530/1531, investigating iOS's popup-Paste percent-encoding behavior; that investigation was superseded by v0.1636's explicit Paste button, which bypasses this handler's link branch entirely via navigator.clipboard.readText(). It only ever observed and never changed paste behavior, so removing it changes nothing about how paste actually works -- only that it now completes silently. _diagChecksum/_diagSample helpers untouched (still used by the separate, silent COPY-PLAIN/COPY-SPEECH diagnostics elsewhere). Suite unchanged: 256/256 on cases v19 / goldens v20.
  v0.1642 - FIX: init() crashed entirely on iOS 12 Safari, reported by Lynn testing an old iPhone. Cause: window.matchMedia(...).addEventListener('change', ...) for system dark-mode detection -- addEventListener() on a MediaQueryList wasn't added until Safari 14/iOS 14; iOS 12 only has the older addListener() method. Since nothing caught the throw at that point, EVERY listener registered later in init() (every toolbar button, context menu, dialog) silently never got wired up -- far bigger than just dark-mode switching, which is presumably why it "seemed to work" but was actually missing most interactivity underneath. Fixed with a feature-detected fallback: addEventListener when available, else the older addListener. Not a general iOS-12 compatibility audit -- just this one reported, confirmed crash; other legacy-Safari gaps may still exist and aren't ruled out. Suite unchanged: 256/256 on cases v19 / goldens v20.
  v0.1641 - R&R Select Lines mode gets range-select, per Lynn's request for a way to select many lines without requiring a drag (senior-user constraint). TWO independent additions, both purely additive -- ordinary tap-to-toggle is completely unchanged, so scattered/non-contiguous selection still works exactly as before: (1) Mobile: long-press a line to mark it the pending range anchor (added to the selection immediately, shown with a dashed amber outline via new .range-anchor-pending CSS) -- delegated through attachLongPress()'s new optional `selector` param (backward compatible, all 3 existing call sites unaffected) so it survives note re-renders with no re-attaching needed. The very next ordinary tap, on any line, selects everything between the anchor and that line inclusive; tapping the anchor itself cancels range mode and falls through to a normal toggle. The iOS synthesized click that follows a long-press's touchend is swallowed via the SAME e.preventDefault()-on-fired pattern note-item long-press already uses -- without it, that synthetic click would immediately cancel the range mode it had just armed. (2) Desktop/trackpad: shift-click extends from the last line involved in any select-mode tap, matching the standard file-manager convention -- real Shift is never present on a touch tap, so this is a pure addition invisible to mobile use. Overshooting a range needs no special undo: any extra lines come out with ordinary individual taps, same as scattered selection always has. Deliberately NOT built yet (per Lynn, ship the basic version first and see if it's needed): re-long-pressing to redraw/redo a range rather than trim it manually. Suite unchanged: 256/256 on cases v19 / goldens v20.
  v0.1640 - Add Text to Note dialog: (1) removed the "(- )" and example checkbox glyph from the end of the hyphen/checkbox marker labels -- both were purely illustrative and wrapped confusingly onto their own line at large font sizes; the checkbox label's now-unneeded wrapper <span> was removed along with it. (2) Added a scroll-shadow indicator to the scrollable middle region (.qa-scroll-fade, new CSS class) using the well-known zero-JS "CSS scroll shadows" technique: two solid fade gradients pinned to the CONTENT (background-attachment: local) sit in front of two soft shadow gradients pinned to the VIEWPORT (background-attachment: scroll) -- a shadow only becomes visible at an edge once its fade has scrolled out of the way, i.e. exactly when there's more content past that edge, with no scroll-position listener needed. Suite unchanged: 247/247 on cases v18 / goldens v19.
  v0.1639 - Add Text to Note dialog polish, per Lynn's first-look feedback on v0.1638's scrollable middle region: (1) the native scrollbar was overlapping the Paste button and textarea -- gave #quick-add-scroll its own 16px lane via padding-right + equal negative margin-right, which relocates the scrollbar without narrowing the dialog's visual width; (2) added padding-bottom so the last visible row (checkbox or not) never sits flush against the scroll edge. Suite unchanged: 247/247 on cases v18 / goldens v19.
  v0.1638 - Add Text to Note dialog: collapsible "More options" section holds all six checkboxes (blank line before/after, dividing line, hyphen, checkbox marker, and v0.1637's tag-sort toggle, which gets extra top margin to set it apart from the other five). Reuses the existing .settings-section/-header/-chevron/-body CSS (same look as Settings/Help, not scoped to #settings-sheet) but with its own standalone toggle handler and a fully-tappable header, NOT the Settings screen's section-persistence system (sectionStates/jump-nav/search-expand) -- that's built for a whole navigable sheet and isn't a fit for one dialog control. Always starts collapsed on open for now (sticky may come later, per Lynn). Also fixes a real bug this surfaced: the Cancel/Add button row could get clipped at the bottom on smaller screens because .confirm-box (max-height: 80vh, overflow: hidden) had no internal scroll region of its own. Restructured so the title and button row are pinned (flex-shrink: 0) and everything between them (position picker, textarea, Paste button, More options) scrolls as one region -- same pattern .confirm-msg already uses for plain confirm dialogs -- so the buttons can no longer be clipped regardless of how tall the content gets. Suite: 247/247 on cases v18 / goldens v19 (new buildTagOrderMap coverage from the previous build; _qaTagSortTarget remains untested pending a harness fix -- see that build's changelog entry).
  v0.1637 - NEW: Add Text to Note dialog gets an "Insert tagged items in sorted order" checkbox, shown only when the current note already has a real @tag and a pre-existing [tag order] line (reuses _getNoteTags/findTagOrderLine -- no new detection). When checked, each new checkbox-ish line whose first @tag is on the [tag order] line is placed before the first existing item whose own primary tag is equal-or-later in tag order (or after the last matching-tag item, if none sort later); items with no usable tag fall back silently to the position chosen above. New pure helpers buildTagOrderMap() and _qaTagSortTarget() reuse isChecklistLine()/primaryTagOfLine() rather than reimplementing checkbox-ish or tag detection. Multiple sorted insertions (plus any leftover fallback content) are combined into one list of anchored ops and applied bottom-to-top through the existing computeChecklistInsertion() pipeline, so each op's anchor index -- computed once against the original note -- stays valid without manual reindexing; indentation and double-spacing (smartIndentBlankLine) are therefore inherited automatically from that same shared pipeline, not reimplemented here. Sticky preference: settings.plusSortedByTag, only updated when the checkbox was actually shown. Suite unchanged: 243/243 on cases v17 / goldens v18.
  v0.1636 - Android paste, round 3 -- THE PRODUCT ANSWER. Lynn's v0.1635 gesture log was decisive: three genuine long-presses (2116/2991/1768 ms) reached quick-add-input cleanly (matched touchstart/touchend pairs, focused=true throughout, nothing cancelled the gesture), Android still showed no paste popup, no contextmenu event ever fired, and the v0.1635 user-select ancestor-chain fix did not cure it. Conclusion: the failure lives in the OS/keyboard selection layer (possibly Samsung-keyboard-specific -- its clipboard tends to hide behind the keyboard toolbar's expander), which page code can neither observe nor fix. Correction to the v0.1635 interpretation guide: Android Chrome does not reliably fire a DOM contextmenu event for text-field long-presses even when its popup IS working, so 'no contextmenu line' alone must not be read as proof the gesture was eaten. Instead of a fourth OS-layer theory, this build adds an explicit PASTE BUTTON to both dialogs (below each textarea, styled like the qa-pos-btn row), using the async Clipboard API inside the button's own user gesture: reads text, inserts at the caret (replacing any selection), restores focus with the caret after the insertion, and dispatches a real input event so dirty-tracking, smart features, and the Bold Cursor all update through their normal paths. Failure modes handled loudly: clipboard API unavailable, permission denied (persistent toast pointing at site permissions), and empty clipboard. This also removes the OS 'secret handshake' (long-press vs caret-handle vs keyboard chip, all platform-specific) for the app's senior audience on EVERY platform -- the original UX concern behind this whole investigation. The gesture diagnostics stay in one more build. Suite unchanged: 243/243 on cases v17 / goldens v18.
  v0.1635 - Android paste, round 2, from Lynn's decisive new report: the paste POPUP never appears at all in Quick Add / Edit Paragraph on Android -- she cannot even start a paste, so the v0.1634 event tracer stays silent by design (its silence correctly indicated the failure is in the OS selection layer, before any DOM event). Candidate fix shipped: body defaults to user-select:none with selective opt-ins that covered the FIELDS (input,textarea rule) but never the dialogs' ANCESTOR chains -- Android's OS-drawn selection/paste UI consults ancestors, and long-press in an EMPTY field is its most fragile path (note-editor escapes this because pasting there targets existing text: tap caret, handle, Paste). New scoped CSS opts #quick-add-box / #edit-block-dialog and their input wraps + textareas back to user-select:text (labels inside become cosmetically selectable; buttons unaffected). NOT verifiable off-device -- Lynn's next Android test is the experiment. Also added GESTURE-level diagnostics that fire regardless of whether any popup appears: touchstart (touch count), touchend (press duration in ms -- a long-press shows as 500ms+), contextmenu (Android fires this for text-field long-press; logged and never preventDefaulted), and focus, on both dialog textareas. Interpretation: no touch lines = touches never reach the textarea (something overlays it); touchend with 500ms+ but no contextmenu = the long-press gesture is being eaten before the OS menu stage; contextmenu fires but no popup = OS suppressed its own UI (the user-select fix is then the likeliest cure). Additional popup-independent test paths for Lynn: the Gboard clipboard chip (clipboard icon in the keyboard's toolbar) and Ctrl+V on any attached keyboard -- if either inserts text, insertion itself works and ONLY the popup layer is broken. Suite unchanged: 243/243 on cases v17 / goldens v18.
  v0.1634 - Two things for the Quick Add / Edit Paragraph dialogs, per Lynn's report. (1) BOLD CURSOR DRIFT, likely root cause found by inspection: the measuring mirror's width was ed.offsetWidth (the v0.1607 choice) -- correct ONLY when the textarea has no visible scrollbar. These dialogs are fixed-height, SELF-SCROLLING textareas: a long paragraph summons a scrollbar, which (on Android and desktop classic scrollbars) consumes interior width, so the REAL text wraps in a narrower box than the mirror -- every wrap lands slightly later in the mirror and the error compounds line after line, precisely matching "drifts when the paragraph is long" and "fine when short". Fixed: width is now ed.clientWidth (which excludes any scrollbar) + the two border widths -- mathematically IDENTICAL to offsetWidth when no scrollbar exists, so zero change for short text, overlay-scrollbar iOS, and the main note editor. Additionally, every remaining wrap-affecting property is now COPIED from the textarea's computed style instead of omitted or hardcoded: overflow-wrap, word-break, hyphens, font-style, font-variant-ligatures, font-kerning, font-feature-settings, font-stretch, text-indent, text-rendering (the copied overflow-wrap deliberately overrides the older hardcoded word-wrap:break-word, since the cascade takes the later declaration) -- mirroring truth can only converge, never diverge, and ligature/kerning/wrap differences between a div and a textarea are the remaining credible iOS drift source. The v0.1624 green-line diagnostic stays in for on-device verification. (2) ANDROID PASTE in these dialogs: code inspection PROVED there is no app-side paste interceptor on either textarea (no paste/beforeinput preventDefault paths reach a paste, no readonly, no touch-action, no user-select, mark overlay is pointer-events:none) -- so rather than guess, this build adds a debug-gated paste-path tracer (paste / beforeinput / input / compositionstart / compositionend, logging inputType, data length, and value-length deltas) on both dialog textareas. Lynn's next Android session will show exactly which stage dies: no events at all (OS gesture/popup layer), events but no value change (app interference), or change-then-revert. Suite unchanged: 243/243 on cases v17 / goldens v18.
  v0.1633 - Two things, both per Lynn. (1) NEW: "Rename note" in the home-screen long-press context menu (right under Open note), reusing the SAME rename sheet the in-note title tap opens -- select-all and clear buttons, dirty tracking, duplicate-name confirm, autoNamed handling all included, one dialog for both entry points instead of a second implementation. openRenameDialog()/applyRename() gained a target-note parameter (_renameTargetNoteId, defaulting to currentNoteId so every existing call site is unchanged); the sheet already lived at top level in the DOM so it works from the home screen as-is, and renaming now refreshes the notes list. Renaming view-only notes is allowed from the menu, matching the existing in-note behavior (view-only protects content, not the name). (2) FIX for the long-deferred iOS no-keyboard-on-rename problem, root cause finally identified: every name dialog focused its input inside setTimeout(...,50), and iOS WebKit only shows the virtual keyboard for a focus() that runs SYNCHRONOUSLY within the user's tap gesture -- an async focus sets the caret but deliberately suppresses the keyboard (Apple anti-popup policy; Android/desktop honor async focus, which is exactly the asymmetry Lynn observed). Fixed platform-conditionally exactly as she proposed: new _isIOSDevice() helper (userAgent + the MacIntel/maxTouchPoints check for iPadOS masquerading as Mac); on iOS both the rename sheet and the folder-name dialog now focus+select synchronously in the gesture, everywhere else the original setTimeout path is untouched -- zero behavior change on Android/Windows, where it already worked. Physical keyboards: text arrives selected (select-all for auto-named/folder, caret-at-end for user-named notes, both unchanged), so typing replaces immediately. No pure-function changes: suite 243/243 on cases v17 / goldens v18.
  v0.1632 - Find/Replace split into two More-menu items per Lynn (the common editor pattern): "Find in note" opens the bar find-only (replace row hidden -- one row shorter on a phone), and a new "Find and replace" item right below it opens the same bar with the replace row shown. The new item is registry-managed and hidden on view-only notes (plus a runtime guard in openFindReplace and the existing CSS hide as belt-and-braces). The bar remembers nothing between modes except the query: switching items while the bar is open just toggles the row in place, keeping the search. Toolbar find button and Cmd/Ctrl+F stay find-only; NEW Cmd/Ctrl+H toggles Find and replace (standard shortcut), ignored on view-only notes. Implemented as a _findReplaceMode flag + a replace-mode class on the find bar; openFind(keepReplaceMode) resets the flag unless called from openFindReplace(), so every existing openFind() call site is find-only automatically. closeFind resets the mode. No pure-function changes: suite unchanged at 243/243 on cases v17 / goldens v18.
  v0.1631 - NEW: Replace added to Find in note. The find bar gains a second row (hidden on view-only notes) with a "Replace with" field plus Replace and All buttons; Enter in the replace field replaces the current match. Replace-current swaps the highlighted match and lands on the next match AFTER the replaced text (so a replacement that contains the search text, like cat -> cats, can never loop or re-match in place); Replace All swaps every occurrence in one action with a toast reporting the count. Matching is the same case-insensitive literal (non-regex) search Find has always used -- the scan was extracted verbatim from runFind() into a shared findMatchPositions() helper so Find and Replace can never disagree about what counts as a match, plus a pure applyReplaceAll() (both harness-testable; 10 new cases lock literal-matching semantics incl. regex chars staying literal, adjacent matches, case-insensitive replace, deletion via empty replacement, and replacement-contains-query safety -- cases v17, 243 total, goldens v18). Both operations pushUndo first; Replace All additionally snapshots via snapshotNoteBeforeOp. Works in both Typing and R&R modes through the existing find machinery (offsets are recomputed from content at replace time since R&R stores DOM marks, and both lists come from the same ordered scan). Housekeeping note for a future build: isHrLine() (which pre-dates this build) and the sort module's inline HR regex are duplicate expressions of the same pattern and should be consolidated.
  v0.1630 - FIX per Lynn's bug report: Settings > Reinstall Sample Notes is now purely ADDITIVE. createInitialNotes() historically OVERWROTE anything sharing an id with a sample: the Sample Notes folder record (which is exactly why renaming that folder appeared to "delete" it on reinstall -- the rename lives on the folder object, and the object was replaced wholesale, resetting the stock name and any parent move), every fixed-id sample note (destroying user edits to them), and every fixed-id sample theme -- directly contradicting the Reinstall dialog's own "Your existing notes will not be affected" promise. Implemented as a snapshot-and-restore wrapper inside createInitialNotes rather than ~20 per-registration guards: everything existing at entry (notes, folders, themes) is snapshotted, a restoration pass just before the final saves puts back any record this run replaced (preserving renames, folder moves, edits, and custom themes), and newly created notes whose EXACT name already exists are dropped so reinstall never makes duplicates (Lynn's unique-names suggestion) -- deleted samples still reinstall, and renamed copies coexist with the restored stock original. No-op on fresh installs. logSys reports preserved/skipped counts. No behavior change to any tested function: suite still 233/233 on cases v16 / goldens v17.
  v0.1629 - Tag Sort Sampler rebuilt per Lynn's testing feedback: ~46 checkbox-ish items across 12 top-level parents, nesting now FIVE levels deep (great-great-grandchildren), every parent has 3-4 children mixing tagged and untagged, and NOTHING starts pre-sorted -- child lists are deliberately scrambled and untagged items sit in FIRST/middle positions (never already at the end) so the sort visibly moves things. Several sibling groups share the same primary tag (including three @prio3 top-level parents) to verify stable ordering, and plain paragraphs are interspersed between three separate item groups to verify consolidation around stationary text. Also one Sort-by-tag refinement the new sampler exposed: separator blank lines now join the sorted region ONLY when they sit between two region lines -- a blank run trailing stationary content (e.g. a paragraph between item groups) stays in place with that content instead of traveling into the consolidated list as a stray gap (was visible in double-spacing-OFF mode when groups merged across paragraphs). Two new test cases lock the rule (cases v16, 233 total; goldens v17). Note the v0.1628 migration flag is unchanged, so a device that already ran the v0.1628 migration keeps the OLD sampler (delete it and use Settings, Reinstall Sample Notes, to get this one).
  v0.1628 - NEW: "Sort by tag" in the More menu (works in BOTH Typing and R&R modes) sorts checkbox-ish items to match the note's [tag order] line, keyed by each item's FIRST tag; children travel with their parent and are sorted among themselves; untagged items sink to the bottom of their own sibling list (after a confirm dialog); all items consolidate at the position of the first item while every other line stays put; sorting only selected lines works via the Select Lines tool OR a live text selection (a new snapshot in openMoreMenu captures the selection before the menu's existing selection-clearing destroys it). PURE-PERMUTATION design agreed with Lynn: the result is exactly the original lines reordered -- nothing added/removed/edited -- and three sanity checks (line count, checkbox-ish count, full multiset comparison) refuse to touch the note and log to the system log on any mismatch. Blank-line handling honors the double-spacing setting (ON: separator blanks pooled and redistributed one-per-seam; OFF: blanks glue to the item they followed). Original preserved TWICE before applying: snapshotNoteBeforeOp ring + a visible backup note named with an em-dash "before sort N" suffix (always numbered, never overwritten, same folder). Structural lines are never treated as items: the [tag order] line and horizontal rules (the same pattern notePreview already skips) stay in place -- detection itself is ONLY the canonical isChecklistLine(), never reimplemented. New "Tag Sort Sampler" demo note (createInitialNotes for fresh installs + a one-time STORAGE-FLAG-gated migration for existing installs, flag not name so deleting it sticks) showcasing the locked built-in tag roster (@inbox @prio0 @asap @prio1-9 @prioSomeday @prioUnset primaries; @next @today @inProgress @mightBeDone @waiting @wontDo @done secondaries). New test files: cases v15 (+21 cases = 231), goldens v16.
  v0.1627 - REAL fix for the black-square multi-level bullet, per Lynn's report that v0.1625's U+FE0E fix did not work. Researched properly this time (v0.1625's theory was backwards): U+25AA's DEFAULT presentation is already text/monochrome per Unicode's own reference -- appending U+FE0E was a no-op that never addressed the real cause. Far more likely cause: Atkinson Hyperlegible (this app's chosen accessibility font) is a text-focused typeface unlikely to include the rarely-used Geometric Shapes Unicode block -- if the glyph is missing, the browser substitutes a fallback font for just that character, and some fallback/symbol fonts render shapes with their own fixed color, ignoring inherited CSS color entirely. Sidestepped the whole font-substitution problem: the third-level bullet (reordered to index 2 per Lynn's request, was index 1) is now drawn as an actual CSS box (.viewer-bullet-box, sized proportionally to font-size) instead of relying on any font's glyph for it -- exactly what Lynn originally asked for, "just a plain flat square that uses a single flat color."
  v0.1626 - CORRECTION, no code change needed: v0.1625's color fix was correct and IS working (confirmed by Lynn), but the changelog/comments mislabeled which level the square bullet belongs to. The _bullets array is ['•', '▪︎', '◦', '‣', '⁃'] -- zero-indexed, so the square is index 1 (the SECOND level), never the third as every earlier round of this investigation assumed and stated. Lynn's own testing caught this by noticing the square moved from where they remembered it (their original report was about "third level," but the square was always level 2 -- the earlier bug reports and this file's own comments simply had the wrong level number attached to a real, correctly-diagnosed color bug). No behavior change in this build; recorded so the level number is accurate in future reference rather than perpetuating the mislabel.
  v0.1625 - Fixed the third-level (square) multi-level bullet not showing its default color on iOS, per Lynn's precise description ("black square... gradient... 3D look... no hint of color").
