Root cause: U+25AA (BLACK SMALL SQUARE) is a known emoji-presentation character on iOS -- Apple's emoji font renders it as a small glyph with its own built-in shading instead of plain flat text, and CSS color has zero effect on an emoji glyph, since it's rendered as a tiny image rather than colorable text. Fixed with U+FE0E (the Unicode text-presentation selector) appended right after the character, forcing plain flat-text rendering in the current color, matching the other levels. Also expanded the bullet cycle from 3 to 5 styles per Lynn's request (added ‣ and ⁃), with the modulo now derived from the array's own length instead of a hardcoded 3, so it stays correct if the set size changes again. Not added to the automated regression suite -- this behavior depends on global settings state (showMultiLevelBullets) that the current test harness has no mechanism to pre-set before a case runs; verified instead via a direct jsdom smoke test for this build.
  v0.1624 - DIAGNOSTIC ONLY, for Lynn's report of an intermittent ~200px Y gap in Quick Add that the full mirror overlay was too hard to visually verify (it covers the real dialog content entirely, making a direct comparison difficult). Log analysis of the same session found scrollTopAtCall=-1 reappearing for quick-add-input during a rapid multi-step scroll sequence (782->419->84->84->-1->0->35) while Lynn was scrolling within a long pasted paragraph -- the same iOS elastic-overscroll condition already clamped at the source, but never specifically confirmed to hold through an active, fast multi-step scroll episode in the dialogs. Added a simpler diagnostic than the full mirror: a bright green line drawn directly ON the real dialog at the mark's exact computed Y, visible whenever the debug toggle is on -- directly overlays the real content instead of competing with it, making any Y mismatch immediately and unambiguously visible even during active scrolling, without needing to compare two separate text blocks.
  v0.1623 - Two things. (1) REAL FIX for Quick Add/Edit Paragraph losing the bold cursor after the very first render, per Lynn's log: computed top=8 (correct) but visibleTop=-70 due to a nonzero vvOffsetTop=77.65625 -- but these dialogs are FIXED-POSITION overlays whose own coordinate space is already anchored to the visual viewport, unlike note-editor's panning layout-viewport content. The v0.1619 offsetTop subtraction was correct for note-editor but wrongly applied to the dialogs too, double-counting an offset that doesn't apply to fixed-position elements -- exactly matching "works once at open (offsetTop still 0) breaks once the keyboard settles (offsetTop becomes nonzero)." Scoped the subtraction to note-editor only. Note this was ONLY a sanity-check bug, never a real position error -- getCursorXYFor's own math never referenced offsetTop at all, so the dialogs' mark was likely correctly positioned the whole time and just being wrongly hidden. (2) Per Lynn's explicit decision, recorded Bold Cursor bleeding through the More menu and scratch pad on ANDROID specifically as an ACCEPTED KNOWN QUIRK, not planned for further investigation -- same underlying Android compositor/z-index behavior already documented in this codebase for OS-drawn selection handles (a pre-existing issue that predates Bold Cursor entirely). The Android note-editor cursor desync Lynn also newly reported remains open -- no Android-specific log data yet to diagnose it from.
  v0.1622 - Two things. (1) PRAGMATIC fix for the sanity check's persistent false-positive: three consecutive attempts at fixing visualViewport's own height-reporting timing (v0.1619 offsetTop subtraction, v0.1620 direct read, v0.1621 event-driven cache) all failed identically per Lynn's repeated logs, always the same four lines, always a modest 30-90px overshoot. Rather than attempt a fourth timing fix against an evidently elusive iOS quirk, widened the bottom-edge tolerance to 100px (roughly 3+ lines) -- a borderline near-bottom overshoot is a weak signal not worth repeatedly hiding an otherwise-correct cursor over; top/left/right bounds keep their original strict tolerance. (2) Fixed Bold Cursor bleeding through the scratch pad (both iOS and Android per Lynn's report) -- a real, previously untouched gap; wired the same hide-on-open/show-on-close pattern already used for the More menu. The Android More-menu bleed-through Lynn also reported remains open -- the existing fix's code is platform-agnostic and its call site is confirmed correctly wired, so this is likely a genuine Android-specific rendering quirk (this app already documents Android's OS-drawn selection handles ignoring normal z-index in this exact function) rather than a gap in the fix itself; needs an Android-side log/screenshot before guessing further.
  v0.1621 - REAL fix for the sanity check's false-positive, this time confirmed against Lynn's log DISPROVING v0.1620: taVisibleHeight=409 STILL persisted with vvOffsetTop=0 even after switching to a direct, synchronous visualViewport.height read -- ruling out the "stale clientHeight" theory entirely. The actual cause: visualViewport.height ITSELF can lag its own resize event on iOS -- offsetTop (driven by scroll/pan repositioning) snaps back to 0 quickly on keyboard dismiss, but .height can genuinely update a beat later, and a synchronous read at an arbitrary moment can land in that gap. Fixed by no longer reading .height cold: added a module-level cache (_bcCachedViewportHeight) updated ONLY when visualViewport's own real 'resize' event fires, so the sanity check always compares against a value known to be current as of the last actual viewport change, never a mid-transition snapshot that races against iOS's own event timing.
  v0.1620 - REAL FIX for the sanity check's remaining false-positive, from Lynn's precise report (works when the keyboard is showing, fails when it's closed) and the log proving it directly: taVisibleHeight=409 (the keyboard-SHRUNK value) persisted on lines where vvOffsetTop=0 (keyboard genuinely closed, viewport back to full height) -- an internally impossible combination if both were read fresh together, meaning clientHeight itself was the STALE value in that moment, not visualViewport. Root cause: the old code only ever let visualViewport.height shrink taVisibleHeight downward, assuming clientHeight was always the more-current, larger value -- but this session showed clientHeight lagging behind after a keyboard-dismiss animation, still reporting the keyboard-open height after the keyboard had actually closed. Fixed by trusting visualViewport.height directly as the primary visible-height source whenever available, rather than only allowing it to shrink a value that can itself be wrong.
  v0.1619 - REAL FIX for the sanity check's remaining false-positive, from Lynn's log showing a consistent ~35px overshoot (visibleBottom=444 vs taVisibleHeight=409) repeating across nearly every failure, plus Lynn's precise behavioral report (works once right after opening a note, breaks after dismissing the keyboard and tapping again, only fixed by a fresh note-open). Root cause: visualViewport doesn't just SHRINK when the keyboard is up, it also SHIFTS DOWN by offsetTop -- the check never subtracted this, so the "visible window" it compared against was anchored at the wrong vertical origin whenever offsetTop was nonzero. Verified arithmetically against Lynn's actual numbers before writing the fix: subtracting a plausible offsetTop brings the overshoot back to zero, consistent with a real, changing offsetTop value already visible elsewhere in the same log (69 in one successful state, 0 in another). Fixed by subtracting visualViewport.offsetTop from visibleTop. Also added vvOffsetTop to the failure log itself (previously only captured on SUCCESS via bc-keyboard-check, never at the moment of an actual failure) so the next log shows the real value directly if this doesn't fully resolve it.
  v0.1618 - REAL FIX for the sanity check itself, from Lynn's log: it WAS firing for note-editor (v0.1617's exemption theory was wrong), nearly succeeding each time (only ~53px over) -- but that "near miss" was an illusion. ta.clientHeight for note-editor is the ENTIRE document's height (~4844px, matching a long note's real length), not the visible screen -- confirmed against the same log's OWN successful line, which showed wrapperClientHeight=812 (a real, screen-sized number) alongside the failing lines' taVisibleHeight=4844. The check was comparing two document-scale numbers that happen to land close together near the END of a long note, never actually validating against the visible viewport. Fixed by using scrollAncestor (the wrapper) as the visible-height/scroll source when it's the real scroller (same "which element actually matters" distinction already applied to the position math itself), then further clamping to visualViewport.height when the keyboard makes even that too generous (Lynn's log: vvHeight=409 vs wrapperClientHeight=812 with the keyboard open).
  v0.1617 - DIAGNOSTIC ONLY, no behavior change, for Lynn's newest report: the last ~4 lines of a long note (near/under the iOS keyboard) show the native cursor but not the bold one, specifically when tapping there after scrolling. Checked the existing sanity check first -- it can't be the cause here: for note-editor, ta.scrollTop is always 0 (the WRAPPER scrolls, not the textarea itself), so the check compares against the textarea's ENTIRE height, effectively never firing for this element. Working theory: the mark's position may be CORRECT (in document space) but physically covered by the OS keyboard, which iOS draws outside the web content layer entirely -- no code-side z-index can affect it. Added a targeted log (bc-keyboard-check) capturing the mark's computed top alongside visualViewport height/offsetTop and the wrapper's own scroll/visible state, to confirm or rule this out with real numbers before writing a fix (which, if confirmed, would likely mean proactively scrolling the note to keep the cursor above the keyboard -- the same principle already established elsewhere in this file for the trackpad-heal work).
  v0.1616 - THE REAL BUG, found by actually tracing the call graph instead of assuming: Lynn's screenshot showed the note-editor's bold cursor landing in the TOOLBAR area after scrolling. Investigation revealed update() (shared by all 3 Bold Cursor instances) ALWAYS calls getCursorXYFor(), never the shared getCursorXY() -- meaning v0.1605's and v0.1615's fixes to getCursorXY(), while real and correct fixes for that function's OTHER 16+ callers (nudge buttons, scroll preservation, find-in-note, trackpad heal), never had any effect on Bold Cursor at all. The actual live bug was in getCursorXYFor()'s own v0.1608 fix: it read scroll ONLY from ed (correct for the self-scrolling Quick Add/Edit Paragraph dialogs), but ed (note-editor) never scrolls itself -- the WRAPPER (note-content-area) does -- so note-editor's real scroll offset was always read as 0, exactly matching "mark renders where it would be if the note had never been scrolled" (i.e. near the top, under the toolbar). Fixed generically for both architectures at once: since exactly one of ed/scrollAncestor actually scrolls in each real case (the other is always 0 by construction), the fix now sums both clamped values instead of reading only one.
  v0.1615 - Fixed the main note editor's Y drift after scrolling, per Lynn's precise iOS PWA screenshot (correct X, wrong Y, worsening the further scrolled down). Root cause: the exact same iOS elastic-overscroll/bounce bug already found and fixed in getCursorXYFor() (v0.1613, confirmed via a deployed log showing scrollTop=-1) was never carried over to the SHARED getCursorXY() -- the function that actually backs the main note editor, caret nudge buttons, mode-switch scroll preservation, find-in-note, and trackpad heal. Clamped area.scrollTop/scrollLeft to 0 at the source here too. Note the two functions correctly use different scroll sources for their different architectures (getCursorXY reads the WRAPPER's scroll, correct since note-editor sits in a separately-scrolling div; getCursorXYFor reads the TEXTAREA's own scroll, correct since Quick Add/Edit Paragraph are self-scrolling) -- both needed the identical clamp against the same transient iOS quirk despite reading different elements. The v0.1612 sanity check never fired for this bug because the wrong position stayed WITHIN the textarea's visible bounds (a smaller, non-off-screen error) -- consistent with Lynn's own description of it as "almost correct," not wildly wrong.
  v0.1614 - Fixed Bold Cursor bleeding through the More menu, per Lynn's report. Root cause: the mark's z-index (10011, raised in v0.1601 to clear the Quick Add/Edit Paragraph dialogs' stacking at 10010) was never checked against toolbar-more-menu specifically (z-index 10000) -- a separate overlay this app already has an established fix-pattern for (the very same function already clears text selection to stop OS-drawn selection handles bleeding through the same way, for the same reason: some UI ignores normal z-index or sits at an unexpectedly high value). Rather than keep chasing every current/future overlay's z-index number, added shared hide()/show() to each Bold Cursor instance plus window._boldCursorHideAll()/_boldCursorShowAll(), called from openMoreMenu()/closeMoreMenu() directly. Also: Lynn's screenshot of the note-editor position bug showed what looks like a SEPARATE issue -- a second, differently-colored (blue, likely the native system caret) cursor at a different character position than the red mark, rather than simple wrong math on one cursor. Flagged for focused investigation next -- didn't want to guess at a fix for a bug whose actual shape may be different from every prior position bug in this investigation.
  v0.1613 - The v0.1612 sanity check worked exactly as intended: Lynn's first deployed iOS PWA test caught real, self-reporting SANITY-FAIL log lines instead of silent wrong positions. One of them revealed a genuinely impossible reading: scrollTop=-1. Negative scrollTop is physically meaningless, but iOS Safari -- especially the standalone PWA context Lynn is now testing for the first time (previously mostly tested via the Claude app browser tab) -- can transiently report negative values during elastic overscroll/bounce (the rubber-band effect past a content edge) before settling back to rest. Clamped scrollTop/scrollLeft to 0 at the source in getCursorXYFor(), and consistently in the sanity check's own read, instead of trusting a transient physically-impossible value. Other sanity-check firings in the same log (taVisibleHeight=132, consistent with quick-add-input's actual unconstrained rows=4 height) may reflect the textarea genuinely being very short relative to a long pasted paragraph, a real and separate case worth investigating next if failures continue after this fix.
  v0.1612 - Per Lynn's explicit correction (a screenshot I mis-read as a good result was actually the mark 5+ lines away from the real caret) and explicit request: added an automated sanity check that catches an impossible cursor position BEFORE it renders, instead of relying on a human to spot it in a screenshot. A caret can never legitimately sit outside its own textarea's currently-visible area -- the computed Y is translated into the textarea's own visible-viewport coordinate space and checked against [0, clientHeight]. If it fails, the mark hides and logs loudly (boldcursor-SANITY-FAIL, with the exact numbers at that instant) instead of silently drawing a known-wrong position. This does not fix the underlying cause -- it makes failures self-reporting and precisely timestamped, which the next debug log should make possible to actually diagnose, rather than reconstructing the bug from a screenshot after the fact.
  v0.1611 - REAL explanation and fix for the position jitter after opening Edit Paragraph on a long paragraph, confirmed by Lynn's v0.1610 diagnostic log: scrollTopAtCall and scrollTopNow matched on every line (the scroll READ was never stale -- three earlier timing theories, all now conclusively closed), but Lynn clarified the dialog auto-scrolls on open, not from a manual scroll -- and the log showed why: openEditBlockDialog() deliberately places the caret at the END of the text (setSelectionRange to the full length), which for a long paragraph forces the browser's OWN native scroll-to-caret. That scroll doesn't complete in one step -- the log showed the identical selection (sel=418, completely unchanged) with scrollTop drifting 170->214->211->174->...->170 over ~2.5 seconds as it settled, and update() faithfully (and correctly, for each instant) redrew the mark at every intermediate step -- individually accurate but visually jittery, and any single screenshot taken mid-settle would show a "wrong" position that was really just an in-progress native scroll animation. Fixed with a scroll-settle debounce (120ms of quiet before redrawing) replacing the previous unthrottled scroll listener.
  v0.1610 - DIAGNOSTIC ONLY, no behavior fix, after Lynn's screenshot showed the mirror correctly reflecting its own cursor position but STILL disagreeing with the real editor's native cursor after a plain scroll+tap (a different scenario than v0.1609's auto-scroll-during-typing fix, which doesn't apply here -- a deliberate scroll then tap involves no browser auto-scroll-to-caret at all). Also confirmed Lynn's observation that the debug mirror's on-screen box position doesn't reflect scroll -- expected (it's meant to sit below the dialog, not track internal scroll), but made the underlying scroll VALUES fully visible in the log instead: new bc-scroll-check log line captures scrollTop at the moment update() is called vs. inside the deferred measurement callback (to test whether it's changing between those points), and bc-mirror now logs edScrollTop/edRect.top alongside the geometry it already reports. Next log from Lynn should show definitively whether the scroll read itself is unstable, or whether the bug is elsewhere (e.g. how the mark's position gets applied after a correct measurement).
  v0.1609 - Two fixes for the remaining vertical drift Lynn found (cursor landing below the real box when text was near the bottom edge). (1) Double-rAF: a single requestAnimationFrame isn't guaranteed to run AFTER the browser's own native auto-scroll-to-caret has fully settled (it may itself be driven by a later layout pass) -- deferred to the frame after the one already scheduled, giving that browser-driven scroll a full cycle to land before ed.scrollTop is measured. (2) Per Lynn's direct observation that the mirror visibly had more padding at top/bottom than the real box: switched padding (a shorthand, same risk class as the border bug already found) to the four individual longhand properties, and added min-height/max-height to the mirror so its box height matches the real textarea's (which has min-height:12em, taller than a short paragraph's actual content) -- tests whether box-height difference was shifting where text visually starts within the box. Applied to both getCursorXYFor() and the shared getCursorXY().
  v0.1608 - REAL bug behind the bold cursor landing in the wrong place after scrolling inside Quick Add/Edit Paragraph, confirmed by Lynn's two-screenshot comparison (which also confirmed the v0.1607 wrapping fix DID work -- both boxes now wrap identically). Root cause: the position math read scrollAncestor.scrollTop for the scroll offset, but scrollAncestor is the WRAPPER <div> added in v0.1601 (needed since a <textarea> can't render child elements) -- the wrapper itself never scrolls; the TEXTAREA scrolls internally. scrollAncestor.scrollTop was always reading the wrapper's scrollTop (always 0), never the textarea's real internal scroll position. Fixed: the scroll offset now always comes from ed (the textarea) itself -- correctly a no-op for note-editor (which doesn't scroll itself) and correctly live for the self-scrolling dialogs. scrollAncestor remains used only as the positioning reference frame, never as the scroll source.
  v0.1607 - REAL bug behind the mirror being narrower than the real textarea, and WHY v0.1606's border fix made it worse instead of better -- from Lynn's screenshot showing the gap increased. Root cause: ed.clientWidth (content+padding, EXCLUDES border) was applied as a box-sizing:border-box width value (content+padding+border TOGETHER) -- double-subtracting the border from the mirror's available text space. Before v0.1606 the border was silently missing (the shorthand-serialization bug), which accidentally masked most of this by not subtracting a border at all; once v0.1606 correctly restored the border, it got double-subtracted, making the mirror measurably narrower than before -- exactly what Lynn observed. Fixed with the W3C-correct pairing: ed.offsetWidth (content+padding+border together), which matches border-box's own definition of 'width' with no double-counting, in both getCursorXYFor() and the shared getCursorXY(). Also added rounded corners (border-radius, copied from the real element) to the debug-visible mirror per Lynn's request, for a more accurate side-by-side visual comparison in future screenshots.
  v0.1606 - Bold Cursor position bug, likely the real final fix -- from Lynn's direct observation comparing the two boxes in the screenshot: the mirror's rightmost edge sat inside the real box's edge, meaning the mirror was NARROWER than it should be. Root cause: 'border:' + cs.border used the SHORTHAND computed style, which is not reliably serialized across browsers -- WebKit/Safari has a known history of returning an empty string for shorthand computed properties even when the underlying longhand values are genuinely set. If that happened here, the mirror silently rendered with NO border at all, and under box-sizing:border-box that makes its actual text-content area WIDER than the real element's by the border width on each side -- shifting exactly where lines wrap, in both getCursorXYFor() and the shared getCursorXY() (same fix applied to both). Also fixed a second, compounding bug specific to the debug-visible mirror: its own extra 'border:2px dashed' for the yellow highlight was DOUBLE-COUNTING width on top of the real copied border -- moved to outline, which never affects box layout/width, so the debug view now measures the same box the real (invisible) production path does.
  v0.1605 - REAL ROOT CAUSE for the Quick Add/Edit Paragraph position drift, finally confirmed by Lynn's screenshot showing the wrap points genuinely differ between the visible debug mirror and the real textarea (three earlier theories -- wrapper box mismatch, math error, staleness race -- were each individually disproven). Cause: getCursorXYFor() (and the shared getCursorXY(), same latent gap, fixed too) never copied letter-spacing or word-spacing to the mirror -- both real, pervasive properties in this app's CSS cascade. If the textarea's own computed value for either ever resolves to non-default (inherited from any ancestor), the mirror's text packs differently and wraps at different points than the real textarea -- worse the longer the text, matching every symptom reported across this whole investigation. Fixed by copying letter-spacing, word-spacing, text-transform, and tab-size in addition to the properties already copied. Also: per Lynn's confirmation that showing both cursors together fixed the missing drag-handles bug, updated the Bold Cursor settings description to accurately describe current behavior (shows alongside the native cursor, not instead of it) and added the requested warning that two cursors may appear together. Noted for a future build, not yet implemented: Lynn wants platform-conditional behavior (skip showing both cursors on non-iOS, where caret-color may work reliably) -- deferred until tested on other platforms rather than guessed at.
  v0.1604 - Per Lynn's decision: Bold Cursor no longer hides the native caret. Root cause search led to bugs.webkit.org #177489 -- caret-color is a long-standing, unresolved WebKit bug on iOS Safari (open since 2017; doesn't reliably respect changes, can bleed from a previously-focused field). This very likely explains BOTH the intermittent "still see the native cursor" reports AND the missing text-selection drag handles Lynn found (both plausibly downstream of the same broken iOS caret-rendering pipeline) -- not fixable from the page side. Removed ta.style.caretColor entirely; Bold Cursor now draws alongside the native caret rather than replacing it. Lynn's reasoning, worth recording: the audience this feature serves (low vision) is unlikely to even notice a second, thin native line once the bold one is visible, and this sidesteps the WebKit bug rather than continuing to fight it. The Quick Add/Edit Paragraph position-drift bug remains open and unrelated to this fix -- the v0.1603 debug-mirror toggle stays in for one more round of investigation.
  v0.1603 - Bold Cursor position bug in Quick Add/Edit Paragraph, round 3. Lynn's screenshots showed the mark landing in plausible-but-wrong locations (inside an unrelated checkbox label; past the end of the visible textarea) even after v0.1602's full-text-mirror fix. Three theories (wrapper-div box mismatch, a math error, a selectionStart staleness race) were each proposed and discarded by code inspection without conclusive proof -- rather than guess a fourth time, DIAGNOSTIC ONLY this build: getCursorXYFor()'s mirror can now be rendered genuinely ON SCREEN (not off at -9999px) via a new temporary Settings toggle ("Show Bold Cursor debug mirror," Note Appearance > Bold Cursor), with its measurement marker highlighted, positioned directly below whichever text box is focused -- same technique that found the real v8 wrapping bug in the standalone demo originally. No behavior change to the actual Bold Cursor logic. Please enable this toggle, reproduce the bug in Quick Add or Edit Paragraph, and screenshot the yellow debug box alongside the real text box.
  v0.1602 - REAL ROOT CAUSE for Bold Cursor drifting increasingly ahead of the real position in Quick Add/Edit Paragraph on longer text, eventually landing outside the box (Lynn's report). Both getCursorXYFor() (written fresh for this feature) and the app's shared getCursorXY() (16+ other callers: caret nudge buttons, mode-switch scroll preservation, find-in-note, trackpad heal) used a TRUNCATED-PREFIX mirror -- only the text BEFORE the cursor, nothing after. Word-wrap is a greedy fill algorithm whose line-break decisions depend on what comes AFTER the visible point too, so a truncated prefix wraps differently than the real textarea once a line is long enough to actually wrap -- exactly matching "fine on a short line, increasingly wrong on longer ones." This is the SAME structural bug a standalone demo already found and fixed once (v8 of that demo); it had silently regressed back to the truncated-prefix version in both of this app's copies. Fixed both the same way the demo was fixed: full text, with a zero-width marker inserted AT the real cursor position, nothing truncated. Full regression suite run specifically to catch any behavioral drift in getCursorXY()'s many other callers. Native-cursor-duplication: still real per Lynn's re-confirmation (blink-timing coincidence likely explains why it wasn't always noticed), but no code-level cause has been found (caret-color:transparent is set correctly, unopposed by any competing CSS rule) -- left as a known, watched issue rather than guessing at a fix with nothing concrete to go on.
  v0.1601 - ROOT CAUSE finally found for "Bold Cursor invisible in Quick Add/Edit Paragraph" -- Lynn's log showed PERFECT math, parenting, and CSS (markParentPos=relative, markParentIsContainer=true, sane left/top values, updating live) yet total invisibility, which meant the bug wasn't in any of the logic already instrumented. Real cause: quick-add-input/edit-block-input are <textarea> elements, which per the HTML spec do NOT render arbitrary child DOM nodes inside their own box at all -- the mark was a real, correctly-positioned DOM node appended somewhere the browser will never paint it, no matter how correct the math was. note-editor's case worked only because its mark was parented to note-content-area, a real <div> -- never to the textarea itself. Fixed by wrapping each self-scrolling textarea in a real positioning <div> (hugs the textarea 1:1, no layout effect) and registering that wrapper as the scroll ancestor/container instead of the textarea. Also fixed a real, separate latent bug found while investigating: the mark's z-index (5) was far below these dialogs' own stacking (up to 10010) -- didn't matter while the mark never rendered at all, but would have surfaced the moment the wrapper fix made it paintable, so raised in the same build.
  v0.1600 - Bold Cursor position bug in Quick Add/Edit Paragraph, round 2 -- from Lynn's log showing it working correctly once (top=216/226px) then reverting to the top=6px bug on the SAME dialog's next open in the same session. Root cause: position:relative enforcement on the mark's container only ran when CREATING the mark or when its parent CHANGED -- never on a plain re-open where the mark already existed with the same parent reference. If the container's position ever reverted to static between opens for any reason, a static mark falls back to the nearest positioned ANCESTOR up the tree, explaining both the wrong position and its intermittent, per-open nature. Fixed: position:relative is now enforced unconditionally on every getMark() call. Also fixed the diagnostic log itself, which was reading ta.parentElement (a separate, possibly-different lookup than the actual resolveScrollAncestor() result) -- now logs the real mark container and confirms it matches the resolved scroll ancestor.
  v0.1599 - Two more real bugs from Lynn's log/report. (1) Bold Cursor appearing near the TOP of Quick Add/Edit Paragraph (in the title/label area, not the textarea): top=6px on every log line was the giveaway -- getMark() unconditionally parented the mark to ta.parentElement (quick-add-box, which ALSO contains the title/position-buttons/label ABOVE the textarea), a DIFFERENT element than scrollAncestorId used in the coordinate math. The mark's container and the math's reference frame must be the same element; fixed by having getMark() parent to the actual resolved scroll ancestor, consolidated into one shared resolveScrollAncestor() used by both. (2) Selection hide: double/triple-tap word/line selection was showing the caret at the selection start, reading as a stray artifact rather than standard editor behavior (no caret glyph while a real range is selected -- the browser's own highlight is the indicator). Fixed: update() now hides the mark whenever selectionStart !== selectionEnd. Native-caret-duplication in the main editor was NOT reproduced this session (Lynn confirmed it's gone) -- no code change was knowingly made to fix it; flagged as possibly intermittent, watch for recurrence.
  v0.1598 - REAL BUG FOUND from Lynn's on-device log (erratic Bold Cursor position in Quick Add/Edit Paragraph, jumping non-monotonically as left=40/59/40/76px). Root cause: the app's pre-existing getCursorXY() hardcodes document.getElementById('note-content-area') as the scrolling ancestor for its coordinate math -- correct for note-editor (that IS its real ancestor) but silently wrong for quick-add-input/edit-block-input, whose real scrolling ancestors are themselves (both are self-scrolling textareas, no wrapper) -- measuring against the wrong box entirely produced garbage. Also confirmed from the SAME log that "Edit Paragraph doesn't show Bold Cursor" was never real -- it fires correctly once the setting is confirmed on; the earlier report came from a session where the toggle had silently reset to off. Fixed: added getCursorXYFor(ed, scrollAncestor), a Bold-Cursor-specific measurement function taking the correct ancestor per instance (getCursorXY() itself untouched -- 16+ other callers depend on its existing note-content-area-specific behavior). Confirmed via jsdom: both dialogs now produce sane, calibration-consistent coordinates matching the main editor's known-good values.
  v0.1597 - Two things from Lynn's testing. (1) REAL BUG FOUND AND FIXED: the note-editor + FAB (note-fab) intermittently disappearing was two independent systems fighting over the same element's display -- the pre-existing showNoteFab setting (applySettings(), writes 'flex'/'none') and the newer toolbarPlacement system (applyToolbarHiddenOverrides(), writes ''/'none'), whichever ran last silently won, and note-fab's own CSS defaults to display:none, so the placement system's '' fallback (intended for elements with a class-level flex default) could hide a FAB showNoteFab had just correctly shown. Fixed: note-fab is now excluded from the placement system's display-writing loop entirely -- it stays governed by showNoteFab alone, as it always was; its settings-row pairing with quick-add-btn (for reordering/menu purposes) is untouched. (2) Bold Cursor diagnostics added for two unconfirmed reports (Edit Paragraph not showing it; native caret still visible alongside it in the main editor) -- both reproduce correctly in jsdom, meaning the bug (if real) is a real-device-only timing/rendering difference; logging added to apply()/update() to capture what's actually happening on Lynn's device rather than guessing further.
  v0.1596 - Bold Cursor extended to Quick Add and Edit Paragraph, per Lynn's request (deliberately NOT the scratch pad -- Lynn's explicit design call, a future low-vision App Theme covers that audience via a curated bundle instead). Generalized the single hardcoded implementation into a reusable initBoldCursorFor() factory, one instance per textarea -- found and handled a real architecture difference while porting: note-editor is a non-scrolling textarea inside a scrolling WRAPPER, but quick-add-input and edit-block-input are each SELF-scrolling with no wrapper, so each instance's mark element now attaches to that textarea's own parent rather than a hardcoded note-content-area id. getCursorXY()'s coordinate math needed no changes -- it already accounts for whichever element's own scrollTop/scrollLeft is passed to it.
  v0.1595 - Two features. (1) Default Checkbox & Bullet Color: checkboxColor now also colors bullet glyphs (viewer-bullet-char -- covers *, +, and hyphens-as-bullets when showBulletsForHyphens is on, including multi-level styles) in R&R mode, per Lynn's explicit design call that checkboxes and bullets should share one default color concept. NEVER applied to literal hyphens (viewer-hyphen-marker) -- Lynn's reasoning: too thin to carry color well. Color resolution extracted into a shared getDefaultMarkColor()/MARK_COLOR_PALETTE so checkboxes (which keep their [x:color] override) and bullets (no override, none planned) use the identical chain rather than duplicating it. Settings row description updated to reflect the broadened scope. (2) Bold Cursor: an app-drawn caret replacing the native one, ported from a standalone demo after 16 rounds of on-device calibration (X=-2.5px fixed, Y=-0.31em scaled to font, height=100%, same getCursorXY() mirror-div technique already used elsewhere in this file). Off by default -- Lynn's explicit risk call, since hiding the real caret and substituting a synced one is a bigger claim across the full editor than across a standalone demo. New Note Appearance settings: boldCursorEnabled, boldCursorWidth (2-7px, default 4), boldCursorColor (default a Bear-red #d6336c, Lynn's pick).
  v0.1594 - Caret nudge fixes per Lynn's testing: (1) the four buttons now appear only in EMTIP (keyboard open), like Dictate and Dismiss-keyboard -- switched from edit-only-btn to the kb-only class (dismiss-keyboard-btn's exact pattern), removing the inline display:none that would have overridden the CSS. (2) Nudge scrolling now uses the keyboard-aware _tpadEnsureCaretVisible (visualViewport band + pinned-toolbar clearance, new quiet flag so held repeats don't flood the log) instead of scrollCursorIntoView, whose band ignores the keyboard/toolbar overlay and left the caret slightly hidden under them. Flagged: scrollCursorIntoView vs _tpadEnsureCaretVisible duplication is now explicit; consolidation deferred.
  v0.1593 - Caret nudge buttons (the user-facing answer to the iOS trackpad WebKit bug, TODO #20): four new lower-toolbar buttons -- Cursor left/right (one character) and Cursor up/down (one line, column-preserving via new pure top-level caretLineMove()) -- press for one step, hold to repeat (450ms delay then ~11/s). Pointer Events with pointerdown preventDefault keep the keyboard open (same goal as indent-btn's established mousedown pattern). Wired into every existing toolbar system: HIDEABLE_BUTTONS, SETTINGS_ROW_PAIRS (left/right and up/down each share a settings row), TOOLBAR_ONLY placement category (a nudge in the More menu is useless), DEFAULT_TOOLBAR_ORDER after Unindent, toolbar-help catalog, edit-only-btn mode gating. New test files: cases v14 (adds caretLineMove cases), goldens v15 (paired with cases v14).
  v0.1592 - Trackpad (TODO #20) FINAL MITIGATION, scaling back per Lynn's decision after CKEditor and Trinket were found to share this WebKit platform bug: removed the continuous caret-follow (it fought iOS's own autoscroll -- the reported jumpiness), the snap-restore experiment, the app-drawn debug I-beam, the touch counters, and all per-event tpad diagnostics. Kept exactly one surgical piece: the HEAL -- a caret jump >=500 chars landing during a detected drag scrolls the wrapper so the caret is visible again (single 'tpad-heal:' log line when it fires). Trackpad now behaves no worse than other web editors, with recoverability they lack. getCursorXY/getCursorY refactor kept (one mirror implementation). Caret nudge toolbar buttons come next build as the real user-facing answer.
  v0.1591 - Debug caret upgraded to a true tracking I-BEAM at the actual caret position (red, blinking, serif caps, sized to the editor's line height), per Lynn's request -- the previous left-gutter bar only showed the line. getCursorY refactored to getCursorXY returning {x,y} (the mirror span's rect always contained X; it was never returned) with getCursorY kept as a thin wrapper so all pre-existing callers are untouched -- one mirror implementation, not two. No behavior changes to the v0.1590 follow/snap-guard logic.
  v0.1590 - Trackpad round 4, from v0.1589's log (which finally showed tpad-follow running and the heal working). (1) EARTHQUAKE FIX: the follow's visible band now comes from the visualViewport (offsetTop/height) instead of the area's full box height -- with the keyboard open the bottom half of the area is UNDER the keyboard (vvH 409 vs areaH 812), the old math called carets there "visible", never corrected, and iOS panned violently (vvOff bouncing to 403) while the toolbars re-pinned each pan. (2) SNAP-GUARD EXPERIMENT: the v0.1589 log proved the sel=0 snap kills the whole trackpad session (19s of zero selection events on the subsequent downward drag) -- mid-drag teleports to 0 (jump >=500 chars from >=500 in) are now RESTORED to the pre-snap position instead of accepted; genuine gradual arrivals at the top are unaffected (their final step is small). Whether iOS keeps tracking after the restore is what this build's on-device test answers.
  v0.1589 - Trackpad detector RETUNE from v0.1588's deployed log: real drags are far slower than assumed (measured step gaps 200-1098ms; the sel=0 snap arrived 2168ms after the last step), so the 350ms/3-step/600ms gate died mid-drag (drag=1 at one logged step flipping to drag=0 at the next while still dragging) and the snap always landed on a dead latch. New values: steps qualify up to 1500ms apart, engage at 2 steps, latch 3000ms. Mode-switch scroll preservation remains immune by construction (its programmatic moves coalesce into a single selectionchange per task -- can never make two small steps). No mechanism changes; thresholds only.
  v0.1588 - Trackpad bug, round 3, from v0.1587's deployed log: the follow logic was STARVED BY ITS OWN THROTTLE -- the detector runs on every selection change (not just the 200ms-logged ones), every in-view check consumed the 80ms window, and the sel=0 snap arrived <80ms after an unlogged nothing-to-do check, so the one event that mattered never scrolled (log proof: drag=1 printed at the snap with areaScroll unmoved, and the log statement runs AFTER the scroll code). Fixes: (1) caret jumps >=200 chars bypass the throttle (the snap always qualifies, by the same threshold that disqualifies it as a drag step); (2) a trailing 150ms re-check scheduled during live drags catches a final move with no event behind it; (3) per Lynn's request, an app-drawn DEBUG CARET (red blinking bar inside the scroll content, tracking the real selection) shows ground truth regardless of whether iOS renders its I-beam -- diagnostic only, remove with the tpad logging. Ensure-visible logic extracted to a shared _tpadEnsureCaretVisible (live + trailing paths); its topClear also addresses Lynn's Android observation (OS autoscroll parks the I-beam under the pinned toolbars, which the OS knows nothing about).
  v0.1587 - FIX the v0.1586 fix, which never actually ran: its touch gate could never fire because the trackpad drag happens on the VIRTUAL KEYBOARD (OS chrome -- its touches never reach the page; deployed log showed touch=0 throughout and zero tpad-follow lines). Replaced with a drag-SIGNATURE detector: 3+ consecutive caret-only selection changes, each under 350ms apart and moving under 200 chars, engage follow mode, latched 600ms past the last step -- so the mid-drag sel=0 snap is caught and self-healed. Programmatic moves (two huge jumps) can't match the signature by construction; held arrow keys now also follow (standard behavior). Diagnostics extended: 'tpad:' lines add drag=, 'tpad-follow:' adds vvOff, and 'tpad-drag: engaged' marks detector activation. The reported toolbar shaking correlates in the log with iOS violently oscillating visualViewport.offsetTop (21<->403) trying to reveal the offscreen caret AFTER the snap -- v0.1586's own code never scrolled anything (it never ran), so the shake wasn't the follow logic; whether healing the snap quickly also removes the shake window is exactly what this build's logs will show.
  v0.1586 - FIX for TODO #20, from v0.1585's deployed diagnostics: during an iOS trackpad-mode drag toward the top edge, WebKit's autoscroll-to-caret only scrolls the focused textarea's OWN scrollTop (a no-op -- the wrapper note-content-area is the real scroller), then snaps the selection to index 0 far above the visible window, losing the OS-drawn I-beam permanently (captured: caretY 2538->2238 with areaScroll frozen at 2123, then sel=0/caretY=168). The app now follows the caret with the WRAPPER's scroll, only while a touch is active (trackpad drags always have one): caret outside the usable band (pinned header+toolbar overlay respected at top) scrolls note-content-area to bring it back with a margin -- giving real edge-autoscroll and making any residual sel=0 snap self-healing. Programmatic caret moves are immune by construction (no active touch); taps are already-visible no-ops; arrow keys deliberately not covered yet. 'tpad:'/'tpad-follow:' logging kept one more build for on-device verification.
  v0.1585 - DIAGNOSTIC build for TODO #20 (iOS trackpad-mode caret drag loses the I-beam off the top): logs 'tpad:' lines on caret-only selection movement (selection changed with no input event -- trackpad drags/taps/arrows; typing filtered out), throttled to 200ms, capturing selectionStart, caret Y in the scroller's content space, note-content-area scrollTop/clientHeight, note-editor's own scrollTop and rect top, and visualViewport offset/height. Hypothesis under test: the textarea never scrolls itself (the wrapper scrolls), so iOS's built-in autoscroll-to-caret has nothing to scroll. No behavior change; instrumentation only, removed after the fix is confirmed.
  v0.1584 - Replaced the v0.1583 placement segmented control with a custom dropdown (button + popup list), per Lynn's request for something more compact -- deliberately NOT a native <select>, per the documented iOS font-size bug, so it honors the app's UI font size like every other Settings control. At most one dropdown can be open at a time (module-level singleton with a shared closer), fixing a real leak found while building it: removing a stale popup's DOM node on row-rebuild didn't remove its document-level outside-click listener, since each row's close logic was a closure with no way to reach it again afterward.
  v0.1583 - Toolbar/More-menu button PLACEMENT redesign (per Lynn's request): replaced the binary toolbarHidden array with a sparse toolbarPlacement map (btnId -> 'toolbar'|'more'|'both'|'neither'), and the per-row on/off toggle in Lower Toolbar Buttons with a 4-option segmented control (options vary by category: toolbar-only buttons get Toolbar/Neither; dual-mode buttons -- which already have a static More row -- get all four; everyone else gets Toolbar/More/Neither). 'Neither' (fully hidden) is new and available on nearly everything, including the + button, which is now hideable and paired with note-fab (confirmed both open the identical Quick Add dialog) via the existing SETTINGS_ROW_PAIRS mechanism. Dual-mode static More-menu rows previously had NO tie to visibility at all (always shown); added an explicit, hand-verified id map (DUAL_MODE_STATIC_ROW_IDS) and a force-hide-only sweep so 'neither' actually suppresses them without disturbing their existing mode-based logic. Nothing in this system is exempt from 'neither' -- Settings and swipe-to-delete both live on the home screen, entirely outside this toolbar/menu system, so full note-editor decluttering can never strand anyone. One-time migration converts any saved toolbarHidden. (Design note, not yet built: a savable named profile -- e.g. for Lynn's father -- is deliberately deferred to when the Note Style/App Theme engine has real storage; this build is just the plain data/UI change, unrelated to that timing.)
  v0.1582 - FIX: Lower Toolbar Buttons reorder/visibility rows ("mostly missing" -- Lynn's report) had two real bugs. (1) toggleSection()'s rebuild hook checked header.parentElement.querySelector('#toolbar-visibility-rows'), which only works while that container's nearest collapsible ancestor is a top-level zone section -- fragile today and would silently break once subsections become independently collapsible (planned next). (2) A SEPARATE, independent click listener on settings-btn also called buildToolbarVisibilityRows(), alongside a dead unused _origOpenSettings variable -- redundant wiring that depended on listener registration order to run after openSettings() populated the dialog. Fix: removed both, and call buildToolbarVisibilityRows() directly inside openSettings() itself (same place Voice's list is populated), so the rows are always built exactly once, correctly, regardless of how Settings was opened or what section state it starts in. CAUGHT BY THE REQUIRED jsdom SMOKE CHECK: wiring the new openSettings() call first exposed that buildToolbarVisibilityRows() itself was declared with `function` INSIDE init() -- invisible to openSettings() at module level, the exact rule this project already documents. Promoted to the standard var-stub-plus-init-assignment pattern (same as applyOutlineMode etc.) in this same build, before delivery.
  v0.1581 - Check-off animation (demo option F, exact timing): new Note Behavior setting checkAnimation ('none' | 'draw' [default] | 'confetti' | 'both'), radio row in Checklist Behavior. Draw animates the optimistic tap-swap checkmark polyline (0.4s); confetti spawns six plain-span dots (0.22s delay + 0.6s flight -- spans, not injected SVG, per the WebKit lesson). On animated checks the full re-render defers until the animation ends (data/undo/save still immediate); the animation is force-skipped whenever the item will auto-move to bottom, keeping that path byte-identical to before (stale-line-index hazard). Honors prefers-reduced-motion. Also per Lynn's request: doMoveCompletedToBottom now takes a pre-operation note snapshot like every other multi-line-rewriting operation.
  v0.1580 - FIX pinch-zoom regression from v0.1570: the permanent iOS maximum-scale=1 relied on "iOS ignores the cap for user pinches," which is only true in the Safari browser -- standalone PWA mode ENFORCES it, killing pinch there. The cap is now dynamic: normal state keeps the original maximum-scale=5 (pinch works in browser and PWA); the cap applies only while a text field is focused (set on touchstart before focus so it precedes the iOS auto-zoom decision, plus focusin for programmatic/tab focus; restored ~350ms after focusout unless focus moved to another text field). Small-font focus auto-zoom stays suppressed; accepted trade-off: no pinch while the keyboard is up. iOS only, as before.
  v0.1579 - Rounded highlight corners, "subtle" option per Lynn's pick from the demo: plain ==highlight== radius 2px -> 3px (it already had 2px + padding; the REAL missing piece found while implementing was box-decoration-break:clone, without which wrapped highlights get square line-break edges -- the squareness actually being seen). clone added to all three highlight variants (plain, ==#hex:, ==#bg|#fg:); the two hex variants already had 3px radius and keep their existing 4px side padding unchanged. Scratch pad Copy/Undo/Redo button gaps widened 6px -> 14px.
  v0.1578 - FIX for a v0.1577 mistake: omitting a ui-xlarge selector for .fab does NOT mean "stay at large size" -- body size classes are mutually exclusive, so with no ui-xlarge rule .fab fell all the way back to its 56px BASE at xlarge, visibly shrinking below large (the "shrinks to medium/small" Lynn reported). Meanwhile #note-fab's own pre-existing ui-xlarge rule (80px) was untouched, growing it further (the "huger" Lynn reported). Both FABs now have an EXPLICIT ui-xlarge rule pinned to the same values as their ui-large rule, so large and xlarge look visually identical for both FABs, as requested.
  v0.1577 - FIX for a v0.1576 mistake: home-screen "+" FAB (.fab) was accidentally made BIGGER (64px base, plus a new 90px xlarge step) instead of matching the smaller note-editor "+" FAB (#note-fab, 56px base) as intended. While fixing, also found a genuinely pre-existing second ui-xlarge .fab rule (48px icon) that predated this session, stacked underneath the mistaken one. Rewrote the whole .fab size block as one clean set of rules: base and large tier now match #note-fab exactly (56px/68px); per Lynn's explicit follow-up, NEITHER FAB grows past the large tier anymore -- no xlarge rule at all, since that step looked too big. Desktop/mouse override sizes scaled down proportionally to match.
  v0.1576 - Home header padding bottom +2px (12/10 -> 12/12) per Lynn's follow-up. Home-screen "+" FAB (.fab) now has an xlarge size tier, matching the note-editor "+" FAB (#note-fab), which already scaled at both large and xlarge -- same relative proportions, .fab's own larger base. Notes list gets real bottom padding (16px -> 100px, more at larger icon tiers) so the FAB can no longer sit over the last note and block swipe-to-delete there -- a gap against the project's own standing rule that scrolling panels always get extra blank space at the end.
  v0.1575 - (backfilled while preparing v0.1576: this entry was missed at the time) Home header padding top/bottom raised 10/8 -> 12/10, per Lynn's request to compare against the v0.1574 trim.
  v0.1574a - Home header padding adjusted 10/8 -> 12/10 per Lynn's request, to compare against the v0.1574 trim.
  v0.1574 - Vertical space: home-screen header padding trimmed (18/16 -> 10/8). Lower toolbar height now tracks the icon size instead of being effectively constant: the injected button min-height was Math.max(36, iconPx) + 10, so small (22px) and medium (28px) icons both produced a 46px button (identical bar height at small/medium/large, growing only at extra-large) -- it is now Math.max(28, iconPx + 10), keeping a real floor (needed: a blanket min-width:0 previously collapsed other toolbar buttons) but one that can never sit above the intended size. The toolbar scroll arrows' hardcoded min-height:44px, which would have silently become the new height floor, now tracks the icon size too. Safe by construction: every consumer of header/toolbar height measures it at runtime (offsetHeight / getBoundingClientRect) -- no pixel height is hardcoded in any calculation.
  v0.1573 - New Note Behavior setting newItemDefaultTags ('Default tags for new items', e.g. '@inbox @prioUnset'): auto-appended to any newly added checkbox-ish item (checkbox OR plain bullet, detected through the standard formatting strippers) that has item text and no @tag yet. Applied on Enter at the end of a line in the editor, per-line in Add Text to Note (after its hyphen/checkbox toggles), and to ?action=new / share-target content; new optional URL parameter tags= overrides the setting per action (present-but-empty suppresses; commas and missing @ signs accepted everywhere and normalized). New top-level pure functions isMarkerLineRaw (the outline's isCheckboxRaw real implementation now delegates to it, one source of truth usable before the note screen exists), normalizeTagListSetting, appendDefaultTagsToLine/appendDefaultTagsToText. TODO #19 done: _tokenAtCaret promoted to top level for harness access. New test files: cases v13, goldens v13.
  v0.1572 - Tag-autocomplete data-loss fix: tapping a suggestion spliced from the '@' that ORIGINALLY opened the popup to the current caret, so a stale popup plus a caret tap elsewhere silently deleted everything in between (the 'delete 3000 characters?' guard hits). The @token is now re-derived at the caret at tap time via a new shared _tokenAtCaret() (also used by update(), replacing its duplicate scan); a tap with the caret not in an @tag changes nothing and just closes the popup (debug-logged). Caret movement (tap, arrow keys) now re-runs the anchor logic while the popup is open, re-filtering to the tag under the caret or closing. Removed the confirmed v0.1570/1571 position diagnostics.
  v0.1571 - iOS tag-autocomplete popup positioning FIX (root cause confirmed by v0.1570 deployed diagnostics): position:fixed is relative to the layout viewport, which iOS pans upward by visualViewport.offsetTop (observed up to 240px) when the keyboard opens, so the constant header-based top rendered the popup that far above the visible screen. The popup top now adds the pan offset, is clamped inside the visible viewport, and re-positions live on visualViewport scroll/resize while open (fixes it scooting upward when the keyboard reopens with the popup left up). Diagnostics kept for one confirmation build. Also commented out the applyRuledLines() debug-log line that was flooding the log.
  v0.1570 - Diagnostics for the iOS tag-autocomplete popup appearing too high: every popup render now debug-logs visualViewport offsetTop/height, window scroll, pinned header height, the computed top, and the popup's actual on-screen rect, so the real fix is written from deployed data. Also suppresses iOS's automatic zoom on focusing text fields with font sizes under 16px by setting viewport maximum-scale to 1 on iOS ONLY at startup (iOS ignores it for manual pinch, so pinch-zoom still works; Android keeps the original viewport untouched since Android Chrome would honor the cap and lose pinch-zoom).
  v0.1569 - CRITICAL FIX: v0.1568's own changelog entry broke the entire app. It described an HTML comment by writing out the actual comment-open and comment-close markers as literal text -- but HTML has no concept of nested comments, so that inner closing marker prematurely ended the outer BUILD CHANGELOG comment block itself, and everything from that point until the next real comment start rendered as raw, visible page text instead of being hidden. Fixed by rephrasing the description in plain words instead of literal comment syntax. Verified far more rigorously than a normal build this time: scanned the entire file simulating real browser comment-parsing (every comment open is closed by the very next close marker found, no exceptions) and confirmed zero premature closures anywhere in the file, not just the one found; loaded the file in a real DOM environment and confirmed no stray text nodes leaked directly into the page body; confirmed openSettings/applySettings still run cleanly. Lesson for all future changelog/comment writing in this file: never describe HTML comment syntax by writing the literal open/close markers -- describe it in words only.
  v0.1568 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 3i (last individual-section migration): View Only Notes (small, single-bucket General, moved as one unit) and Checklists (15 rows, mixed row-by-row like Note Display -- 4 General rows: today/last suffix, time format sitting in the MIDDLE of otherwise-behavior settings; 4 Note Appearance rows: list-item/checkbox-section rules, hyphen bullets, multi-level bullets at the END; 7 Note Behavior rows: confetti, move-completed, strikethrough, check sound, smart-indent). Caught and fixed a real extraction bug during this build: Checklists' section closes with an HTML comment marker reading "end settings-section-body" instead of the plain "</div>\n    </div>" pattern every other section used, so my generic end-boundary search returned -1 (not found) -- which Python's string slicing silently treats as "one character short" rather than erroring, producing a truncated, unbalanced Note Appearance group (16 opens/17 closes) that I caught via the balance-verification step before it ever touched the live file. Redid the extraction with the correct boundary before proceeding. Both old sections and their jump-nav entries removed -- this completes the individual-section migration; only Debugging (untouched by design) and the old Themes section (retired in a later step once the new Note Style UI exists) remain outside the three zones.
  v0.1567 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B: fully retired the old "Note Display" grab-bag section (per Lynn's reminder it was still outstanding) -- the most finely-mixed section yet, 23 individual settings interleaved across all three buckets with almost no subsection structure. Split row-by-row (not by subsection): 12 rows -> App Theme (editing mode, tabs-on-paste, edge-swipe, toolbar-collapse-button, both wrap-name/preview settings, show-note-preview, deletion-warning, oops-button, double-tap-to-edit, retain-quiet-mode, floating-plus-button); 5 rows -> Note Appearance (tag-color-borders, tag-color-pills, wrap-note-title, show-tags, paragraph-rules); 6 rows -> Note Behavior (tag-scope, default-view-mode, plus-position, outline-mode, outline-collapse-on-open, indent-spaces). Old section and jump-nav entry removed. All 23 row boundaries located precisely, 3 combined groups verified balanced before reassembly, all three destination zones re-verified, full settings-sheet depth-walked clean -- every one of the 23 affected element ids confirmed present exactly once.
  v0.1566 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 3h: migrated two sections. Reading Ruler (a top-level section my earlier inventory missed -- its id "ruler-settings-section" lacks the "settings-section-" prefix every other section uses) was itself mixed: rulerEnabled (General, "Show ruler toggle in More menu") split into App Theme; the preview area, color, opacity, and thickness (all Note Appearance) went into Note Appearance as their own "Reading Ruler" subsection. Ruled Lines & Grid (style, tone, all three position nudges, margin line color) is fully Note Appearance -- moved as one atomic unit into the same zone. Both old sections and their jump-nav entries removed. All 3 extracted pieces verified individually balanced, both destination zones re-verified, full settings-sheet depth-walked clean.
  v0.1565 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 3g: migrated Speech (Voice, Speech Speed, Speak on Open, Pause between lines, Show Speak button variants, always-speak-whole-note, skip list markers) into Note Behavior, as its own subsection. All 10 settings are Note Behavior -- clean, single-bucket, no mixing, moved as one atomic unit. Old section and jump-nav entry removed.
  v0.1564 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 3f: migrated Lower Toolbar Buttons (toolbarOrder, toolbarHidden -- button visibility and drag-to-reorder) into App Theme, as its own subsection. Single-bucket (General), no internal mixing -- moved as one atomic unit. Old section and jump-nav entry removed.
  v0.1563 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 3e: migrated Layout & Margins -- finely mixed at the individual ROW level (not by subsection like Text/Colors), since Note Appearance and Note Behavior settings were interleaved with no subsection markers between them: view/heading indent + chars-per-line (Note Appearance) sat directly next to list/paragraph continuation indent (Note Behavior) with nothing separating them. Split into 3 destinations: Note Appearance zone gets indent width, heading indent, chars-per-line, and note content margins (new "Layout & Indent" subsection); Note Behavior zone gets list/paragraph continuation indent (new "Continuation Indent" subsection); General/App Theme zone gets notes-list margins (unchanged "Notes List" subsection). Old section and jump-nav entry removed. All 5 extracted pieces verified individually balanced before reassembly, each destination zone re-verified, full settings-sheet depth-walked clean.
  v0.1562 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 3d: migrated the Appearance section (Color Theme, Button Size) -- small, clean, single-bucket (General), no internal mixing. Moved to the start of App Theme zone, ahead of Light/Dark Mode colors, since theme mode and button size are the most fundamental app-wide choices. Old section and jump-nav entry removed.
  v0.1561 - Scroll-to-last-position button now scrolls to the BOTTOM of the dialog when no position has been saved yet (e.g. first-ever use), instead of doing nothing indistinguishable from "already there" -- avoids the button looking broken before it's ever recorded a real position, per Lynn.
  v0.1560 - Two fixes. (1) Note Texture (light/dark) moved from App Theme to Note Appearance -- it was embedded inside the "Light Mode"/"Dark Mode" subsections migrated wholesale in step 3c, which correctly moved the surrounding General-bucket colors but carried the Note-Appearance-bucket texture rows along by accident since I treated each titled subsection as atomic without checking for internal mixing. (2) New icon-only "scroll to last position" button next to Collapse All -- deliberately unlabeled (per Lynn: area is cluttered, wordy buttons are for seniors who rarely touch Settings, this is a power-user shortcut). New settingsScrollPosition (Excluded bucket) records #settings-sheet's scroll position on every close path -- Save, the no-changes-early-return close, and Cancel/X -- so wherever you were is remembered regardless of how you left.
  v0.1559 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 3c: migrated the Colors section, the largest and most-mixed section (39KB, 10 subsections). Split by SETTING_BUCKETS classification, NOT simply in half -- General (App Theme zone): Light Mode, Dark Mode, Accent Color, Notes List Entry Background. Note Appearance zone: Highlight Color, Color-Coding Palette, Custom Colors, Note Toolbar Colors, Note Paper Colors, Note Accent Color. Old Colors section and its jump-nav entry removed entirely. All 10 subsections extracted and verified individually balanced before reassembly (not hand-typed, given the size), each combined group re-verified balanced, then both destination zones and the full settings-sheet depth-walked clean end to end.
  v0.1558 - Moved Debugging to always be the LAST collapsible section in Settings, per Lynn -- a standing preference independent of the ongoing Phase B reorg, worth locking in now so it doesn't need re-fixing every time sections get added/reordered/removed as migration continues. Extracted and relocated programmatically (not hand-typed) given the section's size; jump-nav list reordered to match.
  v0.1557 - Three fixes. (1) Reverted v0.1556's font FACE/WEIGHT freeze on the Settings dialog -- per Lynn, live face/weight tracking throughout the dialog while open is actually wanted, and the freeze never worked anyway (dialog rows reference var(--font-body)/var(--font-weight-ui) directly, which always resolves to the live global value regardless of any inline style on an ancestor). SIZE freezing is kept -- it genuinely works via relative (1em) inheritance, and stays wanted since live size changes caused disorienting auto-scroll. (2) Moved Note & Folder Name Font/Size and Note Preview Font/Size (plus their live previews) from the old Note Display section into App Theme, directly under the UI font settings -- these are General-bucket settings describing the home screen list, never belonged in a note-specific-sounding section. (3) The two margin slider groups (note content and notes list, left+right, 4 sliders total) changed from a 4px to a 2px increment, per Lynn's request.
  v0.1556 - Fixed three real bugs in v0.1555's dialog-typography fix, plus two more requested items. (1) Font size still reflowed the dialog: most dialog text uses relative (1em) sizing that inherits the live root font-size directly, never touching the --font-ui CSS VARIABLE the old fix redefined. (2) Font weight wasn't reflected in the preview: the preview box's font-weight also came from the now-frozen --font-weight-ui, so it silently showed the wrong (stale) weight regardless of the slider. Replaced the static CSS-variable approach entirely with a JS-driven one: openSettings() snapshots your last SAVED font/size/weight and sets them as real inline properties directly on #settings-sheet, once, at open -- not touched again until the next open (so the dialog now correctly adopts your saved choice after a Save+reopen, not frozen forever as v0.1555 was). Preview boxes (font-preview-text, and via removing the stale CSS override, ui-line-height-preview) are unaffected by the dialog chrome's fixed typography since inline properties don't shadow CSS custom properties. (3) select.settings-select's max-width:200px truncated longer font names ("Atkinson Hyperlegible") in all four font pickers (UI font, note font, both notes-list fonts) -- changed to max-width:100%. (4) Settings-dialog-specific dark mode background fix: #settings-sheet now uses the regular --bg instead of the shared --bg-dialog (a deliberately lightened value used by every dialog/modal/toast) -- scoped to Settings only, since testers found it overused there specifically while accepting it as an occasional highlight elsewhere (e.g. Help's warning callouts).
  v0.1555 - Two fixes: (1) the Settings dialog now has its own FIXED typography (Atkinson Hyperlegible, 20px, weight 400) instead of tracking live UI font/size/weight changes -- achieved by redefining --font-body/--font-ui/--font-weight-ui at #settings-sheet scope, so every existing var() reference throughout the dialog picks it up automatically with zero other changes. Fixes the disorienting "whole dialog reflows while you're mid-drag on a slider" behavior. (2) new uiLineHeight/uiBlankLineHeight (General) -- dedicated line/paragraph spacing for Help's long-form content specifically, NOT shared with any other dialog/sheet/menu/the tour (all stay fixed, non-configurable) and NOT the note-specific lineHeight/blankLineHeight (viewer-line based, structurally different from Help's plain HTML paragraphs). New sliders + live preview + explanatory text in Settings > App Theme.
  v0.1554 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 3b: migrated the old Text/Typography section, the most-mixed section in the dialog. It was already visually split into "UI Text" and "Note Content Text" subsections, which happened to line up exactly with the General/Note Appearance bucket boundary -- "UI Text" (font face/size/weight) moved into the App Theme zone, "Note Content Text" (note font face/size/weight, line spacing, blank line height, letter/word spacing, number style, ligatures, and the nested Advanced Typography subsection) moved into Note Appearance. Old Text section and its jump-nav entry removed entirely. Extracted and reinserted the ~13KB of HTML programmatically (not by hand-typing OLD strings) specifically to avoid transcription risk on a block this size; verified every affected row id still exists exactly once, both destination zones balance correctly, and the full settings-sheet depth-walks cleanly end to end.
  v0.1553 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 3a of the dialog reorg: three new EMPTY collapsible sections (App Theme / Note Appearance / Note Behavior) added to Settings, using the existing generic section-header collapse wiring (zero new JS needed -- confirmed the delegated #settings-sheet .settings-section-header listener picks up new sections automatically). No settings rows moved yet -- all 12 existing sections remain fully intact and functional. Rows migrate in over the next several builds, verified with the nesting-check script after each. Debugging and the old Themes section are deliberately excluded from migration (see code comment).
  v0.1552 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 2 of 6: the pin/unpin/apply engine for Note Styles and App Themes -- pure functions only, no storage or UI wiring yet. createStyleObject(), pinSetting()/unpinSetting() (explicit action only, never implied by editing a value), pinAllSettings() (one-time action, does NOT retroactively cover settings added later), computeEffectiveSettings() (the actual apply step -- always resets from base then overlays pins, never merges onto live state; this discipline, not sparse-vs-complete storage, is what actually fixes the old per-note "themes" bug), and validatePinsForBucketScope() (rejects a pin outside its style's allowed buckets -- Note Style may only pin Note Appearance/Behavior, App Theme only General; Excluded settings are never pinnable by either).
  v0.1551 - Renamed matchGeneralNoteToolbarColors/matchGeneralNotePaperColors/matchGeneralNoteAccentColor to matchAppThemeNoteToolbarColors/matchAppThemeNotePaperColors/matchAppThemeNoteAccentColor. These three specifically mean "this note-specific setting should defer to a DIFFERENT, non-note-specific setting" (the note toolbar/paper/accent color deferring to the General/App Theme color) -- genuinely different from an ordinary unpinned Note Style setting, which defers to that SAME setting's own base value. The old name conflated the two; the new name marks the cross-setting reference explicitly, per Lynn's correction. matchGeneralNotesListEntryColors intentionally NOT renamed -- both it and lightNotesListEntryBgColor live in the General bucket, so it's a same-bucket reference, not the cross-bucket case the rename is about. CSS variable names (--note-toolbar-bg etc) are unaffected -- only the settings-object keys and their UI element ids changed.
  v0.1550 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase B, step 1 of 6: SETTING_BUCKETS, a complete classification of all 138 DEFAULT_SETTINGS keys into General/Note Appearance/Note Behavior/Excluded, built by cross-referencing Lynn's classification spreadsheet against the ACTUAL shipped code (not the spreadsheet alone -- caught and documented one real divergence: lightBgColor/lightFgColor/darkBgColor/darkFgColor are GENERAL per the shipped Phase A implementation, not "Note appearance" per the spreadsheet's pre-Phase-A plan). Includes this session's two new resolutions (rulerPosition and activeHighlightHex -> Excluded/global-sticky). Pure data, no UI or behavior change -- validated 1:1 against DEFAULT_SETTINGS (zero orphans, zero stale entries) via a new validateSettingBuckets() safety check that runs once at startup and logs loudly (not silently) if the map and DEFAULT_SETTINGS ever drift apart. Foundation for the dialog reorg (step 2) and snapshot engine (step 3+); no visible change in this build.
  v0.1549 - Two real bugs found via testing: (1) Notes list entry background never took effect in LIGHT MODE specifically — a separate, more-specific pre-existing rule (body:not(.dark) .note-item) uses its own --bg-card variable (computed as lightenHex(activeBg,8), a deliberate "cards slightly lighter than page" light-mode design touch) which my v0.1548 code never fed into or overrode; fixed by pointing that rule at --notes-list-entry-bg too, with a corrected fallback that replicates the exact lightened value so matching stays a true visual no-op. (2) "Two things selected at once" in color palettes — found and removed 14 redundant markRow() calls left over from before v0.1539 introduced syncColorRowSelection(); both ran for the same rows (light/dark bg, light/dark fg, accent, highlight-color, both preview-color rows, both note-toolbar rows, all four note-paper rows), a genuine duplicate-logic bug of exactly the kind this project's own philosophy warns about. syncColorRowSelection() is a strict superset of markRow() (handles custom-button selection too, which markRow() never did), so removing the redundant calls is a pure simplification, not a behavior change for the intended case.
  v0.1548 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase A, build 5 of 5 (final Phase A build): new lightNotesListEntryBgColor/darkNotesListEntryBgColor (Profile) + matchGeneralNotesListEntryColors toggle (default ON, visual no-op) -- the note/folder card background on the home screen, independent of General UI background. Same resolveThemedColor + scoped CSS variable pattern as every other Phase A color. Hover/active states (var(--bg3)) intentionally left untouched -- pre-existing, fixed per-theme, not derived from any user color choice, out of scope for this build. This completes Phase A; Phase B (SETTING_BUCKETS classification map + Settings dialog reorg into Profile/Note-appearance/Note-behavior zones) is next whenever Lynn is ready.
  v0.1547 - Retrofit: added live preview boxes above build 4's two new font setting groups (Note & Folder Name Font/Size, Note Preview Font/Size), matching the existing note-font-preview-text pattern -- standing rule going forward, per Lynn: every new font-related setting (face, size, or weight) gets a live preview above it in Settings. New shared updateNotesListFontPreviews(), true top-level (not nested) since it's called from four separate change/input handlers plus openSettings()'s init-sync.
  v0.1546 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase A, build 4 of 5: new notesListNoteNameFont/notesListNoteNameFontSize (Profile; applies to both note and folder names on the home screen) and notesListPreviewFont/notesListPreviewFontSize (Profile). Each falls back to the matching general setting when unset (note name -> settings.font/uiFontSize; preview -> settings.noteFont/editorFontSize -> settings.font), same fallback pattern noteFont itself already uses -- no new "match general" toggle needed. UI reuses the existing native-select font-face pattern (already used for the main note font) rather than generalizing the touch-friendly font-face-bar bottom sheet, since that sheet is specific to being inside an open note and doesn't fit a home-screen-level setting.
  v0.1545 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase A, build 3 of 5: the single wrapNoteNames boolean (which forced BOTH note/folder names AND the note preview to a hardcoded 3 lines together) is now three independent settings, each a 1-4 line count via a segmented control: wrapNoteNames (Profile; note & folder names on the home screen), wrapNotePreview (Profile; the preview line under each note), and wrapNoteNameInNoteEditor (Note Appearance; the note editor's own title). All four affected elements (.note-item-name, .note-item-preview, .folder-item-name, .note-inline-title) now always use the -webkit-line-clamp box technique with a CSS custom property for the line count, instead of a body-class-toggled pair of rulesets — a line count of 1 renders identically to the old single-line-ellipsis default. No migration logic for old true/false values, per the alpha-testers-only, no-backward-compatibility policy already agreed for this project — fresh defaults just start at 1.
  v0.1544 - Profiles & Note Styles Phase A, build 2 of 5: new accentColorNoteEditor (Note Appearance) + matchGeneralNoteAccentColor toggle (default ON, visual no-op). Scoped to the ~18 CSS rules genuinely inside the open note screen: toolbar toggle-button active-glow (move line, bold, italic, color, strikethrough, checkbox), the move-group border, viewer-line drag-over indicators, the heading picker, Edit Paragraph's focus ring, note-tag filtering, tag autocomplete, the tag filter bar's AND/OR button and include-chips, outline actions' check button, and the highlight quick-palette's active ring. Everything else (home screen, Settings, tour, Help, calendar, quick-add, find bar, rename dialogs, voice preview) deliberately stays on General accent — of 144 total var(--accent) usages in the file, only these ~18 are genuinely "about" the note being viewed rather than shared app chrome.
  v0.1543 - CRITICAL FIX: v0.1542's markRow() call for margin-line-color-swatches was inserted in the wrong function scope — markRow() is a helper declared INSIDE updateSwatches(), and my edit landed in code that runs AFTER updateSwatches() actually closes (confirmed by brace-counting: the real closing brace is at a much earlier line than where I inserted). This threw "ReferenceError: markRow is not defined" every single time Settings was opened, breaking the dialog entirely. Moved the call to its correct location, inside updateSwatches(), immediately before its real closing brace. Root cause of this class of mistake: I verified nearby CONTENT looked plausible without verifying the actual function boundary via brace-counting — added as a lesson for future scope-sensitive edits in this file.
  v0.1542 - Systematic scan of every color-swatch row against the CCP keyMap, wireSwatchRow calls, and the selection-ring sync map (requested after the v0.1541 fix). Found and fixed one more real, pre-existing gap: margin-line-color-swatches never showed ANY selection indicator at all (its dialog-open sync only updated the hex label text; its click handler never toggled .selected) — now marks the active preset like every other row. ruler-color-swatches and margin-line-color-swatches were both flagged by the automated scan as "unwired" but turned out to have their own working (non-shared-helper) click handlers on closer inspection — false positives, left alone. Not yet resolved, flagged for a decision: checkbox-color's custom-picker button maps to customColor while its preset row maps to checkboxColor (a different setting) — pre-existing, not touched.
  v0.1541 - ROOT CAUSE FOUND for "note preview text color didn't get applied": the CCP Apply handler's keyMap had NO entry at all for light-preview-color/dark-preview-color, so a custom preview color's Apply button updated the saved-color memory and the button's own appearance but silently never wrote to lightNotePreviewColor/darkNotePreviewColor — genuinely never applied, not just missing visual confirmation. Added the missing keyMap entries and included both rows in the v0.1539 selection-ring sync. Preview BACKGROUND color settings do not exist yet (lightNotesListEntryBgColor/darkNotesListEntryBgColor are planned for a later Phase A build, not yet started).
  v0.1540 - Fixed the lower toolbar's two scroll-fade gradient overlays (.toolbar-scroll-fade-left/-right, the "two rectangles near both scroll arrows") — same hardcoded var(--bg2) bug as the arrows/collapse-tab themselves.
